How WordPress works in QUILL and QUILL Lite: a UX walkthrough

Written 2026-11-14 from the code on branch codex/workbench-combined-2026-11-10, uncommitted tree, while other sessions were still changing WordPress files. It is a snapshot for review, not a user guide.

How to read this

  • Each H2 is one area; each H3 is one feature or command. Jump by heading.
  • Under a feature, bullets say how to reach it, what opens, what each choice does, the exact preview or confirmation wording, what is announced after, what Ctrl+Z does, the F1 help, what Cancel does, what a role without permission hears, and, for a command with no key, its written reason. A bullet is left out when it does not apply; “not found in code” means it was looked for and is not there.
  • Quoted text is what the code says, with placeholders in braces, such as {site} or {title}.
  • “Review notes” under a feature are the UX concerns: oddities, inconsistencies, dead ends and awkward wording. They are collected again, ranked, in the last H2, “Review notes, all in one place”.
  • Keystroke paths spell the access letters: “Alt+F, W, O” means File, then WordPress, then Open Page or Post. Where a submenu has no access letter, the path says to arrow to it.
  • “WordPress key, then O” means press Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Grave (Grave is the key left of 1), let go, then press O.
  • The Workbench’s context menu opens with the Applications key or Shift+F10 on a row. Its first row is what Enter does, Properties is last, and past eight rows the rest go under More.
  • Everything was read from code, not run against a live site or with a screen reader. Where a finding was inferred rather than certain, the text says so (“verify”, “plausible”, “not verified”).
  • “Code:” bullets name the module files, for follow-up.

Keys at a glance

In both editors: the WordPress keys

Press Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Grave, then one letter, within four seconds. QUILL says “WordPress keys” when the layer starts, “Left WordPress keys” on Escape, and “No WordPress command on X.” for a letter with nothing on it. The letters are the same in QUILL and QUILL Lite (quill/core/wordpress/menu.py LAYER_KEYS; quill/core/forms/menu.py for F).

  • A - Connect a Site…
  • B - WordPress Blocks… (View)
  • C - Compare with Site
  • D - Save Draft to Site
  • E - Schedule Publish…
  • F - Responses… (Forms)
  • G - New Page…
  • H - Open WordPress Workbench…
  • Shift+H - This Post in the Workbench
  • I - Open on Site
  • K - Check Before Publishing…
  • L - Open Local Draft…
  • M - Media Library…
  • N - New Post…
  • O - Open Page or Post…
  • P - Publish or Update…
  • R - Properties…
  • S - Switch Site…
  • Shift+S - Sites…
  • T - Move to Trash…
  • U - Upload Files…
  • V - Previous Versions…
  • W - Open by Web Address…
  • X - Alt Text Review…
  • Y - Copy Address
  • J, Q, Z and the digits have no command.

In the editors: the menus

  • File > WordPress: Alt+F, W.
  • Insert, Format, View and Edit take WordPress rows at their ends (Format and Edit inside a WordPress submenu).
  • Tools > WordPress: Alt+T, then arrow to WordPress (the submenu has no access letter in either editor).
  • Tools > Forms holds the Forms commands (see “Forms (QAF)”).

In the WordPress Workbench window

These are the window’s own keys (quill/core/wordpress/workbench_window_menu.py). They act only in the Workbench, never in the editor.

Moving around:

  • F6 and Shift+F6 - next and previous pane (tree, filter, list, details, status bar)
  • Alt+Left, Alt+Right - Back, Forward (Backspace is Back too)
  • Alt+Up - Parent Node
  • Ctrl+9 - Start Here
  • Ctrl+1 - Needs My Attention
  • Ctrl+2 - Current Site
  • Ctrl+3 - Calendar
  • Ctrl+5 - Watches
  • Ctrl+6 - Practice Site
  • Ctrl+0 - Across Sites
  • Ctrl+Shift+1 to Ctrl+Shift+9 - Posts, Pages, Media, Comments, Users, Plugins, Appearance, Settings, Site Health
  • Alt+1 - Where Am I; Alt+2 - Say Selection

The list and its view:

  • Ctrl+E - Filter; Ctrl+Shift+F - Filter Builder; Ctrl+Alt+O - Show Only Like This; Ctrl+Alt+H - Hide Like This; Ctrl+Alt+M - Only Mine
  • F5 - Refresh; Ctrl+Shift+L - Load More; Ctrl+Alt+L - Load All; Ctrl+Alt+C - Columns; Ctrl+Alt+G - Group By
  • Ctrl+Shift+X - Export List; Ctrl+Shift+I - Import Posts from a Spreadsheet; Ctrl+Alt+Shift+I - Import a Folder of Documents

The selected row:

  • Enter - its default action; Alt+Enter - Properties; Shift+Enter - Open on the Site; Ctrl+Enter - Preview on the Site; Ctrl+Shift+C - Copy Link
  • Ctrl+N - New Here
  • Ctrl+Shift+P - Publish or Update
  • Ctrl+Shift+A - Approve, or Not Spam; Ctrl+Shift+U - Unapprove; Ctrl+Shift+S - Spam; Ctrl+Shift+M - Reply; Ctrl+Alt+W - Not Spam, and Allow This Sender
  • Ctrl+Shift+G - Merge Into; Ctrl+Shift+K - Convert to Category or Tag
  • Ctrl+Shift+D - Approve and Schedule; Ctrl+Shift+B - Send Back with a Note; Ctrl+Shift+J - Assign a Reviewer; Ctrl+Alt+Shift+D - Move to Another Day
  • Ctrl+Shift+Y - Copy to Site; Ctrl+Shift+W - Send Password Reset; Ctrl+Alt+Shift+M - Show Its Comments
  • Ctrl+Alt+N - Entry Notes; Ctrl+Alt+F - Star or Unstar Entry; Ctrl+Alt+U - Mark Entry Read or Unread; Ctrl+Alt+I - Resend Notifications (Gravity Forms entries)
  • Delete - Move to Trash; Ctrl+Shift+R - Restore from Trash; Shift+Delete - Delete Permanently
  • Ctrl+Z - Undo WordPress Change; Ctrl+Shift+H - Change History (Delete, Shift+Delete and Ctrl+Z act only in the list)

The site and the window:

  • Alt+Shift+Enter - Site Properties; Shift+F1 - What Can I Do Here?; Ctrl+Alt+V - View As Role; Ctrl+Alt+A - Workbench Access; Ctrl+Alt+R - Roles and What They May Do
  • Ctrl+Alt+E - Empty Spam; Ctrl+Alt+Shift+E - Empty Comment Trash; Ctrl+Alt+Shift+C - Comment Counts; Ctrl+Alt+Shift+P - Your Profile
  • Ctrl+Alt+K - Compare Sites; Ctrl+Alt+T - Site Sets; Ctrl+Alt+Y - Across Sites Scope; Ctrl+Alt+S - Take a Snapshot; Ctrl+Alt+Shift+S - Put Back from a Snapshot; Ctrl+Alt+D - Read Site as Document
  • Ctrl+Shift+O - Workbench Options; Ctrl+Shift+T - REST Console; Ctrl+Alt+Q - Run a Saved Request; Ctrl+Alt+Shift+F - Forget a Saved Request; Ctrl+Alt+B - Plain-Language Batch; Ctrl+Alt+P - Practice Site
  • Ctrl+Shift+E - Switch to Editor; Alt+F4 - Close Workbench; F1 - Help for This View

Review notes:

  • Three of these chords are reported not to fire as listed, because the window’s key hook runs first: Ctrl+Shift+F and Ctrl+Alt+Shift+F move to the filter box, and Ctrl+Alt+R refreshes (see “The WordPress Workbench window”).
  • Several layer letters differ from the menu access letter of the same row (Check Before Publishing is P in the menu, K on the layer; Media Library is B in the menu, M on the layer), so the menu does not always teach the key.

Getting connected

Everything WordPress in QUILL hangs off one feature switch, “core.wordpress”, and a list of connected sites kept in “wordpress-sites.json” in QUILL’s data folder (names, addresses, usernames, roles and WordPress versions only; each site’s application password is kept in Windows Credential Manager, keyed by the site’s id). A site is connected with an application password, typed by hand or fetched through the browser. One site in the list is the “default site”, which is only where New and Open start; a document carries its own site for life. Switch Site changes the default, the Sites window manages the list, and Sign Out This Site (in Tools > WordPress > Manage) revokes and forgets a connection. Nearly every command that needs a site and finds none says “Connect a WordPress site first.” and opens the Connect dialog. On first load, any WordPress connections saved by the older publishing framework are imported once.

The WordPress feature switch

  • What it is: the feature id “core.wordpress”, named “WordPress” in the feature catalogue. Description in code: “Write, open, edit and publish WordPress pages and posts on as many sites as you like, self-hosted or WordPress.com, and manage their Media Library, comments, categories and tags, from File > WordPress. Nothing reaches a site without Save Draft to Site or Publish. Replaces the older Publishing features. On by default in every profile except Essential.”
  • How to reach it: Tools > Customize and Support > Profiles and Features… (“Alt+T, U, P”). The Essential profile sets it to off; every other profile leaves it on.
  • When off: File > WordPress, and the WordPress rows in Insert, Format, View, Edit and Tools, are not built at all; the commands are not registered, so they are not in the Command Palette either (rows are registered as they are appended to the menu); the WordPress key prefix is ignored (the char hook checks the feature before starting the layer), so Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Grave passes through to the editor.
  • Every command id starting “wordpress.” maps to this feature (features.py feature_for_command).
  • Code: features.py, feature_catalog.py, main_frame_wordpress.py.
  • Review notes:
    • The catalogue description promises WordPress.com, but WordPress.com sign-in is not available in this build (see Connect a Site). The description is the first thing a person reads when deciding to turn the feature on.
    • The description says “from File > WordPress”, but since the 2026-11 placement most commands live in Insert, Format, View, Edit and Tools. A person told to look in File will not find Media Library, Comments or Check Before Publishing there.

Connect a Site

  • How to reach it: File > WordPress > Connect a Site… (“Alt+F, W, A”). WordPress key, then A (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Grave, then A). Also from the Sites window’s Add… button, from any site command when no site is connected, and from Open by Web Address when the address is not on a connected site. Not document-only.
  • What opens: a dialog titled “Connect a WordPress Site” (resizable). Controls in tab order:
    • “&Problem:” read-only multi-line field, present only when re-shown after a failure; holds the reason.
    • “&Kind of site:” choice: “My own WordPress site (self-hosted)” (default) or “A WordPress.com site”. Choosing WordPress.com disables the Username and Application password fields.
    • “Site &address:” edit field.
    • “&Username:” edit field.
    • “Application &password:” password field.
    • “&Name for this site (optional):” edit field.
    • “Get Password with My &Browser” button.
    • “Connect” (default button, the OK button) and “Cancel”. Neither carries an access key, on purpose (GATE-14).
  • Connect with an empty Site address on a self-hosted site: focus goes back to Site address and the computer beeps (wx.Bell). Nothing is spoken.
  • Get Password with My Browser with an empty Site address: the same beep and focus move.
  • The address is tidied before use: spaces and a trailing slash removed, and “https://” added when there is no scheme. An “http://” address is kept as typed and then refused at the first request with: “QUILL only connects to WordPress sites over HTTPS, so your password is never sent in the clear. Check that the address starts with https://.” (the one exception is the local Practice Site address).
  • The manual route (Connect pressed): if Username or Application password is empty the dialog comes back with the Problem field reading “Enter the username and the application password, or press Get Password with My Browser.” Otherwise QUILL reads the account and the site’s own name in the background (“WordPress: connecting”), and only then saves. The site’s name is, in order, what you typed in Name, the site’s own title, the existing entry’s name, or the address. Reconnecting with the same address and username updates the existing entry (same id) rather than adding a second one.
  • The browser route (Get Password with My Browser): QUILL first reads the site’s REST index in the background (“reading the site”).
    • If the site has no Authorize Application address, the dialog comes back with: “This site has application passwords turned off, so QUILL cannot ask for one. Ask the site’s administrator to turn them on, or make one in your WordPress profile if you can.”
    • Tier 1, WordPress 7.0 or later (an assumption in signin.py, marked “Not yet confirmed”): QUILL listens once on 127.0.0.1 for up to 300 seconds, opens the site’s Authorize Application page in the browser, and says “Approve QUILL in your browser. QUILL is waiting for .” (or “the site” when it has no name). The browser tab, after approval or refusal, shows a small page headed “You’re signed in” or “QUILL was not connected”, with “You can close this tab and go back to QUILL.”
    • Tier 2, older sites: QUILL opens the Authorize Application page and immediately shows “Paste the Application Password”, with one field “Application &password:” (password style) and “Connect”/“Cancel”. If the clipboard already holds something shaped like an application password (24 letters and digits, run together or in six groups of four), it is put in the field. If the username was left empty, a second dialog “Your WordPress Username” asks “&Username:”. Then the manual route’s verify-and-save runs.
  • Announced after:
    • Success: “Connected to as .”
    • Manual-route failure: the dialog reopens with the failure in the Problem field (for example “ did not accept the sign-in. Check the username and application password, then reconnect.”).
    • Browser tier 1, refused in the browser: “You chose not to connect QUILL. Nothing was saved.”
    • Browser tier 1, nothing came back in 300 seconds: “QUILL stopped waiting for the browser. Try again when you are ready.”
    • WordPress.com chosen: “WordPress.com sign-in is not available in this build yet. If your WordPress.com plan allows plugins, connect with an application password at your site’s own address instead.” The dialog has already closed.
    • Failure reading the site on the browser route: the failure sentence is spoken (the dialog does not reopen).
  • F1: dialog: “Connect QUILL to a WordPress site so you can write, open and publish its pages and posts. You can connect as many sites as you like.” Kind: “Self-hosted means the site runs on its own web address and hosting. Choose WordPress.com for a site hosted by WordPress.com; you will sign in with your browser.” Site address: “The site’s web address, for example https://example.org. QUILL only connects over HTTPS, so your password is never sent in the clear.” Username: “The username or email you sign in to WordPress with.” Application password: “An application password: a separate password WordPress makes for one app. It is not the password you sign in to the site with. Press Get Password with My Browser and QUILL fetches one for you; or make one in your WordPress profile and paste it here.” Name: “What QUILL calls this site in lists and confirmations. Leave it empty to use the site’s own title.” Browser button: “Opens the site’s own Authorize Application page in your browser. Approve QUILL there and the password comes back to QUILL by itself; on an older site, WordPress shows it to you to copy and paste here. For WordPress.com this is how you sign in.” Problem: “Why the last attempt to connect did not work, and what to try.” Paste dialog: “WordPress is showing a new application password in your browser. Copy it there, come back, and paste it here.”; its field: “Paste the application password WordPress showed you. It is 24 letters and numbers, often in groups of four.” Username dialog: “The username you approved QUILL with in the browser.”
  • Cancel: closes silently; nothing saved. Cancel on the Paste dialog also silent. Cancel on the Username dialog leaves the username empty, which reopens Connect with the “Enter the username and the application password” problem.
  • Keyless: has a key (A).
  • Code: wordpress_connect.py (ConnectFlow), wordpress_dialogs.py (ConnectSiteDialog, PastePasswordDialog), signin.py, signin_loopback.py, signin_base.py, sites.py, api.py.
  • Review notes:
    • When the dialog comes back after a failure, the password field is always empty: ConnectSiteDialog never fills it from the previous answer. Any failure (a typo in the address, a slow host, a 500) means retyping or re-fetching the application password. On the browser route this is worse: the fetched password is thrown away and a new one must be minted, leaving an orphan password on the site each time.
    • Tier 2 reads the clipboard the instant the browser opens, before WordPress has even shown the password, so the “offer” is whatever was on the clipboard before. The module docstring says it offers the value “when focus comes back”; the code does not.
    • Tier 2 asks for a username when it is empty and, if cancelled, reopens Connect with an empty password field, so the pasted password is lost.
    • The Problem field’s “&Problem:” and the password field’s “Application &password:” both claim P whenever the error is shown, so Alt+P cycles between them instead of pressing (a GATE-14 duplicate that only exists on the retry, which is when a person is most likely to need it).
    • WordPress.com is offered as a choice, its help says “you will sign in with your browser”, and the browser button’s help says “For WordPress.com this is how you sign in”; the result is a dead end sentence after the dialog has already closed. Either hide the choice or keep the dialog open with the explanation in the Problem field.
    • “Approve QUILL in your browser…” is spoken at the moment the browser takes focus, so the screen reader is likely to talk over it. There is no way to cancel the 300-second wait from QUILL, and a listener failure is swallowed silently (the failure callback only cancels the listener).
    • The browser’s done page says “You’re signed in” before QUILL has verified anything; if verification then fails, the browser and QUILL disagree.
    • A failure on the browser route speaks a sentence and drops you back in the editor, while the same failure on the manual route reopens the dialog with a Problem field. Two behaviours for one dialog.
    • A malformed or state-mismatched reply from WordPress raises inside the success callback; no code here turns that into a spoken sentence (not found in code).
    • Connect and Get Password both beep with no words when the address is empty. A beep is not an explanation; a sentence in the Problem field, or speaking “Type the site’s address first”, would be.
    • “then reconnect” in the 401 sentence names an action with no command of that name; the way to replace a password is to run Connect a Site again with the same address and username, which nothing tells you.

Sites

  • How to reach it: File > WordPress > Sites… (“Alt+F, W, S”). WordPress key, then Shift+S. Not document-only.
  • What opens: a list window titled “WordPress Sites”. Controls in tab order:
    • “&Sites:” list. Each row: “, , ” (or “WordPress.com” in place of the host for a WordPress.com site), with “, default” on the default site. The first row is selected.
    • Buttons: “&Add…”, “&Rename…”, “&Verify”, “Make &Default”, “Re&move…”, “&Which Password”, “Close” (no access key; Escape).
    • There is no default button and no Enter action on a row.
  • What each button does:
    • Add…: runs Connect a Site (above).
    • Rename…: dialog “Rename Site”, field “&Name:” filled with the current name, OK/Cancel. OK with a non-blank name saves it and the list refreshes. Nothing is spoken.
    • Verify: in the background, reads the account. Announced: “ accepts QUILL. You are there.” The stored role is updated. Failure: the plain failure sentence.
    • Make Default: saves and says “ is now your default site.”
    • Remove…: confirmation titled “Remove Site”: “Remove from QUILL? This forgets the connection and its password on this computer. Nothing on the site is changed, and your local drafts are kept.” Buttons “Remove” and “Cancel”; Cancel is the default (NO_DEFAULT). On Remove: “ removed from QUILL.” The application password is not revoked on the site.
    • Which Password: in the background asks the site which application password QUILL signs with. Announced: “On , QUILL is using the application password named , made , last used .” or “ did not say which application password QUILL is using.” On a site older than 5.9: “ cannot say which application password QUILL is using. That needs WordPress 5.9 or later.” For a WordPress.com token: “QUILL signs in to with a WordPress.com token, not an application password.”
  • F1: window: “Your connected WordPress sites. The default site is where New and Open start; every document stays with the site it came from.” List: “Each site’s name, your role on it, and where it is.” Buttons: “Connect another WordPress site.”, “Change what QUILL calls the selected site.”, “Check the selected site still accepts QUILL’s sign-in.”, “Use the selected site for New and Open.”, “Forget the selected site on this computer.”, “Say which of your application passwords QUILL signs in to the selected site with. Sign Out This Site revokes exactly that one.” Rename field: “What QUILL calls this site in lists and confirmations.” Close: “Close this window. Escape does the same.”
  • With no sites: the window still opens with an empty list; every button except Add… is disabled. No sentence says the list is empty.
  • Cancel: Close or Escape closes silently.
  • Code: wordpress_commands.py (cmd_sites), wordpress_dialogs.py (ListWindow), wordpress_api_writing_commands.py (which_password_action), site_features.py (password_sentence).
  • Review notes:
    • Add… is backwards. If you cancel the Connect dialog, the whole Sites window closes (the action returns CLOSE when the flow returns None). If you complete it, the list refreshes immediately, before the background verification has saved the new site, so the site you just added is not in the list until you close and reopen the window.
    • Verify, Which Password and Remove all go through the site client, which on first use in a session also counts waiting comments and may speak “: N comments waiting.” in the middle of a sign-in check (the same is true of Sign Out). Unrelated news arriving with a diagnostic.
    • Remove forgets the password locally but leaves it live on the site; Sign Out This Site (which does revoke it) lives in a different menu (Tools > WordPress > Manage). A person managing sites in this window will not find the safer action here.
    • There is no way to change the address, username or password of an existing site from this window. “Verify” can only tell you it is broken.
    • Sites and Switch Site both change the default site (Make Default, and Switch Site), with different sentences: “ is now your default site.” versus “Default site: .”

Switch Site

  • How to reach it: File > WordPress > Switch Site… (“Alt+F, W, H”). WordPress key, then S. Not document-only.
  • What opens: with two or more sites, a choice dialog titled “Switch Site”, list “&Site:” of rows “, , ”, with the default site selected; “OK” (default) and “Cancel”.
  • Announced after: “Default site: .” With one site: “ is your only site.” With none: “Connect a WordPress site first.” and the Connect dialog opens.
  • F1: “Choose which of your WordPress sites to use. Your default site is selected.” (the list adds “ Press Enter to choose the selected one.“).
  • Cancel: silent.
  • Code: wordpress_commands.py (cmd_switch_site, pick_site, need_site).
  • Review notes:
    • “Switch Site” suggests the document in front moves to another site; it only changes where New and Open start. The help text (“Choose which of your WordPress sites to use”) does not say so either.
    • It has the everyday key S while the Sites list has Shift+S, yet Switch Site is a subset of the Sites window’s Make Default.
    • After “Connect a WordPress site first.” the Connect dialog opens at once, so the sentence is very likely talked over by the dialog title. After connecting, the original command does not resume. This is true of every command that calls need_site.

Sign Out This Site

  • How to reach it: Tools > WordPress > Manage > Sign Out This Site… (Tools > WordPress has no access letter, so this is “Alt+T”, arrow to WordPress, then “M, I”). No WordPress key. Not document-only. Listed here because it is the other half of connecting.
  • What opens: with more than one site, the “Sign Out This Site” site chooser (as in Switch Site). Then a choice dialog titled “Sign Out This Site”, list “&After signing out:” with “Remove from QUILL” and “Keep in the list, to connect again later”; “OK” (default) and “Cancel”.
  • Then in the background: QUILL asks which application password it signs with and revokes exactly that one on the site, then forgets it on this computer and, for the first choice, removes the site from the list.
  • Announced after: “Signed out of : the password is revoked on the site and forgotten here, and the site is removed from QUILL.” (or “kept in your list”). When the site would not say which password: “Signed out of : the password is forgotten here, and the site is kept in your list. The site did not say which password QUILL used, so revoke it in Application Passwords or the dashboard if you want it gone there too.”
  • Failure: if the site cannot be reached, the failure sentence is spoken and nothing is forgotten locally.
  • F1: “Signing out revokes QUILL’s application password on itself and forgets it on this computer. Your local drafts are kept either way.”
  • Cancel: silent at either step.
  • Keyless: no chord; no written reason found (Tools rows are not in DEFAULT_KEYMAP, so the bound-command gate never sees them).
  • Code: wordpress_people_commands.py (cmd_sign_out), site_features.py.
  • Review notes:
    • The confirmation is a two-row list with a generic “OK” button. The action is not reversible on the site, so the button should say “Sign Out”, as Remove Site’s says “Remove”.
    • “Keep in the list, to connect again later” leaves a site in the list with no password; every later command on it will fail with the 401 sentence. Nothing marks such a site in the Sites list.
    • It lives four levels deep under Tools while Connect a Site, Sites and Switch Site are in File > WordPress.

When no site is connected

  • Commands that need a site (New Page, New Post, Open Page or Post, Switch Site, Send This Document to WordPress, Send a Folder as Drafts, Duplicate, Download My Writing, Sign Out, and others) call need_site: they say “Connect a WordPress site first.” and open the Connect dialog. On cancel, nothing more is said.
  • Open by Web Address says “That address is not on any of your connected sites. Connect the site first, then try again.” and opens Connect with the address’s scheme and host filled in.
  • Document-only commands reached outside a WordPress document say one of two things. Commands that pass their name say “ works in a WordPress page or post. Open one with File, WordPress.” Most do not, and say the generic: “This is not a WordPress document. Open one with Open Page or Post, or start one with New Page or New Post, in the WordPress menu under File.”
  • A WordPress document whose site has been removed: “The site this document belongs to is no longer in your list of sites. Connect it again with Connect a Site, and the document will find it.”
  • Review notes:
    • Which of the two “not a WordPress document” sentences you hear depends on whether the handler happened to pass its name; Save Draft, Publish, Properties, Open on Site, Copy Address, Trash, Compare, every block command and Image Size use the generic one.
    • “Connect it again with Connect a Site, and the document will find it” is only true when you reconnect with the same address and username and the old id survives; a removed site’s id is gone, so the document’s site id will not match a fresh connection (sites.py upsert matches by id, and a new connection after Remove gets a new id). Not verified at runtime, but nothing in the connect code looks up an orphaned document’s site id.

The editor’s WordPress commands

In QUILL the WordPress commands are spread over the editor’s own menus. File > WordPress (“Alt+F, W”) holds what is about a whole document or a site, including a “Send and Copy” submenu. Insert, View and the Format > WordPress and Edit > WordPress submenus hold what you do inside a page. The table is quill/core/wordpress/menu.py; QUILL builds it in main_frame_wordpress.py, each row calling one shared handler on WordPressCommands. Rows in DOCUMENT_ONLY are dimmed unless a WordPress document is in front; reached another way (the WordPress keys, the Command Palette) the handler speaks why. Any row with a chord shows it in brackets at the end of its menu label, for example “Open Page or Post… (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Grave, O)”, because a two-press sequence cannot sit after the tab in a wx menu label. Every command that touches the site runs on a background task named “WordPress: ” and speaks its outcome in one sentence; failures speak the site’s plain error sentence (for example “Your account on is not allowed to do that.”). No WordPress command has an alias in DEFAULT_ALIASES.

The WordPress keys (the layer)

  • How to reach it: press Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Grave (the key above Tab), release, then press one key within 4 seconds.
  • Spoken: “WordPress keys” when the prefix is pressed. Escape: “Left WordPress keys”. A letter with no command: “No WordPress command on X.” (with X spelled as the layer saw it, modifiers included).
  • The letters (LAYER_KEYS, plus the Forms key): G New Page, N New Post, O Open Page or Post, W Open by Web Address, L Open Local Draft, D Save Draft to Site, P Publish or Update, E Schedule Publish, I Open on Site, Y Copy Address, R Properties, V Previous Versions, C Compare with Site, T Move to Trash, S Switch Site, Shift+S Sites, A Connect a Site, M Media Library, B WordPress Blocks, K Check Before Publishing, U Upload Files, X Alt Text Review, H Open WordPress Workbench, Shift+H This Post in the Workbench, and F Forms Responses (from forms/menu.py).
  • Rules in code: the letters are read from the live keymap, so a key moved in the Keyboard Manager moves here too. A non-character key (arrow, Tab, Enter) leaves the layer silently and goes on to the editor. A bare modifier going down (Shift on the way to Shift+S) is ignored. A key pressed after the 4 seconds is not the layer’s and is typed as usual. Focus leaving or a menu opening cancels silently. If the user has put the QUILL key on Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Grave, the QUILL key wins and the WordPress layer never starts. The prefix only works while the WordPress feature is on.
  • Document-only commands run from the layer even when their menu row is dimmed; the handler then speaks why.
  • Code: keys.py, menu.py, main_frame_wordpress.py (_wordpress_key_event, _wordpress_prefix_matches), keymap.py (DEFAULT_KEYMAP via default_bindings).
  • Review notes:
    • Any modifier still held when the letter is pressed makes a different key. Holding Ctrl+Alt+Shift through from the prefix and pressing O says “No WordPress command on Ctrl+Alt+Shift+O.” Holding Shift only turns O into “Shift+O” and S into Sites instead of Switch Site. Since the prefix itself needs Shift, this will happen often; the layer could ignore modifiers held over from the prefix, or the “No WordPress command” sentence could say “release the keys first”.
    • The timeout is silent. Press the prefix, think for five seconds, press P, and a “p” lands in the document with nothing said. “Left WordPress keys” on timeout, as on Escape, would cost one sentence.
    • The menus spell the chord as “(Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Grave, O)” on every row, read aloud in full on every visit to File > WordPress. The QUILL key is displayed as “QUILL Key + S”; the WordPress layer gets no friendly name (“WordPress Key + O” would be shorter and match the spoken “WordPress keys”).
    • Only 24 of the about 45 editor-side commands have a letter. The per-page navigation commands (Next Block, Previous Block, Where Am I), the most frequent things a writer does in a block document, have none, and nothing records a reason (see “Keyless” below).
    • A command failure from the layer goes through _run_command, which reports exceptions with _set_status (status bar), not speech (not verified that the status bar is spoken).

About “Keyless” for this section

  • Rows with no chord in QUILL: Custom Fields, Plugin Fields, all five Send and Copy rows, all five Edit > WordPress rows, Preview Blocks, Block Notes, Play Media Here, all seven Insert rows, and all three Format > WordPress rows.
  • No written reason was found for any of them. They are not in DEFAULT_KEYMAP at all (only the layer letters are), and the bound-command gate (tests/unit/core/test_family_rules_and_gates.py, “every editor command has a key or a written reason”) only walks DEFAULT_KEYMAP, so it never sees them. Rule 8 (“every command has a key or a written reason”) is therefore unenforced for WordPress.

File > WordPress (the submenu itself)

  • How to reach it: File > WordPress, “Alt+F, W”. The access letter W is free in the File menu (File’s own rows use N, O, P and others, not W).
  • Within the submenu every row has a unique access letter (checked: P, N, O, W, L, D, U, E, I, Y, K, R, F, G, V, M, T, C, H, S, A).
  • Review notes:
    • The submenu has 22 rows and four separators plus a submenu; with each label carrying “(Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Grave, X)”, arrowing through it is long.
    • Several access letters are not the word’s first letter and do not match the layer key (New Page is P in the menu and G on the layer; Publish is U in the menu and P on the layer; This Post in the Workbench is K in the menu and Shift+H on the layer; Compare is M in the menu and C on the layer; Sites is S in the menu and Shift+S on the layer, while S on the layer is Switch Site whose menu letter is H). The menu.py comment says “A row’s own access letter wherever it is free, so the menu teaches the key”, but for these rows the menu teaches a different key from the layer.

New Page

  • How to reach it: File > WordPress > New Page… (“Alt+F, W, P”). WordPress key, then G. Not document-only.
  • What opens: dialog “New Page”. Controls: “&Site:” choice (default site selected), “&Title:” field, “S&lug:” field (filled from the title as you type until you edit it), “Write in” radio box “Markdown” / “HTML” (last choice remembered for the session), then “Create” (default) and “Cancel”. Pages show no categories or tags.
  • Announced after: “New page for . Ctrl+S keeps it on this computer; nothing is sent until you choose Save Draft to Site or Publish.” The new document opens in the editor.
  • F1: dialog: “Start a new page. Nothing is sent to the site until you choose Save Draft to Site or Publish.” Site: “Which of your sites this belongs to. It stays with that site for good.” Title: “The title. Your theme shows it at the top of the page, so the body does not need it again as a heading.” Slug: “The last part of the web address. It is filled in from the title as you type; leave it to keep the suggestion.” Write in: “Markdown is readable text characters for headings, lists and links; QUILL turns it into HTML when you send it. HTML is for exact control. This cannot change later.”
  • Cancel: silent.
  • No site: “Connect a WordPress site first.” and Connect opens.
  • Code: wordpress_commands.py (_new), wordpress_item_dialogs.py (NewItemDialog).
  • Review notes:
    • The “Write in” radio box has no access letter, and its label is not “&Write in”; every other field in the dialog has one.
    • The site is chosen inside the dialog, but for posts categories and tags are loaded from the default site before the dialog opens; choosing another site silently drops any categories and tags checked (the code sets them only when the chosen site is the default). Not an issue for pages, but the same dialog is used.

New Post

  • How to reach it: File > WordPress > New Post… (“Alt+F, W, N”). WordPress key, then N. Not document-only.
  • What opens: first a background read of the default site’s categories and tags (“loading categories and tags”), then dialog “New Post”, the same as New Page plus two check-box groups, “Categories” and “Tags”, when the site has any.
  • Announced after: “New post for . Ctrl+S keeps it on this computer; nothing is sent until you choose Save Draft to Site or Publish.”
  • F1: as New Page (“Start a new post…”); category group: “Check each category this post belongs to. Space checks or unchecks.”; tag group: “Check each tag for this post. Space checks or unchecks.”
  • Failure: if the site cannot be reached for its categories, the failure sentence is spoken and no dialog opens, so you cannot start a post offline.
  • Code: wordpress_commands.py, wordpress_item_dialogs.py.
  • Review notes:
    • Categories and tags are lost without a word if you change the Site choice away from the default (see New Page).
    • Starting a post needs the network; starting a page does not. A writer offline cannot start a post at all, and the sentence they hear is a connection error, not “QUILL needs the site to list categories”.
    • A site with hundreds of tags puts hundreds of check boxes in a scrolled group with no filter.

Open Page or Post

  • How to reach it: File > WordPress > Open Page or Post… (“Alt+F, W, O”). WordPress key, then O. Not document-only.
  • What opens, in order:
    • With several sites, the “Open Page or Post” site chooser.
    • Background “reading what holds”, then a choice “Open Page or Post”, list “&Show:” with “Posts”, “Pages” and any other content types the site lists.
    • Background “listing on ”, then a list window “Open from ”: “&Find by title or status:” field (Enter filters), “&Posts:” / “&Pages:” / “&Items:” list (rows “, <status>, last changed <date>”, every status including Trash), buttons “&Open”, “Move to &Trash…”, “&Restore from Trash”, “Close”. Enter or double-click on a row opens it.</li> <li>If you already have a working copy of that item, it opens straight away.</li> <li>Otherwise, background load, then a check for a newer autosave: if there is one, choice “Unsaved Version”, prompt “There is an unsaved version from <when>. Open that &instead?”, rows “Yes, open the unsaved version from <when>” and “No, open the copy on the site”.</li> <li>Then choice “Open <title>”, prompt “Open &as:”, rows “HTML, exactly as the site has it” (selected) and “Markdown, easier to read; anything Markdown cannot show is kept exactly”.</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: “Opened <title> from <site>.” For an existing working copy: “Opened your working copy of <title>. Compare with Site shows what changed on the site since.” Empty list: “<site> has no <kind>s yet.”</li> <li>Move to Trash and Restore from Trash in this window: as Move to Trash below; restore says “‘<title>’ is back on <site> as a draft. Open it to publish it again.” or “‘<title>’ is not in the Trash.”</li> <li>F1: Show: “Posts are dated articles in the blog or news. Pages are lasting pages like About or Contact. Any other kinds are the site’s own, such as events.” Window: “Every <kind> on <site>, newest change first. Enter opens the selected one.” List: “Each row says the title, its status, and when it last changed.” Filter: “Type part of a title, or a status such as draft or trash, and press Enter.” Open as: “HTML keeps every block exactly. Markdown shows ordinary text plainly and puts other blocks on short ::: lines; nothing is lost either way.” Unsaved version: “An autosave newer than the copy on the site was found, from a browser or from QUILL. Whichever you open, the other stays on the site.”</li> <li>Cancel: silent at every step.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_open_commands.py, wordpress_page_commands.py (offer_newer_autosave, restore_listed), wordpress_dialogs.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Four to five dialogs to open one post (site, kind, list, autosave, format). The format question comes every time and always starts on HTML; New remembers the last format, Open does not.</li> <li>The status filter only matches the raw status: rows say “Published” but typing “published” finds nothing; you must type “publish”. “Pending review” rows need “pending”, “Scheduled” needs “future”.</li> <li>Trash and Restore from this window do not refresh the list; the row still says its old status until you close and reopen.</li> <li>Opening an item you already have a working copy of opens the local copy without checking the site, so you may edit a stale copy; the sentence tells you to run Compare yourself.</li> <li>Trashed items are mixed in with live ones in the default list.</li> <li>“<site> has no <kind>s yet.” builds the plural by adding “s” to the type slug, so a custom type such as “tribe_events” is read as “tribe_eventss”.</li> <li>“Opened <title> from <site>.” follows a document opening, whose new window title the reader announces anyway; the site name is the only new information.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="open-by-web-address">Open by Web Address</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: File > WordPress > Open by Web Address… (“Alt+F, W, W”). WordPress key, then W. Not document-only.</li> <li>What opens: if the clipboard holds anything starting “http://” or “https://”, nothing opens; that address is used at once. Otherwise a dialog “Open by Web Address”, field “Web &address:”, OK/Cancel.</li> <li>Then the address is matched to a connected site by host (ignoring “www.”), and found by “?p=”, “?page_id=”, “?post=”, its slug, or a custom type’s slug; then it opens as in Open Page or Post (autosave offer, then Open as).</li> <li>Announced after: as Open Page or Post. Not on any site: “That address is not on any of your connected sites. Connect the site first, then try again.” and the Connect dialog opens with that site’s address. Not found: “QUILL could not find a page or post at that address on <site>. It may be an archive or a category page, which are not single items.”</li> <li>F1: “Paste or type the address of any page or post on one of your sites.”</li> <li>Cancel: silent.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_open_commands.py (cmd_open_by_address, find_by_address).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Using the clipboard without asking is a trap: any web address copied for another reason is used silently, and if it is not on a connected site you are dropped into the Connect dialog for a stranger’s site. At minimum the dialog should open with the clipboard address filled in, so the person can see and change it.</li> <li>Nothing is spoken when the clipboard is used, so the person does not know which address QUILL took.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="open-local-draft">Open Local Draft</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: File > WordPress > Open Local Draft… (“Alt+F, W, L”). WordPress key, then L. Not document-only.</li> <li>What opens: choice “Open Local Draft”, list “&Drafts:” with rows “<title or (untitled)>, <kind>, <site name or ‘a site no longer connected’>, saved <date>”, OK/Cancel.</li> <li>Announced after: nothing; the document opens. No drafts: “You have no local WordPress drafts yet.”</li> <li>F1: “Your WordPress working copies on this computer, newest first.”</li> <li>Cancel: silent.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_open_commands.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Silent on success, while Open Page or Post says “Opened <title> from <site>.” Inconsistent, though the silent one is the GATE-13-friendlier of the two.</li> <li>“Drafts” here means every working copy, including copies of published posts; the name suggests only unsent drafts.</li> <li>No filter on a list that will grow to every item ever opened.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="save-draft-to-site">Save Draft to Site</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: File > WordPress > Save Draft to Site (“Alt+F, W, D”). WordPress key, then D. Document-only.</li> <li>What happens: QUILL saves the file first (if that fails, it stops silently), then sends in the background (“WordPress: sending to <site>”). On a published item, a choice first: title “Save Draft to Site”, prompt “This <page or post> is published. Save as a draft and take it off the site, or update the published <page or post>? &Choose:”, rows “Update the published <kind>” (selected) and “Save as a draft and take it off the site”.</li> <li>If Markdown lines could not become blocks: “<N> lines could not be turned into blocks; Check Before Publishing lists them.” (the send continues).</li> <li>Conflict (the site changed since you opened it): “The copy on <site> was changed on <when>, after you opened it. Nothing was sent.” then choice “The Site Copy Changed”, “&What now:”, rows “Compare the two”, “Open the site’s copy”, “Send mine anyway”. Send mine anyway confirms: “Replace the newer copy on <site> with yours? The site’s copy is kept in Previous Versions.” buttons “Send Mine”/“Cancel”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Draft saved to <site>. It is not public.” For a pending item: “Submitted for review on <site>.” Network failure: QUILL re-reads the site; if nothing landed, a confirm “Not Sent”: “‘<title>’ did not reach <site>. QUILL checked the site and nothing was changed there. Your copy is kept on this computer. Try again now?” buttons “Try Again”/“Cancel”; if the site is still unreachable: “<site> cannot be reached, so ‘<title>’ is queued. QUILL sends it when the site answers again; your copy is kept on this computer.”</li> <li>Outside a WordPress document: the generic “This is not a WordPress document…” sentence.</li> <li>Without permission: the site’s sentence, for example “Your account on <site> is not allowed to change this item.” or “Your account on <site> can only change items you wrote yourself.”</li> <li>Undo: nothing on the site to undo; Previous Versions keeps the site’s copy from before each update.</li> <li>F1: conflict list: “Someone changed the copy on the site after you opened yours. Nothing was sent.” Save Draft choice: the question plus “Update keeps it on the site with your changes; a draft is only seen by people who can edit it.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_more_commands.py (_send, send_work, _conflict, _sent), wordpress_offline_commands.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Choosing “Update the published <kind>” from Save Draft updates the live page with no further confirmation, while Publish or Update asks “Update ‘<title>’ on <site>?”. Save Draft is the command people press when they think it is safe.</li> <li>The conflict sentence is announced immediately before the conflict dialog opens, so the dialog title is likely to cut it off; the dialog’s own prompt does not repeat what happened.</li> <li>“Open the site’s copy” discards your working copy (it is kept in Previous Versions) without a confirmation.</li> <li>The “lines could not be turned into blocks” sentence is spoken and then the send carries on; for Publish it is spoken just before the confirmation dialog, where it will be lost.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="publish-or-update">Publish or Update</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: File > WordPress > Publish or Update… (“Alt+F, W, U”). WordPress key, then P. Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: after saving the file and running Check Before Publishing’s checks, a confirmation titled “Publish on <site>” (or “Update on <site>” for a published item): “Publish ‘<title>’ on <site>?” followed by a blank line and the summary (“Ready to publish: no problems found”, or “<N> things to look at: <n> problems, <m> to check”) and up to eight findings as “- <message>” lines. The address is on the clipboard.” The published address replaces whatever was on the clipboard. For a contributor: “Submitted for review on <site>.” A Private or Scheduled item keeps its status. Menus is in File, WordPress, Running the Site.”</li> <li>Cancel: silent; nothing sent.</li> <li>Without permission: a contributor is shown “Publish ‘<title>’ on <site>?” and the item is quietly sent as pending; see notes.</li> <li>F1: Add to a Menu: “Publishing a new page does not put it in the site’s menus, so visitors may not find it. Either ask, or say “The address replaced what was on the clipboard.”</li> <li>Two sentences in the Add to a Menu flow send people to “File, WordPress, Running the Site”, a place that no longer exists; Menus is now Tools > WordPress > Menus.</li> <li>With Cancel as the default, Enter in the confirmation cancels. Safe, but the labels passed to SetYesNoLabels (“Publish”, “Update”, “Publish Anyway”) carry no “&”, so the publish button has no access key; the route is Tab to it, then Space. Worth checking with a reader.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="schedule-publish">Schedule Publish</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: File > WordPress > Schedule Publish… (“Alt+F, W, E”). It should reopen with the values kept and the problem named.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="plugin-fields">Plugin Fields</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: File > WordPress > Plugin Fields… (“Alt+F, W, G”). No WordPress key. Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: background “reading what <site>’s plugins add”, then dialog “Plugin Fields, <title>” with whatever applies: “&Starts:”, “&Ends:”, “&Venue:” (events), “&Regular price:”, “S&ale price:”, “Stoc&k:” (products), “SEO &title:”, “SEO &description:” (SEO plugins). OK button “Save to Site”, and Cancel.</li> <li>What happens: Save to Site sends at once (no next-send wait). A changed price asks first: “Change Price”: “Change the price of <product> on <site>? Customers see it at once. Regular price <x>, sale price <y>.” button “Change Price”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Saved to <site>: the event’s dates and venue, the price and stock, the SEO fields.” (whichever apply). Not sent yet: “This is not on <site> yet. Save Draft to Site first; then its plugin fields can be filled in.” Nothing to edit: “<site>’s plugins add no fields to this <kind>.” Bad dates: “The event ends before it starts. Nothing was saved.” or the parse error plus “ Nothing was saved.“</li> <li>Outside a WordPress document: “Plugin Fields works in a WordPress page or post. Open one with File, WordPress.”</li> <li>Cancel: silent.</li> <li>Keyless: no chord; no written reason found.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_plugin_commands.py, wordpress_plugin_dialogs.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Custom Fields and Properties keep changes until the next send; Plugin Fields, one row below, sends immediately. Same menu, same kind of data, opposite rule; the OK label “Save to Site” is the only hint.</li> <li>Event dates and venue are always re-sent and always reported as saved, even when unchanged.</li> <li>A bad date closes the dialog and loses everything typed.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="previous-versions">Previous Versions</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: File > WordPress > Previous Versions… (“Alt+F, W, V”). WordPress key, then V. Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: background “reading the site’s history”, then a list window “Previous Versions”: “&Versions:” list (QUILL’s own kept versions as “<when>, <title>, <reason>”, then the site’s revisions as “<date>, <title>, On the site” or “Autosave on the site”), buttons “&View…”, “&Restore”, “Close”.</li> <li>View… opens a read-only report “Version” with the title and text, buttons “Copy &All” and “Close”.</li> <li>Restore keeps the current text as a version first, puts the chosen text in the editor, and closes the window.</li> <li>Announced after: Restore: “Version put in the editor. Nothing was sent; your text before is in Previous Versions.” None: “There are no earlier versions of this yet.”</li> <li>Undo: Restore writes through replace_text (select all, then write), an ordinary editor change, so the editor’s Undo should take it back; plus the “before restore” copy in Previous Versions.</li> <li>F1: window: “Earlier versions of this document, newest first: the ones QUILL kept and the site’s own history.” List: “Each row says when, the title, and why it was kept.” View: “The full text of this version. Copy a paragraph from it, or close and choose Restore.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_more_commands.py (cmd_versions, _show_versions).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Restore replaces the text but not the title; a version with a different title restores only its body.</li> <li>There is no “compare this version with mine”; the only way to see differences is to read both.</li> <li>The View report is titled just “Version”, which says nothing about which one.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="compare-with-site">Compare with Site</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: File > WordPress > Compare with Site (“Alt+F, W, M”). WordPress key, then C. Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: background “loading the site’s copy”, then a report “Compare with Site”: a header “<n> lines only on <site>, <m> only in yours.” and lines starting “Site:” or “Yours:”, with “Copy &All” and “Close”. Identical: “Your copy and the copy on <site> are the same.” (in the report).</li> <li>Not sent yet: “This is not on the site yet, so there is nothing to compare with.”</li> <li>F1: “The differences between the site’s copy and yours. Lines starting ‘Site:’ are the site’s; ‘Yours:’ are yours.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_commands.py (cmd_compare, compare_text).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The comparison is of your text converted to HTML against the site’s HTML, so a Markdown document shows block markup lines as differences the writer never typed.</li> <li>“Same” is shown in a report window rather than simply spoken; a one-line answer does not need a dialog.</li> <li>No action from the report (take the site’s line, keep mine); it is read-only.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="move-to-trash">Move to Trash</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: File > WordPress > Move to Trash… (“Alt+F, W, T”). WordPress key, then T. Document-only. Also the Open list’s “Move to &Trash…” button.</li> <li>What opens: confirmation “Move to Trash”: “Move ‘<title>’ on <site> to the Trash? It can be brought back: choose Trash as the status in Open.” buttons “Move to Trash” / “Cancel” (Cancel default).</li> <li>Announced after: “‘<title>’ moved to the Trash on <site>.” Only local: “This is only on this computer. There is nothing on the site to move to the Trash.”</li> <li>Without permission: “Your account on <site> is not allowed to delete this item.” (site sentence).</li> <li>Undo: none in QUILL; restore from the Open list’s “Restore from Trash”.</li> <li>Cancel: silent.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_commands.py (cmd_trash, trash).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>“choose Trash as the status in Open” describes a control that does not exist; Open has a find field where you type “trash”, and a “Restore from Trash” button.</li> <li>The document stays open in the editor afterwards with its local status set to trash; nothing says what Save Draft or Publish will do next with it.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="send-and-copy-submenu">Send and Copy (submenu)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: File > WordPress > Send and Copy (“Alt+F, W, C”). Rows: Send This Document to WordPress (D), Publish to Another Site (P), Duplicate (U), Send a Folder as Drafts (F), Download My Writing (M). None has a WordPress key; no written reason found for any.</li> </ul> <h3 id="send-this-document-to-wordpress">Send This Document to WordPress</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: File > WordPress > Send and Copy > Send This Document to WordPress… (“Alt+F, W, C, D”). Not document-only (it is for ordinary documents).</li> <li>What opens: the site chooser “Send This Document to WordPress” (several sites), then choice “Send This Document to WordPress”, “&Make it a:”, rows “Post”, “Page”.</li> <li>What happens: the first heading becomes the title (or the file name), pictures on this computer are queued for upload, a new WordPress document opens, and it is sent as a draft.</li> <li>Announced after: “Sending ‘<title>’ to <site> as a new <kind> draft; its <n> pictures go to the Media Library.” then, when the send finishes, “Draft saved to <site>. It is not public.”</li> <li>Refusals: already WordPress: “This is already a WordPress document. Save Draft to Site sends it to its site.” Empty: “This document is empty, so there is nothing to send.” Without permission: “Your account on <site> is <a role>, which cannot write pages or posts.” (or “upload files” when it has pictures).</li> <li>F1: “Whether the document becomes a post or a page. Either way it goes as a draft: nothing is public until you publish it.”</li> <li>Cancel: silent.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_route_copies.py, cross_site.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Two sentences for one action, the first while a new document window opens (likely talked over).</li> <li>The title is taken from the first heading without showing it; a person cannot correct it before it lands on the site.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="publish-to-another-site">Publish to Another Site</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: File > WordPress > Send and Copy > Publish to Another Site… (“Alt+F, W, C, P”). Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: with three or more sites, choice “Publish to Another Site”, “&Copy to:”; with exactly two, no question at all. If categories or tags are missing on the target: confirm “Categories and Tags”: “<target> has no <names>. Create them there? Cancel copies without them.” buttons “Create Them” / “Cancel”.</li> <li>Announced after: “‘<title>’ is a draft on <target>. <n> pictures were uploaded there. Left without: <names>.” Only one site: “<site> is your only site. Connect another with Connect a Site first.”</li> <li>Outside a WordPress document: “Publish to Another Site works in a WordPress page or post. Open one with File, WordPress.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_route_copies.py (cmd_publish_elsewhere, _copy_to).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The command is named “Publish” but always creates a draft. “Copy to Another Site” would say what it does.</li> <li>With two sites there is no confirmation before content is created on the other site.</li> <li>In the Categories and Tags message, “Cancel” does not cancel: it carries on and copies without them. A Cancel button that continues the action breaks a universal expectation; the buttons should be “Create Them” and “Copy Without Them”, with a real Cancel.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="duplicate">Duplicate</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: File > WordPress > Send and Copy > Duplicate… (“Alt+F, W, C, U”). Not document-only.</li> <li>What opens: in a WordPress document already on the site, straight to “Duplicate”, “&Where:” (when you have several sites), rows “This site, <site>” and the others. Elsewhere: site chooser “Duplicate”, then “&Duplicate a:” Post/Page, then background listing and a choice “&Post:” or “&Page:” of items, then Where.</li> <li>Announced after: same site: “‘<title>’ is a new draft on <site>.” and the new draft opens. Another site: as Publish to Another Site. Empty: “<site> has no <kind>s to duplicate.”</li> <li>F1: “Whether to duplicate a post or a page.”; “The <kind> to make a new draft from.”; “Where the new draft of ‘<title>’ goes.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_route_copies.py (cmd_duplicate).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>In a WordPress document, Duplicate copies the site’s copy, not your unsent edits; Publish to Another Site copies your local text. Neighbouring rows, different sources, no word about it.</li> <li>With one site, Duplicate in a document creates a draft on the site with no confirmation at all.</li> <li>The item list is a plain choice with no filter.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="send-a-folder-as-drafts">Send a Folder as Drafts</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: File > WordPress > Send and Copy > Send a Folder as Drafts… (“Alt+F, W, C, F”). Not document-only.</li> <li>What opens: site chooser “Send a Folder as Drafts”, then the Windows folder picker titled “Send a Folder as Drafts”, then a confirmation “Send a Folder as Drafts”: “Send <n> files to <site> as drafts? Check Before Publishing found things to look at in <k> of them.” (or “found nothing to look at.”), then up to 12 lines “- <file>: <title> (<k> things to look at)” and “- and <n> more”; button “Send <n> Drafts” / “Cancel”.</li> <li>Announced after: “<n> drafts from <folder> are on <site>.” plus “ <k> could not be sent: <first failure>“. No usable files: “<folder> has no Markdown, text, web-page or Word files to send. Nothing was sent.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_route_archive.py, writing_archive.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Every file becomes a post; there is no Post or Page choice, unlike Send This Document.</li> <li>Only the first failure is named.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="download-my-writing">Download My Writing</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: File > WordPress > Send and Copy > Download My Writing… (“Alt+F, W, C, M”). Not document-only (uses the document’s site when in one, otherwise asks).</li> <li>What opens: the folder picker “Download My Writing from <site>” (new folders allowed), then, when Word is available, choice “Download My Writing”, “&Save each page and post as:”, rows “Markdown files (.md)” and “Word documents (.docx)”.</li> <li>Announced: at start “Saving your writing on <site> to <folder>, in the background. QUILL says when it is done.” (or “Carrying on saving” when resuming). At the end: “Your writing on <site> is in <folder>: <n> saved, <m> already up to date, <p> pictures. Left out: comments, revisions, categories, tags, excerpts, featured images, custom fields, and pictures kept on other sites (their links stay).” plus “ <k> could not be saved; run it again to try those.“</li> <li>F1: “Markdown keeps every block and opens in any editor; Word documents open in Word and QUILL. The pictures and the contents page are the same either way.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_route_archive.py, writing_archive.py, workbench_carry.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>“Saving … QUILL says when it is done.” is spoken before the permission check; a refused account then hears a second sentence contradicting the first.</li> <li>The final sentence always ends with a long “Left out:” list, every time, even on a resume that saved nothing new.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="insert-menu-rows-overview">Insert menu rows (overview)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach them: Insert (“Alt+I”), at the foot after a separator. All seven are document-only except Make a Picture. None has a WordPress key; no written reason found.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Every one of the seven WordPress access letters collides with an existing Insert row in QUILL: “WordPress &Block…” (B) with “Insert Code &Block”; “Pict&ure from the Media Library…” (U) with “Q&uick Insert…”; “Picture from This &Computer…” (C) with “Insert &Citation…”; “Li&nk to a Page on This Site…” (N) with “I&nsert HTML Tag…”; “N&ote on This Block…” (O) with “Insert Em&oji…”; “Make a Pictu&re…” (R) with “Insert Horizontal &Rule”; “WordPress &Icon…” (I) with “L&ist”. In a Windows menu a repeated letter cycles the highlight instead of choosing, so none of these rows can be chosen by its letter. menu.py chose these letters for QUILL Lite’s menus; nothing checks them against QUILL’s.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="wordpress-block">WordPress Block</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Insert > WordPress Block… (“Alt+I”, then B, but B is shared; see overview). Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: background “loading the site’s blocks and patterns”, then a list window “Insert Block”: “&Find:” field, “&Blocks:” list (QUILL’s core blocks first, then the site’s blocks, patterns and synced patterns), “&Insert” button, any Pattern Directory buttons, “Close”. If the block needs answers, a form “Insert <block>” with OK labelled “Insert”.</li> <li>Announced after: “<block> inserted.” Nothing to insert: “<block> has nothing to insert.” No blocks in document is not a condition here.</li> <li>Undo: one insert_text; one Undo takes it back (the module docstring states this).</li> <li>F1: window: “The blocks you can insert on <site>: QUILL’s own core blocks first, then the site’s other blocks, its patterns and its synced patterns.” List: “Each block’s name and what it is for. Press Insert to add it.” Find: “Type part of a name and press Enter. "but" finds Button.” Form: “What the new <block> block needs. Everything else can wait.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_block_commands.py, block_catalog.py, wordpress_pattern_commands.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Menu says “WordPress Block”, the window says “Insert Block”, and the no-blocks sentence elsewhere says “Insert one with Insert Block”. Three names for one command.</li> <li>Enter on a row does nothing (no on_enter); you must Tab to Insert. Every other picker in this family (Open, Notes, Fix Link Text) honours Enter.</li> <li>It needs the network even for QUILL’s own core blocks; offline, nothing can be inserted.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="picture-from-the-media-library">Picture from the Media Library</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Insert > Picture from the Media Library… (“Alt+I”, then U, shared with Quick Insert). Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: the WordPress Workbench, on the site’s Media, in picking mode, announced “Picking a file for <document>: Enter on a file inserts it.” Without a window to come back to, a choice “Insert from Media Library”, “&Files:”. A picture then opens the picture dialog “Picture: <name>”: “&Alternative text:”, “&Suggest Alternative Text” button, “&Decorative: it adds nothing a visitor needs” check box, “&Caption:”, “Display &width (<size>):” combo, “File si&ze:” choice, “Use as &featured image” check box, “&Link to:” combo (“Nothing”, “The picture file”), “Describe &Image…” button, “Insert” (default) and “Cancel”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Picture inserted: <alt or name>.” For audio or video: “<Kind> inserted: <name>.” If the AI wrote a fuller description, a confirm “Fuller Description”: “Your AI also wrote a fuller description, as it does for a chart or a diagram. Add it as a paragraph after the picture, for you to edit?” button “Add Description”. Empty library: “<site>’s Media Library is empty. Use Upload Files.” Document changed while picking: “<title> is no longer in front, so nothing was inserted. Bring it forward and choose Insert again.”</li> <li>Undo: one insert_text (plus a second for the fuller description).</li> <li>Code: wordpress_media_library.py, wordpress_workbench_editor_side.py, wordpress_item_dialogs.py (ImageDialog).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Inserting a picture now opens a whole second window (the Workbench) rather than a dialog; for a screen-reader user that is a heavy context switch for a common task, and you must find your way back.</li> <li>“Use Upload Files” points at a command in Tools > WordPress > More Media without saying where.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="picture-from-this-computer">Picture from This Computer</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Insert > Picture from This Computer… (“Alt+I”, then C, shared with Citation). Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: the Windows file picker “Insert Picture from This Computer” (Pictures or All files), then the picture dialog as above (without File size).</li> <li>Announced after: “Picture inserted: <alt or name>. It is uploaded when you next send.” Then the fuller-description offer, if any.</li> <li>Undo: one insert_text; the pending upload stays recorded in the working copy even if you Undo the text (not found in code that it is removed).</li> <li>Code: wordpress_media_library.py, wordpress_media_dialogs.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Undoing the insert leaves the picture queued for upload (the pending upload is kept in the working copy and uploaded on the next send if the file exists; _upload_pending replaces the address only if it is in the text but uploads either way).</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="link-to-a-page-on-this-site">Link to a Page on This Site</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Insert > Link to a Page on This Site… (“Alt+I”, then N, shared with Insert HTML Tag). Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: background “listing what <site> has published”, then choice “Link to a Page on This Site”, “&Page or post:”, rows “<title>, <kind>” (pages first, then posts; the document itself excluded).</li> <li>Announced after: “Link to ‘<title>’ inserted.” None: “<site> has no published pages or posts to link to.”</li> <li>F1: “The published pages and posts on this site. The link goes in at the caret, using the selected text, or the page’s title when nothing is selected.”</li> <li>Outside a WordPress document: “Link to a Page on This Site works in a WordPress page or post. Open one with File, WordPress.”</li> <li>Undo: one insert_text.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_link_commands.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>No filter on what can be hundreds of rows; first-letter navigation in a list box is the only help.</li> <li>Drafts and scheduled pages cannot be linked to, with no word about why they are missing.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="note-on-this-block">Note on This Block</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Insert > Note on This Block… (“Alt+I”, then O, shared with Insert Emoji). Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: in a Markdown document where the block would become protected, a confirm “Add Note”: “Markdown has no way to write which note belongs to a block, so this block will become one protected line that keeps the note’s link exactly. Its words can then be changed only through WordPress or an HTML document. Add the note?” button “Add Note”. Then a multi-line text dialog “Add Note on <place>”, “&Note:”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Note added on <place> on <site>. Save Draft to Site or Publish to tie it to the block there.” Block moved meanwhile: “Note added on <site>, but the block moved while it was sent, so it is not tied to a block yet.” Not in a block: “The caret is not in a block. Move to one with Next Block, then add the note.” Not on the site: “This is not on <site> yet, so it has no notes. Save Draft to Site first, then notes can be read and added.”</li> <li>Without permission: “Your account on <site> is <a role>, which cannot read or write notes on blocks.” Old WordPress: “Notes on blocks need WordPress 6.9 or later, and <site> runs <version>. Ask the site’s administrator to update it.”</li> <li>F1: “A note on <place>, for the people editing <site>. Visitors never see it; people in the block editor see it beside the block.”</li> <li>Undo: the note itself is on the site at once and Undo does not remove it; the block change is one replace_text.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_notes_commands.py, block_notes.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The note is sent to the site immediately, while the tie to the block waits for the next send; Undo in the editor takes back the tie but leaves an orphan note on the site.</li> <li>The “protected line” warning is a heavy consequence (the block’s words become uneditable in Markdown) for adding a comment; worth surfacing in the menu row or offering to switch to HTML.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="make-a-picture">Make a Picture</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Insert > Make a Picture… (“Alt+I”, then R, shared with Horizontal Rule). Not document-only.</li> <li>What opens: “Picture Maker” (shared with Studio): “&Describe the picture, or the change you want”, “S&hape”, “Bac&kground”, “Co&lour”, “&Make the picture”, “&Versions”, “Suggest from &Page Content”, “Make a video &with this picture…”, “Open &folder”, “Close”. Before anything is sent, a confirm “Make the Picture” with the AI consent wording, button “Make the Picture”.</li> <li>Announced: “Making the picture. This can take a minute.” Refusal: “Nothing was sent.” Failure: “No picture was made: <error>”. No AI: “AI help is not available in this window.” Safe Mode: “QUILL is in Safe Mode, so AI is off and nothing was sent.” Suggest: “Sending the selection to your chosen AI for a picture suggestion.” (or “the page’s text”), then “Suggestion ready. <why>”. Insert into the document uploads at once and opens the picture dialog.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_picture_commands.py, studio_picture_maker.py, wordpress_picture_ai.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>It is not document-only, so a person can spend AI time making a picture in an ordinary document and only learn at Insert that “This is not a WordPress document…”.</li> <li>A made picture uploads the moment you insert it; a picture from this computer waits for the next send. Same menu, two rules.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="wordpress-icon">WordPress Icon</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Insert > WordPress Icon… (“Alt+I”, then I, shared with List). Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: background “reading <site>’s icons”, then a list window “Insert Icon”: “&Find:”, “&Icons:”, “Insert with a &Label…”, “Insert as &Decorative”, “Close”. Insert with a Label asks “Icon Label”, “&Label:” (filled with the icon’s name).</li> <li>Announced after: “Icon inserted, labelled ‘<label>’.” or “Decorative icon <name> inserted; screen readers skip it.” Blank label: “An icon needs a label that says what it means, or to be inserted as decorative. Nothing was inserted.” No icons: “<site> has no icons to insert.” Old WordPress: “<site> has no icon library. Icons arrived in WordPress 7.0.”</li> <li>Without permission: “Your account on <site> is <a role>, which cannot insert icons.”</li> <li>Outside a WordPress document: “Insert Icon works in a WordPress page or post. Open one with File, WordPress.” (the menu calls it “WordPress Icon”).</li> <li>F1: window: “<site>’s icons by name. Each goes in with a label that says what it means, or marked decorative so screen readers skip it; never unnamed.” Label: “What the icon means, said by screen readers in its place: for example ‘Phone us’. An icon that only decorates should be inserted as decorative.”</li> <li>Undo: one insert_text.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_route_links.py, icons.py.</li> <li>Review notes: the refusal names it “Insert Icon”; the menu row says “WordPress Icon”.</li> </ul> <h3 id="format--wordpress-submenu">Format > WordPress (submenu)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Format > WordPress (“Alt+O, P”; Format is “F&ormat”, the submenu “Word&Press”). Rows: Block Settings (S), Image Size (Z), Mark Language (L). All document-only; none has a key; no written reason found.</li> </ul> <h3 id="block-settings">Block Settings</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Format > WordPress > Block Settings… (“Alt+O, P, S”). Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: background “reading the theme’s colours”, then a form “Block Settings” (fields depend on the block). In Markdown, if the change would protect the block, confirm “Block Settings”: “Markdown has no way to write these settings, so this block will become one protected line that keeps them exactly. Its words can then be changed only through WordPress or an HTML document. Change it?” button “Make Protected Block”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Block settings changed.” plus a contrast warning when a colour pair is weak. Not in a block: “The caret is not in a block. Move to one with Next Block, then try again.” Others: “QUILL could not read this block.”, “This block has no settings QUILL can change.”</li> <li>F1: purpose “The settings of this <block> block.” plus “ <site> did not share its theme’s colours.“ when it did not.</li> <li>Undo: one replace_text.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_block_commands.py, block_settings.py.</li> <li>Review notes: needs the network just to read colours; offline, no settings can be changed at all.</li> </ul> <h3 id="image-size">Image Size</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Format > WordPress > Image Size… (“Alt+O, P, Z”). Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: background “reading the picture’s sizes”, then choice “Image Size”, “&Size:”, rows of sizes (“, the size it is now” on the current one), with “Back to Earlier Size: <label>” first when there is one.</li> <li>Announced after: “Picture shown at <label>.” Same size: “The picture is already that size.” No caret on a picture: “Put the caret on a picture in the document first, then choose Image Size.” Not in the library: “This picture is not in <site>’s Media Library, so it has no other sizes. Upload it first.” Gone: “The picture has moved or gone from the document, so nothing changed.”</li> <li>F1: “How wide the picture shows on the page, from the sizes WordPress made of it. Proportions are kept and nothing is cropped; only the markup changes. Back to Earlier Size puts the previous choice back.”</li> <li>Undo: one replace_text.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_image_commands.py.</li> <li>Review notes: replace_text rewrites the whole document; the caret is then put back at the picture with go_to_offset, which is good.</li> </ul> <h3 id="mark-language">Mark Language</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Format > WordPress > Mark Language… (“Alt+O, P, L”). Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: text dialog “Mark Language”, “&Language:” (last answer filled in).</li> <li>Announced after: “The selection is marked as <language>.” or “The whole page is marked as <language>.” Unknown: “QUILL does not know ‘<text>’ as a language. Type a name such as French, or a code such as fr or pt-BR.” Empty page: “There is nothing on the page to mark yet.”</li> <li>F1: “The language of the selected words, as a name such as French or a code such as fr or pt-BR. With nothing selected, the whole page is marked. A visitor’s screen reader switches voice there.”</li> <li>Undo: one insert_text or replace_text.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_check_commands.py, page_report helpers.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>With nothing selected the whole page is wrapped with no confirmation; forgetting to select is the likely mistake, and the result changes the language of everything. A confirm, or “Nothing is selected: mark the whole page?”, would catch it.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="view-menu-rows">View menu rows</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach them: View (“Alt+V”), at the foot after a separator: WordPress Blocks (B, also WordPress key B), Preview Blocks (V), Block Notes (L), Play Media Here (M). All document-only.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Three of the four access letters collide in QUILL’s View menu: “WordPress &Blocks…” (B) with “&Browser Preview…”; “Pre&view Blocks” (V) with “Pre&view Side by Side”; “B&lock Notes…” (L) with “Announce &Lists”. Only Play Media Here’s M is free (from the rows found in main_frame_menu.py).</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="wordpress-blocks">WordPress Blocks</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: View > WordPress Blocks… (“Alt+V”, then B, shared with Browser Preview). WordPress key, then B. Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: a list window “Blocks”: “&Blocks:” list (each block’s kind and first words, “, inside <parent>” when nested), buttons “&Go To”, “Move &Up”, “Move Do&wn”, “&Duplicate”, “&Remove…”, “&Change To…”, “Gr&oup”, “U&ngroup”, “Close”. Remove confirms: “Remove <block> from this document? Undo in the editor brings it back. Nothing changes on the site until you send it.” button “Remove Block”. Change To asks “Change To”, “&Kind of block:”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Moved up.”, “Moved down.”, “Duplicated. The copy is straight after it.”, “Block removed.”, “Changed to <kind>.”, “Grouped.”, “Ungrouped.”, or the reason a change cannot be made (“QUILL cannot change this kind of block to another.”). No blocks: “This document has no blocks yet. Insert one with Insert Block.”</li> <li>Undo: each change is one replace_text; Undo in the editor takes it back (stated in the window’s help).</li> <li>F1: window: “Every block in this document, in order. Each change here is one step that Undo in the editor takes back, and nothing else in the document moves.” List: “Each block’s kind and its first words; nested blocks say what they are in.” Change To: “What this block becomes. Its words are kept.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_block_commands.py, block_edit.py, block_spans.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>After Move Up or Move Down the list keeps the same row number selected, so the selection stays put while the block moves; pressing Move Up twice swaps the pair back.
