The Rows type in wordpress_dialogs.py exists for exactly this (“a row moved down follows the row”) and is not used here.</li> <li>No Enter action; Enter on a row does nothing (Go To is a button).</li> <li>Menu “WordPress Blocks”, window title “Blocks”, and the no-blocks sentence names “Insert Block”, which the Insert menu calls “WordPress Block”.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="preview-blocks">Preview Blocks</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: View > Preview Blocks (“Alt+V”, then V, shared with Preview Side by Side). Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: background “rendering the blocks”, then a report “Preview Blocks” with the rendered page as plain text in reading order (“(The page is empty.)” when empty), “Copy &All”, “Close”.</li> <li>Announced after the report closes, if any block failed: “<site> could not show <n> block(s): <first reason>”.</li> <li>F1: “This document as <site> renders its blocks, as plain text in reading order. Nothing was saved to make it.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_block_commands.py, block_preview.py.</li> <li>Review notes: the failure count is spoken only after you close the report, when it is no longer useful; it belongs at the top of the report.</li> </ul> <h3 id="block-notes">Block Notes</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: View > Block Notes… (“Alt+V”, then L, shared with Announce Lists). Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: background “reading notes on <site>”, then a list window “Notes, <site>”: “&Notes:” list, “&Details:” read-only field, buttons “&Go to It”, “&Reply…”, “Re&solve or Reopen”, “&Add Note…”, “Close”. Enter goes to the block. Reply asks “Reply to <author>”, “&Reply:” (multi-line).</li> <li>Announced after: Reply: “Reply to <author>’s note saved on <site>.” Resolve: “<author>’s note resolved on <site>.” (or “reopened”). None: “No notes on this <kind> on <site>. Add Note puts one on the block at the caret.” Block gone: “That note’s block is not in your copy. It may have been removed here, or the note is on a newer version on the site.”</li> <li>Without permission and old WordPress: as Note on This Block.</li> <li>F1: window purpose “The block editor’s notes on this <kind>: <n>, <m> open. Each says who wrote it, on which block, and what it says. Visitors never see notes.” List: “Each note’s author, the block it is on, its words, how many replies, and whether it is resolved. Enter goes to the block.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_notes_commands.py.</li> <li>Review notes: the list is updated optimistically before the site answers; if the reply fails, the list still shows it.</li> </ul> <h3 id="play-media-here">Play Media Here</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: View > Play Media Here (“Alt+V”, then M). Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: a player window “Play: <title>” with QUILL’s player panel (play/pause, stop, rewind and forward, speed, position, volume), a video picture panel for video, and “&Captions” (video), “&Go to Position…”, “Open in &Quill Media Player”, “Open in &Browser”, “Close”.</li> <li>Announced: nothing at caret: “There is no audio or video block at the caret. Move to one, or insert one with Insert from Media Library.” Captions: the caption text, “No caption at <time>.”, or “This video has no captions track on <site>.” No video picture: “On this computer the video’s sound plays here; Open in Browser shows the picture.” Player app: “Quill Media Player is not part of this release yet. Use Open in Browser.” or “Opening <title> in Quill Media Player.”</li> <li>F1: window: “Plays <title> from <site>, streamed from the site over HTTPS; nothing is saved on this computer. … Closing the window stops playback.” Video panel: “The video’s picture. It is not a control: Tab moves past it, and the buttons control playback.”</li> <li>Outside a WordPress document: “Play Media Here works in a WordPress page or post. Open one with File, WordPress.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_player_commands.py, wordpress_player_window.py, media_play.py.</li> <li>Review notes: “Insert from Media Library” is not the menu’s name (“Picture from the Media Library…”, which also inserts audio and video despite saying “Picture”).</li> </ul> <h3 id="edit--wordpress-submenu">Edit > WordPress (submenu)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Edit > WordPress. The submenu title has no access letter (“WordPress”, not “&WordPress”): press “Alt+E” and arrow to it. Rows: Next Block (N), Previous Block (P), Where Am I (W), Hear Where a Link Goes (H), Fix Link Text (L). All document-only. None has a WordPress key; no written reason found.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The one submenu a writer would visit most while working in a page has no letter (the Edit menu’s W is “&Word Prediction…”), and none of its rows has a chord, so moving block by block means Alt+E, arrows, Enter, N for every block. This is the strongest case in the section for a key.</li> <li>The Command Palette now has two commands titled “Next Block” (navigate.next_block and wordpress.next_block) and two titled “Where Am I” (verbosity.where_am_i and wordpress.where_am_i), with no way to tell them apart by name.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="next-block">Next Block</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Edit > WordPress > Next Block (“Alt+E”, arrow to WordPress, then N). Document-only.</li> <li>What happens: moves the caret to the start of the next block.</li> <li>Announced after: the block’s kind, position and first words, from spoken_move, in the shape “Image block, 4 of 12: Volunteers at the spring fair” (the docstring’s example). End: “No more blocks after this one.” No blocks: “This document has no blocks yet. Insert one with Insert Block.”</li> <li>Outside a WordPress document: the generic “This is not a WordPress document…” sentence.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_block_commands.py, block_spans.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="previous-block">Previous Block</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Edit > WordPress > Previous Block (“Alt+E”, arrow to WordPress, then P). Document-only.</li> <li>Announced after: as Next Block; at the start: “No blocks before this one.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_block_commands.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="where-am-i">Where Am I</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Edit > WordPress > Where Am I (“Alt+E”, arrow to WordPress, then W). Document-only.</li> <li>Announced: which block the caret is in and what it sits inside, in the shape “Paragraph block: Morning sessions, inside column 1 of Columns” (where_am_i’s docstring). Between blocks: “Not in a block. The next one is <block>.” or “Not in a block, and there are no blocks after this point.” No blocks: “This document has no blocks yet. Insert one with Insert Block.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_block_commands.py, block_spans.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="hear-where-a-link-goes">Hear Where a Link Goes</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Edit > WordPress > Hear Where a Link Goes (“Alt+E”, arrow to WordPress, then H). Document-only.</li> <li>What happens: on a link, asks the site in the background (“asking <site> where the link goes”) and speaks the target’s title. With no link at the caret, a text dialog “Hear Where a Link Goes”, “&Address:”; then the title is spoken and a confirm “Hear Where a Link Goes”: “<target sentence>” blank line “Insert a link to it, with the words ‘<words>’?” button “Insert Link”.</li> <li>Announced after: the target sentence: “Goes to ‘<title>’. <description>.” or “<url> has no title.” or “<url> could not be reached: <why>”. After inserting: “Link to ‘<title>’ inserted.”</li> <li>Without permission: “Your account on <site> is <a role>, which cannot read where links go.” Old WordPress: “<site> cannot read where a link goes. That needs WordPress 5.9 or later.”</li> <li>F1: “There is no link at the caret. Type or paste a web address: QUILL asks the site what page is there and reads its title, then offers to insert the link.”</li> <li>Undo: one insert_text.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_route_links.py, link_targets.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The target sentence is spoken and then repeated as the first line of the confirm that opens at once, so it is said twice, and the spoken copy is the one likely to be cut off.</li> <li>A “hear” command that turns into an “insert a link” command when there is no link at the caret is a surprise; the two would be clearer as separate rows.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="fix-link-text">Fix Link Text</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Edit > WordPress > Fix Link Text… (“Alt+E”, arrow to WordPress, then L). Document-only.</li> <li>What opens: background “asking <site> where <n> links go”, then a list window “Fix Link Text”: “&Links:” (rows “‘<words>’ goes to ‘<title>’”, or “… goes to <url>, which has no title”, or “Fixed: now ‘<title>’”), buttons “&Use the Page’s Title”, “Use &All Titles”, “Close”. Enter uses the title.</li> <li>Announced after: “The link now says ‘<title>’.” or “That link has no page title to use; write its words yourself.” Use All: “<n> links now say where they go.” None: “No ‘click here’ or ‘read more’ links in this document.”</li> <li>Without permission and old WordPress: as Hear Where a Link Goes.</li> <li>F1: window: “Links whose words do not say where they go, with the title of the page each one goes to. The site read each page; nothing was sent anywhere else.” List: “Each vague link’s words, and the title of the page it goes to.”</li> <li>Undo: each fix is its own replace_text, so Use All Titles over ten links is ten Undo steps.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_route_links.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Each fix rewrites the whole document with replace_text and never puts the caret back, so after closing the window the caret is at the end of the document, far from where you were.</li> <li>Use All is several Undo steps, not one, unlike every Blocks window change.</li> <li>There is no way to type your own words for a link from this window; the only choices are the page title or nothing.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="tools--wordpress-at-a-glance">Tools > WordPress, at a glance</h3> <ul> <li>Where it is: Alt+T opens Tools (the menu bar label is “&Tools” in main_frame_menu.py). The WordPress rows sit at the foot of Tools after a separator, in a submenu titled “WordPress” with no ampersand, so it has no access letter in QUILL either: arrow to it and press Right Arrow. menu.py says the letter was dropped because QUILL Lite’s Tools uses W for Say Word Summary; QUILL builds the same title verbatim from the shared table.</li> <li>It only exists while the WordPress feature (“core.wordpress”) is on.</li> <li>The WordPress key is a two-step layer. Press Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Grave, hear “WordPress keys”, then press one letter within 4 seconds. Escape says “Left WordPress keys”. A letter with no command says “No WordPress command on X.” In this doc, “WordPress key, then K” means that sequence. If the user has moved the QUILL key onto Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Grave, the QUILL key wins and the WordPress layer goes quiet.</li> <li>In the menu, a row with a layer key shows it in brackets after the title, for example “Check Before Publishing… (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Grave, K)”. It goes in brackets rather than after a tab so wx cannot turn it into a fake accelerator (main_frame.py _menu_label).</li> <li>There are no DEFAULT_ALIASES entries for any WordPress command (keymap.py).</li> <li>The rows, in order: <ul> <li>Open WordPress Workbench… (no access letter; WordPress key, then H)</li> <li>separator</li> <li>Check Before &Publishing… (P; WordPress key, then K; document-only)</li> <li>Check &This Page (T)</li> <li>Check the &Whole Site… (W)</li> <li>&Undo Site Fix… (U)</li> <li>Site-wide &Find and Replace… (F)</li> <li>Write an &Accessibility Statement… (A)</li> <li>Pa&lette Report (L)</li> <li>In &Short… (S; document-only)</li> <li>separator</li> <li>Media Li&brary… (B; WordPress key, then M; opens the Workbench)</li> <li>Alt Te&xt Review… (X; WordPress key, then X; document-only)</li> <li>Alt Text for the Whole Lib&rary… (R)</li> <li>&Visual Review… (V)</li> <li>M&ore Media (O), a submenu: <ul> <li>&Upload Files… (U; WordPress key, then U)</li> <li>Upload from &Web Address… (W)</li> <li>&Paste Image (P)</li> <li>Upload a &Large File… (L)</li> <li>&Regenerate Sizes… (R)</li> <li>Make a S&maller Copy… (M)</li> <li>Edit a Picture on the S&ite… (I)</li> <li>Site L&ogo… (O)</li> <li>Previ&ew and Review… (E)</li> <li>separator</li> <li>Captions for &Video… (V)</li> <li>&Transcript for Audio… (T)</li> <li>Make a Listening Versio&n… (N; document-only)</li> <li>Name Em&beds (B; document-only)</li> <li>Publish a &Document as a Page… (D)</li> </ul></li> <li>separator</li> <li>&Comments… (C; opens the Workbench)</li> <li>Categories and Ta&gs… (G; opens the Workbench)</li> <li>Me&nus… (N; opens the Workbench)</li> <li>Menu &History… (H)</li> <li>Site Chec&k (K; opens the Workbench)</li> <li>Site S&ettings… (E; opens the Workbench)</li> <li>&Design (D), a submenu: <ul> <li>Style &Variations… (V)</li> <li>Site &Styles… (S)</li> </ul></li> <li>Plug&in Extras (I), a submenu: <ul> <li>Events &Coming Up… (C)</li> <li>New &Event… (E)</li> <li>Shop &Orders… (O)</li> </ul></li> <li>&Manage (M), a submenu: <ul> <li>&Users… (U; opens the Workbench)</li> <li>&Application Passwords… (A; opens the Workbench)</li> <li>P&lugins… (L; opens the Workbench)</li> <li>T&hemes… (H; opens the Workbench)</li> <li>&Fonts… (F; opens the Workbench)</li> <li>S&ynced Patterns… (Y; opens the Workbench)</li> <li>separator</li> <li>&Widgets… (W; opens the Workbench)</li> <li>Site Ma&p… (P; opens the Workbench)</li> <li>Template &Outline (O)</li> <li>separator</li> <li>&Bulk Edit… (B)</li> <li>Page O&rder… (R)</li> <li>separator</li> <li>Previous Loo&ks… (K)</li> <li>&Space Used (S)</li> <li>Download a Copy of the Site’s &Design… (D)</li> <li>separator</li> <li>AI Abili&ties… (T; opens the Workbench)</li> <li>Ask th&e Site… (E)</li> <li>separator</li> <li>S&ign Out This Site… (I)</li> </ul></li> <li>&Your Sites (Y), a submenu: <ul> <li>Coming &Up… (U)</li> <li>&What Changed on My Sites… (W)</li> </ul></li> </ul></li> <li>Only five of the 57 rows in Tools > WordPress have a WordPress key: Workbench (H), Check Before Publishing (K), Media Library (M), Alt Text Review (X) and Upload Files (U). The other layer letters belong to File and View rows.</li> <li>Document-only rows are dimmed unless a WordPress document is in front. This is checked by _refresh_wordpress_menu_items, which looks up the front document’s working copy. In Tools the document-only rows are Check Before Publishing, In Short, Alt Text Review, Make a Listening Version and Name Embeds.</li> <li>Several more rows need a WordPress document but are not on the DOCUMENT_ONLY list, so they stay enabled and only refuse once chosen: Visual Review, Preview and Review, Paste Image, Captions for Video and Transcript for Audio. Details are under each command.</li> <li>Common behaviour for the site-wide rows: <ul> <li>The site is the front document’s site.</li> <li>Otherwise, with one site connected, that site is used without asking.</li> <li>Otherwise a chooser opens, titled after the command: list “&Site:”, default site selected, OK (default) and Cancel. Its F1 says “Choose which of your WordPress sites to use. Your default site is selected.” Cancel is silent.</li> <li>With no site connected, the command says “Connect a WordPress site first.” and the Connect a Site flow starts.</li> </ul></li> <li>Common behaviour on failure: any background failure is spoken as one sentence from failure_sentence. A refused request reads like “Your account on {site} is not allowed to do that.” or “{site} did not accept the sign-in. Check the username and application password, then reconnect.” (api.py plain_error).</li> <li>Common side announcement: the first time any command touches a site in a session, QUILL also says “{site}: N comments waiting.” (wordpress_admin_commands.py announce_waiting_once), and the same goes for new form responses. So the first command on a site can produce an extra sentence nobody asked for.</li> <li>Common list window (wordpress_dialogs.py ListWindow). It has an optional “&Search:” box, then the list, then an optional read-only “&Details:” box, then the action buttons, then a Close button (Escape). Its F1 says “Close this window. Escape does the same.” <ul> <li>The window’s “purpose” sentence is set only as help text (SetHelpText). It is never shown as text on screen, so F1 is the only way to hear it once the window is open.</li> </ul></li> <li>Common confirmation: a Yes/No message box whose Yes button is renamed to the action and whose No button is renamed Cancel. No is the default (wx.NO_DEFAULT), so Enter cancels.</li> <li>Common report window (ReportDialog): a read-only “&Report:” text box, a “Copy &All” button and a Close button.</li> <li>Review notes for the whole submenu: <ul> <li>15 of the 57 rows now open the WordPress Workbench instead of their own window. workbench_door() calls open_from_door(), which always returns True. The docstrings in wordpress_commands.py and at each door still say “behind its flag” and “the developer flag sends this door”. Both are stale, and every legacy window under those doors can no longer be reached from this menu.</li> <li>The menu teaches one letter and the WordPress key uses another, against menu.py’s own stated rule (“A row’s own access letter wherever it is free, so the menu teaches the key”): <ul> <li>Check Before Publishing has access letter P, but its layer key is K. WordPress key, then P, publishes.</li> <li>Media Library has access letter B, but its layer key is M. WordPress key, then B, opens View > WordPress Blocks.</li> <li>Site Check holds the access letter K, the same letter that runs Check Before Publishing from the WordPress key.</li> </ul></li> <li>Four rows start with or contain “Check”: Check Before Publishing, Check This Page, Check the Whole Site and Site Check. Site Check is really Site Health and lives in another group. That is easy to confuse by ear.</li> <li>“Coming Up” appears twice: Plugin Extras > Events Coming Up (its site chooser is titled “Coming Up”) and Your Sites > Coming Up (scheduled posts). Both lists are titled “Coming Up, …” or “Coming Up”.</li> <li>Keyless rows have no written reason. The bound-command gate (test_family_rules_and_gates.py) only walks DEFAULT_KEYMAP, the WordPress rows are registered at menu build time with try_register, and NOT_EDITOR_COMMANDS has no “wordpress.” family. About 50 Tools rows therefore have neither a key nor a recorded rule 7 or rule 8 decision.</li> <li>Every bracketed key is spoken as “Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Grave, K” as part of the item name. That is long on every arrow press, and the prefix is not the friendly “QUILL Key + …” form the QUILL key gets.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="open-wordpress-workbench">Open WordPress Workbench…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, then arrow to WordPress (no access letter) and press Right Arrow. “Open WordPress Workbench…” is the first row and has no access letter, by design: the menu.py comment says every letter of its name is taken. WordPress key, then H.</li> <li>What opens: the one WordPress Workbench window (wordpress_workbench_window.open_workbench). If it is already open, it is brought forward and keeps the place, row and filter it was left at. When it first opens, it starts at the current site’s Inbox, or at the Start node when no site is connected. Focus goes to the tree.</li> <li>Announced after: nothing from this command itself. The Workbench window title is left to the screen reader.</li> <li>Safe Mode: QUILL_SAFE_MODE=1 is passed through to the Workbench.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_commands.py (cmd_workbench), wordpress_workbench_window.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>It is the only row in the submenu with no access letter. Opening it from the menu means arrowing to the top of the submenu or knowing the H layer key.</li> <li>The trailing “…” suggests a dialog, but it opens a full peer window.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="check-before-publishing">Check Before Publishing…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, then P. WordPress key, then K.</li> <li>Document-only: dimmed outside a WordPress document. Reached some other way, for example from the Command Palette, it says: “This is not a WordPress document. Open one with Open Page or Post, or start one with New Page or New Post, in the WordPress menu under File.” It uses the generic NOT_WORDPRESS sentence because cmd_check calls self.current() without a command name.</li> <li>What happens: the document is converted to what would be sent and checked on this computer (checks.check_content). The summary is spoken first, for example “Ready to publish: no problems found” or “2 things to look at: 1 problem, 1 to check”.</li> <li>What opens: when there is at least one finding, a list window titled “Check Before Publishing”. <ul> <li>The list “&Things to look at:”. Its F1 says “Each one says what to look at and why. None of them stops you publishing.”</li> <li>A “&Go to It” button. Its F1 says “Close this and go to that place in the document.”</li> <li>Close.</li> <li>The window’s F1 purpose is the same summary sentence.</li> </ul></li> <li>Go to It: moves the caret to the excerpt and closes the window. If the spot cannot be found, it says “QUILL could not find that spot in the text; it may be inside a block line.” Markdown-to-block conversion problems are listed too, as “Line N: …”, but have no excerpt, so Go to It on them always gives that sentence.</li> <li>Undo: nothing to undo. It changes nothing.</li> <li>Cancel: Escape or Close closes the window silently.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_more_commands.py, checks.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The summary is announced just before a modal opens, so the dialog title announcement may cut it off. The purpose text that repeats it is reachable only with F1.</li> <li>When there are no findings, nothing opens and only the summary is spoken. That is good, but it is the only feedback.</li> <li>Go to It on a conversion-problem row is a guaranteed dead end. Those rows could go to their line number instead.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="check-this-page">Check This Page</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, then T. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Not document-only. <ul> <li>In a WordPress document, it checks that page’s published address.</li> <li>In any other document, it uses the site chooser (titled “Check This Page”) and checks the site’s home page.</li> </ul></li> <li>Refusals before anything runs: <ul> <li>The document is not published: “Check This Page reads the published page, and this is not published on {site} yet. Check Before Publishing looks at your own text now.”</li> <li>axe-core is missing: “Check This Page needs axe-core, which is not in this copy of QUILL. Nothing was checked and nothing was downloaded. Check Before Publishing still checks your own text.”</li> <li>No browser: “Check This Page needs Microsoft Edge or Google Chrome, and neither is on this computer. QUILL does not download a browser. Check Before Publishing still checks your own text.”</li> </ul></li> <li>What happens: the published page loads in a hidden browser and axe-core runs on it. The site’s url-details route checks dead links when the site has that route.</li> <li>Announced after: a summary such as “{site}, {title}: 3 problems, 2 to check” or “{label}: no problems found by axe-core”. When dead links were checked, a dead-link sentence is added.</li> <li>What opens: a list window titled “Check This Page”. <ul> <li>Its purpose (F1 only) is the summary plus “. Checked with axe-core; the page was sent nowhere.”</li> <li>The list “&Findings:”. Its F1 says “Each problem axe-core found, in plain words: how many places, whose it is, and who it affects.”</li> <li>“&Explain…” opens a report titled “Explain”: the WCAG criterion, who it affects, and each place.</li> <li>“&Go to It” goes to the spot in the document. <ul> <li>If the finding belongs to the theme or a plugin, it says “That one is in the theme or a plugin, not in your document. Write a Note for the Theme Author makes a report you can send them.”</li> <li>If no WordPress document is open, it says “Open the page in the editor first, then Go to It can take you there.”</li> <li>If the spot cannot be found, it says “QUILL could not find that place in your document; Explain shows what it looks like on the page.”</li> </ul></li> <li>“Write a &Note for the Theme Author…” opens a report titled “Note for the Theme Author”, ready to paste. On a content finding it says instead “That one is in your own content, so you can fix it yourself; Go to It takes you there.”</li> <li>“Page &Shape…” opens a report titled “Page Shape”: landmarks and headings in order.</li> <li>“Dead &Links…”, only when dead links were checked, opens a report titled “Dead Links” that starts “N of M links go nowhere.”</li> <li>Close.</li> </ul></li> <li>On failure: “Check This Page could not check {label}: {reason}”</li> <li>Cancel: the site chooser’s Cancel is silent. The window’s Close is silent.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_check_commands.py, page_audit.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The summary is announced right before the modal opens, so it can be cut off.</li> <li>A list with zero findings still opens a window with an empty list. Unlike Check Before Publishing, there is no early return when nothing was found.</li> <li>The name says “This Page”, but from a non-WordPress document it silently checks the site’s home page. The chooser does not say so.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="check-the-whole-site">Check the Whole Site…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, then W. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Site: the front document’s site, otherwise the chooser (titled “Check the Whole Site”).</li> <li>What happens: every published page and post is read on a background task, with no spoken progress while it reads. If none are found, it says “{site} has no published pages or posts to check.”</li> <li>How thorough: when axe-core and a browser are available, a chooser titled “Check the Whole Site” opens with the list “&How thorough:”. <ul> <li>The rows are “Your content only: fast, N pages” and “Your content and the visitor’s view with axe-core: slower, loads every page in a hidden browser”.</li> <li>Its F1 says “The visitor’s view also checks the theme, menus and footer, the way Check This Page does. Neither sends anything anywhere.”</li> <li>Without axe-core or a browser, the content-only check runs without asking.</li> </ul></li> <li>Progress for the visitor’s view: after each 10 pages, “Checked 10 of 42 pages on {site}.” If it fails partway: “The visitor’s view stopped: {reason} The report has your content checks.”</li> <li>Announced after: for example “{site}: 47 problems on 17 of 42 pages. 52 in October 2026.” or “{site}: no problems found on N pages.” The comparison with the last check comes from local history.</li> <li>What opens: a list window titled “Check the Whole Site”, only when there are problems. <ul> <li>The list “&Problems:”.</li> <li>“&Details:”, which lists the page titles.</li> <li>“&Pages…” opens a list window titled with the problem name. <ul> <li>Its list is “&Pages:”.</li> <li>“&Open” opens the page in the editor.</li> <li>“Open on the &Site” opens it in the browser.</li> </ul></li> <li>“Fix in &Bulk…” works like this: <ul> <li>For a problem it cannot fix itself, it says “QUILL cannot fix this one by itself: each needs your words or your eyes. Choose Pages to open them one at a time.”</li> <li>When nothing is left to fix, it says “There is nothing left to fix for that problem.”</li> <li>Otherwise a report titled “Fix in Bulk: Preview” opens, with “Before:” and “After:” lines for each page. Its F1 says “Every page that will change, and how. Nothing has been sent yet.”</li> <li>Then a confirmation titled “Fix in Bulk on {site}”: “{fix label} on N pages on {site}? A copy of each page as it is now is kept on this computer, so Undo Site Fix can put them all back.” Yes is “Fix N Pages”.</li> </ul></li> <li>Close.</li> <li>The purpose (F1 only) is the summary. For a content-only check it adds “Only your content was checked; Check This Page shows the visitor’s view.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after a bulk fix: “Fixed N pages on {site}. Undo Site Fix puts them back.” It may add “N pages could not be changed; try those again later.” and “N pages changed on the site since and were left alone.” If nothing changed: “Nothing was changed on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: Tools > WordPress > Undo Site Fix. Ctrl+Z does nothing, because the change is on the site.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_site_check_commands.py, site_report.py, site_fixes.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Access-key collision (GATE-14): the list label “&Problems:” and the “&Pages…” button both claim P in one window, so Alt+P cycles focus instead of pressing. check_access_keys.py cannot see this, because the labels are runtime arguments to the shared ListWindow.</li> <li>Reading every page can take a long time with no progress sentence; only the visitor pass reports progress.</li> <li>Fix in Bulk is two modals in a row, the preview report and then the confirmation. Closing the preview with Escape does not cancel; the confirmation still follows.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="undo-site-fix">Undo Site Fix…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, then U. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Site: the front document’s site, otherwise the chooser (titled “Undo Site Fix”).</li> <li>When there is nothing to undo: “There are no site fixes to undo on {site}.”</li> <li>What opens: a list window titled “Undo Site Fix”. <ul> <li>Its purpose (F1 only): “Fixes and replacements made across {site}, newest first. Undo puts each page back as it was, except pages changed on the site since.”</li> <li>The list “&Fixes:”. Its F1 says “When, what was done, and how many pages it changed.”</li> <li>“&Undo…” works like this: <ul> <li>On a fix already undone, it says “That fix has already been put back.”</li> <li>Otherwise the window closes and QUILL re-reads the pages.</li> <li>If every page changed since, it says “Every page in that fix has changed on {site} since, so nothing was put back. Previous Versions on each page still has the older text.”</li> <li>Otherwise a confirmation titled “Undo Site Fix on {site}” opens: “Put back N pages on {site} as they were before: {label}?” It can add “N pages changed on the site since and will be left alone: {titles}.” Yes is “Put Back N Pages”.</li> </ul></li> <li>“&View…” opens a report titled “Site Fix” that lists the pages.</li> <li>Close.</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: “Put back N pages on {site}.” It may add “N could not be changed; try Undo again later.”</li> <li>Cancel: Close or the confirmation’s Cancel is silent.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_site_check_commands.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Only covers Fix in Bulk and Site-wide Find and Replace. Manage > Bulk Edit has its own separate “Undo Bulk Edit”, and Style Variations and Site Styles have “Put Back the Previous Look”. That is three undo models for site-wide changes.</li> <li>Fixes that were already undone stay in the list. It is not clear from the code whether their spoken rows say so.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="site-wide-find-and-replace">Site-wide Find and Replace…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, then F. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Site: the front document’s site, otherwise the chooser (titled “Site-wide Find and Replace”).</li> <li>What opens: three dialogs in turn, all titled “Site-wide Find and Replace”. <ul> <li>First, a text box “&Find:”. Its F1 says “The words to find in every page and post on the site, published or not.”</li> <li>Second, a text box “&Replace with:”. Its F1 says “What to put in their place. Leave it empty to remove the words.”</li> <li>Third, a list “&How to match:”. The rows are “Plain words, any case”, “Plain words, matching case”, “A pattern (regular expression), any case” and “A pattern (regular expression), matching case”. Its F1 says “Plain words is right almost every time. A pattern is for the technical.”</li> <li>Each has OK (default) and Cancel.</li> </ul></li> <li>A bad pattern is spoken as the pattern error’s sentence.</li> <li>Announced after the search: <ul> <li>“N matches on M pages on {site}.”</li> <li>Or “No matches for ‘{find}’ on {site}.”</li> <li>Or “‘{find}’ is only inside block settings on {site}, which Find and Replace leaves alone unless you choose Include Block Settings.”</li> </ul></li> <li>What opens: a list window titled “Site-wide Find and Replace”. <ul> <li>Its purpose (F1 only): “Every match for ‘{find}’ on {site}, with its sentence. All are included to start with.”</li> <li>The list “&Matches:”. Each row reads “Included: {title}: {context}” or “Skipped: …”.</li> <li>“&Include or Skip” toggles the selected match.</li> <li>“Include &All” and “S&kip All” set every match.</li> <li>“Include &Block Settings” appears only when matches inside block comments were hidden. Its F1 warns that changing those can break a block.</li> <li>“&Replace…”: <ul> <li>With nothing included, it says “Nothing is included, so nothing was replaced.”</li> <li>Otherwise a confirmation titled “Replace on {site}” opens: “Replace N matches of ‘{find}’ with ‘{replace}’ on M pages on {site}? A copy of each page as it is now is kept on this computer, so Undo Site Fix can put them all back.” Yes is “Replace in M Pages”.</li> </ul></li> <li>Close.</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: “Changed N pages on {site}. Undo Site Fix puts them back.” It may add the failed and skipped tails described under Check the Whole Site.</li> <li>Undo: Tools > WordPress > Undo Site Fix.</li> <li>Cancel: Cancel on any of the three questions ends silently.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_find_replace_commands.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Grammar: the confirmation always says “matches”, so one match reads “Replace 1 matches of …”.</li> <li>It takes three modal steps before any search. A single dialog with Find, Replace with, Match case and Pattern controls would be faster and closer to Word’s Replace.</li> <li>Toggling a match does not say the new state, but the row text changes (“Included” or “Skipped” first), which the list re-reads. That is fine.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="write-an-accessibility-statement">Write an Accessibility Statement…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, then A. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Site: the front document’s site, otherwise the chooser (titled “Write an Accessibility Statement”).</li> <li>What opens: after it reads the admin email in the background (only administrators can), a text dialog titled “Write an Accessibility Statement” with the box “&Contact:”, prefilled with that email when it could be read. Its F1 says “How a visitor reaches you about a problem: an email address, a phone number or a contact page. The site’s admin email is offered when your account can see it.” OK and Cancel.</li> <li>What happens: a new local page draft (slug “accessibility”) opens in the editor. It is built from the site name, the contact and the last whole-site check result.</li> <li>Announced after: “Accessibility statement draft for {site} is open. It is yours to edit; nothing is sent until you choose Save Draft to Site or Publish.”</li> <li>Cancel: silent.</li> <li>Without permission: non-administrators just get an empty Contact box. There is no sentence saying why.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_check_commands.py, site_report.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The draft is Markdown or HTML according to the format last chosen in New Page or New Post (self._last_format), not a choice offered here.</li> <li>Leaving the contact empty and pressing OK still makes a statement with an empty contact. No check is made.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="palette-report">Palette Report</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, then L. No WordPress key. The title has no “…”, yet it opens a window.</li> <li>Site: the front document’s site, otherwise the chooser (titled “Palette Report”).</li> <li>Announced after: “{theme} on {site}: N pairings, M below the WCAG AA minimum for body text.” If it cannot read a palette: “QUILL could not read a colour palette from {site}’s theme.”</li> <li>What opens: a report titled “Palette Report”. The summary comes first, then every colour on every background with its ratio. Its F1 says “Every colour in the theme’s palette on every background, with its contrast ratio. Body text needs 4.5 to 1; large text 3 to 1.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_check_commands.py, contrast.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The title has no ellipsis but opens a report. That is inconsistent with the other rows.</li> <li>A classic theme silently gives “could not read a colour palette”, with no hint that block themes are needed. Design > Style Variations does explain this.</li> <li>The same palette data is in Design > Style Variations and in Manage > Previous Looks > Palette Report. That is three doors to one report.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="in-short">In Short…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, then S. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Document-only: dimmed outside a WordPress document. Reached anyway, it speaks the generic NOT_WORDPRESS sentence.</li> <li>Refusals: <ul> <li>“AI help is not available in this window.”</li> <li>“There is nothing to summarise yet.”</li> <li>The editor’s own AI gates speak for themselves: AI switched off, agreement not accepted, or a connection that cannot do text.</li> </ul></li> <li>What happens: up to 12,000 characters of the page’s text go to the chosen AI with no confirmation step. The summary is put at the top of the document, the caret moves to the start, and a “before AI” version is kept locally.</li> <li>Announced after: “In Short is at the top of the page. Edit it like any paragraph; Undo takes it out.” If the AI returns nothing: “The AI sent back nothing to use. Nothing was changed.” AI errors are spoken as they come.</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z, as the announcement says. The change is made by selecting the whole buffer and writing new text (host.replace_text). File > WordPress > Previous Versions also has the “before AI” copy.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_check_commands.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>It sends page text to AI with no “this will send N words to {AI}” confirmation. Visual Review asks first; this does not.</li> <li>The trailing “…” suggests a dialog, but none opens.</li> <li>It is a content-writing command, not site upkeep. It sits oddly in Tools beside site checks.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="media-library">Media Library…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, then B. WordPress key, then M.</li> <li>What opens: the WordPress Workbench at the current site’s “Media” node. workbench_door(“wordpress.media_library”) always opens it. The old Media Library list window below that line in wordpress_media_library.py cannot be reached from here.</li> <li>With no site connected: the Workbench opens at Start. There is no “Connect a WordPress site first.” and no Connect flow, unlike every non-door row.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_media_library.py, wordpress_workbench_window.py, workbench_tree.py (_DOORS).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The code comment “the developer flag sends this door to the WordPress Workbench” is stale. There is no flag check.</li> <li>Access letter B, but the layer key is M. WordPress key, then B, opens View > WordPress Blocks instead.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="alt-text-review">Alt Text Review…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, then X. WordPress key, then X. This is the one Tools row whose access letter and layer key agree.</li> <li>Document-only: dimmed outside a WordPress document. Reached anyway, it speaks the generic NOT_WORDPRESS sentence.</li> <li>When there are no pictures: “This document has no pictures.”</li> <li>What opens: a list window titled “Alt Text Review”. <ul> <li>Its purpose (F1 only): “Every picture in this document with its alternative text: N pictures, M to look at. Empty, file-name-like and very long alternative text are marked.”</li> <li>The list “&Pictures:”.</li> <li>“&Details:” shows “Picture N: source”, “Alternative text: …” and either “Problem: …” or “Decorative”.</li> <li>“&Edit…” opens a multiline text dialog titled “Alternative Text: {name}” with the box “&Alternative text:”. Its F1 says “What this picture contributes here, for someone using a screen reader. If a person is shown and the page does not name them, add the name if it matters. Nothing changes until you choose OK.”</li> <li>“Mark Decorati&ve” empties the alt text on purpose.</li> <li>“&Suggest…” asks the AI and then opens the same Alternative Text dialog prefilled. On OK it says “Alternative text set for picture N.”</li> <li>“&Go to Picture” closes the window with the caret on the picture.</li> <li>Close.</li> </ul></li> <li>If the picture moved or was removed while the window was open: “That picture has moved or gone from the document.”</li> <li>Undo: each Edit or Mark Decorative rewrites the whole buffer (host.replace_text), so each one is a separate whole-document edit for Ctrl+Z.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_image_commands.py, media_alt.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Edit and Mark Decorative speak nothing afterward; only the row text changes. Suggest does announce. That is inconsistent.</li> <li>The Alternative Text box is multiline, so Enter adds a new line instead of pressing OK. The user must Tab to OK.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="alt-text-for-the-whole-library">Alt Text for the Whole Library…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, then R. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Site: the front document’s site, otherwise the chooser (titled “Alt Text for the Whole Library”).</li> <li>Without permission: <ul> <li>“Your account on {site} is a {role}, which cannot change pictures in the Media Library.”</li> <li>Without the media route: “{site} does not offer its Media Library to QUILL.”</li> </ul></li> <li>When nothing is missing: “Every picture in {site}’s Media Library has alternative text.”</li> <li>What opens: a list window titled “Alt Text for the Whole Library, {site}”. <ul> <li>Its purpose (F1 only) says how many pictures lack alt text and that every batch of them is saved together, with the rest saved on close.</li> <li>The list “&Pictures:”. Enter writes. Its F1 says “Each picture’s name, and whether its alternative text is written, saved, or still missing. Enter writes it.”</li> <li>“&Details:”.</li> <li>“&Write…” opens a multiline dialog titled “Alternative Text: {name}”. Its F1 says “What this picture shows, for someone using a screen reader. This becomes the library’s default; each page can still say what it means there.”</li> <li>“&Suggest…” works the same way. On OK it says “Alternative text written for {name}. It is saved with the next batch.”</li> <li>“Save &Now”. With nothing waiting it says “Nothing is waiting to be saved.”</li> <li>Close.</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after each save: “N descriptions saved on {site}.” It may add “N could not be saved; {site} refused them, so try those again.”</li> <li>Cancel: closing the window saves whatever is still waiting. This is not a discard.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_picture_edit_commands.py, media_edit.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Close saves. That is good for a long session, but Escape (usually “cancel”) silently sends pending descriptions to the site.</li> <li>The save on close announces after the window is gone. That is fine.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="visual-review">Visual Review…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, then V. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Not on the DOCUMENT_ONLY list, so it is never dimmed. It still needs a WordPress document and, reached without one, speaks the generic NOT_WORDPRESS sentence.</li> <li>Refusal when the page is not published: “Visual Review looks at the published page, and this is not published on {site} yet. Publish it first. A draft opens only for someone signed in to WordPress in a browser, so QUILL cannot capture it.”</li> <li>What happens: <ul> <li>QUILL says “Capturing the desktop and phone views of {url}.” and captures the page on this computer.</li> <li>A confirmation titled “Visual Review” then says exactly what would go to the AI (vr.consent_text). Yes is “Send for Review”. Cancel says “Nothing was sent. The screenshots are in {folder}.”</li> <li>After Yes, the AI describes the page.</li> </ul></li> <li>What opens after: a report titled “Visual Review”. It ends “This report is saved as text in {path}. After you Update the page, choose Visual Review again to review it again.” Its F1 says “What a sighted visitor sees on the published page, on a desktop and a phone, then image by image. Each finding is labelled likely a problem, a design choice you may want, or worth checking.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_review_commands.py, visual_review.py, page_capture.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>It should be on DOCUMENT_ONLY (or work on the home page the way Check This Page does). Today it is enabled everywhere and refuses with a sentence that names File > WordPress rather than this command.</li> <li>It is a good model of consent before sending; In Short and Ask the Site should match it.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="more-media--upload-files">More Media > Upload Files…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, O for More Media, then U. WordPress key, then U.</li> <li>Site: the front document’s site, otherwise the chooser (titled “Upload Files”).</li> <li>What opens: <ul> <li>A standard Open dialog titled “Upload Files” that takes many files.</li> <li>Then, for each file, a dialog titled “Upload: {name}”. Its F1 says “What {site}’s Media Library keeps with this file. Upload sends the file and these details; Cancel skips this file and the others carry on.”</li> <li>For a picture, the controls in order: <ul> <li>“&Alternative text:” (multiline).</li> <li>“&Suggest Alternative Text” (AI).</li> <li>“&Decorative: it adds nothing a visitor needs” check box. Checking it disables the alt box.</li> <li>“&Title:”.</li> <li>“&Caption:”.</li> <li>“Describe &Image…”.</li> <li>“Upload” (default) and “Cancel”.</li> </ul></li> <li>For any other file: Title, Caption, Upload and Cancel.</li> </ul></li> <li>Duplicates: a confirmation titled “Already in the Library”: “{file} is already in {site}’s Media Library, as {title}. Use the one already there instead of uploading a second copy?” Yes is “Use the One There”.</li> <li>Progress: “Uploading 2 of 5: {file}.” or “Uploading {file} to {site}.”</li> <li>Announced after: <ul> <li>“{file} uploaded to {site}.” or “N of M files uploaded to {site}.”, plus a count of files that were already there.</li> <li>Or “Nothing was uploaded to {site}.”</li> <li>A file that cannot be read: “{file} could not be read: {reason}. The others carry on.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Cancel: Cancel in the file picker is silent. Cancel in one file’s details dialog skips only that file.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_media_upload.py, wordpress_media_dialogs.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>There is no way to cancel the whole batch from a details dialog. With 20 files, the user must Cancel 20 times.</li> <li>Enter in the multiline Alt box adds a line instead of pressing Upload.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="more-media--upload-from-web-address">More Media > Upload from Web Address…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, O, then W. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Site: the front document’s site, otherwise the chooser (titled “Upload from Web Address”).</li> <li>What opens: a text dialog titled “Upload from Web Address” with the box “&Address:”. Its F1 says “The https:// address of a picture, sound, video or document. QUILL fetches it from that site onto this computer, then uploads it to {site}.”</li> <li>Then: “Fetching the file from {host}.” After that the same “Upload: {name}” details dialog and duplicate check as Upload Files run.</li> <li>Announced after: the same upload summary sentence. An address that cannot be used is spoken as its MediaDownloadError sentence.</li> <li>Cancel: an empty address or Cancel ends silently. Cancel in the details dialog says “Nothing was uploaded to {site}.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_media_upload.py, media_tools.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="more-media--paste-image">More Media > Paste Image</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, O, then P. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Not on the DOCUMENT_ONLY list, so it is always enabled. It needs a WordPress document and, reached without one, speaks the generic NOT_WORDPRESS sentence.</li> <li>Refusal: “There is no picture on the clipboard. Copy a picture first, then choose Paste Image.”</li> <li>What opens: the picture dialog titled “Picture: Pasted picture”, with these controls in order: <ul> <li>“&Alternative text:”.</li> <li>“&Suggest Alternative Text”.</li> <li>“&Decorative: it adds nothing a visitor needs”.</li> <li>“&Caption:”.</li> <li>“Display &width ({size}):”.</li> <li>“File si&ze:” (only when sizes are known).</li> <li>“Use as &featured image”.</li> <li>“&Link to:”.</li> <li>“Describe &Image…”.</li> <li>“Insert” (default) and “Cancel”.</li> </ul></li> <li>What happens: the picture is uploaded (with the duplicate check), and the markup is inserted at the caret.</li> <li>Announced after: “Picture inserted: {alt or file name}.” On failure: “The picture was not uploaded. {problem}”</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z removes the inserted markup. The uploaded file stays in the Media Library.</li> <li>Cancel: silent.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_media_upload.py, wordpress_item_dialogs.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>It is an insert-into-this-page command filed under Tools > More Media, while Insert has “Picture from This Computer”. Paste Image belongs beside it, or in Edit.</li> <li>It should be on DOCUMENT_ONLY.</li> <li>No “…” even though a dialog opens.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="more-media--upload-a-large-file">More Media > Upload a Large File…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, O, then L. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Site: the front document’s site, otherwise the chooser (titled “Upload a Large File”).</li> <li>What opens: <ul> <li>A file picker titled “Upload a Large File”.</li> <li>For a big file, after QUILL measures the site’s space, a confirmation titled “Upload a Large File” with the ss.upload_warning text. Yes is “Upload”. Cancel says “Nothing was uploaded.”</li> <li>Then “Uploading {file}, {size}, to {site}. A file this size can take a while; QUILL says when it is there.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: “{file} is in {site}’s Media Library. Its address is on the clipboard.”</li> <li>After the upload: a field dialog titled “Details of {title}”. Its purpose: “What {site} says about the file. Cancel keeps what WordPress made from the file’s name.” The OK button is “Save Details”. After saving: “Saved the details of {title} on {site}.”</li> <li>Without permission: “Your account on {site} is a {role}, which cannot upload files.” Without the route: “{site} does not offer its Media Library to QUILL.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_route_media.py, media_pieces.py, site_space.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>It puts the address on the clipboard without being asked, and Upload Files does not. That is inconsistent and overwrites the clipboard.</li> <li>Having both “Upload Files” and “Upload a Large File” makes the user decide what “large” means. The code could choose for them.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="more-media--regenerate-sizes">More Media > Regenerate Sizes…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, O, then R. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Site: the front document’s site, otherwise the chooser.</li> <li>Refusals: <ul> <li>“{site} cannot remake a picture’s sizes. Its F1 explains it remakes the thumbnail, medium and large copies.</li> <li>Announced after: “{site} remade N sizes of {name}.” or “{site} remade the sizes of {name}.”</li> <li>Confirmation: none. It runs as soon as the picture is chosen.</li> <li>Cancel: silent.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_route_media.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="more-media--make-a-smaller-copy">More Media > Make a Smaller Copy…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, O, then M. No WordPress key.</li> <li>What it works on: <ul> <li>In a WordPress document with the caret on a picture, it uses that picture. If the picture is not in the library: “This picture is not in {site}’s Media Library. Upload it first.”</li> <li>Otherwise a chooser titled “Make a Smaller Copy” opens with the list “&Picture:”.</li> </ul></li> <li>What opens next: <ul> <li>A chooser “&Largest width:” (rows like “800 pixels wide”). Upload it to {site} as a new file? The original stays in the library.” Yes is “Upload”.</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: “Smaller copy uploaded to {site} as {file}. The original is still in the library.” In a document it may instead say “Smaller copy uploaded to {site} and used here. The original is still in the library.”</li> <li>Undo: when used in the document, Ctrl+Z restores the old address. The new file stays on the site.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_image_commands.py, media_tools.py.</li> <li>Review notes: three modals plus a confirmation for one shrink. A single dialog holding the width and format choices would halve the steps.</li> </ul> <h3 id="more-media--edit-a-picture-on-the-site">More Media > Edit a Picture on the Site…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, O, then I. No WordPress key.</li> <li>What it works on: the picture at the caret in a WordPress document, otherwise a chooser titled “Edit a Picture on the Site” with the list “&Picture:”.</li> <li>Refusals: <ul> <li>“{site} does not offer picture editing. The original is unchanged.” Then up to three more prompts follow in a row: <ul> <li>In a document, “Use the edited copy here, in place of the original?” (Yes is “Use It Here”).</li> <li>A “Regenerate Sizes” confirmation (Yes is “Regenerate Sizes”).</li> <li>When AI is on, Describe Image runs.</li> </ul></li> <li>Code: wordpress_picture_edit_commands.py, media_edit.py.</li> <li>Review notes: one command can chain up to five modals and several announcements. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Site: the front document’s site, otherwise the chooser (titled “Site Logo”).</li> <li>Refusals: <ul> <li>“Site Logo needs the Administrator role on {site}: changing the logo needs the manage_options permission, and your account does not have it.”</li> <li>“{site} does not use the standard site logo. That is harmless but means pressing O twice.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="more-media--preview-and-review">More Media > Preview and Review…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, O, then E. It includes the customer’s name and shipping address, which then go wherever you paste them.” Yes is “Copy Order”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Order {n} copied.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_shop_commands.py.</li> <li>Review notes: a confirmation before a clipboard copy is unusual, but it is justified by the personal data, and it is clearly worded.</li> </ul> <h3 id="manage--users">Manage > Users…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, M for Manage, then U. No WordPress key.</li> <li>What opens: the Workbench at “Application Passwords”.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_people_commands.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="manage--plugins">Manage > Plugins…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, M, then L. No WordPress key.</li> <li>What opens: the Workbench at “Plugins”.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_manage_commands.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="manage--themes">Manage > Themes…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, M, then H. No WordPress key.</li> <li>What opens: the Workbench at “Themes”.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_manage_commands.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="manage--fonts">Manage > Fonts…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, M, then F. No WordPress key.</li> <li>What opens: the Workbench at “Fonts”.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_design_commands.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="manage--synced-patterns">Manage > Synced Patterns…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, M, then Y. No WordPress key.</li> <li>What opens: the Workbench at “Patterns”.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_design_commands.py.</li> <li>Review notes: the menu says “Synced Patterns”, but the node is “Patterns”. Whether the Workbench filters it to synced patterns is not in this code.</li> </ul> <h3 id="manage--widgets">Manage > Widgets…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, M, then W. No WordPress key.</li> <li>What opens: the Workbench at “Widgets and Sidebars”.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_menu_commands.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="manage--site-map">Manage > Site Map…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, M, then P. No WordPress key.</li> <li>What opens: the Workbench at “Pages”.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_structure_commands.py.</li> <li>Review notes: “Site Map” opens a node named “Pages”, while Page Order (two rows down) is a separate legacy tree window over the same pages.</li> </ul> <h3 id="manage--template-outline">Manage > Template Outline</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, M, then O. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Site: the front document’s site, otherwise the chooser.</li> <li>Refusal: “{site} uses a classic theme, so it has no block templates. Its sidebars and footer are in Widgets.”</li> <li>What opens: a report titled “Template Outline, {site}” with the templates and parts and their blocks, indented by depth. When opened from a document, that document’s template comes first. Its F1 says “The theme’s templates and template parts, with the blocks in each, indented by depth. They explain things on a page that are not in the page. Change them in the site editor.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_structure_commands.py, site_structure.py.</li> <li>Review notes: there is no “…” although a window opens. Indentation by depth is hard to hear in a plain text box; a tree, or “level N” prefixes, would carry the structure.</li> </ul> <h3 id="manage--bulk-edit">Manage > Bulk Edit…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, M, then B. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Site: the front document’s site, otherwise the chooser (titled “Bulk Edit”).</li> <li>What opens: <ul> <li>A chooser titled “Bulk Edit” with the list “&Edit:” and the rows “Posts” and “Pages”.</li> <li>Then a list window titled “Bulk Edit Posts, {site}” (or Pages). <ul> <li>Its purpose (F1 only): “Select the posts to change, then Change. Every change is one confirmation and one request, and Undo Bulk Edit puts the items back.”</li> <li>The list “&Posts:” or “&Pages:”. Selected rows start “Selected, “.</li> <li>“&Select” toggles the row and speaks nothing.</li> <li>“Select &All” toggles all and says “N posts selected.”</li> <li>“&Change…” opens a chooser titled “Change” with “&Change:”, then a chooser titled after the field with “&To:”. With nothing selected it says “Select the posts to change first, with Select.”</li> <li>“&Undo Bulk Edit…”.</li> <li>Close.</li> </ul></li> </ul></li> <li>Confirmations: <ul> <li>“Bulk Edit”: “{field}: {value}, for N posts on {site}? Undo Bulk Edit can put them back.” Yes is “Change N”.</li> <li>“Undo Bulk Edit”: “Put N items on {site} back as they were?” Yes is “Undo”. With nothing to undo: “There is no bulk edit on {site} to undo.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: “N posts changed on {site}.” and “N items put back on {site}.”</li> <li>Without permission: <ul> <li>“Your account on {site} is a {role}, which cannot edit other people’s posts.”</li> <li>This edit_others_posts check is applied to Pages too.</li> </ul></li> <li>Undo: Undo Bulk Edit inside this window. It keeps only the last edit per site.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_structure_commands.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The list rows are not refreshed after Change, so status and author still read the old values.</li> <li>“1 posts” grammar.</li> <li>The Workbench has its own batch editing (Phases 7 to 10), so this window duplicates it.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="manage--page-order">Manage > Page Order…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, M, then R. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Without permission: “Your account on {site} is a {role}, which cannot arrange pages.” When there are no pages: “{site} has no pages yet.”</li> <li>What opens: a list window titled “Page Order, {site}”. <ul> <li>The list “&Pages:” (each title with its level and place).</li> <li>“Move &Up”.</li> <li>“Move &Down”.</li> <li>“Move &In”.</li> <li>“Move &Out”.</li> <li>“Op&en”.</li> <li>Close.</li> </ul></li> <li>What happens: each move saves to the site at once with no confirmation, then speaks the move sentence from site_structure.page_move. A move that cannot be made says “{title} cannot move that way.”</li> <li>Undo: none. There is no undo for a page move.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_structure_commands.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Moves go live on the site with no confirmation and no undo, so a mistaken Alt+I re-parents a page in public menus.</li> <li>Focus returns to the list after a move, but because the rows are rebuilt the selection stays at the old index, not on the page that moved. The row the user hears next may be a different page.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="manage--previous-looks">Manage > Previous Looks…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, M, then K. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Refusals: <ul> <li>“{site} keeps no history of its site styles. That needs a block theme and WordPress 6.3 or later.”</li> <li>“Your account on {site} is a {role}, which cannot change the site’s styles.”</li> <li>“{site}’s theme has no site styles of its own, so it has no earlier looks.”</li> <li>“{site}’s styles have not been changed yet.”</li> </ul></li> <li>What opens: a list window titled “Previous Looks”. <ul> <li>The list “&Looks:”.</li> <li>“&Details:” holds the palette.</li> <li>“&Palette Report…” opens a report titled “Palette Report”.</li> <li>“&Go Back to This Look…”.</li> <li>Close.</li> </ul></li> <li>Confirmation: “Go Back to This Look”: “Make the look from {when} {site}’s current look? The look it has now is kept, so Previous Looks can bring it back.” Yes is “Go Back”.</li> <li>Announced after: “{site} has the look from {when} again.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_route_history.py.</li> <li>Review notes: the window title lacks the site name, unlike its siblings (“Style Variations, {site}”).</li> </ul> <h3 id="manage--space-used">Manage > Space Used</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, M, then S. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Refusals: <ul> <li>“{site} does not say how much space it uses. That needs WordPress 5.6 or later.”</li> <li>“Your account on {site} is a {role}, which cannot see how much space the site uses.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: “{site} uses {size} altogether.” or “{site} could not measure the space it uses.”</li> <li>What opens: a report titled “Space Used” that starts “Space used on {site}:” with each part on its own line.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_route_media.py, site_space.py.</li> <li>Review notes: no “…” although a report opens. The total is announced and then repeated in the report.</li> </ul> <h3 id="manage--download-a-copy-of-the-sites-design">Manage > Download a Copy of the Site’s Design…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, M, then D. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Refusals: <ul> <li>“{site} cannot export its design. Only a block theme on WordPress 5.9 or later can.”</li> <li>“Your account on {site} is a {role}, which cannot download the site’s design.”</li> </ul></li> <li>What opens: after the export, a Save As dialog titled “Download a Copy of the Site’s Design” for zip files.</li> <li>Announced after: “A copy of {site}’s design is saved as {file}, {size}.” Cancel says “Nothing was saved.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_route_media.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="manage--ai-abilities">Manage > AI Abilities…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, M, then T. No WordPress key.</li> <li>What opens: the Workbench at the current site’s “Abilities” node.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_abilities_commands.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The menu says “AI Abilities”, but the node and the legacy window say “Abilities”.</li> <li>Ask the Site’s refusal tells the user to “Turn them on one by one in Abilities”, which now means a Workbench node. This code does not confirm that the Workbench node writes the same per-site choices (ability_choices).</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="manage--ask-the-site">Manage > Ask the Site…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, M, then E. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Site: the front document’s site, otherwise the chooser (titled “Ask the Site”).</li> <li>Refusals: <ul> <li>“{site} offers no abilities for AI. That needs WordPress 6.9 or later, and a site or plugin that describes some.”</li> <li>“No ability of {site} is turned on. Turn them on one by one in Abilities, then ask again.”</li> <li>The AI-off gate speaks for itself.</li> </ul></li> <li>What opens: a multiline text dialog titled “Ask the Site, {site}” with the box “&Question:”. Its F1 says “Ask about your site. The AI you chose in Choose Your AI may ask to use one of the abilities you turned on; QUILL asks you before it runs.”</li> <li>Confirmation: when the AI plans to use an ability, “Run Ability” opens with ab.approval_message. Yes is “Run {ability}”. Cancel says “Nothing was run on {site}.”</li> <li>What opens after: a report titled “Ask the Site, {site}”. Its F1 says “The AI’s answer, then which of the site’s abilities ran to get it.”</li> <li>Cancel: an empty question or Cancel ends silently.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_abilities_commands.py, abilities.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The question is sent to the AI with no consent step. Only running an ability asks.</li> <li>The Question box is multiline, so Enter adds a line and Tab then Enter is needed to ask.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="manage--sign-out-this-site">Manage > Sign Out This Site…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, M, then I. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Site: always the chooser (titled “Sign Out This Site”) when more than one site is connected, even inside a WordPress document. With one site, that site is used without asking.</li> <li>What opens: a chooser titled “Sign Out This Site” with the list “&After signing out:”. The rows are “Remove {site} from QUILL” (selected) and “Keep {site} in the list, to connect again later”. Its F1 says “Signing out revokes QUILL’s application password on {site} itself and forgets it on this computer. Your local drafts are kept either way.” OK (default) and Cancel.</li> <li>What happens: QUILL revokes exactly the password it signs in with (introspect) and forgets it here. With the Remove choice, it also removes the site from the list.</li> <li>Announced after: “Signed out of {site}: the password {name} is revoked on the site and forgotten here, and the site is removed from QUILL.” or “… kept in your list.” When the site cannot say which password QUILL used, it adds “The site did not say which password QUILL used, so revoke it in Application Passwords or the dashboard if you want it gone there too.”</li> <li>Undo: none. Reconnecting needs a new application password.</li> <li>Cancel: silent.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_people_commands.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>It cannot be undone, and the only step is a two-row chooser whose preselected row is the more destructive one (“Remove”), with OK as the default. Enter twice (menu, then dialog) signs out and removes the site. There is no named confirmation, unlike every other destructive command here.</li> <li>With one site there is no site chooser, so nothing on screen names the site before the chooser.</li> <li>It calls self.api(profile) first, so on first use in a session it may announce “{site}: N comments waiting.” just before signing out.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="your-sites--coming-up">Your Sites > Coming Up…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, Y for Your Sites, then U. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Site: every connected site at once. With none, it says “Connect a WordPress site first.” and the Connect flow starts.</li> <li>Announced after: <ul> <li>“N items are scheduled on your N sites. The next is {title} on {site}, {when}.”</li> <li>Or “Nothing is scheduled on your site.”</li> <li>It may add “N sites did not answer.”</li> </ul></li> <li>What opens: only when something is scheduled, a list window titled “Coming Up”. <ul> <li>Its purpose (F1 only): “Everything scheduled on your sites, soonest first.”</li> <li>The list “&Scheduled:” (items, then “Did not answer: {site}” rows). Enter opens the item.</li> <li>“&Open”.</li> <li>Close.</li> </ul></li> <li>Code: wordpress_route_archive.py, across_sites.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Bug: Open or Enter on a “Did not answer: …” row reads items[index] past the end of the list (open_it has no bounds check), which raises an exception instead of speaking.</li> <li>“1 sites did not answer” grammar.</li> <li>When every site fails and nothing is scheduled, no list opens, so the failed sites are named nowhere, unlike What Changed.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="your-sites--what-changed-on-my-sites">Your Sites > What Changed on My Sites…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+T, WordPress submenu, Y, then W. No WordPress key.</li> <li>Site: every connected site.</li> <li>Announced after: <ul> <li>“N changes on M of your N sites.”</li> <li>Or, with one site, “N changes on your site since you last looked.”</li> <li>Or “Nothing new on your N sites since you last looked.”</li> <li>It may add “N sites did not answer.”</li> </ul></li> <li>What opens: a list window titled “What Changed on My Sites”. <ul> <li>Its purpose (F1 only): “What is new on your sites since you last looked here.”</li> <li>The list “&Changes:” (then “Did not answer:” rows).</li> <li>“Open in &Browser”. On a row with no page it says “That one has no page to open.”</li> <li>Close.</li> </ul></li> <li>Side effect: opening it marks every site that answered as looked at now, so the same changes do not show next time.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_route_archive.py, across_sites.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The “looked at” mark is set before the user has read the list, so closing it by accident loses the changes.</li> <li>Enter on a row does nothing (no on_enter), while Enter opens the item in Coming Up. The two sibling windows behave differently.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h2 id="quill-lite-differences">QUILL Lite differences</h2> <p>QUILL Lite reaches every WordPress and Forms command QUILL has, through the same shared handlers: each Lite <code>cmd_wp_*</code> and <code>cmd_forms_*</code> method hands straight to the one <code>WordPressCommands</code> or <code>FormsCommands</code> object QUILL also uses, and both editors build their menus from the same two tables (<code>quill/core/wordpress/menu.py</code> and <code>quill/core/forms/menu.py</code>). A script check of every row in both tables found a Lite handler for each one, so no command is missing on either side. The differences are in how you reach things. Lite has no menu-route pass, so its labels never show an “(Alt+T, …)” path and some submenus have no access letter at all. Lite’s MDI windows change how the one Workbench behaves. And one feature gate (Form Payments) does not apply in Lite. The sections below cover only those differences. The commands themselves are described in the other sections.</p> <h3 id="where-the-rows-sit-in-quill-lite">Where the rows sit in QUILL Lite</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: the rows go where the tables put them in both editors. File > WordPress (submenu), the foot of Edit (Edit > WordPress submenu), the foot of View, the foot of Insert, the foot of Format (Format > WordPress submenu), and the foot of Tools (Tools > WordPress submenu, then Tools > Forms submenu). Forms adds File > New Form, Insert > Form Field, View > Form Outline, Format > Form Rules and Tools > Forms.</li> <li>In Lite’s File menu, New Form comes straight after New Plain Text Document and File > WordPress comes after the Export rows. QUILL puts File > WordPress after Snapshots (<code>main_frame_menu.py</code>). Both are in File, but at a different point in the menu.</li> <li>The Tools menu tail order is the same in both: Tools > WordPress, then Tools > Forms, as the last two entries (<code>lite_placed_rows</code> WordPress first, then Forms; QUILL calls <code>_place_wordpress_rows</code> then <code>_place_forms_rows</code>).</li> <li>Practical Lite route: Tools > Forms is the last row of Tools and Tools > WordPress the second to last. Neither has a letter (see below), so the fastest way in is Alt+T, then Up Arrow once (Forms) or twice (WordPress), then Right Arrow. Edit > WordPress is the last row of Edit: Alt+E, Up Arrow, Right Arrow.</li> <li>Code: lite_window_wordpress.py, lite_window_forms.py, core/lite/commands.py, core/wordpress/menu.py, core/forms/menu.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Lite’s Tools menu has 30 entries, and the two submenus a WordPress user needs most are the last two, with no letter. That only works if the user knows that Up Arrow wraps to the bottom. No label says so.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="access-letters-what-lite-has-and-quill-computes">Access letters: what Lite has and QUILL computes</h3> <ul> <li>Lite takes its letters straight from the tables, as written (<code>lite_window_menus.py</code> <code>_fill_menu</code>). A script walk of Lite’s command table found no duplicate letters in File > WordPress, Tools > WordPress, Tools > Forms, Insert, View or Format.</li> <li>Tools > WordPress: no letter in Lite. The table title is plain “WordPress” because no letter of the name is free in Lite’s Tools: Say &Word Summary holds W, and O, R, D, P, E and S are all held too. The module docstring in <code>wordpress/menu.py</code> says the same.</li> <li>Tools > Forms: no letter in Lite. F (File Encoding), O (Sound Scheme), R (Read-Only Guard), M (Manage Abbreviations) and S (Spelling) are all held (<code>forms/menu.py</code> docstring, confirmed by the table walk).</li> <li>Edit > WordPress: also no letter in Lite (the Edit table title is plain “WordPress”).</li> <li>Format > WordPress: “Word&Press”, so P.</li> <li>File > WordPress: “&WordPress”, so W in Lite’s File.</li> <li>File > New Form: “New For&m…”, so M. Lite’s Close Window (MDI) gives up M; its route is Ctrl+F4.</li> <li>Format > Form Rules: “For&m Rules…”, so M. Lite’s Switch Document Mode gives up M; its route is Alt+Shift+F.</li> <li>View > Form Outline: “&Form Outline…”, so F. Insert > Form Field: “Form &Field…”, so F.</li> <li>Tools > WordPress > Open WordPress Workbench: no letter in either editor (“Every letter of its name is taken in this menu, so its key is the WordPress layer’s H”). It is the first row of the submenu, so Enter right after the submenu opens selects it.</li> <li>In QUILL, <code>menu_routes.py</code> runs over the built menu bar. It gives every submenu without a letter a free one, appending “(&Z)” when none of its own letters is free. It also appends the full Alt path to every enabled row that has no shortcut, for example “Check This Form… (Alt+T, x, C)”. So in QUILL, Tools > Forms and Tools > WordPress get a letter whenever QUILL’s Tools menu has one free. The exact letter depends on QUILL’s whole Tools menu at runtime and was not computed for this walkthrough.</li> <li>QUILL’s pass skips disabled rows, so it re-runs on every refresh (<code>_reapply_menu_routes</code>). A document-only row that becomes enabled gains its route then.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>This is the biggest Lite-versus-QUILL gap in this area. In QUILL every keyless Forms and WordPress row says its Alt path aloud. In Lite the same row says nothing, and its parent submenu has no letter to type. The house rule that every enabled item shows a keyboard route is met in QUILL and not in Lite. That is the permitted direction (Lite behind QUILL), but it is a real cost on every visit.</li> <li>The layer letters and the menu letters disagree for eight rows. The table comment says “A row’s own access letter wherever it is free, so the menu teaches the key”, but File > WordPress has New &Page (layer G), P&ublish (layer P), Co&mpare (layer C), Switc&h Site (layer S), &Sites (layer Shift+S) and This Post in the Wor&kbench (layer Shift+H). Tools > WordPress has Check Before &Publishing (layer K) and Media Li&brary (layer M). The menu does not teach the layer key for these. Because the bracketed chord is in the label, you hear both and have to ignore one. This is the same in both editors.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-wordpress-key-layer-shared-with-three-small-differences">The WordPress key layer (shared, with three small differences)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Grave, release, then one letter, in both editors. The prefix and the letters come from <code>wordpress/menu.py</code> <code>LAYER_KEYS</code> plus Forms’ F (<code>forms/menu.py</code> <code>LAYER_KEYS</code>). The layer’s state machine is <code>quill/core/wordpress/keys.py</code> <code>WordPressKeyLayer</code>, one implementation for both editors.</li> <li>Letters: A Connect a Site, B WordPress Blocks, C Compare with Site, D Save Draft to Site, E Schedule Publish, F Responses (Forms), G New Page, H Open WordPress Workbench, I Open on Site, K Check Before Publishing, L Open Local Draft, M Media Library, N New Post, O Open Page or Post, P Publish or Update, R Properties, S Switch Site, Shift+S Sites, T Move to Trash, U Upload Files, V Previous Versions, W Open by Web Address, X Alt Text Review, Y Copy Address, Shift+H This Post in the Workbench.</li> <li>Announced after: “WordPress keys” when the prefix is pressed. Escape: “Left WordPress keys”. A letter with no command: “No WordPress command on Q.” (with the key spelled). A key with no character (an arrow, Tab, Enter) leaves the layer silently and goes on to the editor. The layer times out after 4 seconds (<code>TIMEOUT_SECONDS</code>), and a key pressed after that is typed normally.</li> <li>How each editor sees the key: <ul> <li>QUILL checks it in the frame’s char hook (<code>main_frame.py</code> <code>_wordpress_key_event</code>), and only while focus is in the document surface.</li> <li>Lite checks it first thing in the editor control’s key-down handler (<code>lite_window_typing.py</code> <code>_on_key_down</code>, then <code>_wordpress_key_down</code>). Both effectively answer only in the document.</li> </ul></li> <li>How each editor decides the layer is on: <ul> <li>QUILL needs the <code>core.wordpress</code> feature on, and steps aside when the user has moved the QUILL key onto the same chord (“the user put the QUILL key here; it wins”).</li> <li>Lite needs at least one <code>cmd_wp_</code> handler in the resolved command table (“the WordPress area is switched off: not our key, Forms’ F or no”). Lite has no QUILL-key check.</li> </ul></li> <li>How each editor runs the command: <ul> <li>QUILL calls <code>_run_command(command_id)</code>.</li> <li>Lite calls <code>run_unless_read_only(handler)</code>, so a layer key in Lite passes through the Read-Only Guard check. No WordPress or Forms handler is listed in <code>TEXT_CHANGING_HANDLERS</code>, though, so in practice the guard never refuses one.</li> </ul></li> <li>Both editors read the letters from the live keymap (<code>layer_from_bindings</code>), so moving a command in the Keyboard Manager moves its layer letter.</li> <li>Keyless: rows not in <code>LAYER_KEYS</code> have no chord and rely on menu letters and the Command Palette. <code>forms/menu.py</code> gives rule 9 as the reason for every Forms row except Responses.</li> <li>Code: core/wordpress/keys.py, lite_window_wordpress.py, main_frame_wordpress.py, lite_window_typing.py, lite_window_menus.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Responses’ label advertises a key that can be dead.
