If Forms is on and WordPress is off (separate features in QUILL, separate areas in Lite), both editors still show “Responses… (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Grave, F)”. The prefix is refused, though, because the layer belongs to WordPress. A Forms-only user is shown a chord that does nothing and is not told why.</li> <li>In Lite, a layer command reached on a document under the Read-Only Guard runs anyway. Every Forms command that rewrites the document (Insert Form Field, Outline moves, Form Rules, Form Properties, Plain Language, Payments Setup) writes through <code>WriteText</code> on the control. Whether a read-only native edit control accepts that is not proven here; verify by hand. The menu route behaves the same way, since <code>_dispatch</code> also goes through <code>run_unless_read_only</code> and finds nothing to refuse.</li> <li>The “WordPress keys” announcement is correct under GATE-13, because the prefix changes nothing on screen.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="document-only-dimming">Document-only dimming</h3> <ul> <li>Both editors dim the rows in each table’s <code>DOCUMENT_ONLY</code> set unless the right kind of document is in front. <ul> <li>WordPress rows: a document with a local WordPress copy (<code>local.copy_for_path</code>).</li> <li>Forms rows: a path ending .qaf or .bfl (<code>is_form_path</code>).</li> </ul></li> <li>QUILL refreshes them from <code>_refresh_contextual_menu_items</code> (<code>_refresh_wordpress_menu_items</code>, <code>_refresh_forms_menu_items</code>). Lite refreshes them from <code>_sync_enabled_items</code> on every document change and on menu open.</li> <li>A dimmed row reached another way still runs: from the Command Palette, or in Lite from a layer key. Its handler then says why. For example: “Check This Form works in a form document, a .qaf file. Start one with New Form in the File menu, or open one.”</li> <li>Not dimmed, by design: New Form, Insert Form Into Page, Responses and Draft a Form. Insert Form Into Page needs a WordPress page rather than a form, and says so: “Insert Form Into Page works in a WordPress page or post. Open one with File, WordPress, then try again.”</li> <li>Code: lite_window_forms.py (<code>_sync_forms_items</code>), lite_window_wordpress.py (<code>_sync_wordpress_items</code>), main_frame_forms.py, main_frame_wordpress.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Insert Form Into Page is enabled in every document, but it only works in a WordPress page. It could be dimmed by the WordPress <code>DOCUMENT_ONLY</code> test instead of always being enabled, and right now it is the only Tools > Forms row that is enabled in places it cannot work.</li> <li>A form is recognised only by its file name. An unsaved buffer holding QAF text, or a form saved under another extension, dims every Forms row.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="opening-the-wordpress-workbench-from-quill-lite">Opening the WordPress Workbench from QUILL Lite</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: <ul> <li>Tools > WordPress > Open WordPress Workbench (no letter; first row of the submenu), or WordPress key then H.</li> <li>On a WordPress document: File > WordPress > This Post in the Workbench (Alt+F, W, K in Lite), or WordPress key then Shift+H.</li> <li>In both editors several Tools > WordPress doors (Comments, Users, Plugins and others, <code>workbench_tree.py</code> <code>_DOORS</code>) open the Workbench at their node instead of a dialog.</li> </ul></li> <li>What opens: the same Workbench frame as in QUILL (<code>open_workbench</code>). There is only one per process (<code>_OPEN["wordpress"]</code>). Opening it again brings the existing frame forward, reloads the profiles, rebuilds the tree, and keeps the node, row and filter where you left them.</li> <li>Undo: the Workbench has its own Edit > Undo WordPress Change (Ctrl+Z), separate from the editor’s undo.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_commands.py (<code>cmd_workbench</code>, <code>cmd_this_post_in_workbench</code>), wordpress_workbench_window.py (<code>open_workbench</code>, <code>open_from_door</code>), workbench_frame_actions.py.</li> <li>Review notes (Lite-specific, because Lite has a window per document): <ul> <li>The single Workbench is bound to the Lite window that first opened it. <code>editor=commands.host.parent</code> is that window, and the provider keeps that window’s commands object and announcer. Opening the Workbench again from a second Lite window brings the same frame forward, still tied to the first window. Switch to Editor then returns to the first window, not the one you came from. If the first window has closed, it says “The editor that opened this window is closed.”</li> <li>The Workbench’s Safe Mode comes only from the <code>QUILL_SAFE_MODE</code> environment variable (<code>cmd_workbench</code>, <code>open_from_door</code>). Forms decides Safe Mode with <code>safe_mode_on(host)</code>, which also reads the editor’s own flag and the command line (<code>--safe-mode</code>). Launched with the flag rather than the variable, Forms commands refuse to reach the site while the Workbench may still write to it. This affects both editors.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="command-palette-profiles-and-feature-areas">Command Palette, profiles and feature areas</h3> <ul> <li>Both editors put every row in the Command Palette (Ctrl+Shift+P). QUILL registers each row with <code>commands.try_register</code>, using the title without “&” and without the trailing “…”. Lite’s palette reads its command table.</li> <li>Lite switches the two features as areas named “wordpress” and “forms”. The WordPad and Notepad profiles leave both off (“Neither program had a way to publish to a website”, “Neither program ever made a form”). QUILL switches them as <code>core.wordpress</code>, <code>core.forms</code> and a third feature, <code>core.forms_payments</code> (“Form Payments”), which is off in the Essential profile.</li> <li>Code: core/lite/features.py, core/lite/command_areas.py, core/features.py, core/feature_catalog.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Lite has no Form Payments gate. <code>_payment_document</code> asks the host’s editor for <code>_feature_enabled("core.forms_payments")</code>. A Lite window has no <code>_feature_enabled</code> method, so the check is skipped and Form Payments Setup, Insert Payment Field and Verify Payment Setup always run in Lite whenever Forms is on. In QUILL, with Form Payments off, the same rows stay visible and say “Turn on Form Payments in Features before using this command.” This is a divergence, and in the “Lite has it, QUILL may not” direction the house rule forbids.</li> <li>In QUILL, those three payment rows stay on the menu and enabled with the feature off. You find out the feature is off only by choosing one.</li> <li>Both editors describe Forms as local when it is not. The Lite area description ends “Everything here runs on this computer”, and QUILL’s feature description says “Runs on this computer”. But Publish Form, Insert Form Into Page, Test on Site, Responses, Verify Payment Setup and the three AI rows all reach a site or an AI.</li> <li>Lite’s Everything profile calls AI help “the one area here that does something outside this computer”. WordPress and Forms also reach the network.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="keys-no-divergence">Keys: no divergence</h3> <ul> <li>Every WordPress and Forms default key is the same in both editors. QUILL’s <code>DEFAULT_KEYMAP</code> takes them from <code>wordpress/keys.py</code> <code>default_bindings()</code>. Lite’s rows carry the same layer chords from <code>lite_rows()</code>. The parity table <code>quill/core/lite/parity.py</code> maps each Lite layer handler to its QUILL command id, for example <code>cmd_forms_responses</code> to <code>forms.responses</code>, and has no <code>DIVERGENCES</code> entry for any WordPress or Forms row.</li> <li>No <code>DEFAULT_ALIASES</code> entry exists for any WordPress or Forms command (none found in <code>keymap.py</code>).</li> <li>The Workbench’s own keys (Ctrl+Shift+A, Ctrl+Shift+S, Ctrl+Z, Alt+Enter and the rest) belong to the Workbench window (<code>workbench_window_menu.py</code>, <code>WORKBENCH_KEYMAPS</code> in <code>app_keymaps.py</code>), so they are identical whichever editor opened it.</li> <li>How labels show the layer chord: <ul> <li>In Lite, a two-press chord goes in brackets after the label, for example “Responses… (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Grave, F)” (<code>lite_window_menus.py</code>), because wx would drop it after a tab.</li> <li>QUILL’s <code>_menu_label</code> shows the bound chord the same way.</li> </ul></li> <li>Code: core/keymap.py, core/lite/parity.py, core/wordpress/keys.py, lite_window_menus.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="words-that-say-quill-inside-quill-lite">Words that say QUILL inside QUILL Lite</h3> <ul> <li>The shared Forms code speaks as QUILL in both editors. For example: New Form’s purpose “QUILL writes it as plain text in the QAF form language”, the failure “QUILL could not save {name}: …”, Add Rule’s “QUILL writes it into the form”, and Test on Site’s “so QUILL opened it in QUILL Accessible Forms on the site”.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>A Lite user hears “QUILL” for the editor they are in. Whether that is acceptable branding, or whether these strings should name the product through the host, is the owner’s call.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h2 id="the-wordpress-workbench-window">The WordPress Workbench window</h2> <p>The WordPress Workbench is one top-level window (title “WordPress Workbench”) built on the shared Workbench frame: a Navigator tree on the left, then a filter box, an Items list, a read-only Details box and a focusable status bar on the right and bottom, with its own nine-menu bar (File, Edit, View, Go, Item, Site, Tools, Window, Help). The tree holds fixed nodes (Start Here, Needs My Attention, Sites, Calendar, Across Sites, Search, Practice Site, Help and Learning) and, under Sites, one copy of a large per-site template for every connected site. Lists load in the background when a node is selected; Enter runs a row’s default action, the Applications key opens its context menu, Alt+Enter opens Properties. Everything below is read from the code (the frame in quill/ui/workbench_frame*.py, the provider in quill/ui/wordpress_workbench_window.py and its mixins, and the wx-free tables in quill/core/wordpress and quill/core/workbench). The per-node content (Posts, Media, Comments, Plugins and so on) is covered in other sections; this section covers the window itself. The review notes are the point: several are real defects found by reading the key routing, not style preferences.</p> <h3 id="opening-it">Opening it</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it from QUILL: Tools > WordPress > Open WordPress Workbench… (the first row of the Tools > WordPress submenu). The row has no access letter by design; the code comment says every letter of its name is taken in that menu. Its key is the WordPress layer: Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Grave, then H (menu.py LAYER_KEYS “H”: “wordpress.workbench”).</li> <li>This Post in the Workbench: File > WordPress > This Post in the Wor&kbench (Alt+F, W, K), or the WordPress layer then Shift+H. Note it lives in File > WordPress, not Tools > WordPress.</li> <li>The Tools > WordPress “doors” always open the Workbench now. Each classic command (Comments, Media Library, Manage Terms, Menus, Site Check, Site Settings, Site Map, Users, Application Passwords, Plugins, Themes, Fonts, Synced Patterns, Widgets, Abilities) calls workbench_door, which opens the Workbench on the matching node (door_node table in workbench_tree.py: for example Comments opens Comments > Waiting, Site Map opens Pages, Site Check opens Site Health).</li> <li>“Workbench as default” option: not found in code for WordPress. There is no setting; open_from_door always returns True and the retired classic windows are not an alternate route (wordpress_workbench_window.py docstring, “W6”). The GitHub Workbench has its own preference; WordPress has none.</li> <li>QUILL Lite: the same two commands, Tools > WordPress > Open WordPress Workbench and File > WordPress > This Post in the Workbench (lite_window_wordpress.py cmd_wp_workbench, cmd_wp_this_post_in_workbench), with the same WordPress layer keys, reaching the same shared code.</li> <li>What opens: one window per session. If it is already open, it is brought forward and, when no node was asked for, stays on the node, row, filter and scroll you left. Otherwise a new frame is built (980 by 680 DIP).</li> <li>Where it lands: with at least one site connected, Needs My Attention > Inbox; with none, Start Here. A door lands on its node of the first connected site in your site list.</li> <li>What is focused first: the Navigator tree (show() ends with tree.SetFocus()), with the landing node selected. The Inbox then loads in the background from every site.</li> <li>What is said on opening: nothing (GATE-13). The reader says the window title and the selected tree item. If the first load fails: “Could not load Inbox: “ followed by the error text.</li> <li>With no sites connected: the Details box says Start Here’s text and “You have 0 sites connected.”; every Go key and every site command says “Connect a site first: File > WordPress > Connect a Site, in the editor.”</li> <li>This Post in the Workbench, said in the editor before anything opens: “<title> is not on <site> yet, so the Workbench has no post to show. Save Draft to Site sends it first.” when the document was never sent; “<site> is not among the Workbench’s sites.” when the site is not connected. Otherwise the window opens on Posts, Pages or the site’s content type node, waits for discovery, selects the post’s row and moves focus to the list. If the row is not in the first load it types the title into the filter box once; if still not found: “<title> is not in this list on <site>. It may have been deleted, or be in a status this list does not show.” If the content type is unknown to the site: “<site> does not show this document’s content type in its REST index, so <title> has no list here.”</li> <li>Picking mode (Insert > Picture from the Media Library, other section): opens on the document site’s Media and says “Picking a file for <document>: Enter on a file inserts it.”</li> <li>Closing: Alt+F4, or File > Close Workbench (Alt+F, C). Escape never closes it. The editor stays open. Closing cancels pending background work and stops the Practice Site process if the Workbench started one (provider.close calls stop_process); nothing is said about that.</li> <li>Switch to Editor: Ctrl+Shift+E, or Window > Switch to Editor (Alt+W, E). Raises and focuses the editor that opened the window; says nothing on success, and “The editor that opened this window is closed.” if it is gone.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_window.py (open_workbench, open_from_door, _start_ref), wordpress_workbench_editor_side.py, wordpress_commands.py, lite_window_wordpress.py, core/wordpress/menu.py, workbench_frame.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Start Here still says “This is an early preview, behind a developer setting. Your Tools > WordPress windows still work.” (workbench_help.py START_HERE). Both sentences are false now: there is no developer setting and the doors always open the Workbench. The first thing a new user reads is wrong.</li> <li>A door opens on the FIRST site in the store, not the default site and not the site of the open document: current_site() falls back to next(iter(self._profiles)) because frame.current is still None when _start_ref runs. With two sites, Tools > WordPress > Users from a document on site B opens site A’s Users.</li> <li>The window does not remember its place across closes. The frame supports recall_place/remember_place hooks, but the WordPress provider implements neither, so every fresh open starts at Inbox with the tree collapsed. The GitHub Workbench does remember.</li> <li>Closing the Workbench silently stops a running Practice Site. A person who opened the practice site from here and closes the window to write in the editor loses it without a word.</li> <li>The workbench_door docstring (wordpress_commands.py) still says it returns False “without the developer flag”; it never does. Harmless to users, misleading to maintainers.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="layout-and-panes">Layout and panes</h3> <ul> <li>Panes in F6 order: Navigator (tree), Filter (text box), Items (list), Details (read-only multi-line text), then the status bar. F6 moves forward, Shift+F6 back; also View > Next Pane (Alt+V, N) and View > Previous Pane (Alt+V, P). From the status bar F6 wraps to the tree. A status bar with no cell showing is skipped.</li> <li>Spoken names: the tree is named “Navigator”, the list “Items”, the filter box “Filter”, the details box “Details”. On screen the filter has the label “Filte&r:” (Alt+R jumps to it) and the details box the label “&Details” (Alt+D jumps to it). Neither letter collides with a menu title (F, E, V, G, I, S, T, W, H).</li> <li>Tab also moves tree, filter, list, details; the status bar takes no Tab stops (only F6 reaches it).</li> <li>F1 help strings: tree “Where you are. Arrows move, Right and Left open and close a branch, and typing jumps to a name. F6 moves to the filter box.” (then replaced by the node’s own help as you move); list “What is here. Enter does the default action, the Applications key lists the rest, and Alt+Enter opens Properties.” (then replaced by node and row help); details “The selected row in full, or what this view shows and needs.”</li> <li>Start Here: Go > Start Here, Ctrl+9 (Alt+G, S). Selects the Start Here node and puts focus on the tree. Start Here has no list; its text (START_HERE plus “You have N sites connected.”) is put in the Details box.</li> <li>Keys the frame owns outside the menu: Escape clears the filter if it has text, otherwise goes Back; Backspace in the tree or list goes Back; Alt+Home goes to Inbox; Ctrl+R reloads (hidden alias of F5); Ctrl+Shift+F moves to the filter box (see review notes); Ctrl+Break says “Not available here.”</li> <li>Code: workbench_frame.py (_build, panes, cycle_pane), workbench_frame_commands.py (_on_char_hook), workbench_frame_keys.py, workbench_help.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Start Here’s words live in the Details box, but Ctrl+9 leaves focus on the tree, over an empty list. A first-time user has to press F6 three times (filter, list, details) to read the page they asked for. Ctrl+9 should land in Details, or Start Here should open its text where focus goes.</li> <li>Escape outside the filter box goes Back to the previous node, from any pane, including Details. “Read in Full” (Enter on a comment, plugin, fact and many other rows) moves focus to Details; the natural Escape to return to the list instead navigates the tree to a different node and throws away the list you were reading. Escape in Details should return to the list.</li> <li>Backspace in the tree goes Back. The native tree’s type-ahead uses letters, so this is safe, but Backspace as navigation is a browser idiom that surprises in a tree.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-tree">The tree</h3> <ul> <li>Built from WORDPRESS_TREE (workbench_tree.py) plus places from workbench_places.py, workbench_dashboard_places.py, workbench_sweep_places.py, workbench_pmpro_tree.py, workbench_shop.py and workbench_gravity_actions.py. The native wx.TreeCtrl with a hidden root; the reader says level and expanded state itself.</li> <li>Top-level nodes, in order, with their children: <ul> <li>Start Here (no list).</li> <li>Needs My Attention (folder): Inbox; Comments Waiting; Pending Review; Scheduled; Drafts; Updates; Site Health; Accessibility; Broken Links; Held as Spam; Form Responses; Membership Orders; Watches.</li> <li>Sites: lists the connected sites (columns Site, Address, Role); under it one node per site, labelled with the site’s name.</li> <li>Calendar: lists days; under it one Day node per day (dynamic).</li> <li>Across Sites (folder): Tags and Categories; Plugins; Themes; People; Settings; Menus; Synced Patterns; Application Passwords; Fleet.</li> <li>Search (words typed in the filter box are searched on every site).</li> <li>Practice Site.</li> <li>Help and Learning (folder): How WordPress Thinks (folder, text in Details); Words; Keys.</li> </ul></li> <li>Each site’s children, in order (the site node itself is a folder): <ul> <li>Overview (At a Glance; Activity; wp-admin Screens).</li> <li>Posts (Published; Drafts; Pending; Scheduled; Private; Trash; Group).</li> <li>Pages (Published; Drafts; Pending; Scheduled; Private; Trash; Group).</li> <li>More Content Types (one node per post type the site describes, each with its own status children).</li> <li>Media (Pictures; Audio; Video; Documents; Unattached; Without Alt Text; Group; Housekeeping).</li> <li>Comments (Waiting; Approved; Spam; Trash; Group).</li> <li>Categories; Tags; More Taxonomies (one node per extra taxonomy).</li> <li>Menus and Navigation.</li> <li>Appearance (folder): Themes; Global Styles; Templates; Template Parts; Additional CSS; Patterns; Fonts; Widgets and Sidebars.</li> <li>Forms (Responses; Held as Spam; Feeds; Results).</li> <li>Memberships (folder): Membership Levels; Orders; Subscriptions; Renewals Due; Discount Codes.</li> <li>Shop (Shop Orders; Draft Products; On Sale; Out of Stock; Customers; Coupons; Shop Reports; Store Settings; Product Reviews; Shipping Zones; Tax Rates; Tax Classes; Attributes; Brands; Webhooks).</li> <li>Podcast.</li> <li>Events Calendar (Past Events; Venues; Organizers; Event Categories; Event Tags).</li> <li>SEO (Site SEO).</li> <li>People (folder): Users; Application Passwords.</li> <li>Plugins (Group).</li> <li>Settings.</li> <li>Site Health (Information; Security).</li> <li>Abilities.</li> <li>By Name (folder): Every Term; Field Values; Authors; Media Usage; Patterns Used; Links; Blocks and Shortcodes; Forms Used; Template Parts.</li> <li>Content Audit; Privacy Requests; Redirects (404 Log); Search and Upkeep; Administration Areas; Content Model.</li> <li>More from This Site (one node per REST namespace).</li> </ul></li> <li>Group nodes are labelled “Group” and are empty until View > Group By fills them with “value (count)” children.</li> <li>Lazy loading: the tree is built in full at once; nothing loads on expand. A list loads, off the UI thread, each time a node is SELECTED. Moving to another node before an answer arrives drops the stale answer (a generation counter). Discovered nodes (content types, taxonomies, namespaces) are added when each site’s discovery finishes, by rebuilding the whole tree.</li> <li>What is announced on expand or select: nothing by the app (GATE-13). The Details box changes to the node’s help; the tree’s F1 text becomes the node’s authored sentence (workbench_node_help.py NODE_HELP) plus the provider’s words (“<label>, <site>.”, plus “Gathered from every connected site, newest first.” for Needs My Attention nodes, or “Open it with Right Arrow to see what it holds.” for folders). A failed load is spoken: “Could not load <node>: <error>”.</li> <li>Enter on a tree node moves focus to the list (even for a folder, whose list is empty). The tree’s context menu is the “node” row type (see The context menu).</li> <li>Code: workbench_tree.py, workbench_places.py, workbench_dashboard_places.py, workbench_sweep_places.py, workbench_node_help.py, core/workbench/tree.py, workbench_frame.py (_build_tree, rebuild_tree, _show_node).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Every site gets every plugin’s branch whether or not the plugin is installed: Shop alone is 16 nodes, Events Calendar 6, Memberships 6, plus SEO, Podcast, Forms’ Feeds and Results. That is well over 100 nodes per site, most of which, on a typical site, load only a sentence such as “<site> does not list WooCommerce’s wc/v3 routes in its REST index, so it has no shop QUILL can read.” For a screen-reader user arrowing through a site this is the single biggest cost in the window. Hide or collapse branches whose namespace is absent from the site’s REST index.</li> <li>Every tree selection fires a network load. Arrowing down through a site sends a request per node passed (stale answers are dropped, but the requests still go out). A short delay before loading, or loading only on Enter or a pause, would cut the traffic and the “Loading” churn in the status bar.</li> <li>Discovery rebuilds the whole tree once per connected site shortly after opening. rebuild_tree deletes and re-adds every item, so any branch you expanded in the first seconds collapses again, and the current node is re-selected (the reader may say it again) and its list reloaded. Keep expansion state across rebuilds, or add discovered nodes in place.</li> <li>“Drafts” under Needs My Attention is “Your own drafts, on every site” (NODE_HELP), but the label is just “Drafts”, the same word as Posts > Drafts. “My Drafts” would say what it is.</li> <li>“Site Health” and “Held as Spam” each appear twice with different meanings (across sites under Needs My Attention, and per site). The labels do not say which is which; only the position does.</li> <li>Search has no Go key and no menu row; it is reachable only through the tree, and it only works with words typed into the filter box, which is not obvious from the node name.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-list">The list</h3> <ul> <li>Columns: each node names its own (for example Posts: Title, Status, Author, Modified; Comments: Author, On, Status, When; Needs My Attention > Inbox: What, Site, When). Headers are made by capitalizing the column key with underscores turned to spaces (“alt_text” reads “Alt text”).</li> <li>View > Columns…, Ctrl+Alt+C (Alt+V, C): a dialog titled “Columns” with one check box per column, in a group labelled “Columns to show:”, then OK and Cancel. The node’s own columns come first, then extras for the row kind (for posts: Date, Slug, Number, Type, Comments, Sticky, Format, Parent, Site). Announced after: “<n> columns.”; with nothing checked: “A list needs one column at least, so nothing changed.” Not on a list node: “Choose a list in the tree first.”</li> <li>View > Group By…, Ctrl+Alt+G (Alt+V, G): works on Posts, Pages, Media, Comments and Plugins (and their Group node). A list dialog “Group By” with the prompt “Group by:” and rows “Nothing (no groups)” then each column title. Choosing a column fills the Group node with one child per value, “value (count)”, and says “<n> groups under Group, by <column>.”; choosing Nothing says “No groups.” Elsewhere: “Group By works on Posts, Pages, Media, Comments and Plugins.”</li> <li>View > Load More, Ctrl+Shift+L (Alt+V, M): asks for three more pages of 100. Says “Reading up to <n> rows.” (600 after the first press) and reloads.</li> <li>View > Load All, Ctrl+Alt+L (Alt+V, L): asks for up to 100 pages (10,000 rows). Says “Reading every row. A big site takes a while.” and reloads.</li> <li>First load is 3 pages (300 rows). When a list stops at its limit, the Items status cell adds “, more on the site”.</li> <li>View > Refresh, F5 (Alt+V, R); Ctrl+R does the same. Reloads and keeps your place: the same item is selected and focused in one change, and a reload that found nothing new does not touch the list at all (list_place.py landing_index, same_cells).</li> <li>Multi-select: the list is a multi-selection report list: Shift+Arrows extend, Ctrl+Arrows and Ctrl+Space pick. Alt+2 says the count. Batch-aware commands (Trash, Restore, Publish, Approve and the others, Properties) act on every selected row.</li> <li>Enter: the row type’s default action (the first row of its context menu): Open in Editor on posts and pages, View or Play on media, Read in Full on comments and most others (which moves focus to Details), Show Its Posts on terms and users, Open This Site on a site row.</li> <li>Empty list: nothing is said and no “empty” sentence is written; the Details box keeps the node’s help and the Items cell says “0 shown”. The frame has an empty_text hook for “why it is empty and what fills it”, but the WordPress provider does not implement it.</li> <li>Code: workbench_frame.py (_set_columns, _fill_list, default_action), core/workbench/list_place.py, wordpress_workbench_lists.py, core/wordpress/workbench_listing.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Column headers and the Columns dialog disagree on names: the list header is name.replace(“_”, “ “).capitalize() in the frame, the dialog uses column_title, which renames some (“mime_type” is “File type” in the dialog, “Mime type” in the header; “id” is “Number” in the dialog and “Id” in the header). One function should name a column everywhere.</li> <li>The Columns dialog’s help says “Check each column the list shows, in this order. The first is what your screen reader says first.” but the dialog cannot reorder anything; the order is fixed. Either offer Move Up and Move Down or drop “in this order”.</li> <li>Column choices, groupings and Load More depth are kept in memory only (column_choice, grouping, paging on the provider) and are lost when the window closes.</li> <li>Group By counts come from the rows currently loaded and filtered (frame.rows), not from the site, so “Draft (12)” can be the 12 among the first 300, not the site’s total. The node label gives no hint.</li> <li>Load More, Load All, Refresh and remote filters announce when they START but never when they finish. A blind user hears “Reading every row. A big site takes a while.” and then silence, with no signal that the list is ready except polling the Loading status cell. GATE-13 does not forbid saying a background result; “Loaded 1,240 rows.” is exactly the kind of thing the reader cannot know.</li> <li>The precise sentence cut_words builds (“The first 300 are shown, and the site has more. View > Load More reads 300 more; Load All reads every one.”) is computed and stored but never shown or spoken; only the terse “, more on the site” reaches the Items cell.</li> <li>“More on the site” is decided by count >= limit, so a site with exactly 300 posts is told there are more.</li> <li>An empty list says nothing. “No comments are waiting on Radio.” in Details (via the empty_text hook) would answer the first question a listener has.</li> <li>Ctrl+A and Ctrl+C do nothing in the list: the frame has select_all and copy verbs, but the WordPress menu binds neither, and a native list view does not select all on Ctrl+A. Select-all-and-act is a common screen-reader workflow (for example Approve all waiting comments).</li> <li>After any refresh the landing row is selected alone (focus_row deselects the others), so a multi-selection is lost after every batch action.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-filter-box-and-filter-commands">The filter box and filter commands</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+E, or Edit > Filter… (Alt+E, F), or Alt+R on its label, or F6 from the tree. The Filter status cell’s Enter also moves there.</li> <li>What it does: Enter runs the filter. Plain words and key:value qualifiers are parsed into one query. On posts, pages, media, comments, users, terms, patterns and Search, both words and qualifiers go to the site (search argument and REST parameters), so the filter reaches the whole site; on other lists the words filter the loaded rows on this computer. Escape clears it. Moving to another node clears it silently (“A filter belongs to the list it was typed for”).</li> <li>Help text (F1 in the box): “Type words to search the whole site, or qualifiers such as status:draft, category:news, -tag:old, author:3, after:2026-01-01 or modified:>2026-10-01. Enter runs it, Escape clears it, and Ctrl+Shift+F opens the Filter Builder.”</li> <li>Aliases a person may type: category, tag, published (after), modified and changed (modified_after), type (media_type), mime, role, words (search), comment_type. A qualifier the route does not take fails the load: “Could not load <node>: This list cannot be filtered by <name>.”</li> <li>Announced after Enter: “<n> shown.” when filtered locally; “Filtering.” when the site is asked (and nothing when the answer arrives).</li> <li>Filter Builder…, Ctrl+Shift+F (Alt+E, B): three dialogs in a row, each titled “Filter Builder”: a list “Filter on:” (for posts: Status; Author number; Category, by name or number; Tag, by name or number; Published; Last changed; Sticky, yes or no; Parent number; Slug; Search in: post_title, post_content or post_excerpt; media, comments and users have their own short lists; other nodes offer their column names); a list “How:” with is, is not, after, before; a text box “Value:” (“Several values are separated by commas. Dates are written 2026-11-09.”). The condition is appended to the filter box text and run. On Forms the Gravity Forms builder runs instead (other section).</li> <li>Show Only Like This…, Ctrl+Alt+O (Alt+E, O), and Hide Like This…, Ctrl+Alt+H (Alt+E, D): a list dialog titled “Show Only” or “Hide”, prompt “Like this row by:”, rows such as “Status: Draft” and “Author: Sam” (on site-filtered lists only status and author; on local lists every non-empty cell). The condition is added and run. With no row: “Choose a row first.”; with nothing to offer: “This list can be narrowed by its words only. Type them in the filter box.”</li> <li>Only Mine, Ctrl+Alt+M (Alt+E, M): toggles author:<your user number>. Adding runs the filter (says “<n> shown.” or “Filtering.”); removing says “Showing everyone’s.” Outside a site’s list: “Only Mine works in a site’s lists once the site has said who you are.”</li> <li>Alt+1 reads the filter in words: “<path>, filtered to status is draft, containing radio”.</li> <li>Code: core/workbench/filters.py, core/wordpress/workbench_filter.py, wordpress_workbench_people.py (do_filter_builder, _like, do_only_mine), workbench_frame.py (apply_filter, clear_filter).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Ctrl+Shift+F never opens the Filter Builder. The frame’s key hook (_on_char_hook: “if event.ControlDown() and event.ShiftDown() and code is F: self.filter.SetFocus()”) runs before menu accelerators (the keep_list_key docstring states that order), so the chord only moves to the filter box. The menu shows Ctrl+Shift+F, the filter box help and the Keys node both teach it, and only the menu click works. This is a GitHub-only alias leaking into WordPress.</li> <li>The same hook catches Ctrl+Alt+Shift+F (it checks Control and Shift, not Alt), so Tools > Forget a Saved Request’s chord also just moves to the filter box.</li> <li>“Filtering.” is said and then nothing: the listener never hears how many rows the site sent back. Say “<n> shown.” when the remote answer fills the list.</li> <li>Filter wording is raw: Only Mine adds author:7, so Alt+1 says “filtered to author is 7”, and the Filter Builder asks for “Author number” and “Parent number”. People do not know their user number. describe() takes attribute labels but the WordPress window never passes any.</li> <li>The Filter Builder offers “after” and “before” for every attribute, including Status and Slug, where they mean nothing, and offers no “contains”. Operators should follow the attribute.</li> <li>Only Mine’s refusal (“once the site has said who you are”) is wrong when the real problem is that you are on Needs My Attention or another non-site node.</li> <li>Edit > Filter… has an ellipsis but opens no dialog; it only moves focus.</li> <li>Filters are cleared without a word when you change nodes. A listener who filters Posts, checks Pages and comes back finds the filter gone. At least the Filter status cell shows “No filter”.</li> <li>describe_scope in workbench_filter.py (the “Filtered on this computer, 12 rows loaded” sentence the docstring promises) is not used anywhere in the UI.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-properties-sheet-and-site-properties">The Properties sheet and Site Properties</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach Properties: Alt+Enter, Item > Properties… (Alt+I, P), the last row of every context menu (“Properties…”). With focus in the tree, or no row selected, Alt+Enter opens the site’s sheet instead (or says “Alt+Enter on a row shows its Properties; Alt+Shift+Enter, the site’s.” when not inside a site).</li> <li>How to reach Site Properties: Alt+Shift+Enter, Site > Site Properties… (Alt+S, P), or the Site status cell’s context menu, “Site Properties”.</li> <li>What opens (one row): the object is read again first (off the UI thread; nothing is said while it reads), then a dialog titled “<title> Properties - <site>” (for example “Hello World Properties - Community Radio”). A notebook of pages; on each page a label and a control per field; then Copy as Text, OK, Cancel and Apply. Ctrl+Tab moves between pages.</li> <li>Item pages (workbench_sheets.py _item_sheet): General (Title, Slug, Status as a choice of publish, draft, pending, future, private; Password, masked and always empty, spoken “Password, hidden as you type”; Author as a choice; Published as text like 2026-11-09T14:00; Last changed and Address read only); Organize (Categories and Tags as comma lists by name or number; Format and Sticky on posts); Featured Image (chosen from the library, with the buttons “C&hoose from Library…” and “&Remove Featured Image”); Excerpt; Discussion (Comments and Pingbacks, open or closed); Template and Parent on pages (Template, Parent number, Order); Meta and an Advanced Custom Fields page where the site shows them. Other row kinds have their own static sheets or one built from the route’s schema.</li> <li>Controls: text, numbers, dates and lists are text boxes (lists comma separated); choices are choice boxes; flags are check boxes; read-only or locked fields are read-only text boxes (Tab reaches them), with the reason in their F1 help: “<help> Read only for you: <reason>”.</li> <li>Buttons, in Tab order: Copy as &Text, OK (no access key; Enter), &Apply, Cancel (no access key; Escape). On screen the order is Copy as Text, then OK, Cancel, Apply. A sheet you cannot save has only Copy as Text and Close.</li> <li>What each button does: OK saves what changed and closes at once; Apply saves and keeps the sheet open; Cancel and Escape close without saving and without asking; Copy as Text copies every page as plain text and says “Copied.”</li> <li>Saving: only fields whose value changed are sent. The site’s modified time is checked first; a conflict sends nothing and asks, in a Yes/No box titled “Changed on the Site”: “<title> was changed on <site> after you opened it. Nothing was sent. Send your changes anyway, replacing theirs?” with the buttons “Use Mine” and “Cancel” (Cancel is the default).</li> <li>Preview: none for posts, pages, media, comments, terms or users. A site setting is previewed in a dialog “Change a Setting”: summary “Change <label> on <site>. Ctrl+Z puts it back.” and a line “<label> (<key>): <before> becomes <after>”, button “Change”. A setting that would lock someone out is refused with its reason.</li> <li>Announced after: “Saved <n> change(s) to <title> on <site>.” plus “ The site did not keep: <fields>.“ when the site ignored some; “Nothing changed on this row.” when the body was empty; the error text on failure.</li> <li>Several rows (Alt+Enter with more than one selected, same kind and same site): a batch sheet titled “<n> items Properties - <site>” for posts, pages, custom items and media. Fields that differ show “Mixed” (choice “(Mixed: leave as it is)”, check box third state) and are not sent; categories and tags have add and remove fields. Saving asks in “Change Several”: “Change <n> items on <site>: <field> to <value>?” followed by up to 25 titles, button “Change Them”. Announced: “Changed <good> of <total> on <site>.” Failures ask “Some Were Not Changed”: “<n> were not changed: … Try them again?” with “Try Again”. Refusals: “Several rows at once need to be the same kind, from one site.”; “For several comments, use Approve, Unapprove, Spam or Delete.”; “Several at once can be changed for posts, pages, custom items and media.”</li> <li>Site Properties: titled “<site> Properties”. Pages General (Name, Tagline, Address, WordPress version), Your Role (Role, What your role allows), Connection (Signs in as, Kind of site, Workbench access), REST Namespaces, Spam (Comments wait for approval, Spam plugins found, Form plugins found, Form spam checks, About spam here). All read only except the QUILL Accessible Forms part of the Spam page where the site allows it.</li> <li>Your Profile (Site > Your Profile…, below) uses the same editor.</li> <li>Undo: a single-row save records an undo (“Put <title> back as it was.”); a batch records “Put <n> back as they were.” Ctrl+Z.</li> <li>F1: on each field, its help; on the sheet, “Everything about it on one sheet. Ctrl+Tab moves between pages; OK saves and closes, Apply saves and stays.”</li> <li>Without permission: every editable field is locked with the reason, and OK and Apply are not offered, when the row’s kind is read only here (“This is read only here.”), when Workbench access or View As Role forbids writing, or when your role lacks the capability (“Editing pages needs the Editor role or higher. You are an Author on Radio.”). Single fields with their own requirement are locked one by one.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_sheets.py, workbench_sheet_editor.py, core/wordpress/workbench_sheets.py, core/wordpress/workbench_edit.py, wordpress_workbench_sheet_guards.py, core/workbench/properties.py. workbench_properties_sheet.py and workbench_properties_dialog.py are NOT used by the WordPress window (only GitHub’s).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>WordPress gets the simpler sheet. GitHub’s PropertiesSheet has Ctrl+S to apply, Ctrl+Enter for OK from a multi-line box, F5 reload, Ctrl+1 to Ctrl+9 page jumps, Ctrl+Shift+C copy, a “Sheet status” line showing what the last save did, and opens on the last page used. The WordPress sheet has none of these, so the two Workbenches teach different keys for the same window.</li> <li>OK closes before the save is done. If the save then fails, or a conflict is answered Cancel, the typed changes are gone with no way back. Keep the sheet open until the site answers, or reopen it with the edits on failure.</li> <li>Apply twice in one sheet will likely raise a false conflict: on_save always passes the modified time read when the sheet opened, so the second Apply finds the site’s time changed by your own first Apply and asks “changed on the site after you opened it … replacing theirs?”. (Plausible; depends on the modified time changing between saves.)</li> <li>Apply updates the sheet as if the save succeeded before the site answers; there is no status line on this sheet to show a failure.</li> <li>Changing Status to publish on the sheet publishes immediately on OK, with no “Visitors will see them” confirmation, while Item > Publish or Update asks first. The same outcome should not have two confirmation levels.</li> <li>Status values are raw slugs (publish, future, pending), and Comments and Pingbacks are “open” and “closed”. WordPress’s own screen says Published, Scheduled, Pending Review, Allow comments. A choice box can show words and send slugs.</li> <li>The batch confirmation prints list values as Python tuples: “Add categories to (‘News’, ‘Events’)” (the f-string formats the tuple from value_of). Format lists as words.</li> <li>The conflict box offers “Use Mine” and “Cancel”; the module docstring promises “Use Mine or Keep Theirs”. “Keep Theirs” (and re-reading their version) is the more useful second choice than a bare Cancel that discards your edits silently.</li> <li>Escape and Cancel discard edits without asking, even after many changes across pages.</li> <li>Tab order (Copy as Text, OK, Apply, Cancel) does not match the visual order (OK, Cancel, Apply) because Apply is created before Cancel.</li> <li>Field labels carry no access keys, so a long General page can only be walked with Tab.</li> <li>Site Properties is almost all read-only text boxes inside a modal sheet with OK/Cancel semantics; “Close” is right, but a listener must Tab through every read-only box to reach it or press Escape.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-context-menu">The context menu</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: the Applications key or Shift+F10 on a list row (or right-click). With no row selected, or in the tree, you get the node’s menu.</li> <li>Structure (core/workbench/actions.py ActionTable.menu_for, workbench_frame_actions.py _popup): the row type’s rows in order, the first being the default (Enter); at most 8 entries show, so when there are more than 7 actions, 6 show, then a “More” submenu with the rest; Properties… is always the last row. A More holding a single row is not made. The frame also supports “Show Only” and “Hide” cell submenus before Properties, but the WordPress provider does not implement cell_rows, so they never appear.</li> <li>Examples (from WORDPRESS_ACTIONS): <ul> <li>A post or page: Open in Editor; Preview on the Site; Visual Review…; Publish or Update…; Move to Trash; Restore from Trash; More (Delete Permanently…, Copy Link, Copy to Site…, Approve and Schedule…, Send Back with a Note…, Assign a Reviewer…, Move to Another Day…, Show Its Comments, Earlier Versions…, Duplicate as Draft, Counts by Status, Empty Trash…, Open in wp-admin); Properties…</li> <li>A comment: Read in Full; Approve, or Not Spam; Not Spam, and Allow This Sender; Unapprove; Spam; Reply…; More (Move to Trash, Restore from Trash, Delete Permanently…); Properties…</li> <li>A user: Show Their Posts; Change Role…; Show Memberships…; Check Membership Access…; Change Membership Level…; Cancel Membership…; More (Their Application Passwords…, Sign Out Everywhere…, Invite from a Spreadsheet…, Send Password Reset, Set New Password…, Roles and What They May Do, Remove…); Properties…</li> <li>A tree node: Refresh; New Here…; Load More; Load All; Columns…; Group By…; More (What Can I Do Here?, Counts by Status, Empty Trash…, wp-admin for This Place…); Properties…</li> <li>A fact, help or problem row: Read in Full; Properties…</li> </ul></li> <li>Tiers (Tier in actions.py): READ (0), ORGANIZE (1, local or easily undone: Trash, Restore, moderation), WRITE (2, changes the site), DESTRUCTIVE (3, deletes or cannot be undone). The tier decides what Workbench Access allows (Read Only allows READ; Organize adds ORGANIZE; Full allows all). How each is confirmed is up to each handler, not the tier: <ul> <li>READ: never confirmed.</li> <li>ORGANIZE: one row is done at once (Move to Trash of one post, Approve, Spam); several rows ask (“Move <n> items on <site> to the Trash?”, button “Move to Trash”); moderation never asks, even for many.</li> <li>WRITE: a preview or Yes/No naming the site and the items (Publish: “Publish <n> items on <site>? Visitors will see them.” button “Publish”; Change Role preview with gains and losses, button “Change Role”; plugin “Activate <name> on <site>?”).</li> <li>DESTRUCTIVE: a Yes/No with No as default and “This cannot be undone.” (Delete Permanently: “Delete <n> items from <site> permanently? This cannot be undone: <names>.” button “Delete Permanently”; Revoke: button “Revoke”; Merge Into: a preview, button “Merge”; Convert: button “Convert”).</li> </ul></li> <li>Greying by requirement: before the menu shows, each row is enabled only if provider.supports passes, which checks Workbench access (Safe Mode and View As Role count as Read Only) against the row’s tier, then the capability from users/me (for example “edit” on a page means edit_pages, “publish” means publish_posts or publish_pages, “delete” on a term means manage_categories).</li> <li>What greyed rows say: in a context menu, nothing; the row is simply disabled. In the menu bar, the reason is put into the row’s help string. If you run a refused command by key, the refusal is spoken: “<Doing this> needs the <Role> role or higher. You are <a role> on <site>.” or “Workbench access on <site> is Read Only. Site > Workbench Access changes it.” or “Safe Mode is on, so the Workbench only reads.” or “You are viewing <site> as <Role>, which only reads. Site > View As Role ends it.”</li> <li>Code: core/workbench/actions.py, core/wordpress/workbench_actions.py, workbench_frame_actions.py, wordpress_workbench_people.py (refusal_for), core/wordpress/workbench_permissions.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The reason a row is greyed is never heard. The menu-bar reason goes into the item’s help string, but the frame calls SetStatusBarPane(-1), so menu help is shown nowhere, and screen readers do not read menu help strings. In context menus no reason is set at all. The only place the real sentence appears is when a command is run by key.</li> <li>The WordPress provider has no reason() hook, so the frame’s fallback is used: every greyed row’s reason is “Not available here.” (workbench_frame_commands.py reason), including in Shift+F1’s list (“, greyed: Not available here.”). The good refusal sentences exist in refusal_for but are not wired to reason().</li> <li>Context menu rows show no keys and have no access letters, and “More” has none either. The help strings carry the keys (“Delete.”, “Ctrl+Shift+P.”) but, as above, are never displayed. A listener learns no chords from the context menu.</li> <li>On a post, Copy Link and Delete Permanently sit inside More, behind Visual Review and Restore from Trash. Restore from Trash is shown on top even for a post that is not in the Trash (it then says “Nothing selected is in the Trash.”). The top six should be the ones that apply to the row’s state.</li> <li>“Empty Trash…” (a whole-site, permanent action) is offered on a single post’s context menu, under More. A row menu should hold only actions on the row; this one belongs on the node.</li> <li>On a user, “Cancel Membership…” (DESTRUCTIVE) is on top while “Send Password Reset” is buried in More.</li> <li>Greying only reflects permission, never applicability: a comment command on a post row is enabled and then says “Choose a comment or a form entry.” when run (see the Item menu below).</li> <li>Merge Into and Convert are DESTRUCTIVE and confirmed, but record nothing in Change History at all, while Delete Permanently records an entry marked cannot be undone. Change History is therefore not “every change made here”.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="history-and-undo">History and undo</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach Undo: Ctrl+Z in the list, or Edit > Undo WordPress Change (Alt+E, U) from anywhere.</li> <li>How to reach Change History: Ctrl+Shift+H, or Edit > Change History… (Alt+E, H).</li> <li>What Undo does: takes the newest change in this session’s history and runs its inverse off the UI thread, then refreshes. Spoken: the inverse’s own sentence (“Restored <n> items from the Trash.”, “Put <n> items back as they were.”, “Put <title> back as it was.”, “<name> is <Role> again.”, “Moved them back to the Trash.”). With nothing: “Nothing to undo.” When the newest change has no inverse: “<summary> This cannot be undone.”</li> <li>What Change History shows: a report dialog titled “Change History”, a read-only multi-line box labelled “Report:” with “1. <summary>” lines newest first, “ (cannot be undone)“ after those without an inverse, then Copy All and Close. With nothing: “No changes made in the Workbench yet.”</li> <li>Undoable here: Trash (restores), Restore (trashes again), Publish (puts the old statuses back), moderation (old statuses), one-row sheet saves, batch sheet saves, Change Role, plugin activate or deactivate.</li> <li>Recorded but not undoable: Delete Permanently, Remove person, Revoke application password, Set New Password, imports from a spreadsheet or folder, Plain-Language Batch, REST Console writes, Put Back from a Snapshot.</li> <li>Not recorded at all: Merge Into, Convert, New Here, Empty Spam, Empty Comment Trash, Reply, Allow This Sender (other sections may add more).</li> <li>Limits: 200 changes (ChangeHistory.limit); kept in memory for this window only, lost when it closes; no Redo command on the WordPress menu, though the model supports it.</li> <li>Ctrl+Z outside the list: in the filter box it undoes typing; in the tree or Details it does nothing and says nothing (keep_list_key swallows it). From the status bar: “Delete and Ctrl+Z act on the list. F6 moves back to it.”</li> <li>Code: core/workbench/history.py, wordpress_workbench_sheets.py (do_undo, do_history), wordpress_workbench_commands.py, workbench_frame_keys.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>A change that cannot be undone blocks every earlier undo. next_undo() always returns the newest change, and undo() never removes one without an inverse. After one Delete Permanently, Import, REST Console write or Plain-Language Batch, every Ctrl+Z repeats “<summary> This cannot be undone.” and the Trash you did five minutes earlier can no longer be undone from here. Ctrl+Z should skip (and say it skipped) entries with no inverse, or Change History should let you undo a chosen entry.</li> <li>Change History is read only: you cannot pick an entry to undo, and entries have no times (Change.when is never set).</li> <li>The undo message for summaries like the batch (“move all drafts by Sam to pending on Radio This cannot be undone.”) reads as one run-on sentence; the summary needs its own full stop.</li> <li>Ctrl+Z in the tree is silently ignored; a short “Ctrl+Z works in the list, or use Edit > Undo.” would be better than nothing.</li> <li>A failed undo leaves the change on top, so pressing Ctrl+Z again retries; that is reasonable, but the failure text is the raw error.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-status-bar">The status bar</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: F6 (after Details) or Shift+F6 from the tree. Left and Right (and Up and Down) move between cells, Home and End jump to the ends, Enter or Space does a cell’s action, the Applications key or Shift+F10 opens its menu, Escape returns to where you were. Tab leaves it. The same facts are mirrored into a native status bar line, so a screen reader’s read-status-bar key says them too.</li> <li>Cells, in order, each a button named “<name>, <value>” and shown only when it has a value: <ul> <li>Site: the site of the selected node (on Search, the selected row’s site or “All connected sites”). Enter: the site’s Overview. Menu: Site Properties.</li> <li>Your role: “<Role> on <site>”, or “Viewing as <Role> on <site>”. Enter: the role-aware What Can I Do Here report. Menu: View As Role…</li> <li>Items: “<n> shown”, plus “, more on the site” when the list was cut, plus “, <n> selected” when more than one is selected. Enter: focus to the list.</li> <li>Filter: the filter in words, or “No filter”. Enter: the filter box. Menu: Clear Filter.</li> <li>Loading: “Loading” or “Up to date, <a href="HH:MM">HH:MM</a>”. Enter: refresh. Menu: Refresh.</li> <li>Waiting: “<n> comments waiting”. Enter: Needs My Attention > Comments Waiting.</li> <li>Link check: Check Every Link’s progress while it runs. Enter stops it. Menu: Stop Checking Links.</li> <li>Workbench access: Read Only, Organize or Full. Enter: Workbench Access.</li> </ul></li> <li>Every cell menu ends with Copy and Help for This Cell; Help for This Cell says “<name>. <help>”.</li> <li>Nothing is announced when cells change (GATE-13).</li> <li>Where Am I, Alt+1 (View > Where Am I, Alt+V, W): says the tree path and the filter: “Sites, Community Radio, Posts, Drafts, filtered to author is 3”. Before anything is shown: “Nowhere yet”.</li> <li>Say Selection, Alt+2 (View > Say Selection, Alt+V, S): “<n> of <total> selected”.</li> <li>Code: workbench_status_bar.py, wordpress_workbench_window_status.py, workbench_frame.py (where), workbench_frame_actions.py (say_selection).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The Waiting cell’s count is filled only when Needs My Attention > Comments Waiting has been loaded in this window; before that the cell is absent, and it goes stale until that node is loaded again. It reads like a live count and is not.</li> <li>“1 comments waiting”: the plural is fixed.</li> <li>The Role cell’s Enter opens the provider’s role-aware report (“You are <Role> on <site>. … <Action>: yes” or the refusal), which is what Shift+F1 promises but does not open (see Help). Two different What Can I Do Here windows exist.</li> <li>The Filter cell is always present with “No filter”, so F6 into the bar always lands on Site, Your role, Items, Filter, Loading before anything useful changes; fine, but the Items cell already says the count, so most visits read five cells to learn one fact.</li> <li>A site name containing “&” becomes an access-key mnemonic in the cell’s button label (SetLabel with the raw name). Minor, but it can swallow the character.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="f1-shiftf1-and-help-for-this-view">F1, Shift+F1 and Help for This View</h3> <ul> <li>F1, or Help > Help for This View (Alt+H, H): the shared Context Help dialog, titled “WordPress Workbench”, with “Where you are: <node help>” and then the focused control’s own help (the tree’s is the node’s sentence; the list’s is the node’s words plus “Enter on a row: <default action>. Shift+F1 lists everything you can do with the selected row, Alt+Enter shows all about it.”; a row’s is ROW_HELP for its kind).</li> <li>Shift+F1, or Site > What Can I Do Here? (Alt+S, W): the frame’s action list, titled “What Can I Do Here”, prompt “Actions for the selection:”, a list of every action for the selected row’s kind (or the node’s), each “<label>, <key>” and greyed ones “, greyed: <reason>”; below it “About this action:” with the action’s help; buttons Run (default, &R) and Close. Enter runs the selected action after the dialog closes; on a greyed one it beeps.</li> <li>Help and Learning nodes in the tree: How WordPress Thinks (a paragraph in Details), Words (18 glossary rows), Keys (18 key rows).</li> <li>Code: workbench_frame_help.py, workbench_help_dialogs.py, core/wordpress/workbench_node_help.py, core/wordpress/workbench_help.py, app_context_help.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Shift+F1 opens the wrong window. “what_can_i_do” is a frame verb, so the frame’s generic list runs and the provider’s do_what_can_i_do (which starts “You are <Role> on <site>. Workbench access on <site> is <Access>.” and gives the real refusal for each action) is unreachable from Shift+F1 and from the menu. The menu row’s help says “What your role allows.” and the Keys node says “Shift+F1: What your role lets you do here.”; neither is what opens. The role-aware report is reachable only from the Your role status cell.</li> <li>In the frame’s list, every greyed row says “greyed: Not available here.” (no reason hook), and “About this action” shows only the help, never the reason. So the window that exists to say why you cannot do something never says why.</li> <li>The list is a plain list box; “greyed” rows are not disabled items, just text, and Enter on them only beeps.</li> <li>F1 has no purpose sentence (no help_purpose hook) and no “open the guide” button: guide routes are registered only for GitHub windows (guide_opener.py), so the WordPress Workbench’s F1 cannot open a user guide.</li> <li>Alt+F1 (What Is This?), Ctrl+F1 (guide chapter), Ctrl+Shift+F1 (Search Help), Alt+Shift+F1 (Keys) and F9 (repeat last result) exist in the shared frame but are not on the WordPress menu, so they are not bound. The Keys node lists only 18 keys and is called “Every key the Workbench answers to” in its help; it omits Ctrl+9, Ctrl+N, F5, the Go row beyond Ctrl+1 and Ctrl+2, and the whole Site and Tools menus.</li> <li>The Keys node teaches Ctrl+Shift+F for the Filter Builder, which does not work (see The filter box).</li> <li>The Help menu has one row. Start Here (Ctrl+9) is on the Go menu, and What Can I Do Here? is on the Site menu; a listener looking for help in Help finds neither.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="workbench-access-view-as-role-and-roles">Workbench Access, View As Role, and Roles</h3> <ul> <li>Workbench Access: Ctrl+Alt+A, Site > Workbench Access… (Alt+S, A), or Enter on the Workbench access status cell. A list dialog titled “Workbench Access”, prompt “How much may the Workbench change on <site>?”, rows “Read Only: look, never change”, “Organize: also Trash, Restore and moderate comments”, “Full: everything your role allows”, the current one selected; OK and Cancel. Kept per site on this computer (workbench_access.json); Full when never chosen. Announced after: “Workbench access on <site> is <Read Only|Organize|Full>.” F1: “A limit on top of your role, kept for this site on this computer.”</li> <li>View As Role: Ctrl+Alt+V, Site > View As Role… (Alt+S, V), or the Your role cell’s menu. A list dialog “View As Role”, prompt “See the site as:”, rows “Myself, as I am”, Subscriber, Contributor, Author, Editor, Administrator. Announced: “Viewing <site> as <Role>. Nothing can be changed until you stop.” or “Viewing <site> as yourself again.” While viewing, access is Read Only and refusals are computed with WordPress’s default capabilities for that role. Kept for this window only.</li> <li>Roles and What They May Do: Ctrl+Alt+R, Site > Roles and What They May Do (Alt+S, R), or a user row’s context menu (under More). A report dialog of each default role and what it adds, then “WordPress’s REST API reads roles but cannot change what a role may do. A role plugin such as Members or User Role Editor does that in wp-admin.”</li> <li>Code: core/wordpress/workbench_permissions.py, wordpress_workbench_people.py, wordpress_workbench_lists.py (do_roles).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Workbench Access, Safe Mode and View As Role are enforced only for commands that appear in the selected row’s action table (refusal_for returns ““ for any command not in that table, and for no row). Menu-bar-only commands do not check access: Site > Empty Spam and Empty Comment Trash (permanent deletes), Edit > Import Posts and Import a Folder of Documents, Tools > WXR Files > Import WXR as Drafts, Site > Put Back from a Snapshot, Tools > REST Console (POST, PUT, PATCH and DELETE go straight through after its own preview), and New Here. So “Read Only: look, never change” and View As Role’s “Nothing can be changed until you stop” are not true, and Safe Mode (“the Workbench only reads”) can still delete every spam comment. This is the most serious finding in the window.</li> <li>Ctrl+Alt+R does not open Roles: the frame’s key hook treats any Ctrl+R without Shift, Alt included, as Reload (“event.ControlDown() and not event.ShiftDown() and code is R”), and it runs before the menu accelerator. Only the menu and the context menu reach it.</li> <li>When you are not inside a site (for example on Needs My Attention), all three silently act on the first connected site; only the Workbench Access prompt names it.</li> <li>View As Role always preselects “Myself, as I am”, not the role you are currently viewing as.</li> <li>View As Role is per window and forgotten on close; Workbench Access is per site and persistent. The status bar shows both, but nothing tells you that one survives and the other does not.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="menu-bar-file">Menu bar: File</h3> <ul> <li>New Here… - Alt+F, N - Ctrl+N - makes something new on the current node (full treatment below).</li> <li>Open on the Site - Alt+F, S - Shift+Enter - the selected row, or the site, in your browser (below).</li> <li>Preview on the Site - Alt+F, V - Ctrl+Enter - a post or page’s preview in your browser (below).</li> <li>(separator)</li> <li>Close Workbench - Alt+F, C - Alt+F4 - closes this window; the editor stays.</li> <li>Access letters: N, S, V, C, no collisions, none missing.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>New Here’s menu help, “A new user or password, where you are.”, is out of date: it also makes posts, pages, custom items, categories, tags, terms, menus, redirects, discount codes, shop and event items and forms.</li> <li>File has no Close Window-style “Open the Editor” or Export; that is fine, but Export List and the imports live in Edit, where a Windows user would look under File.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="menu-bar-edit">Menu bar: Edit</h3> <ul> <li>Undo WordPress Change - Alt+E, U - Ctrl+Z - puts back the newest change (History and undo above).</li> <li>Change History… - Alt+E, H - Ctrl+Shift+H - the session’s changes (above).</li> <li>(separator)</li> <li>Copy Link - Alt+E, L - Ctrl+Shift+C - copies the selected row’s address (below).</li> <li>Filter… - Alt+E, F - Ctrl+E - moves to the filter box (The filter box above).</li> <li>Filter Builder… - Alt+E, B - Ctrl+Shift+F (menu only, see review) - builds a condition in three steps.</li> <li>Show Only Like This… - Alt+E, O - Ctrl+Alt+O - keeps rows like the selected one.</li> <li>Hide Like This… - Alt+E, D - Ctrl+Alt+H - hides rows like the selected one.</li> <li>Only Mine - Alt+E, M - Ctrl+Alt+M - only what you wrote, or everyone’s again.</li> <li>(separator)</li> <li>Export List… - Alt+E, X - Ctrl+Shift+X - this list as Excel, CSV or Markdown (below).</li> <li>Import Posts from a Spreadsheet… - Alt+E, I - Ctrl+Shift+I - posts or pages from a file, previewed (below).</li> <li>Import a Folder of Documents… - Alt+E, T - Ctrl+Alt+Shift+I - each document a draft, previewed (below).</li> <li>Access letters: U, H, L, F, B, O, D, M, X, I, T, no collisions, none missing.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>No Cut, Copy, Paste or Select All rows: Ctrl+C and Ctrl+A do nothing in the list (see The list), and the familiar Edit menu shape is missing.</li> <li>Only Mine is a toggle but is not a check item, so the menu does not say whether it is on.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="menu-bar-view">Menu bar: View</h3> <ul> <li>Refresh - Alt+V, R - F5 - loads this view again (Ctrl+R too).</li> <li>Load More - Alt+V, M - Ctrl+Shift+L - three hundred more rows.</li> <li>Load All - Alt+V, L - Ctrl+Alt+L - every row, up to ten thousand.</li> <li>Columns… - Alt+V, C - Ctrl+Alt+C - choose the columns.</li> <li>Group By… - Alt+V, G - Ctrl+Alt+G - a child node per value.</li> <li>(separator)</li> <li>Back - Alt+V, B - Alt+Left - the view before (“Nothing to go back to.” when none). Backspace and Escape too.</li> <li>Forward - Alt+V, F - Alt+Right - the view you went back from (“Nothing to go forward to.”).</li> <li>(separator)</li> <li>Next Pane - Alt+V, N - F6.</li> <li>Previous Pane - Alt+V, P - Shift+F6.</li> <li>(separator)</li> <li>Where Am I - Alt+V, W - Alt+1 - path and filter, spoken.</li> <li>Say Selection - Alt+V, S - Alt+2 - “<n> of <total> selected”.</li> <li>Access letters: R, M, L, C, G, B, F, N, P, W, S, no collisions, none missing.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>“Load All” says “Read every row the site has.” but stops at 10,000 (ALL_PAGES 100 pages of 100).</li> <li>Back’s help mentions Backspace but not Escape, which also goes Back and is the more surprising of the two.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="menu-bar-go">Menu bar: Go</h3> <ul> <li>Each Go row selects a node and puts focus on the tree. Site rows use the current site (the site of the selected node, else the first connected site); without any site: “Connect a site first: File > WordPress > Connect a Site, in the editor.”</li> <li>Start Here - Alt+G, S - Ctrl+9 - Start Here (text in Details).</li> <li>Needs My Attention - Alt+G, N - Ctrl+1 - lands on Needs My Attention > Inbox (Alt+Home does the same).</li> <li>Current Site - Alt+G, I - Ctrl+2 - the site’s Overview.</li> <li>Posts - Alt+G, P - Ctrl+Shift+1.</li> <li>Pages - Alt+G, A - Ctrl+Shift+2.</li> <li>Media - Alt+G, M - Ctrl+Shift+3.</li> <li>Comments - Alt+G, C - Ctrl+Shift+4.</li> <li>Users - Alt+G, U - Ctrl+Shift+5 - People > Users.</li> <li>Plugins - Alt+G, L - Ctrl+Shift+6.</li> <li>Appearance - Alt+G, E - Ctrl+Shift+7 - lands on Appearance > Themes.</li> <li>Settings - Alt+G, G - Ctrl+Shift+8.</li> <li>Site Health - Alt+G, H - Ctrl+Shift+9 - the site’s Site Health.</li> <li>Across Sites - Alt+G, O - Ctrl+0 - the Across Sites folder (an empty list).</li> <li>Calendar - Alt+G, D - Ctrl+3 - every site’s content by day.</li> <li>Watches - Alt+G, W - Ctrl+5 - Needs My Attention > Watches.</li> <li>Practice Site - Alt+G, R - Ctrl+6 - the Practice Site node.</li> <li>(separator)</li> <li>Parent Node - Alt+G, T - Alt+Up - selects the parent node and focuses the tree; at a top-level node it does nothing and says nothing.</li> <li>Keyless: none. Ctrl+4 and Ctrl+8 are deliberately unbound (window menu comment, decided 2026-11-13).</li> <li>Access letters: S, N, I, P, A, M, C, U, L, E, G, H, O, D, W, R, T, no collisions, none missing.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Every Go key leaves focus on the tree, not the list. After Ctrl+Shift+4 (Comments) you still need F6 twice to reach the comments. A Go key is a “take me to the things” key; landing in the list (or making that an option) would save two presses every time.</li> <li>The Go row’s digits are not in the order of the tree: Ctrl+1 Needs My Attention, Ctrl+2 Current Site, Ctrl+3 Calendar, Ctrl+5 Watches, Ctrl+6 Practice Site, Ctrl+9 Start Here, Ctrl+0 Across Sites, with 4 and 8 empty. The menu lists them Start Here first but Start Here is Ctrl+9. Hard to learn by ear.</li> <li>“Appearance” lands on Themes, and “Needs My Attention” lands on Inbox, not on the folder the label names; the Where Am I words will differ from the menu word.</li> <li>Across Sites lands on a folder with an empty list; Ctrl+0 then Right Arrow is always needed.</li> <li>Practice Site appears as Go > Practice Site (Ctrl+6) and Tools > Practice Site… (Ctrl+Alt+P), two rows with the same name doing different things (a node versus a start/stop dialog).</li> <li>Parent Node at the top silently does nothing.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="menu-bar-item">Menu bar: Item</h3> <ul> <li>Properties… - Alt+I, P - Alt+Enter - the selection’s sheet (The Properties sheet above).</li> <li>Publish or Update… - Alt+I, U - Ctrl+Shift+P - publish the selection after a Yes/No (see the Posts section).</li> <li>Approve, or Not Spam - mark as spam (see the Comments section).</li> <li>Reply… - Alt+I, R - Ctrl+Shift+M - reply to a comment (see the Comments section).</li> <li>Not Spam, and Allow This Sender - Alt+I, L - Ctrl+Alt+W - approve and let the sender through (see the Comments section).</li> <li>Merge Into… - no access letter - Ctrl+Alt+U (see the Forms section).</li> <li>Resend Notifications… - no access letter - Ctrl+Alt+I (see the Forms section).</li> <li>Gravity Forms (submenu) - Alt+I, F - then: Show Its Feeds (F); Show Its Results (R); Test This Form… (T); Change a Field Type… (Y); Edit Form… (E); New Gravity Form… (W); Copy as a QUILL Accessible Form… (Q); Copy QAF Form as a Gravity Form… (G); separator; Activate or Deactivate Feed… (A); Rename Feed… (N). None has a chord; KEYLESS reasons (workbench_gravity_actions.py) include “A form-design change made rarely (rule 9).”, “Renaming a feed is rare (rule 9).”, “New Here (Ctrl+N) on a site’s Forms node reaches it.” (see the Forms section).</li> <li>(separator)</li> <li>Move to Trash - Alt+I, T - Delete (list only) - into WordPress’s Trash; Ctrl+Z restores. No collisions. Missing: the four Gravity entry rows (Entry Notes…, Star or Unstar Entry, Mark Entry Read or Unread, Resend Notifications…) have no “&” at all (GRAVITY_ITEM_ROWS labels). menus.problems() accepts a top-level row without a letter when it has a chord, so the gate passes.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The four Gravity entry rows cannot be reached by letter. Free letters remain (H, J, K, Q, X, Z), so they could have them.</li> <li>The Item menu has 23 top-level rows plus a 10-row submenu, for every row kind at once. Greying is by permission only, so on a post, Approve, Spam, Reply, Merge Into, Convert, Entry Notes, Star and the rest are all enabled and each says a refusal sentence when chosen (“Choose a comment or a form entry.”, “Merge Into works on a category, tag or term.”). A listener walks past 15 irrelevant rows to find Publish. Grey by row kind too, or build the Item menu from the selected row’s action table (which is what the actions.py docstring says the Item menu is: “the same rows, in the same order”).</li> <li>Greying is computed only when a menu opens (EVT_MENU_OPEN), never on selection change. On Windows a disabled menu item’s accelerator does not fire, so a chord greyed the last time you opened the menu (for example while access was Read Only) likely stays dead, silently, until you open that menu again. (Plausible; verify on Windows.)</li> <li>Delete Permanently’s help says “after you confirm by name”, which suggests typing the name; the dialog only lists names and asks Yes or No. Say “after you confirm” or really ask for the name.</li> <li>Delete and Shift+Delete do nothing, silently, in the tree and Details (keep_list_key swallows them); in the filter box they edit text, which is right.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="menu-bar-site">Menu bar: Site</h3> <ul> <li>Site Properties… - Alt+S, P - Alt+Shift+Enter - the site’s sheet (The Properties sheet above).</li> <li>What Can I Do Here? - Alt+S, W - Shift+F1 - the action list (Help above; see review there).</li> <li>View As Role… - Alt+S, V - Ctrl+Alt+V - (Workbench Access above).</li> <li>Workbench Access… - Alt+S, A - Ctrl+Alt+A - (above).</li> <li>Roles and What They May Do - Alt+S, R - Ctrl+Alt+R (menu only, see review) - (above).</li> <li>Empty Spam… - Alt+S, M - Ctrl+Alt+E - deletes every spam comment on the site after “Delete all <n> comments held as spam on <site> permanently? This cannot be undone.” (button “Delete All”) (see the Comments section).</li> <li>Empty Comment Trash… - Alt+S, E - Ctrl+Alt+Shift+E - the same for the comment Trash (see the Comments section).</li> <li>Comment Counts - Alt+S, C - Ctrl+Alt+Shift+C - waiting, approved, spam and Trash counts (see the Comments section).</li> <li>Your Profile… - Alt+S, Y - Ctrl+Alt+Shift+P - your own profile on this site (below).</li> <li>(separator)</li> <li>Compare Sites… - Alt+S, I - Ctrl+Alt+K (see the Across Sites section).</li> <li>Site Sets… - Alt+S, T - Ctrl+Alt+T (see the Across Sites section).</li> <li>Across Sites Scope… - Alt+S, O - Ctrl+Alt+Y (see the Across Sites section).</li> <li>Take a Snapshot… - Alt+S, N - Ctrl+Alt+S (see the Snapshots section).</li> <li>Put Back from a Snapshot… - Alt+S, K - Ctrl+Alt+Shift+S (see the Snapshots section).</li> <li>Read Site as Document - Alt+S, D - Ctrl+Alt+D - the site’s pages as one document in the editor (below).</li> <li>Access letters: P, W, V, A, R, M, E, C, Y, I, T, O, N, K, D, no collisions, none missing.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>“Empty Spam” takes M and “Empty Comment Trash” takes E, so the two siblings that start with the same word do not share a letter pattern; acceptable, but E for “Empty” belonging to the second row is a trap for the first.</li> <li>Empty Spam and Empty Comment Trash run under Read Only, Safe Mode and View As Role (see Workbench Access).</li> <li>What Can I Do Here? belongs in Help.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="menu-bar-tools">Menu bar: Tools</h3> <ul> <li>Workbench Options… - Alt+T, O - Ctrl+Shift+O - which Watches run (below).</li> <li>REST Console… - Alt+T, R - Ctrl+Shift+T - any route on the site (below).</li> <li>Run a Saved Request… - Alt+T, Q - Ctrl+Alt+Q - pick a saved request, then the console (below).</li> <li>Saved Requests (submenu) - Alt+T, V - then 1 to 9 - your saved requests (below).</li> <li>Forget a Saved Request… - Alt+T, F - Ctrl+Alt+Shift+F (menu only, see review) - remove one (below).</li> <li>Plain-Language Batch… - Alt+T, L - Ctrl+Alt+B - a change said in words (below).</li> <li>Practice Site… - Alt+T, P - Ctrl+Alt+P - start, stop, connect or reset the practice site (see the Practice Site section).</li> <li>WXR Files (submenu) - Alt+T, W - then Export Site as WXR… (E) and Import WXR as Drafts… (I); no chords, KEYLESS reasons “Moving hosts or keeping a copy: a few times a site’s life (rule 9).” and “Moving content in happens once per move (rule 9).” (see the WXR section).</li> <li>Access letters: O, R, Q, V, F, L, P, W, no collisions, none missing.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Workbench Options holds only Watches. The name promises the window’s options (columns, landing node, Go-key focus, announcements) and delivers one checklist; “Watches…” would be honest, or the options should grow.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="menu-bar-window">Menu bar: Window</h3> <ul> <li>Switch to Editor - Alt+W, E - Ctrl+Shift+E - brings the editor forward (Opening it above).</li> <li>Access letters: E. One row.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The menu could also list the editor’s other open windows; with one row it is a long way to one command that already has a key.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="menu-bar-help">Menu bar: Help</h3> <ul> <li>Help for This View - Alt+H, H - F1 - the layered Context Help (F1 above).</li> <li>Access letters: H. One row.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Start Here, What Can I Do Here?, How WordPress Thinks, Words and Keys are all help and none is here.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="new-here">New Here</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+N, File > New Here… (Alt+F, N), or New Here… on a tree node’s context menu.</li> <li>What it makes, by node: Posts and its status nodes, a post; Pages, a page; a content type node, an item of that type; Categories, Tags or a taxonomy node, a term; Menus and Navigation, a menu (with a menu row selected it first asks “New Here”, “Make:”, rows “A new item in <menu>” and “A new menu”); Users and Application Passwords, a person or an application password (see the People section); Discount Codes, Redirects, shop and event nodes, Forms (a Gravity form) and Content Model each hand off to their own command (other sections).</li> <li>What opens for posts, pages and terms: one small text dialog per field, in a row, each titled “New <Noun>”: “Title:” (“The title it starts with. It is saved as a draft.”); for terms “Name:”, for categories “Parent number (optional):” (“0 or empty for a top level.”), then “Description (optional):”. OK and Cancel; Cancel at any step stops.</li> <li>Announced after: “Made the <noun> <name> on <site> as a draft.” (items) or without “as a draft” (terms, menus); empty name: “It needs a name, so nothing was made.”; elsewhere: “New Here makes posts, pages, custom items, categories, tags, terms, menus, people and application passwords. Choose one of those nodes first.”</li> <li>Undo: not recorded; Ctrl+Z does not remove what was made.</li> <li>Without permission: not checked (see Workbench Access); the site’s own refusal is spoken if it refuses.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_lists.py (do_new_here), wordpress_workbench_people.py, core/wordpress/workbench_create.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>A new post or page is made on the site and then left there: it is not opened in the editor, the new row is not selected, and the person must find it in the list. “Made the post X on Radio as a draft.” followed by opening it in the editor (or at least selecting the row) is the expected flow.</li> <li>A category asks for its parent by number. The site has the category list; a choice would do.</li> <li>A chain of one-field dialogs, each with the same title, is slow and gives no sense of how many steps remain.</li> <li>Not in Change History, so Ctrl+Z cannot undo an accidental Ctrl+N.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="open-on-the-site-preview-copy-link">Open on the Site, Preview, Copy Link</h3> <ul> <li>Open on the Site: Shift+Enter, File > Open on the Site (Alt+F, S), and on several context menus. Opens the selected row’s address in your browser; with no row (or a row without an address), the site’s home page. Spoken only on failure: “This has no address on the site yet.”</li> <li>Preview on the Site: Ctrl+Enter, File > Preview on the Site (Alt+F, V), the second row of a post’s context menu. A post or page only; adds preview=true to the address unless it is published. Spoken: “Preview works on a post or page.” when not one; “This has no address on the site yet.”</li> <li>Copy Link: Ctrl+Shift+C, Edit > Copy Link (Alt+E, L), a context-menu row (under More on posts). Copies the selected row’s address, or the site’s address when there is none.
