Spoken: “Copied.” or “Could not copy.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (do_open_on_site, do_preview), workbench_frame_actions.py (copy_link, copy_text), wordpress_workbench_window.py (url).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>With nothing selected, or on Needs My Attention, Open on the Site and Copy Link fall back to the FIRST connected site’s address and say only “Copied.” A listener cannot tell they copied a home page rather than the row. Say what was copied (“Copied the address of Hello World.”) or refuse without a row.</li> <li>Shift+Enter and Ctrl+Enter are window accelerators, so they also fire from the filter box, where a listener pressing Shift+Enter by habit opens a browser instead of running the filter.</li> <li>Preview of a draft needs a signed-in browser; nothing says so if the browser is not signed in to wp-admin.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="export-list">Export List</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+X, Edit > Export List… (Alt+E, X).</li> <li>What opens: a standard Save dialog titled “Export List” with types “Excel workbook (*.xlsx)”, “CSV (*.csv)” and “Markdown table (*.md)”, asking before overwriting.</li> <li>What it writes: the list as shown, the chosen columns (by their dialog titles) and the rows currently in the list (filtered, loaded so far).</li> <li>Announced after: “Exported <n> rows to <file name>. Left out: columns you have hidden (<names>), each item’s full text, its pictures and files, and rows outside the list as shown (filtered out, or not loaded yet).” With no rows: “There is nothing in the list to export.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_bold_lists.py (do_export_list), core/wordpress/workbench_io.py, core/wordpress/workbench_carry.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The left-out sentence is spoken in full on every export. It is honest but long; the hidden-columns part is news, the rest is the same every time and could be in the help.</li> <li>When the list stopped at its 300-row limit, the export silently writes 300 rows; the generic “not loaded yet” phrase does not say that this export is short. Say “300 of more than 300; Load All first for every row.”</li> <li>The file is written on the UI thread; a 10,000-row Excel export will freeze the window briefly.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="import-posts-from-a-spreadsheet">Import Posts from a Spreadsheet</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+I, Edit > Import Posts from a Spreadsheet… (Alt+E, I).</li> <li>What opens: an Open dialog titled “Import Posts from a Spreadsheet” filtered to “Spreadsheets (<em>.xlsx;</em>.csv)”. Then a preview dialog titled “Import into <site>”: a “Summary:” box (“<n> posts will be made on <site> as drafts (or pending or private, where a Status column says so); <m> are skipped.”), and an “Each change:” list starting with which column fills which field and which are left out, then a line per row; buttons Import (default) and Cancel.</li> <li>Pages or posts: pages when the current node is under Pages, otherwise posts.</li> <li>Announced after: “Imported <made> of <total> into <site>.” then a report dialog “Import into <site>” with a sentence per row and a “Left out:” section (“Not carried from a spreadsheet: pictures and featured images, authors (each item is yours), custom fields, comments and publishing (published or scheduled rows are drafts).”). An unreadable file: “<name> could not be read: <error>”.</li> <li>Undo: recorded as “Imported <n> posts into <site>.” with no undo; it then blocks Ctrl+Z (see History).</li> <li>Without permission: not checked by the Workbench (see Workbench Access).</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_bold_lists.py, core/wordpress/workbench_io.py, core/wordpress/workbench_carry.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Importing from the Media or Comments node makes posts, without saying that it chose posts; the preview’s first words should say “posts” or “pages” explicitly and let you switch.</li> <li>The Open dialog offers no “All files” choice (the helper choose_spreadsheet in wordpress_workbench_dialogs.py, which says “Spreadsheets saved as CSV”, is a different, unused-here dialog).</li> <li>The import could easily be undone (move the new drafts to the Trash) but is not.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="import-a-folder-of-documents">Import a Folder of Documents</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Alt+Shift+I, Edit > Import a Folder of Documents… (Alt+E, T).</li> <li>What opens: a folder dialog titled “Import a Folder of Documents” (must exist), then the folder is read in the background, then the same “Import into <site>” preview, headed “Each file’s first heading is its title; files are made in name order. Pictures beside a file are uploaded, and links between the files are pointed at their drafts.” and a “Left out: <line>.” per thing not carried.</li> <li>Announced: “<folder> has no Markdown, text or Word files.” when empty; after: “Imported <made> of <total> into <site>.” and a per-file report.</li> <li>Undo, permission: as for the spreadsheet import.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_bold_lists.py, core/wordpress/workbench_carry.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The chord Ctrl+Alt+Shift+I is a four-key chord for an action used a few times a year; rule 9 would say a letter is enough.</li> <li>Same Undo and access gaps as the spreadsheet import.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="workbench-options">Workbench Options</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+O, Tools > Workbench Options… (Alt+T, O).</li> <li>What opens: a dialog “Workbench Options” with a group “Options:” of check boxes, one per Watch: “Watch: New comments waiting”, “Watch: New form responses”, “Watch: Plugin updates available”, “Watch: Scheduled posts going live”, “Watch: A site not answering, and answering again”, “Watch: Items with a reviewer leaving the queue”, “Watch: Site Health status changes”, “Watch: Form spam questions, once a week”; OK and Cancel. All off until checked.</li> <li>F1: “Every Watch is off until you check it: nothing is asked of your sites for Watches before then. A checked Watch asks each connected site over the internet at most every fifteen minutes while the editor is open, and says only what changed; its first look is silent. Space checks or unchecks.”</li> <li>Announced after OK: “Workbench options saved.” Saved in workbench_options.json beside the site list.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_options.py, core/wordpress/workbench_options.py, core/wordpress/workbench_watches.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Every check box starts with “Watch:”, so a listener hears “Watch” eight times; the group could be labelled “Watches” and the boxes left plain.</li> <li>The fifteen-minute interval (watch_minutes) is stored but not offered.</li> <li>The module docstring names quill.core.wordpress.workbench_flag.workbench_window_enabled, a flag the doors no longer read.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="rest-console">REST Console</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+T, Tools > REST Console… (Alt+T, R).</li> <li>What opens: a dialog “REST Console - <site>” with, in Tab order: Route (combo box, completes from the site’s index with Down Arrow, Alt+R), Method (GET, POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE, OPTIONS, Alt+M), Parameters or body (multi-line, name=value lines or JSON, Alt+P), Needs (read only, the capability and the methods the route takes, Alt+N), Accepts (read only, the route’s arguments for the method, Alt+T), Answer (a tree of the answer, Alt+A), then the buttons Send (default, Alt+S), Copy JSON (Alt+J), What It Returns (Alt+W), Save Request (Alt+V), Close (Escape).</li> <li>Send: a missing required argument stops it and is spoken; reads (GET, OPTIONS) go at once; a write first shows a preview “Send to the Site”: “<METHOD> <route> on <site>. This can change the site. It needs: <needs>.” with a line per value and “Check: <problem>” lines, button Send.</li> <li>Announced after: “<METHOD> <route>: answered.” and the Answer tree is filled and its top item selected. “Type a route first.” with no route. Errors are spoken.</li> <li>What It Returns: a report “What <route> Returns” listing the fields from OPTIONS, or “The route does not describe what it returns.”</li> <li>Save Request: asks “Save Request”, “Name:” (prefilled “<METHOD> <route>”); says “Saved <name>. It is in Tools > Saved Requests.”</li> <li>Undo: a write is recorded “<METHOD> <route> on <site> from the REST Console.” with no undo (blocks Ctrl+Z).</li> <li>Code: wordpress_rest_console.py, wordpress_workbench_bold.py (do_rest_console), core/wordpress/workbench_rest_routes.py, core/wordpress/workbench_io.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The console ignores Workbench Access, Safe Mode and View As Role: a DELETE goes through under Read Only after only its own preview.</li> <li>do_rest_console calls self.ensure(…) on the UI thread before the dialog opens, which may block on two network reads for a site not yet read.</li> <li>“answered.” is said even when the site answered with a WordPress error object in a 200 body; the listener has to open the tree to learn it failed.</li> <li>The docstring calls the copy button “Copy Answer”; it is “Copy JSON”. The help on Save Request warns “Do not type a password into the parameters of one you keep.” which is the right warning but the saved file is not encrypted; a stronger guard (refuse to save a parameter named password) would be safer.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="run-a-saved-request-the-saved-requests-submenu-forget-a-saved-request">Run a Saved Request, the Saved Requests submenu, Forget a Saved Request</h3> <ul> <li>Run a Saved Request: Ctrl+Alt+Q, Tools > Run a Saved Request… (Alt+T, Q). A list dialog “Run a Saved Request”, “Request:”; OK opens the REST Console filled in; a GET or OPTIONS is sent at once, a write waits for Send and its preview. With none saved: “No saved requests yet: the REST Console’s Save Request button keeps one.”</li> <li>Saved Requests submenu: Tools > Saved Requests (Alt+T, V), filled each time the menu opens: “&1 <name>” to “&9 <name>” (first nine only), or a disabled “(None yet)”. Choosing one does what Run a Saved Request does. A request whose site is no longer connected runs on the current site.</li> <li>Forget a Saved Request: Ctrl+Alt+Shift+F, Tools > Forget a Saved Request… (Alt+T, F). The same chooser, titled “Forget a Saved Request”; removes it without asking and says “Forgot <name>.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_bold_more.py, core/wordpress/workbench_rest_saved.py, workbench_frame_commands.py (fill_dynamic_menus).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Ctrl+Alt+Shift+F does not reach Forget: the frame’s Ctrl+Shift+F hook catches it and moves to the filter box.</li> <li>run_saved also calls ensure on the UI thread.</li> <li>A saved request for a site that is gone silently runs against a different site (the current one). For a saved write, that is a risk; say which site it will use, or refuse.</li> <li>Only nine show in the submenu with no “More…” row; the tenth is reachable only through Run a Saved Request.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="plain-language-batch">Plain-Language Batch</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Alt+B, Tools > Plain-Language Batch… (Alt+T, L).</li> <li>What opens: a text dialog “Plain-Language Batch”, “What to do:”, with help giving examples (“move all drafts by Sam to pending on Radio; or add tag 2026 to posts in category Events on every site; or remove tag Old from all published pages …”) and “Nothing is sent until you check the list that follows and press Change.”</li> <li>Then, after the sites are read: a check-box dialog “Plain-Language Batch”, “Change these:”, one box per item “<site>: <title>”, all checked, OK and Cancel. Sites refused by access or role appear as a sentence in the summary.</li> <li>Announced: a phrase the grammar cannot read says its error; nothing found: “Nothing to change. <summary>”; after: “Batch done: <ok> of <checked> changed.” and a report dialog with the phrase and a line per item.</li> <li>Undo: recorded with the phrase as its summary and no undo (blocks Ctrl+Z).</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_bold_more.py, core/wordpress/workbench_plain_batch.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The help says “press Change” but the button is OK (ask_checks uses the default OK label). Name the button Change, as the other previews do.</li> <li>The checklist is the only preview: it lists items but not the change itself per item (for example “Draft becomes Pending”); the phrase is only in the F1 help. Put the change sentence above the list.</li> <li>A batch over many items cannot be undone, although every item’s before-state was read.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="read-site-as-document">Read Site as Document</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Alt+D, Site > Read Site as Document (Alt+S, D).</li> <li>What opens: a list dialog “Read Site as Document”, “Read:”, rows “The whole site”, “The pages in one menu”, “The posts in one category”; for a menu or category, a second list of them.</li> <li>What it does: builds a Markdown document (a heading per page) in the background, saves it under the data folder (wordpress_read_site), and opens it in the editor.</li> <li>Announced after: “<site> is open as a document, a heading per page. File > Export makes Word, EPUB or DAISY from it.” None to choose: “<site> has no menus to read.” / “categories”.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_bold.py (do_read_site, _read_scope).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The row has no ellipsis although it opens a dialog first.</li> <li>Nothing is said while a large site is read; a long silence follows the choice.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="your-profile">Your Profile</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Alt+Shift+P, Site > Your Profile… (Alt+S, Y).</li> <li>What opens: your own user sheet, titled “Your Profile - <site>”, the user sheet without its Role page, with the same OK, Cancel, Apply and Copy as Text (The Properties sheet above). Nothing is said while it reads.</li> <li>Announced after save: “Saved <n> change(s) to <your name> on <site>.”; if the site has not identified you: “<site> has not said who you are yet.”</li> <li>Without permission: Workbench access still applies (fields locked under Read Only or View As Role).</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_people_bulk.py (do_your_profile), wordpress_workbench_sheets.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>A four-key chord for an occasional command; most people change their profile a few times ever.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="keyless-rows-and-written-reasons">Keyless rows and written reasons</h3> <ul> <li>Gravity Forms submenu rows and WXR Files rows have no chords; their reasons are in KEYLESS (workbench_gravity_actions.py, workbench_site_files_actions.py), quoted above. The Saved Requests submenu is numbered instead.</li> <li>Context-menu-only rows (Open in Editor, Visual Review…, Duplicate as Draft, Counts by Status, Empty Trash…, wp-admin rows and the rest) have their reasons in the parity and admin KEYLESS tables (other sections), for example “Permanent and occasional: never a bare key (rules 4 and 9).” for Empty Trash.</li> </ul> <h3 id="summary-of-review-notes-most-serious-first">Summary of review notes, most serious first</h3> <ul> <li>Workbench Access, Safe Mode and View As Role do not stop menu-bar-only commands: Empty Spam, Empty Comment Trash, Import Posts, Import a Folder, Import WXR, Put Back from a Snapshot, New Here and REST Console writes all run under Read Only.</li> <li>Three chords are dead because the frame’s key hook runs before the menu: Ctrl+Shift+F (Filter Builder, moves to the filter box), Ctrl+Alt+Shift+F (Forget a Saved Request, same), Ctrl+Alt+R (Roles, reloads instead).</li> <li>One change that cannot be undone blocks every earlier undo; Ctrl+Z repeats “<summary> This cannot be undone.” forever.</li> <li>Shift+F1 and Site > What Can I Do Here? open the frame’s generic list, not the role-aware report; greyed rows everywhere say only “Not available here.”</li> <li>Reasons for greyed rows are put where nobody hears them (menu help with the status bar help pane disabled).</li> <li>Every site shows every plugin’s branch (over 100 nodes), and each tree selection fires a network load.</li> <li>Long operations (Load More, Load All, Refresh, remote filters) announce their start but never their result.</li> <li>The Properties sheet closes on OK before the save is known, loses edits on failure, likely false-conflicts on a second Apply, and lacks the GitHub sheet’s keys.</li> <li>Escape outside the filter box goes Back to another node, including from Details after Read in Full.</li> <li>Start Here still calls the Workbench an early preview behind a developer setting, and its text is three F6 presses from where Ctrl+9 puts you.</li> </ul> <h2 id="posts-and-pages">Posts and pages</h2> <p>Posts, Pages and every content type a plugin adds share one row kind, “item”. Enter opens the item in the editor, Alt+Enter opens its Properties sheet, and the row’s context menu (Applications key or Shift+F10) offers nineteen commands plus Properties. Because the menu shows at most eight entries, six commands sit on top and thirteen go under a More submenu, including several that have chords (Delete Permanently, Approve and Schedule, Send Back, Copy to Site) and several that have none and appear nowhere else (Earlier Versions, Duplicate as Draft, Counts by Status, Empty Trash, Open in wp-admin). Publishing, scheduling and the editorial queue each preview before sending, but they disagree with one another about defaults, wording and whether Ctrl+Z can take them back. What follows is grounded in quill/core/wordpress/workbench_actions.py, workbench_parity_actions.py, workbench_gap_actions.py, workbench_window_menu.py and the quill/ui/wordpress_workbench_*.py handlers.</p> <h3 id="the-tree-nodes-and-what-the-list-shows">The tree nodes and what the list shows</h3> <ul> <li>Posts: Go > Posts, Ctrl+Shift+1 (Alt+G, P). Lists every status at once (the node’s preset is status “any”). Columns: Title, Status, Author, Modified.</li> <li>Under Posts: Published, Drafts, Pending, Scheduled, Private and Trash, each one status, same four columns; and Group, which View > Group By (Ctrl+Alt+G) fills with one child per value.</li> <li>Pages: Go > Pages, Ctrl+Shift+2 (Alt+G, A). Shown as a tree by parent. Columns: Title, Level, Status, Modified. The same six status children and Group sit under it.</li> <li>More Content Types: one row per plugin post type (columns Type, Route, Taxonomies); Enter opens that type’s own node, whose items have the Posts columns.</li> <li>View > Columns (Ctrl+Alt+C) adds, for items: Date, Slug, Number, Type, Comments, Sticky, Format, Parent, Site.</li> <li>The details pane sentence for a row: “{title}. {Status}, scheduled for {date}. Last changed {date}. By {author}.” (the “scheduled for” part only on a scheduled item). An author whose name was not read shows as “user 3”.</li> <li>A list reads 300 rows first. When it stops short the Items cell says “The first 300 are shown, and the site has more. View > Load More reads 300 more; Load All reads every one.” Load More is Ctrl+Shift+L, Load All Ctrl+Alt+L.</li> <li>F1 on the tree, Posts: “The site’s posts, newest change first. Enter opens one in the editor.” Pages: “The site’s pages, as a tree by parent. Enter opens one in the editor.” A content type: “Items of one kind a plugin added. Enter opens one in the editor.” More Content Types: “The kinds of content this site’s plugins add, such as Events.” Status children have no sentence of their own and borrow their parent’s (node_help strips the last “_part”). Group: “The rows of the list above that share one value, from View > Group By.”</li> <li>F1 on an item row: “A post, page or item. Enter opens it in the editor, Alt+Enter its Properties; Earlier Versions on the context menu compares and restores the site’s revisions.”</li> <li>Code: workbench_tree.py, workbench_rows.py (item_row), workbench_listing.py, workbench_node_help.py.</li> <li>Review note: the six status children under Pages use the Posts columns and are not tree-ordered, so Pages > Drafts loses the Level column the Pages node has. Moving from Pages to Pages > Drafts silently changes the column set.</li> <li>Review note: the extra column titled “Comments” shows the item’s comment_status (“open” or “closed”), not a count. A listener will expect a number. Calling it “Comments allowed” would fix that.</li> <li>Review note: dates in the list and details pane are made from modified_gmt and date_gmt by friendly_date, which the code documents as “UTC as given”. Nothing in the cell says UTC, so a site in another time zone hears times that look local and are not.</li> </ul> <h3 id="the-item-rows-context-menu-as-it-is-actually-laid-out">The item row’s context menu, as it is actually laid out</h3> <ul> <li>Shown on top, in order: Open in Editor, Preview on the Site, Visual Review…, Publish or Update…, Move to Trash, Restore from Trash.</li> <li>Then a submenu labelled “More” holding, in order: Delete Permanently…, Copy Link, Copy to Site…, Approve and Schedule…, Send Back with a Note…, Assign a Reviewer…, Move to Another Day…, Show Its Comments, Earlier Versions…, Duplicate as Draft, Counts by Status, Empty Trash…, Open in wp-admin.</li> <li>Then Properties… last.</li> <li>Rows whose capability or Workbench access is not met are disabled (greyed). The menu itself does not say why. The reason is spoken only when you press the row’s chord, or listed by What Can I Do Here? (Shift+F1, Alt+S, W) as “{label}: {reason}” or “{label}: yes”.</li> <li>The tree node’s own context menu (Shift+F10 on the tree, or on an empty list) is: Refresh, New Here…, Load More, Load All, Columns…, Group By…, then More (What Can I Do Here?, Counts by Status, Empty Trash…, wp-admin for This Place…), then Properties….</li> <li>Code: quill/core/workbench/actions.py (menu_for, split_for_menu), quill/ui/workbench_frame_actions.py (_popup).</li> <li>Review note: “More” is appended with no access key, and the context menu labels carry no chord text. The chords live only in each row’s help string, which goes to the status bar and is not normally spoken. So the context menu never teaches a listener that Delete Permanently is Shift+Delete or Approve and Schedule is Ctrl+Shift+D.</li> <li>Review note: actions.py promises that “the same rows, in the same order, are the Item menu, so More never hides anything that only a right-click could reach”. The WordPress Item menu breaks that promise. Open in Editor, Visual Review, Earlier Versions, Duplicate as Draft, Counts by Status, Empty Trash and Open in wp-admin are only on the context menu, and the Item menu’s order differs from the context menu’s.</li> <li>Review note: Delete Permanently, a tier 3 command, sits first under More, directly above the harmless Copy Link. One arrow press separates a copy from a permanent delete. The confirmation stops it, but its placement is still careless.</li> <li>Review note: a greyed row gives no reason when you arrow onto it. The reason is reachable through Shift+F1, but nothing in the menu points there.</li> </ul> <h3 id="open-in-editor">Open in Editor</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter on the row (the default), or the first context-menu row “Open in Editor”. It has no Item menu entry.</li> <li>What opens: the editor’s own open path (WordPressCommands.open_item), so the item opens as a document in the editor. The Workbench stays open behind.</li> <li>Announced after, only on failure: “Choose a post or page to open in the editor.” and “{site} is no longer in your list of sites.”</li> <li>Undo: nothing to undo; it only opens.</li> <li>F1 (row): see the item row help above.</li> <li>Without permission: needs edit_posts, or edit_pages on a page. Enter then says, for example, “Editing pages needs the Editor role or higher. You are an Author on {site}.” With Workbench access set to Read Only, Enter still opens it, because the row is tier 0.</li> <li>Keyless: Enter is its key; no written KEYLESS reason exists because it has a key.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (do_open_in_editor).</li> <li>Review note: an Author pressing Enter on someone else’s page hears a refusal and nothing else. Offering to open it read only, or pointing at Preview on the Site (Ctrl+Enter), would turn the dead end into a choice.</li> </ul> <h3 id="preview-on-the-site">Preview on the Site</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Enter; File > Preview on the Site (Alt+F, V); context-menu row 2.</li> <li>What opens: your browser at the item’s link. When the item is not published, “preview=true” is added to the address.</li> <li>Announced after, only on failure: “Preview works on a post or page.” and “This has no address on the site yet.”</li> <li>Undo: not applicable.</li> <li>Without permission: no requirement; never greyed.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (do_preview).</li> <li>Review note: a draft’s preview link only works for someone signed in to WordPress in that browser. Open in wp-admin says “You must be signed in to wp-admin in your browser” and Visual Review explains the same limit, but Preview says nothing, so the first time someone previews a draft they meet a login page with no warning.</li> <li>Review note: Open on the Site (Shift+Enter, File > Open on the Site, Alt+F, S) is the published address. It is not on the item context menu at all, though it is on the Site row’s.</li> </ul> <h3 id="visual-review-1">Visual Review…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu only, row 3. No chord and no Item menu entry.</li> <li>What opens: the editor’s Visual Review. The page is captured, then a confirmation titled “Visual Review” asks before anything goes to your AI, with the button “Send for Review”. Cancel there says “Nothing was sent. The screenshots are in {folder}.”</li> <li>Announced when the item is not published: “Visual Review looks at the published page, and this is not published on {site} yet. Publish it first. A draft opens only for someone signed in to WordPress in a browser, so QUILL cannot capture it.”</li> <li>Menu help: “The editor’s Visual Review of this published page: it is captured, and described by your AI only after you say yes. Nothing on the site changes.”</li> <li>Without permission: no requirement; never greyed.</li> <li>Keyless: “A page is reviewed after a redesign, now and then (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (do_visual_review), wordpress_review_commands.py.</li> <li>Review note: the row is enabled on drafts, pending and scheduled items, where it can only refuse. It is one of the six top rows, so most of the time it is the wrong thing to offer there. It should be greyed, or say before it starts, when the row’s status is not “publish”.</li> </ul> <h3 id="publish-or-update-1">Publish or Update…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+P; Item > Publish or Update (Alt+I, U); context-menu row 4.</li> <li>What it acts on: the selected items that are not already published. If none qualify: “Nothing selected is waiting to be published.” Items from more than one site: “Publish items from one site at a time.”</li> <li>What opens: a Yes/No message box titled “Publish”. It reads “Publish {n} item(s) on {site}? Visitors will see them.” followed by “- {title}” for up to twenty items and “and {n} more”. The buttons are “Publish” and “Cancel”. Cancel is the default, so Enter cancels.</li> <li>Announced after: “Published {n} items on {site}.” When some failed: “ {k} were not: {reason}; {reason}; {reason}“ (the first three).</li> <li>Undo: recorded. Ctrl+Z sends each item back to its old status and says “Put {n} items back as they were.”</li> <li>Menu help: “Make the selected items public, after a preview. Ctrl+Shift+P.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs publish_posts, or publish_pages on a page. For example: “Publishing pages needs the Editor role or higher. You are an Author on {site}.” Under Organize or Read Only access: “Workbench access on {site} is Organize. Site > Workbench Access changes it.”</li> <li>Cancel: nothing sent, nothing said.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (do_publish), workbench_edit.py (run_batch).</li> <li>Review note: the label promises “or Update”, but published items are filtered out before anything happens, so on a published post the only result is “Nothing selected is waiting to be published.” Either drop “or Update” or make it update.</li> <li>Review note: there are two ways to publish now. This one uses a message box whose default is Cancel and records an undo. Approve and Schedule with an empty time uses a preview list whose default is Publish and records no undo (see below). The same act has two dialogs, two default buttons and two answers to Ctrl+Z.</li> <li>Review note: the help says “after a preview”, but this is a message box, not the Workbench’s preview window.</li> <li>Review note: when every item fails, an undo is still recorded, and Ctrl+Z says “Put 0 items back as they were.”</li> </ul> <h3 id="approve-and-schedule">Approve and Schedule…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+D; Item > Approve and Schedule (Alt+I, C); context menu, under More.</li> <li>What opens first: a one-line text box titled “Approve and Schedule”, with the prompt “Publish on (2026-11-20 9:00 AM), or empty for now:”. OK is the default; Cancel stops. Help: “A day and time on the site’s own clock. A time that happens twice when the clocks go back takes first or second after it. Empty publishes now.”</li> <li>Empty time: a preview window titled “Approve and Publish”. Summary: “Publish {n} on {site} now.” The “Each change” list holds the titles. The button is “Publish” and it is the default, so Enter publishes.</li> <li>With a time: the site’s time zone is read in the background, then a preview titled “Approve and Schedule”. Summary: “Approve {n} on {site} and schedule them for {words}.” The button is “Schedule”. {words} reads like “Friday 20 November 2026, 9:00 AM at the site (Europe/London)”, followed by the time where you are, or “, which is the same time here”.</li> <li>Errors (the text box does not come back; you start again): “Type the day as year, month and day, for example 2026-11-20.”; “Type the time as 24-hour hours and minutes, or like 4:15 PM.”; “{time} does not happen on {day} in {zone}: the clocks go forward then. Choose another time.”; “{time} happens twice on {day} in {zone}, because the clocks go back. Type first or second after the time to say which.”; “Choose a time that is in the future.”; “QUILL does not know the time zone {key}. Choose another.”</li> <li>Announced after: “{n} published.” or “{n} scheduled for {words}.”</li> <li>Undo: a Change History entry is recorded with no undo. Ctrl+Z then says “Published on {site}. This cannot be undone.” or “Scheduled on {site}. This cannot be undone.”</li> <li>Menu help: “Publish it now or at a time you give. Ctrl+Shift+D.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs publish_posts or publish_pages, the same sentences as Publish.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_bold_queue.py (do_approve_schedule), workbench_editorial.py (site_moment, moment_words), schedule.py.</li> <li>Review note: it does not look at status. It will “approve” a post that is already published and reschedule it into the future, which takes it off the site until then. Move to Another Day warns about exactly that (“Published items moved to a future time are scheduled again, and are off the site until then.”) and this command does not.</li> <li>Review note: the items are sent one at a time in a single loop with no per-item outcome. If the third of five fails, the error is said, the first two are already changed, the rest are not, and nothing says which. Publish, Trash and moderation all report each item.</li> <li>Review note: a typing mistake in the time throws away what was typed. The error is spoken after the dialog has closed, and the person must press Ctrl+Shift+D and type it all again.</li> <li>Review note: “{n} published.” and “Publish {n} on {site} now.” say a bare number with no noun (“1 published.”), unlike Publish’s “Published 1 item on {site}.”</li> </ul> <h3 id="send-back-with-a-note">Send Back with a Note…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+B; Item > Send Back with a Note (Alt+I, B); context menu, under More.</li> <li>What opens: a multi-line text box titled “Send Back with a Note”, prompt “What needs changing:”. Enter adds a new line, so Tab to OK. Help: “A note on the draft, which its writer and reviewer read in the editor.”</li> <li>Empty note: nothing happens and nothing is said.</li> <li>Preview or confirmation: none. After OK, each item gets the note (block notes) and is set to draft.</li> <li>Announced after: “Sent back {n} as drafts, with your note. WordPress sends no email.”</li> <li>Undo: recorded with no undo. Ctrl+Z says “Sent back {n} on {site}. This cannot be undone.”</li> <li>Menu help: “Back to draft, with a note for its writer. Ctrl+Shift+B.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs edit_posts or edit_pages (“Editing posts needs the Contributor role or higher. You are a Subscriber on {site}.”).</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_bold_queue.py (do_send_back), block_notes.py.</li> <li>Review note: critical. It does not check status. On a published post it sets the post to draft, so the post disappears from the site, with no preview, no warning and no undo. The module’s own docstring says “every change is previewed”, and this one is not.</li> <li>Review note: an empty note cancels silently. A listener who pressed OK by accident on an empty box hears nothing and cannot tell whether anything was sent.</li> <li>Review note: “Sent back 1 as drafts” reads oddly for one item.</li> </ul> <h3 id="assign-a-reviewer">Assign a Reviewer…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+J; Item > Assign a Reviewer (Alt+I, G); context menu, under More.</li> <li>What opens: first the site is asked for people with edit_others_posts; if it will not list them, for authors (who=authors). If names come back, a list titled “Assign a Reviewer”, prompt “Reviewer:”, help “The people on the site who may edit and publish others’ posts.” If none come back, a text box “Reviewer’s name:”, help “The site would not list its editors, so type the reviewer’s name.”</li> <li>Then a multi-line box titled “Assign a Reviewer”, prompt “A note for {reviewer}:”. It may be left empty; Cancel stops.</li> <li>Preview or confirmation: none.</li> <li>Announced after: “{reviewer} reviews {n}. WordPress sends no email for this: the note is on the draft, where its writer and the reviewer read it, and the Review Watch on this computer says when it leaves the queue.”</li> <li>Undo: recorded with no undo. Ctrl+Z says “{reviewer} asked to review on {site}. This cannot be undone.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs edit_posts or edit_pages.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_bold_queue.py (do_assign_reviewer, _reviewer_then), workbench_editorial.py (assign_reviewer, NOTICE_WORDS).</li> <li>Review note: the fallback list is who=authors, which includes Contributors and Authors who cannot publish others’ posts. The list’s own help still says “who may edit and publish others’ posts”, so the fallback can offer people the help says are not there.</li> <li>Review note: a typed name is not checked against anyone on the site; a misspelt reviewer is accepted and recorded.</li> <li>Review note: the closing sentence is about forty words and is said every time. A short “Asked {reviewer} to review {n}. No email is sent.” would carry the same fact; the Review Watch explanation belongs in F1.</li> </ul> <h3 id="move-to-another-day">Move to Another Day…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Alt+Shift+D; Item > Move to Another Day (Alt+I, Y); context menu, under More.</li> <li>What opens: a one-line text box titled “Move to Another Day”, prompt “New day and time (2026-11-20 9:00 AM):”, already filled with the first selected item’s date as “2026-11-20 09:00” (24-hour). Help: “On the site’s own clock. A time that happens twice when the clocks go back takes first or second after it. The preview says it in your time too.”</li> <li>Then a preview titled “Move to Another Day”, summary “Move {n} on {site} to {words}.”, the titles listed, plus, when a published item moves into the future, “Published items moved to a future time are scheduled again, and are off the site until then.” The button is “Move”, the default.</li> <li>Errors: as Approve and Schedule. “Choose a time that is in the future.” applies only when every selected item is scheduled.</li> <li>Announced after: “Moved {n} to {words}.”</li> <li>Undo: recorded with no undo. Ctrl+Z says “Moved to another day on {site}. This cannot be undone.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs edit_posts or edit_pages.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_bold_queue.py (do_move_day).</li> <li>Review note: the old date of every item is known before the move, so this is one of the easiest changes to undo, and it is not undoable.</li> <li>Review note: with several items selected, the box shows only the first one’s date and every item gets the same new moment. The preview does not show each item’s old date, so a listener cannot hear what is being lost.</li> </ul> <h3 id="copy-link">Copy Link</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+C; Edit > Copy Link (Alt+E, L); context menu, under More.</li> <li>Announced after: “Copied.” or “Could not copy.”</li> <li>Undo: not applicable.</li> <li>Without permission: none.</li> <li>Code: quill/ui/workbench_frame_actions.py (copy_link), wordpress_workbench_window.py (url).</li> <li>Review note: when a row has no link, the provider falls back to the site’s own address, and the outcome is still just “Copied.” A listener pasting expects the post and gets the home page.</li> </ul> <h3 id="copy-to-site">Copy to Site…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+Y; Item > Copy to Site (Alt+I, O); context menu, under More.</li> <li>What opens: with only one site connected, “Connect a second site first: File > WordPress > Connect a Site.” Otherwise a list titled “Copy to Site”, prompt “Site:”, help “Which of your other connected sites.” A practice-site crossing check may ask first. If your role or Workbench access on the target refuses writing, that refusal is said and nothing else happens.</li> <li>Preview: a preview window titled “Copy from {source} to {target}”. Its summary: “Copy from {source} to {target}: {a} made, {b} replaced, {c} skipped, and {d} pictures uploaded. New items are drafts; a replaced item keeps its status on {target}. Links to {source} are pointed at {target}.” Each line is “Make {kind} {title}.”, “Replace {kind} {title}.” or “Skip {kind} {title}: {why}”, then “Upload the picture {url}” and “Left out: {thing}”. The button is “Copy”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Copied to {target}; new items are drafts there.” or “Copied to {target}, except {n}; the report says which.”, and then a report window titled “Copy to {target}”.</li> <li>Undo: recorded with no undo. Ctrl+Z says “Copied {n} from {source} to {target}. This cannot be undone.”</li> <li>Menu help: “To another of your sites, after a preview. Ctrl+Shift+Y.”</li> <li>Without permission: no requirement on the source row, so it is never greyed; the target’s refusal is checked when you choose it.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_bold.py (do_copy_to_site), workbench_site_copy.py.</li> <li>Review note: “Replace” overwrites an existing item on the target site, and nothing can put it back. The preview line “Replace post About.” is the only warning, and it is phrased like the harmless “Make” lines. A replace deserves its own sentence in the summary saying it cannot be undone.</li> </ul> <h3 id="show-its-comments">Show Its Comments</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Alt+Shift+M; Item > Show Its Comments (Alt+I, I); context menu, under More.</li> <li>What happens: the Comments node is selected with “post:{id}” in the filter box, focus goes to the list, and then this is said: “{n} comments on {title}, approved and waiting together.”</li> <li>Failure: “Show Its Comments works on a post or page.”</li> <li>Without permission: none.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_moderation.py (do_show_comments), workbench_moderation.py (post_comment_count).</li> <li>Review note: the count is spoken after focus has already moved to the list, so it competes with the screen reader reading the first focused row. One or the other gets cut off. Saying the count first, or putting it in the status bar, avoids the collision.</li> </ul> <h3 id="earlier-versions-in-progress">Earlier Versions… (in progress)</h3> <ul> <li>Status: the code exists in the working tree and is not committed. Present: the context-menu row (workbench_gap_actions.py, ITEM_VERSIONS), the handler (wordpress_workbench_gaps.py), the core (workbench_item_versions.py), the row F1 sentence, and unit tests (test_wp_workbench_gaps.py, test_wp_workbench_gaps_core.py). Not found: a wordpress.md engineering log entry or user-guide text for Earlier Versions on post rows. Treat everything here as in progress.</li> <li>How to reach it: context menu only, under More. No chord and no Item menu entry.</li> <li>What opens: the item and its revisions and autosaves are read. Versions identical to the item now are left out. If none remain: “{title} has no earlier versions on {site}.” Otherwise a list titled “Earlier Versions of {title}”, prompt “Versions:”, one row each as “Saved {date}, {title}, a revision” or “…, an autosave”, newest first. Help: “Each version the site kept, newest first. OK compares it with the item now, and that preview is where it is restored.”</li> <li>Preview: a preview window titled “Restore {title}”. Summary: “Put back the version of {title} saved {date} on {site}? WordPress keeps the version it has now as a revision too.” Lines: “Title: {now} becomes {then}.” when the title differs, then the content compared line by line. The button is “Restore”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Restored {title} to its version of {date}.”</li> <li>When someone changed the item meanwhile: “{title} was changed on the site after QUILL read it. Nothing was sent; read it again and choose the version there.”</li> <li>Undo: recorded. Ctrl+Z sends back the title and content as they were and says “{title} is back as it was.” If the item changed after the restore: “{title} was changed on the site after the restore, so it was left as it is.”</li> <li>Menu help: “The site’s revisions and autosaves of this item, newest first; each is compared with the item now, line by line, and restored after that preview. The item is read again first, and Ctrl+Z puts it back.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs edit_posts or edit_pages; tier 2, so Organize and Read Only access refuse it.</li> <li>Keyless: “Looked for after a mistake, rarely (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_gaps.py, workbench_item_versions.py, workbench_gap_actions.py.</li> <li>Review note: the date in the summary and the announcement is made by stripping “Saved “ and cutting at the first comma. friendly_date writes “3 November 2026, 4:12 PM”, so the time is cut off. Two versions saved the same day sound identical in the summary and in the closing sentence.</li> <li>Review note: in the list, OK means “compare”, not “restore”. That is a reasonable two-step design, but it is the reverse of Their Application Passwords, where OK in a list means “revoke”. The same list dialog means different things in different places.</li> <li>Review note: only the title and content are restored. WordPress’s own restore also brings back the excerpt; the preview does not say the excerpt is left alone.</li> </ul> <h3 id="duplicate-as-draft">Duplicate as Draft</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu only, under More.</li> <li>What opens: a Yes/No message box titled “Duplicate as Draft”: “Make a draft copy of {title} on {site}, called {title} (copy)? Visitors will not see it.” The buttons are “Make a Copy” and “Cancel”, and Cancel is the default.</li> <li>Announced after: “{title} (copy) is a new draft on {site}. Ctrl+Z moves it to the Trash.”</li> <li>Undo: recorded. Ctrl+Z moves the copy to the Trash and says “Moved {title} to the Trash.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs edit_posts or edit_pages; tier 2.</li> <li>Keyless: “A copy is made now and then, from the row’s context menu (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_parity.py (do_duplicate), workbench_parity.py (duplicate).</li> <li>Review note: after the copy is made the list refreshes, and nothing in the handler moves the selection to the copy. Unless the refresh happens to land there, the next Enter opens the original rather than the copy someone presumably wanted to edit. Offering “Open the copy in the editor?” would close the loop.</li> </ul> <h3 id="counts-by-status">Counts by Status</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: the item row’s context menu (More), or the tree node’s context menu (More). No chord.</li> <li>Announced: “Posts on {site}: All 12, Mine 3, Published 8, Drafts 2, Pending 1, Scheduled 0, Private 0, Trash 2.” Pages say “Pages”, and a content type its own name. A view the site refuses is left out. If every view is refused: “{site} would not count its posts for this account.” In the wrong place: “Counts by Status works in a site’s posts, pages, content types or media.”</li> <li>Menu help: “How many there are in each of wp-admin’s views: All, Mine, Published, Drafts, Pending, Scheduled, Private and Trash; for media, each type and Unattached.”</li> <li>Without permission: none.</li> <li>Keyless: “The tree’s status nodes and the status bar count as you go; every count at once is asked now and then (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_parity.py (do_status_counts), workbench_parity.py (status_counts, counts_text).</li> <li>Review note: a refused view is silently dropped, so “Private” may simply be missing from the sentence with no hint why. “Private: not shown to you” would be honest.</li> </ul> <h3 id="empty-trash">Empty Trash…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: the item row’s context menu (More), or the tree node’s context menu (More). No chord.</li> <li>What opens: every item of that kind in the Trash is read. If there are none: “The Trash on {site} has no posts.” Otherwise a preview window titled “Empty Trash”. Summary: “Delete {n} posts in the Trash on {site} permanently? This cannot be undone, and Ctrl+Z will not bring them back.” The list holds the titles. The button is “Delete Permanently”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Deleted {n} items from the Trash permanently on {site}.” with “ Not done: {reasons}.“ when some failed.</li> <li>Undo: recorded with no undo, so Ctrl+Z says “{that sentence} This cannot be undone.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs delete_posts or delete_pages; tier 3, so only Full access. In the wrong place: “Empty Trash works in a site’s posts, pages or content types.”</li> <li>Keyless: “Permanent and occasional: never a bare key (rules 4 and 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_parity.py (do_empty_trash).</li> <li>Review note: critical. The preview window makes its action button the default, so Enter in this dialog permanently deletes the whole Trash. Delete Permanently on one item uses a message box whose default is Cancel. The most destructive command in this section is the easiest one to confirm by accident. Every tier 3 preview should default to Cancel.</li> <li>Review note: offered on any item row, even a published post in Posts > Published, where it empties the Trash of that whole kind rather than doing anything to the selected row. Its place on the row menu suggests it acts on the row.</li> </ul> <h3 id="open-in-wp-admin">Open in wp-admin</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu only, under More (last before Properties).</li> <li>Announced before the browser opens: “Opening wp-admin’s editor for it, which shows who is editing it now and takes over the lock; WordPress’s REST API does not expose the edit lock. You must be signed in to wp-admin in your browser.”</li> <li>Menu help: “The wp-admin screen for what WordPress’s REST API cannot do here: who is editing it now, and for media, scaling the original by numbers. In your browser.”</li> <li>Keyless: “Leaves QUILL for wp-admin, only for what REST cannot do (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_parity.py (do_dashboard_screen), workbench_parity.py (dashboard_screen).</li> <li>Review note: a hand-off to wp-admin. The sentence is honest but long, and it is said every time. The REST explanation could live in F1, leaving “Opening wp-admin’s editor. Sign in there if asked.”</li> </ul> <h3 id="move-to-trash-1">Move to Trash</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Delete in the list; Item > Move to Trash (Alt+I, T); context-menu row 5.</li> <li>What opens: one item goes straight to the Trash with no question. Several ask first, in a message box titled “Move to Trash”: “Move {n} items on {site} to the Trash?”, with “Move to Trash” and “Cancel” (Cancel default).</li> <li>Announced after: “Moved {n} item(s) to the Trash on {site}. Ctrl+Z puts them back.” With failures, “Moved {k} of {n} items to the Trash on {site}. Not done: {title}: {reason}.” (the first three, then “and {n} more”).</li> <li>Already in the Trash: “That is already in the Trash. Shift+Delete deletes it permanently.”</li> <li>From the status bar: “Delete and Ctrl+Z act on the list. F6 moves back to it.”</li> <li>Undo: recorded. Ctrl+Z restores each moved item to the status it had (published stays published) and says “Restored {n} items from the Trash.”</li> <li>Menu help: “Into WordPress’s Trash, where Restore brings it back. Delete.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs delete_posts or delete_pages (“Deleting pages needs the Editor role or higher. You are an Author on {site}.”); tier 1, so Organize access allows it and Read Only refuses.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (do_trash), workbench_edit.py (trash, restore), workbench_parity.py (each, outcome).</li> <li>Review note: “Ctrl+Z puts them back.” is said for a single item too.</li> </ul> <h3 id="restore-from-trash">Restore from Trash</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+R; Item > Restore from Trash (Alt+I, E); context-menu row 6.</li> <li>What it acts on: the selected items whose status is trash; otherwise “Nothing selected is in the Trash.”</li> <li>Announced after: “Restored {n} items from the Trash, as drafts or waiting on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: recorded. Ctrl+Z moves them back and says “Moved them back to the Trash.”</li> <li>Menu help: “Out of the Trash, as a draft.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs edit_posts or edit_pages; tier 1.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (do_restore).</li> <li>Review note: two restores behave differently. Ctrl+Z after Trash puts a published post back as published. Restore from Trash on the same post brings it back as a draft, which is WordPress’s own rule. A listener who trashed a post yesterday and restores it today finds it is no longer on the site, and the closing sentence’s “as drafts or waiting” is easy to miss.</li> <li>Review note: it is one of the six top rows on every item, including published posts where it can only say “Nothing selected is in the Trash.” It is most useful in the Trash node, where it is the only row that matters.</li> </ul> <h3 id="delete-permanently">Delete Permanently…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Shift+Delete in the list; Item > Delete Permanently (Alt+I, D); context menu, first row under More.</li> <li>What opens: a Yes/No message box titled “Delete Permanently”: “Delete {n} item(s) from {site} permanently? This cannot be undone: {first five titles}” and “ and more“ beyond five. The buttons are “Delete Permanently” and “Cancel”, and Cancel is the default.</li> <li>Announced after: “Deleted {n} items permanently on {site}. This cannot be undone.” with “ Not done: …“ for failures.</li> <li>Undo: recorded with no undo. Ctrl+Z says “Deleted {n} items permanently on {site}. This cannot be undone.” (see the undo section below for what that does to older changes).</li> <li>Menu help: “Gone for good, after you confirm by name. Shift+Delete.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs delete_posts or delete_pages; tier 3, Full access only.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (do_delete_permanently).</li> <li>Review note: “after you confirm by name” suggests typing the name, which some products require for a permanent delete. It is a Yes/No box that lists the names. The help should say “after a confirmation that names it”.</li> <li>Review note: it works on items that are not in the Trash, skipping WordPress’s safety net. wp-admin offers Delete Permanently only from the Trash view.</li> </ul> <h3 id="new-here-ctrln-on-posts-pages-and-content-types">New Here (Ctrl+N), on Posts, Pages and content types</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+N; File > New Here (Alt+F, N); the tree node context menu row 2.</li> <li>What opens: a one-line text box titled “New Post” (or “New Page”, or “New {type}”), prompt “Title:”, help “The title it starts with. It is saved as a draft.” It also works on the status children such as Posts > Drafts.</li> <li>Empty title: “It needs a name, so nothing was made.”</li> <li>Preview or confirmation: none; it is made at once as a draft.</li> <li>Announced after: “Made the post {title} on {site} as a draft.”</li> <li>Undo: not recorded in Change History, so Ctrl+Z afterwards undoes whatever was recorded before it.</li> <li>Menu help: “Make something new here. Ctrl+N.” (context menu) and “A new user or password, where you are.” (File menu).</li> <li>Without permission: no requirement. Nothing checks your Workbench access first, because the check is looked up on the selected row’s own actions, and New Here is a node action. A site that refuses the draft says so.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_lists.py (do_new_here), workbench_create.py (creatable, create).</li> <li>Review note: the File menu’s help, “A new user or password, where you are.”, is stale. New Here now makes posts, pages, terms, menus and more.</li> <li>Review note: in Posts > Pending or Posts > Scheduled the new item is still a draft, so it does not appear in the list you are standing in. Nothing says where it went, and focus does not move to it. Offering to open the new draft in the editor would finish the job.</li> <li>Review note: Read Only access, Safe Mode and View As Role (“Nothing can be changed until you stop”) do not stop Ctrl+N here.</li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-altenter-one-item">Properties (Alt+Enter), one item</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+Enter; Item > Properties (Alt+I, P); the last context-menu row.</li> <li>What opens: the item is read again, then a sheet titled “{title} Properties - {site}” with a page per tab (Ctrl+Tab moves between tabs).</li> <li>General: Title; Slug (“The last part of its address.”); Status, a choice of publish, draft, pending, future, private; Password, a masked box that opens empty (“Visitors type this to read it. Write only: what you type replaces it.”); Author, a choice of “Name (number)”; Published, a date such as 2026-11-09T14:00; Last changed (read only); Address (read only).</li> <li>Organize: one list field per taxonomy the item carries (Categories, Tags and any plugin’s), “by name or number, separated by commas”; on posts, Format (standard, aside, chat, gallery, link, image, quote, status, video) and Sticky (a check box, “Kept at the top of the posts page.”).</li> <li>Featured Image: the picture by title and number, with buttons “Choose from Library…” (Alt+H) and “Remove Featured Image” (Alt+R). Choose from Library lists the site’s pictures as “{title}, alt text: {alt}” or “{title}, no alt text”.</li> <li>Excerpt: one multi-line field.</li> <li>Discussion: Comments and Pingbacks, each open or closed.</li> <li>Template and Parent (pages, and items with a parent): Template (a choice, with “Default template” in words), Parent number (“0 for a top-level page.”), Order.</li> <li>Meta, from the route’s registered meta; and a page for Advanced Custom Fields where the site shows them.</li> <li>Buttons: Copy as Text (Alt+T), OK, Apply (Alt+A), Cancel. A sheet you may not save shows Close instead of Cancel, and fields you may not change are read-only text boxes whose F1 is the reason.</li> <li>Announced after a save: “Saved {n} changes to {title} on {site}.” plus “ The site did not keep: {fields}.“ when the answer differs; “Nothing changed on this row.”; a term name that matches nothing: “{label}: nothing on {site} is called {names}. Make it first, or check the spelling.”</li> <li>Conflict: a message box titled “Changed on the Site”: “{title} was changed on {site} after you opened it. Nothing was sent. Send your changes anyway, replacing theirs?” with “Use Mine” and “Cancel”.</li> <li>After a slug change the redirect offer may follow (Redirection plugin).</li> <li>Undo: recorded. Ctrl+Z says “Put {title} back as it was.”</li> <li>Without permission: the whole sheet is read only with the role or access sentence as every field’s F1.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_sheets.py, workbench_sheets.py (_item_sheet, values_for), workbench_edit.py (save), quill/ui/workbench_sheet_editor.py.</li> <li>Review note: the Status, Comments, Pingbacks and Format choices are WordPress’s raw slugs (“publish”, “future”, “open”). The list column says “Published” and “Scheduled” for the same values. A listener meets two vocabularies for one field.</li> <li>Review note: Published is typed as “2026-11-09T14:00”, while Approve and Schedule and Move to Another Day take “2026-11-20 9:00 AM”. Three ways to set a date, two formats.</li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-on-several-items-at-once">Properties on several items at once</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: select several rows, then Alt+Enter.</li> <li>What opens: a sheet titled “{n} items Properties - {site}”. Fields that differ show “Mixed” and are never sent if left. General: Status, Author number, Comments, Pingbacks, Sticky (three-state), Template, Parent number, Featured image (with Choose from Library and Remove Featured Image). Categories and Tags: Add categories, Remove categories, Add tags, Remove tags.</li> <li>Refusals: rows of different kinds or sites: “Several rows at once need to be the same kind, from one site.”</li> <li>Confirmation: a message box titled “Change Several”: “Change {n} items on {site}: {Field} to {value}?” and the titles (first 25), with “Change Them” and “Cancel” (Cancel default).</li> <li>Announced after: “Changed {k} of {n} on {site}.” Failures then ask, in a box titled “Some Were Not Changed”, “{n} were not changed: … Try them again?” with “Try Again”.</li> <li>Undo: recorded. Ctrl+Z says “Put {n} back as they were.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_sheets.py (_batch_sheet, _bodies, _send_batch), wordpress_workbench_batch_sheets.py.</li> <li>Review note: critical. Add categories, Remove categories, Add tags and Remove tags only understand “News (12)” or “12”. A plain name such as “News” is silently dropped (the code calls term_ids with no name table and ignores what it cannot match). The confirmation still says “Add categories to News”, the save reports success, and nothing was filed. The one-item sheet refuses an unknown name out loud; the batch sheet should resolve or refuse names the same way.</li> <li>Review note: on one item Author is a choice of names; on several it is “Author number”, a text box wanting a number. The same field asks two different things.</li> </ul> <h3 id="ctrlz-and-change-history-as-they-behave-across-these-sections">Ctrl+Z and Change History, as they behave across these sections</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach them: Edit > Undo WordPress Change, Ctrl+Z (Alt+E, U), in the list only; Edit > Change History, Ctrl+Shift+H (Alt+E, H).</li> <li>Change History opens a read-only report titled “Change History”, newest first, numbered, with “ (cannot be undone)“ after entries that cannot be. Copy All copies it. With nothing yet: “No changes made in the Workbench yet.”</li> <li>Ctrl+Z with nothing recorded: “Nothing to undo.”</li> <li>Changes recorded with an undo: Publish, Move to Trash, Restore from Trash, Approve, Unapprove, Spam, Not Spam, Duplicate as Draft, Properties saves (one or several), Earlier Versions, Attach, Detach, Replace File (repoint path), Change Role (one or several).</li> <li>Changes recorded with no undo: Delete Permanently, Empty Trash, Approve and Schedule, Send Back with a Note, Assign a Reviewer, Move to Another Day, Copy to Site, Edit Picture, Replace File (in place), Remove (one or several), Set New Password, Add a Person, Invite from a Spreadsheet, Revoke (both places).</li> <li>Changes not recorded at all: New Here (items, terms and New Application Password), Reply, Not Spam and Allow This Sender, Merge Into, Convert to Category or Tag, Empty Spam, Empty Comment Trash, Regenerate Sizes.</li> <li>Code: quill/core/workbench/history.py, wordpress_workbench_sheets.py (do_undo, do_history).</li> <li>Review note: critical. When the newest entry has no undo, Ctrl+Z says “{summary} This cannot be undone.” and leaves that entry on top. It never moves past it, so every older change that could be undone becomes unreachable for the rest of the session. One Delete Permanently, Send Back or Move to Another Day locks out the undo of everything before it. Skipping past an entry that cannot be undone, and saying so, would keep the rest reachable.</li> <li>Review note: critical, the opposite problem. Reply, Merge Into, Convert, Allow This Sender, New Here and the two Empty commands are not recorded. Pressing Ctrl+Z straight after a Reply does not touch the reply; it undoes whatever came before it (an Approve, a role change) and says so in words that do not mention the reply. A listener expects Ctrl+Z to undo the last thing they did.</li> <li>Review note: when an undo itself fails (a conflict), the error is said and the entry stays on top, so the next Ctrl+Z tries the same failing undo again.</li> <li>Review note: the summary is reused as the start of the refusal, so the listener hears, for example, “Published on {site}. This cannot be undone.”, a sentence about what was done followed by one about what cannot be. “Cannot undo: published on {site}.” would put the answer first.</li> </ul> <h2 id="media">Media</h2> <p>The Media Library is one list with six filtered children. Its rows are “media” rows. Enter opens the file in your browser (View or Play), except in picking mode, where Enter inserts it into the document that asked. The context menu has thirteen commands plus Properties: six on top, seven under More. WordPress keeps no Trash for media, so Delete says so and points at Shift+Delete. Replace File, just built, is the most involved command in the Workbench and has the most review notes. Alt text, the field that matters most to this audience, is on the second tab of Properties.</p> <h3 id="the-tree-nodes-and-what-the-list-shows-1">The tree nodes and what the list shows</h3> <ul> <li>Media: Go > Media, Ctrl+Shift+3 (Alt+G, M). Columns: Title, Type, Alt text, Uploaded.</li> <li>Under Media: Pictures, Audio, Video, Documents, Unattached and Without Alt Text, with the same columns; Group (View > Group By); and the Housekeeping node (files used nowhere, duplicates, very large files and so on, covered in its own section).</li> <li>The Type cell says picture, audio, video or document. The Alt text cell is “none” for a picture without alt text and empty for anything that is not a picture.</li> <li>View > Columns adds: File type, Size, Attached (“post 12” or “not attached”), Author, Number, Slug, Site.</li> <li>The details pane: “{title}, picture, 1.2 MB, alt text: {alt}, uploaded {date}.” or “…, no alt text, …”.</li> <li>F1 on the tree, Media: “Pictures, audio, video and documents in the media library.” Without Alt Text: “Pictures with no alt text, which a screen reader cannot describe.” Unattached: “Media not attached to any post or page.” Pictures, Audio, Video and Documents borrow Media’s sentence.</li> <li>F1 on a media row: “A file in the media library. Enter opens it; Alt+Enter edits its alt text.”</li> <li>Needs My Attention > Accessibility lists pictures without alt text from every site, as media rows with the same menu.</li> <li>Code: workbench_tree.py, workbench_rows.py (media_row), workbench_listing.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="the-media-rows-context-menu">The media row’s context menu</h3> <ul> <li>On top: View or Play, Insert into the Document, Copy Link, Copy to Site…, Replace File…, Edit Picture….</li> <li>Under More: Regenerate Sizes, Attach to a Post or Page…, Detach, Download…, Counts by Status, Open in wp-admin, Delete Permanently….</li> <li>Then Properties….</li> <li>Review note: Insert into the Document is the second row on every media row, and it works only in picking mode. Outside picking mode it is enabled and can only explain itself. It should be hidden, or greyed, unless the Workbench was opened by Insert > Picture from the Media Library.</li> <li>Review note: Edit Picture, Regenerate Sizes and Download are offered on audio, video and documents too. Edit Picture and Regenerate Sizes refuse with “Edit Picture works on a picture in the Media Library.” and “Regenerate Sizes works on a picture in the Media Library.” Greying them on non-pictures would spare a round trip.</li> <li>Review note: the Item menu has none of the media commands except Copy to Site and Delete Permanently, and its Delete row still reads “Move to Trash” on a media row.</li> </ul> <h3 id="view-or-play">View or Play</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter (the default); the first context-menu row; Shift+Enter (Open on the Site) does the same.</li> <li>What opens: your browser at the file’s address (source_url).</li> <li>Failure: “This has no address on the site yet.”</li> <li>Menu help: “Open the file in your browser.”</li> <li>Without permission: none.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (do_open_on_site).</li> <li>Review note: Enter, the most-pressed key, leaves QUILL. Arrowing through the library and pressing Enter to hear what a file is takes the listener into a browser tab every time. Read in Full (the details pane), or an in-app play for audio, would be a gentler default, with View or Play second.</li> </ul> <h3 id="insert-into-the-document">Insert into the Document</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter in picking mode; the context-menu row 2 at any time.</li> <li>Picking mode starts from the editor’s Insert > Picture from the Media Library. The Workbench opens on that site’s Media and says “Picking a file for {document}: Enter on a file inserts it.” Enter on a file then inserts it at the caret through the editor’s picture dialog, and the editor comes back to the front. Picking ends after one insert or when the Workbench closes.</li> <li>Outside picking mode: “Insert into the Document works after Insert > Picture from the Media Library opens the Workbench; then Enter on a file inserts it.”</li> <li>On a file from another site: “That file is on {site}, and {document} is on {other}. Choose a file in {other}’s Media.”</li> <li>Menu help: “At the caret of the document Insert > Picture from the Media Library came from; there, Enter does this.”</li> <li>Keyless: “Enter does it in picking mode, the only time it applies.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_editor_side.py (picked_command, do_insert_picked, open_for_picking).</li> <li>Review note: picking mode changes what Enter does, and nothing on the window says so after the first announcement. A listener who arrows away to the tree and back has no cue that Enter now inserts instead of opening a browser. Saying “Picking” in the status bar or the window title would keep the mode audible.</li> </ul> <h3 id="copy-link-1">Copy Link</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+C; Edit > Copy Link (Alt+E, L); context-menu row 3.</li> <li>Announced: “Copied.” or “Could not copy.”</li> <li>Menu help: “The file’s address. Ctrl+Shift+C.”</li> <li>Code: quill/ui/workbench_frame_actions.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="copy-to-site-1">Copy to Site…</h3> <ul> <li>The same command, dialogs and wording as on posts (see Posts and pages). Recorded with no undo.</li> </ul> <h3 id="replace-file-just-built">Replace File… (just built)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu row 5 on a media row. No chord and no Item menu entry.</li> <li>What opens first: a standard Open box titled “Replace {title} with”, All files. Then size and type are checked: “{name} is larger than the 64 MB QUILL sends in one file.” and “{name} is a {type}, which a WordPress Media Library does not take.”</li> <li>The site’s index, plugins and the file itself are then read in the background.</li> <li>Path one, the site lists a replace route (a plugin’s): a preview titled “Replace File”. Summary: “Replace {title} on {site} with {new name}?” Lines: “Now: {old file in words}.”, “New: {new file in words}.”, “The address stays the same: {address}”, “Pictures have their sizes remade after the file is replaced.”, “The type changes from {a} to {b}.” when it does, “Sent to {route}, a route {site}’s plugin lists; QUILL reads the file back afterwards to check the address.”, and “The old file cannot be put back by QUILL.” The button is “Replace File”, the default.</li> <li>Path one, announced after: “Replaced. The address is the same, {address}, and the site now has {file in words}.” or, when the read-back address differs, “The site took the file, but its address is now {new}, not {old}. Links to the old address may break.” Recorded with no undo.</li> <li>Path two, no replace route: a list titled “Replace File”, prompt “How:”, with “Upload it as a new file and point every use at it (the address changes)” and, only when Enable Media Replace is active, “Open Enable Media Replace in wp-admin (the address stays the same)”. Help: “WordPress has no way to swap a file in place over REST, and {site} lists no plugin route that does. Uploading as a new file gives it a new address; QUILL then points every post, page, pattern and featured image that used the old file at the new one. Enable Media Replace, where it is active, keeps the address, but only in wp-admin.”</li> <li>Enable Media Replace chosen: “Opening {title}’s edit screen, where Enable Media Replace’s Replace Media box is. You must be signed in to wp-admin in your browser. Press F5 here after.” Then the browser opens.</li> <li>Upload and repoint chosen: every use is found, then a preview titled “Replace File”. Summary: “Upload {new name} to {site} as a new file and point every use of {title} at it? The file’s address changes.” Lines: Now and New; “The address changes. The old one is {address}; the new one is whatever the site gives the upload.”; “{n} items use it, and each is pointed at the new file:”; one line per item (“Post About: {where it is used}”); “Not changed: …” and “Not read, so not changed: …”; “Ctrl+Z puts every item back; the new file stays in the library.” The button is “Upload and Repoint”, the default.</li> <li>After that preview, a list titled “The Old File”, prompt “Old file:”, with “Keep the old file in the library” and “Delete the old file permanently once every use is moved (cannot be undone)”. Help: “WordPress’s Media Library has no Trash, so the old file is either kept or deleted for good. Deleting happens only when every use was moved.”</li> <li>Then, only where the Redirection plugin is present, a Yes/No box titled “Add a Redirect”: “Add a redirect on {site} from {old address} to the new file’s address, so links from other websites keep working? It goes through the Redirection plugin.” with “Add Redirect” and “Cancel”.</li> <li>Path two, announced after: “The file’s address changes; QUILL updated {n} places that used it.” then “ {k} were not changed: {first}.“ when some failed, then the redirect outcome (“A redirect now sends the old address to the new one.” or “No redirect was added: the Redirection plugin has no group to keep it in, so links from other websites to the old address will break.” or “The redirect was not added ({reason}), so links from other websites to the old address will break.”) or, with no redirect, “ Links from other websites to the old address will break unless a redirect is added.“, and finally “ The old file is still in the library.” or “ The old file was deleted.“</li> <li>Undo: on path two, recorded. Ctrl+Z puts every edited item back and adds “ The new file stays in the library.“ On path one, recorded with no undo.</li> <li>Menu help: “Put a new file in this one’s place, after a preview of both. Where the site has a replace route the address stays the same; otherwise the new file is uploaded and every page, post and featured image that used the old one is pointed at it, which Ctrl+Z puts back.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs upload_files (“Changing media needs the Author role or higher. You are a Contributor on {site}.”); tier 2, Full access only.</li> <li>Keyless: “Opens a file picker and a preview, now and then (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_media_replace.py, media_replace.py, workbench_site_files_actions.py.</li> <li>Review note: critical. If you chose “Delete the old file permanently”, Ctrl+Z still puts every item back, pointing them at the old file, which no longer exists. The undo turns a working site into one full of broken images and links. Either refuse the undo after a delete, or refuse the delete when an undo is wanted, and say so in the old-file choice.</li> <li>Review note: the two decisions with the largest consequences, deleting the old file and adding a redirect, are asked after the preview, so the preview the listener approved did not include them. Asking them first, and showing them as lines in the preview, would make the preview true.</li> <li>Review note: when Enable Media Replace is not active, the “How:” list has a single row. It is a dialog with one choice, and Enter just accepts it. Saying the fact in the preview’s summary would remove a step.</li> <li>Review note: the help text says “every page, post and featured image” and the dialog says “every post, page, pattern and featured image”. Small, but a listener checking whether patterns are covered hears two answers.</li> <li>Review note: the closing sentence after a repoint can run past sixty words. Splitting it into the outcome now and the rest in a report window would be easier to follow.</li> </ul> <h3 id="edit-picture">Edit Picture…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu row 6. No chord.</li> <li>What opens: the edits the site’s REST index allows are read, then a list titled “Edit {title}”, prompt “Edit:”, with the rows the site takes from: “Turn a quarter to the right”, “Turn a quarter to the left”, “Turn upside down”, “Flip left to right, as in a mirror”, “Flip top to bottom”, “Crop to a square”, “Crop to 4 by 3, landscape”, “Crop to 16 by 9, widescreen”, “Crop to 3 by 4, portrait”, then “Crop by numbers, in percent…” when crop is allowed, and “Scale the original to a size (opens wp-admin)…” always. Help: “The edits {site} takes. It is {w} by {h}. WordPress keeps the original and makes the edit a new picture. Scale opens wp-admin, because the REST route cannot scale.”</li> <li>Crop by numbers: a text box titled “Crop by Numbers”, prompt “Crop, in percent (left, top, width, height):”, filled with “0, 0, 100, 100”. Errors (the dialog closes): “Give four numbers in percent: from the left, from the top, width and height, such as 10, 0, 80, 100.”, “Width and height must be more than 0, and nothing below 0.”, “The crop goes past the picture’s edge: left plus width, and top plus height, may be at most 100.”</li> <li>Scale: hands off to Open in wp-admin.</li> <li>Preview: titled “Edit Picture”. Summary: “Edit {title} on {site}? WordPress keeps the original and makes the edit a new picture in the library.” Lines: the edit in words (“Turn a quarter to the right.” or “The crop keeps 80 percent of the width, starting 10 percent from the left, and 100 percent of the height, starting 0 percent from the top.”) and “Sent to wp/v2/media/{id}/edit”. The button is “Make Edited Copy”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Edited copy made on {site}: {name}. The original is unchanged; Shift+Delete on the copy removes it.”</li> <li>Undo: recorded with no undo, which blocks older undos (see Posts and pages).</li> <li>Without permission: needs upload_files; tier 2. Not a picture: “Edit Picture works on a picture in the Media Library.”</li> <li>Keyless: “Opens a dialog, a few times a site’s life (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_parity.py (do_edit_picture, _send_picture_edit), workbench_parity.py, media_edit.py.</li> <li>Review note: the preview’s line “Sent to wp/v2/media/{id}/edit” is a REST route, not words. It is jargon in a dialog that otherwise speaks plainly.</li> <li>Review note: the edit is undoable in practice (delete the copy), and the closing sentence even says how, but Ctrl+Z will not do it and instead blocks everything older.</li> <li>Review note: a crop typing mistake closes the dialog and loses the four numbers.</li> </ul> <h3 id="regenerate-sizes">Regenerate Sizes</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu, under More.</li> <li>No confirmation. Announced after: “Sizes remade for {title} on {site}: {n} sizes.”</li> <li>Undo: not recorded.</li> <li>Menu help: “Remake the picture’s thumbnail and other sizes from its original.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs upload_files; tier 2.</li> <li>Keyless: “Needed after an edit or a theme change, rarely (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_parity.py (do_regenerate_sizes), media_pieces.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="attach-to-a-post-or-page">Attach to a Post or Page…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu, under More.</li> <li>What opens: a text box titled “Attach {title}”, prompt “Post or page, by words or number:”, help “Words from its title, or its number. QUILL lists what matches.” Words are searched on the site; several matches give a list titled “Attach {title}”, prompt “Attach to:”, rows “{title} ({type})”, help “The post or page the file is attached to.”</li> <li>No match: “Nothing on {site} matches {words}.”</li> <li>Confirmation: a message box titled “Attach”: “Attach {title} to {name} on {site}?” with “Attach” and “Cancel”.</li> <li>Announced after: “{title} is attached to {name}. Ctrl+Z undoes it.” A conflict: “{reason} Press F5 and try again.”</li> <li>Undo: recorded. Ctrl+Z says “{title} put back.”</li> <li>Menu help: “Find a post or page by words or number and attach the file to it. Ctrl+Z puts it back.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs upload_files; tier 2.</li> <li>Keyless: “Uploads from the editor attach themselves; this is the exception (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_parity.py (do_attach, _save_post).</li> <li>Review note: a number skips the search and is not checked. “Attach {title} to number 99 on {site}?” is asked even if post 99 does not exist, and the name in the confirmation is “number 99”, not a title.</li> </ul> <h3 id="detach">Detach</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu, under More.</li> <li>Not attached: “{title} is not attached to anything.”</li> <li>Confirmation: a message box titled “Detach”: “Detach {title} from post {number} on {site}? It stays in the library.” with “Detach” and “Cancel”.</li> <li>Announced after: “{title} is attached to nothing. Ctrl+Z attaches it again.”</li> <li>Undo: recorded. Ctrl+Z says “{title} put back.”</li> <li>Menu help: “Leave it in the library, attached to nothing. Ctrl+Z attaches it again.”</li> <li>Keyless: “The rare undoing of an attachment (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_parity.py (do_detach).</li> <li>Review note: the confirmation names the post by number only (“from post 412”).
