The listener cannot tell what they are detaching it from.</li> </ul> <h3 id="download">Download…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu, under More.</li> <li>What opens: a standard Save As box titled “Download”, with the file’s own name filled in; overwriting asks first.</li> <li>Announced after: “Saved {title} to {full path}.”</li> <li>Menu help: “Save the file to this computer, where you choose.”</li> <li>Without permission: none.</li> <li>Keyless: “Opens a Save As box, now and then (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_parity.py (do_download), media_tools.py.</li> <li>Review note: the closing sentence reads out the whole path, which can be long. “Saved {file name}.” is enough, since the person just chose the folder.</li> <li>Review note: the 2026-11-13 audit table says a file over 512 MB “opens in the browser”. The code does not do that. download_file raises a too-big error and that error is what is said.</li> </ul> <h3 id="counts-by-status-media">Counts by Status (media)</h3> <ul> <li>Announced: “Media on {site}: All {n}, Mine {n}, Pictures {n}, Audio {n}, Video {n}, Documents {n}, Unattached {n}.”</li> <li>Otherwise as on posts.</li> </ul> <h3 id="open-in-wp-admin-media">Open in wp-admin (media)</h3> <ul> <li>Announced: “Opening wp-admin’s Edit Media screen, whose Edit Image scales the original to a width and height; WordPress’s REST edit route only rotates, flips and crops. You must be signed in to wp-admin in your browser.”</li> <li>A hand-off to wp-admin.</li> </ul> <h3 id="delete-permanently-media-and-the-delete-key">Delete Permanently… (media), and the Delete key</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Shift+Delete; Item > Delete Permanently (Alt+I, D); context menu, last under More.</li> <li>The same message box as on posts: “Delete {n} item(s) from {site} permanently? This cannot be undone: {names}.” Cancel is the default.</li> <li>Announced after: “Deleted {n} items permanently on {site}. This cannot be undone.”</li> <li>Delete (the Trash key) on a media row: “WordPress keeps no Trash for this, so it can only be deleted permanently, with Shift+Delete.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs upload_files (“Changing media needs the Author role or higher.”); tier 3.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py.</li> <li>Review note: the confirmation does not say where the file is used. By Name > Media Usage and Housekeeping already know, and the Housekeeping node “shows usage before Move to Trash”. The plain Delete Permanently on a Media row can remove a picture that twenty pages show, with no warning.</li> <li>Review note: the role sentence says “Changing media” for a permanent delete, which undersells it.</li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-altenter-one-file">Properties (Alt+Enter), one file</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: a sheet titled “{title} Properties - {site}”.</li> <li>General: Title; File (read-only link); Type; Size; Dimensions (“{w} by {h}”); Uploaded; Uploaded by.</li> <li>Alt Text and Caption: Alt text (“What a picture shows, for people who cannot see it.”); Caption (multi-line).</li> <li>Description: one multi-line field.</li> <li>Attached To: “Attached to number”.</li> <li>Sizes: read only, one line per size (“thumbnail: 150 by 150”).</li> <li>Buttons, saving, conflicts and Ctrl+Z as on posts (“Saved 1 change to {title} on {site}.”; undo “Put {title} back as it was.”).</li> <li>Without permission: needs upload_files; otherwise the sheet is read only with the reason.</li> <li>Code: workbench_sheets.py (_STATIC “media”), wordpress_workbench_sheets.py.</li> <li>Review note: the row’s own F1 says “Alt+Enter edits its alt text”, but alt text is on the second tab. Alt+Enter lands on Title, and the listener must press Ctrl+Tab to reach Alt text. For a library whose main accessibility job is alt text, Alt Text and Caption should be the first page, or Alt text the first field.</li> <li>Review note: “Uploaded by” reads author_name, which the media route does not send and values_for does not fill. It is very likely always empty.</li> <li>Review note: “Attached to number” is a bare number field. Attach to a Post or Page finds posts by words; the sheet does not.</li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-on-several-files">Properties on several files</h3> <ul> <li>Select several media rows and press Alt+Enter: a sheet titled “{n} items Properties - {site}” with Title, Alt text, Caption and Description, Mixed where they differ.</li> <li>Confirmation “Change Several”, outcome “Changed {k} of {n} on {site}.”, Ctrl+Z “Put {n} back as they were.”</li> <li>Review note: setting one Alt text on many different pictures is rarely right, and nothing warns that every selected picture will get the same description.</li> </ul> <h3 id="new-here-on-media">New Here on Media</h3> <ul> <li>Ctrl+N on Media says: “New Here makes posts, pages, custom items, categories, tags, terms, menus, people and application passwords. Choose one of those nodes first.”</li> <li>Review note: wp-admin’s Media screen has Add New. Uploading from the Workbench is not offered here; the sentence lists everything except what the listener is looking at.</li> </ul> <h2 id="categories-and-tags-1">Categories and tags</h2> <p>Categories, Tags and every taxonomy a plugin adds share the “term” row. Enter shows the posts filed under the term. The context menu has five commands and Properties, so nothing hides under More. Merge Into and Convert are tier 3 and preview with a count, but neither is recorded in Change History, and Convert previews less than Merge does.</p> <h3 id="the-tree-nodes-and-what-the-list-shows-2">The tree nodes and what the list shows</h3> <ul> <li>Categories: a tree by parent. Columns: Name, Level, Count, Slug.</li> <li>Tags: Columns: Name, Count, Slug.</li> <li>More Taxonomies: one row per plugin taxonomy; Enter opens its own node of terms.</li> <li>View > Columns adds: Number, Description, Parent, Site.</li> <li>The details pane: “{name}, {n} items.” followed by the description when there is one.</li> <li>F1 on the tree, Categories: “The site’s categories, as a tree. Show Its Posts lists what is filed there.” Tags: “The site’s tags. Merge Into folds one into another.” More Taxonomies: “The ways of grouping this site’s plugins add.” A plugin taxonomy: “The terms of one taxonomy a plugin added.”</li> <li>F1 on a term row: “A category, tag or term. Enter lists its posts; Merge Into folds it into another.”</li> <li>There is no Go menu row for Categories or Tags.</li> <li>Code: workbench_tree.py, workbench_rows.py (term_row).</li> <li>Review note: Categories and Tags have no Go key, while Posts to Site Health each have one. A listener reaches them by the tree.</li> </ul> <h3 id="the-term-rows-context-menu">The term row’s context menu</h3> <ul> <li>In order: Show Its Posts, Merge Into…, Convert to Category or Tag…, Copy to Site…, Delete Permanently…, Properties….</li> </ul> <h3 id="show-its-posts">Show Its Posts</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter (the default); context-menu row 1.</li> <li>What happens: the Posts node is selected with “categories:{id}” (or “tags:{id}”, or the taxonomy’s own name) in the filter box, the filter runs, and focus goes to the list. The filter says “{n} shown.” or “Filtering.”</li> <li>Menu help: “The posts filed under it.”</li> <li>Without permission: none.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (do_show_posts, _show_filtered).</li> <li>Review note: it always lands in Posts. For a plugin taxonomy that belongs to a custom content type (an Events category, say), the Posts list is filtered to nothing, or refuses the filter. It should open the content type the taxonomy is on.</li> <li>Review note: when the filter goes to the site, the first thing said is “Filtering.”, and the count of what was found is not announced when it arrives.</li> </ul> <h3 id="merge-into">Merge Into…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+G; Item > Merge Into (Alt+I, M); context-menu row 2.</li> <li>What opens: a list titled “Merge {name} Into”, prompt “Into:”, listing the other rows in the current list by name. Help: “Everything filed under {name} is filed under the one you choose, then {name} is deleted.”</li> <li>Refusals: “Merge Into works on a category, tag or term.” and “There is no other term here to merge into.”</li> <li>Then everything filed under it is counted, and a preview titled “Merge {name} Into {target}”. Summary: “{n} items filed under {name} will be filed under {target}, then {name} is deleted. This cannot be undone with Ctrl+Z.” Lines: one per item, as “posts 123”. The button is “Merge”, the default.</li> <li>Announced after: “Merged {name} into {target}.” or “{n} items could not be moved, so {name} was kept. Try again in a moment.”</li> <li>Undo: not recorded. The preview says Ctrl+Z will not undo it; pressing Ctrl+Z will undo whatever change came before.</li> <li>Menu help: “File its posts under another term, then delete it. Ctrl+Shift+G.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs manage_categories (“Managing categories and tags needs the Editor role or higher. You are an Author on {site}.”); tier 3, Full access only.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_lists.py (do_merge_into), workbench_create.py (merge_preview, merge_terms).</li> <li>Review note: the preview’s lines are a route word and a number (“posts 123”, “posts 98”), not titles. A listener hears a column of numbers and cannot check what moves.</li> <li>Review note: the “Into:” list holds only the rows already loaded, by name only. Two categories called “News” under different parents sound identical, and a term past the first 300 is not offered.</li> <li>Review note: the tier 3 preview defaults to Merge, so Enter merges and deletes.</li> </ul> <h3 id="convert-to-category-or-tag">Convert to Category or Tag…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+K; Item > Convert to Category or Tag (Alt+I, V); context-menu row 3.</li> <li>Refusals: “Convert works on a category or a tag.” and “Only a category becomes a tag, or a tag a category.”</li> <li>What opens: a Yes/No message box titled “Make {name} a Category” (or “a Tag”): “Make {name} a category on {site}? Every post filed under it moves to the category, and the old one is deleted.” with “Convert” and “Cancel” (Cancel default).</li> <li>Announced after: “{name} is now a category.” or “{n} posts could not be moved, so {name} was kept.”</li> <li>Undo: not recorded.</li> <li>Menu help: “Make a tag a category, or a category a tag, posts and all. Ctrl+Shift+K.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs manage_categories; tier 3.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_lists.py (do_convert_term), workbench_create.py (convert_term).</li> <li>Review note: Convert does the same kind of change as Merge (move every post, delete the term) but previews less. It gives no count, no list and no “cannot be undone” warning; Merge gives all three. They should share one preview.</li> <li>Review note: on a partial failure the new term has already been made and some posts already moved, yet the sentence says only that the old term “was kept”. The site is left with both terms and the posts split between them, and the listener is not told.</li> <li>Review note: converting a category with child categories or a parent says nothing about the hierarchy, which a tag cannot hold.</li> </ul> <h3 id="copy-to-site-terms">Copy to Site… (terms)</h3> <ul> <li>The same command and dialogs as on posts. Recorded with no undo.</li> </ul> <h3 id="delete-permanently-terms-and-the-delete-key">Delete Permanently… (terms), and the Delete key</h3> <ul> <li>Shift+Delete; Item > Delete Permanently (Alt+I, D); context-menu row 5. The same message box: “Delete {n} item(s) from {site} permanently? This cannot be undone: {names}.” Cancel default.</li> <li>Announced after: “Deleted {n} items permanently on {site}. This cannot be undone.”</li> <li>Delete on a term row: “WordPress keeps no Trash for this, so it can only be deleted permanently, with Shift+Delete.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs manage_categories; tier 3.</li> <li>Review note: the confirmation does not say how many posts lose the term, though the Count column already knows. “News is on 34 posts” belongs in that sentence.</li> </ul> <h3 id="new-here-ctrln-on-categories-tags-and-plugin-taxonomies">New Here (Ctrl+N), on Categories, Tags and plugin taxonomies</h3> <ul> <li>Categories: three text boxes in a row, each titled “New Category”: “Name:” (“Its name, as the site shows it.”), “Parent number (optional):” (“0 or empty for a top level.”), “Description (optional):” (“A sentence about it, or nothing.”).</li> <li>Tags and plugin taxonomies: “Name:” and “Description (optional):”.</li> <li>Empty name: “It needs a name, so nothing was made.”</li> <li>Announced after: “Made the category {name} on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: not recorded.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_lists.py (do_new_here), workbench_create.py.</li> <li>Review note: three separate modal dialogs, one after another, each with its own title read out, where one small form would do. Escape on the second throws away the first.</li> <li>Review note: the parent is asked by number only. Anything that is not a number is silently ignored and the category is made at the top level.</li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-altenter-a-term">Properties (Alt+Enter), a term</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: a sheet titled “{name} Properties - {site}”. General: Name, Slug, Description, Parent number (“0 for none. Categories only.”). Count: Items and Address, read only. Custom Fields, when the taxonomy has registered meta or Advanced Custom Fields.</li> <li>Save, conflicts and Ctrl+Z as on posts.</li> <li>Without permission: needs manage_categories.</li> <li>Several terms at once: “Several at once can be changed for posts, pages, custom items and media.”</li> <li>Code: workbench_sheets.py (_STATIC “term”).</li> <li>Review note: Parent number is shown on tags too, with help that says “Categories only.” A field that cannot apply should not be there.</li> </ul> <h2 id="comments-1">Comments</h2> <p>Comments share one row kind across the site’s Comments node, its four status children, and Needs My Attention > Comments Waiting. Enter reads the comment in the details pane. Moderation (Approve, Unapprove, Spam, Not Spam) is fast, one key each, works on many rows, reports each one, and Ctrl+Z puts them back. Around it, though, Reply posts with no preview and no undo, Not Spam does not mean approved, Allow This Sender cannot actually allow anyone, and the two Empty commands on the Site menu escape the Workbench’s access limits.</p> <h3 id="the-tree-nodes-and-what-the-list-shows-3">The tree nodes and what the list shows</h3> <ul> <li>Comments: Go > Comments, Ctrl+Shift+4 (Alt+G, C). Columns: Author, On, Status, When.</li> <li>Under Comments: Waiting, Approved, Spam, Trash, and Group.</li> <li>Status cells say Waiting, Approved, Spam or Trash.</li> <li>View > Columns adds: Email, Website, IP address, Type, Parent, Post, Number, Site. Email and IP address come only for someone who can moderate comments.</li> <li>The details pane: “{author} on {post}, {date}: {the comment’s text}.”</li> <li>F1 on the tree, Comments: “Every comment on the site. Delete moves one to the Trash.” Waiting, Approved, Spam and Trash borrow it. Needs My Attention > Comments Waiting: “Comments held for moderation on every site. Ctrl+Shift+A approves.”</li> <li>F1 on a comment row: “A comment. Ctrl+Shift+A approves, Ctrl+Shift+S marks it as spam.”</li> <li>Code: workbench_tree.py, workbench_rows.py (comment_row).</li> </ul> <h3 id="the-comment-rows-context-menu">The comment row’s context menu</h3> <ul> <li>On top: Read in Full, Approve or Not Spam, Not Spam and Allow This Sender, Unapprove, Spam, Reply….</li> <li>Under More: Move to Trash, Restore from Trash, Delete Permanently….</li> <li>Then Properties….</li> </ul> <h3 id="read-in-full">Read in Full</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter (the default); context-menu row 1.</li> <li>What happens: focus moves to the details pane, which holds the comment’s sentence. Nothing is announced; the screen reader reads the pane.</li> <li>Menu help: “Move to the details pane to read it.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (do_read).</li> </ul> <h3 id="approve-or-not-spam">Approve, or Not Spam</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+A; Item > Approve, or Not Spam (Alt+I, A); context-menu row 2.</li> <li>What it does: on a waiting or unapproved comment, approves it. On a comment held as spam, it does not approve: it puts the comment back in the Waiting queue (status hold), which tells the site’s spam plugin it was not spam. Works on several selected comments, one outcome each.</li> <li>Preview or confirmation: none.</li> <li>Announced after: “{n} item(s) approved on {site}.” When every selected comment was spam: “Not spam: {n} item(s) on {site}, and the site was told.” With failures: “{k} of {n} items approved on {site}. Not changed: {reason}; …”</li> <li>Refusal on the wrong row: “Choose a comment or a form entry.”</li> <li>Undo: recorded. Ctrl+Z puts each changed comment back to its old status and says “Put {n} items back as they were.”</li> <li>Menu help: “Approve it; on a comment held as spam, Not Spam. Ctrl+Shift+A.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs moderate_comments (“Moderating comments needs the Editor role or higher. You are an Author on {site}.”); tier 1, so Organize access allows it.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (_moderate, do_approve), workbench_edit.py (set_comment_status).</li> <li>Review note: “Not spam: 1 item on {site}, and the site was told.” does not say the comment is now waiting, not published. A listener who pressed Approve on a spam comment reasonably believes it is now visible. It needs a second Ctrl+Shift+A, and nothing says so. Either approve it, or say “back in Waiting; Ctrl+Shift+A again approves it”.</li> <li>Review note: the noun is “item”, not “comment”, in every moderation sentence (“1 item approved”). It is shared with form entries, but in the Comments list it is the wrong word.</li> <li>Review note: a mixed selection of spam and held comments gets the plain “approved” sentence, although the spam ones went back to Waiting instead.</li> </ul> <h3 id="not-spam-and-allow-this-sender">Not Spam, and Allow This Sender</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Alt+W; Item > Not Spam, and Allow This Sender (Alt+I, L); context-menu row 3.</li> <li>What it does: approves the comment (straight to approved, unlike Approve on spam), then reads the site’s discussion settings to explain what that means.</li> <li>Announced after, when the site lets previously approved commenters through: “{author}’s comment is approved, so WordPress lets their next comments through: it allows anyone with an approved comment already. To block them again, mark a comment of theirs as spam.” When the site holds every comment: “{author}’s comment is approved. This site holds every comment for moderation, so their next one will wait too; Settings > Discussion in wp-admin changes that.”</li> <li>Refusal on the wrong row: “Allow This Sender works on a comment.”</li> <li>Undo: not recorded, so Ctrl+Z undoes something older instead.</li> <li>Menu help: “Approve it and let this person’s next comments through. Ctrl+Alt+W.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs moderate_comments; tier 1.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_lists.py (do_allow_sender), workbench_create.py (allow_sender_words).</li> <li>Review note: the name promises an allow list that does not exist. The command is “Approve” plus an explanation of WordPress’s own rule, and on a site that holds every comment the closing sentence admits the sender is not allowed at all. “Approve and Explain” would be honest; or it should be greyed on such sites.</li> <li>Review note: reading the discussion settings needs manage_options. For an Editor the read fails, the empty settings fall back to the first sentence, and an Editor is told the sender’s next comments get through whether or not that is true on this site.</li> <li>Review note: unlike Approve, it is not recorded, so the two moderation commands next to each other have different undo stories.</li> </ul> <h3 id="unapprove">Unapprove</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+U; Item > Unapprove (Alt+I, N); context-menu row 4.</li> <li>Announced after: “{n} item(s) unapproved on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: recorded, “Put {n} items back as they were.”</li> <li>Menu help: “Back to waiting. Ctrl+Shift+U.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs moderate_comments; tier 1.</li> <li>Review note: the sentence says “unapproved” while the list and status cell say “Waiting”. “Back to Waiting” would match what the listener then hears in the list.</li> </ul> <h3 id="spam">Spam</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+S; Item > Spam (Alt+I, S); context-menu row 5.</li> <li>No confirmation. Announced after: “{n} item(s) marked as spam on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: recorded, “Put {n} items back as they were.”</li> <li>Menu help: “Mark it as spam, which the site’s spam plugin learns from. Ctrl+Shift+S.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs moderate_comments; tier 1.</li> </ul> <h3 id="reply">Reply…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+M; Item > Reply (Alt+I, R); context-menu row 6.</li> <li>What opens: a multi-line text box titled “Reply to {author}”, prompt “Reply:”, help “Your reply to {author}, posted as you on {site}.” Enter adds a line, so Tab to OK. An empty reply is not sent and nothing is said.</li> <li>Preview or confirmation: none; OK posts the reply, threaded under the comment.</li> <li>Announced after: “Replied to {author} on {site}.”</li> <li>Refusal: “Choose a comment to reply to.”</li> <li>Undo: not recorded. Ctrl+Z afterwards undoes whatever was recorded before the reply.</li> <li>Menu help: “Write a reply, posted as you. Ctrl+Shift+M.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs moderate_comments; tier 2, Full access only.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (do_reply), comments.py.</li> <li>Review note: a reply is public the moment OK is pressed, and nothing can take it back from here. A multi-line box where Enter does not submit is safe, but there is no preview and no undo for a public post. At least record it, with Ctrl+Z moving the reply to the Trash.</li> <li>Review note: needing moderate_comments means an Author cannot reply to comments on their own posts from the Workbench, although WordPress lets them.</li> <li>Review note: Approve and Reply (wp-admin’s one action) is two commands here: Ctrl+Shift+A, then Ctrl+Shift+M. The audit table says so, but nothing in the Reply dialog notes that replying to a waiting comment leaves it waiting.</li> </ul> <h3 id="move-to-trash-restore-from-trash-delete-permanently-comments">Move to Trash, Restore from Trash, Delete Permanently (comments)</h3> <ul> <li>Move to Trash: Delete; Alt+I, T; under More. Same flow as posts. Announced: “Moved {n} item(s) to the Trash on {site}. Ctrl+Z puts them back.” Ctrl+Z puts each back to its old status.</li> <li>Restore from Trash: Ctrl+Shift+R; Alt+I, E; under More. A comment comes back as Waiting. Announced: “Restored {n} items from the Trash, as drafts or waiting on {site}.”</li> <li>Delete Permanently: Shift+Delete; Alt+I, D; under More. The same message box, Cancel default.</li> <li>Without permission: all need moderate_comments. Move to Trash and Restore are tier 1; Delete Permanently tier 3.</li> <li>Review note: the Restore row’s help says “Out of the Trash, as a draft.”, which is wrong for a comment; comments come back Waiting.</li> <li>Review note: Move to Trash, the action Delete performs and the F1 for the node names, is under More on the comment menu, while Read in Full takes the top.</li> </ul> <h3 id="empty-spam">Empty Spam…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Alt+E; Site > Empty Spam (Alt+S, M). Not on any context menu.</li> <li>What opens: the spam comments are counted first. None: “No comments are held as spam on {site}.” Otherwise a message box titled “Empty Spam”: “Delete all {n} comments held as spam on {site} permanently? This cannot be undone.” with “Delete All” and “Cancel” (Cancel default).</li> <li>Announced after: “Deleted {k} spam comments on {site}.” plus “ {n} could not be deleted.“ for failures.</li> <li>No site: “Choose a site first.”</li> <li>Undo: not recorded.</li> <li>Menu help: “Delete every comment held as spam on this site, after you confirm.”</li> <li>Without permission: no capability is checked before it runs; the site refuses each delete if you may not moderate, and those count as “could not be deleted”.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_lists.py (do_empty_spam), workbench_create.py (spam_comments, empty_spam).</li> <li>Review note: critical. Empty Spam is not a row action, so the Workbench’s access check never sees it. Read Only access, Safe Mode, and View As Role, whose own sentence says “Nothing can be changed until you stop”, all let Ctrl+Alt+E permanently delete every spam comment after one Yes.</li> <li>Review note: “Delete all 1 comments held as spam” for a single comment; Empty Comment Trash gets the singular right.</li> <li>Review note: an Editor without the right role hears the confirmation, says yes, and then hears “Deleted 0 spam comments on {site}. 37 could not be deleted.” with no reason. A refusal before the question would be kinder.</li> </ul> <h3 id="empty-comment-trash">Empty Comment Trash…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Alt+Shift+E; Site > Empty Comment Trash (Alt+S, E).</li> <li>What opens: the Trash is counted. Empty: “The comment Trash on {site} is empty.” Otherwise a message box titled “Empty Comment Trash”: “Delete all {n} comments in the Trash on {site} permanently? This cannot be undone.” with “Delete All” and “Cancel” (Cancel default).</li> <li>Announced after: “Deleted {k} comments from the Trash on {site}.” plus “ {n} could not be deleted.“</li> <li>Undo: not recorded.</li> <li>Menu help: “Delete every comment in this site’s Trash, after you confirm.”</li> <li>Without permission: not checked before; the same access gap as Empty Spam.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_moderation.py (do_empty_comment_trash).</li> <li>Review note: the same critical access gap as Empty Spam.</li> <li>Review note: the two Empty commands sit on the Site menu with access letters M and E, next to each other, and both are destructive. Empty Trash for posts is on the row menu instead. Three “empty” commands live in two places.</li> </ul> <h3 id="comment-counts">Comment Counts</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Alt+Shift+C; Site > Comment Counts (Alt+S, C).</li> <li>Announced: “{site}: 4 waiting, 120 approved, 37 spam and 2 in the Trash; 124 in All, which is waiting and approved together. 2 are yours.”</li> <li>Menu help: “How many comments wait, are approved, are spam and are in the Trash.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_moderation.py (do_comment_counts), workbench_moderation.py (counts_sentence).</li> <li>Review note: “1 are yours” for one comment.</li> <li>Review note: the sentence is long and fixed in order. “4 waiting” is what a moderator wants first, and it is first, which is good; “which is waiting and approved together” is an explanation that belongs in F1 after the first hearing.</li> </ul> <h3 id="show-its-comments-1">Show Its Comments</h3> <ul> <li>On a post or page row; see Posts and pages. It lands here, filtered to the post.</li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-altenter-a-comment">Properties (Alt+Enter), a comment</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: a sheet titled “Comment by {author} on {post} Properties - {site}” (the row title is its “what” cell).</li> <li>General: Author; Email (“Shown only to people who can moderate comments.”); Website; IP address (read only, “Where it was sent from. WordPress shows it only to people who can moderate comments.”); Written, a date in the site’s own time, editable (“When it was written, in the site’s own time, such as 2026-11-03T09:00:00. Change it as wp-admin’s Edit Comment does.”); Status, a choice of approved, hold, spam, trash; Type (read only).</li> <li>Text: the comment, multi-line.</li> <li>Post: Post number and Post, read only.</li> <li>Thread: “Reply to comment number”, read only.</li> <li>Custom Fields when the site registers comment meta.</li> <li>Saving: a comment has no modified time, so its status, text and author fields stand in for the conflict check; a change made elsewhere since opening asks “Use Mine”.</li> <li>Ctrl+Z: “Put {title} back as it was.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs moderate_comments; the sheet is read only otherwise.</li> <li>Several comments at once: “For several comments, use Approve, Unapprove, Spam or Delete.”</li> <li>Code: workbench_sheets.py (_STATIC “comment”), workbench_moderation.py (version_of, SHEET_HELP).</li> <li>Review note: the Status choice offers “hold”, WordPress’s internal word, where the list says “Waiting”.</li> <li>Review note: the sheet’s title is built from the row’s title, which for a comment is “Comment by {author} on {post}”, so the reader hears “Comment by Ann on Hello World Properties - Community Radio”. It is long and runs two names together.</li> </ul> <h3 id="new-here-on-comments">New Here on Comments</h3> <ul> <li>Ctrl+N says: “New Here makes posts, pages, custom items, categories, tags, terms, menus, people and application passwords. Choose one of those nodes first.”</li> </ul> <h2 id="people">People</h2> <p>People has two lists: Users, everyone on the site with their role, and Application Passwords, your own. A user row’s context menu has thirteen commands plus Properties. The three Paid Memberships Pro rows take three of the six top places on every site, whether or not it runs that plugin, so Remove, Set New Password and Send Password Reset are under More. The guards against locking yourself out are careful and well worded. But the Delete and Shift+Delete keys reach Remove and Revoke by a side door that skips the Workbench’s access check, and on Application Passwords Shift+Delete skips the guard that protects the password QUILL itself signs in with.</p> <h3 id="the-tree-nodes-and-what-the-list-shows-4">The tree nodes and what the list shows</h3> <ul> <li>People: a folder holding Users and Application Passwords. F1: “The people on the site and the passwords made for apps.”</li> <li>Users: Go > Users, Ctrl+Shift+5 (Alt+G, U). Columns: Name, Username, Role, Email. View > Columns adds Number, Slug, Registered, Site. F1: “The people on the site, with their roles. Site > Roles explains each role.”</li> <li>A role you lack list_users for refuses the node: “Seeing the people on the site needs the Administrator role or higher. You are an Editor on {site}.”</li> <li>The details pane for a person: “{name}, {username}, {role}, {email}.”</li> <li>F1 on a user row: “A person on the site. Change Role, Remove, and any exposed membership details are on the context menu.”</li> <li>Application Passwords: Columns Name, Created, Last used (“never” when unused). F1: “Your application passwords on this site. Ctrl+N makes one.” The row for the one QUILL uses adds “ QUILL on this computer signs in with it.“ to its details sentence. F1 on the row: “An application password. Delete revokes it.”</li> <li>Code: workbench_tree.py, workbench_rows.py (user_row), workbench_load.py (load_app_passwords).</li> <li>Review note: F1 on Users says “Site > Roles explains each role”, but the Site menu’s row is “Roles and What They May Do” (Ctrl+Alt+R).</li> </ul> <h3 id="the-user-rows-context-menu">The user row’s context menu</h3> <ul> <li>On top: Show Their Posts, Change Role…, Show Memberships…, Check Membership Access…, Change Membership Level…, Cancel Membership….</li> <li>Under More: Their Application Passwords…, Sign Out Everywhere…, Invite from a Spreadsheet…, Send Password Reset, Set New Password…, Roles and What They May Do, Remove….</li> <li>Then Properties….</li> <li>Review note: the three Paid Memberships Pro rows are offered and enabled on every site, including sites without the plugin, and they hold three of the six top places. Remove, Set New Password and Send Password Reset, which every WordPress site has, are pushed under More. Membership rows should appear only where Paid Memberships Pro answers, or go last.</li> <li>Review note: “Invite from a Spreadsheet” adds new people to the site; it is not about the selected person, yet it sits on the person’s row.</li> </ul> <h3 id="show-their-posts">Show Their Posts</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter (the default); context-menu row 1.</li> <li>What happens: the Posts node with “author:{id}” in the filter box; focus moves to the list.</li> <li>Menu help: “The posts they wrote.”</li> <li>Review note: only Posts. Their pages and content-type items are not shown, and nothing says so.</li> </ul> <h3 id="change-role">Change Role…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu row 2. No chord and no Item menu entry.</li> <li>Your own account, refused before any dialog: “You cannot take away your own Administrator role from here: you would lose the right to put it back. Ask another administrator to do it.” or “You cannot change your own role from here: the new role could take away the right to change it back. Ask an administrator to do it.”</li> <li>One person: a list titled “Change Role for {name}”, prompt “Role:”, rows Subscriber, Contributor, Author, Editor, Administrator, starting on their current role. Help: “What this person may do on the site.” Choosing the role they already have closes with nothing said.</li> <li>Then a preview titled “Change Role”. Summary: “Change {name}’s role on {site}. Ctrl+Z puts it back.” Lines: “{name}: Author becomes Editor”, “Can now: {plain words}; …”, “Can no longer: …”. The button is “Change Role”, the default.</li> <li>The last Administrator, refused when sent: “This is the site’s only Administrator. Make someone else an Administrator first.”</li> <li>Announced after: “{name} is now {Role} on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: recorded. Ctrl+Z says “{name} is {old role} again.”</li> <li>Several people: a list titled “Change Role for {n} people”, help “What these people may do on the site. Anyone the change would lock out is left as they are, and the preview says why.” Then a message box titled “Change Role”: “Make {n} people {Role} on {site}? {names}.” and, on a new line, “Left as they are: {who and why}.” The buttons are “Change Role” and “Cancel”, Cancel default. Nobody can change: “Nobody selected changes: {reasons}.” Different sites: “Change roles on one site at a time.” Announced after: “{n} people now {Role} on {site}.” with “ Not changed: …“ Ctrl+Z: “Put {n} people back in their old roles.”</li> <li>Menu help: “Choose another role for this person.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs promote_users (“Changing someone’s role needs the Administrator role or higher. You are an Editor on {site}.”); tier 2.</li> <li>Keyless: no written reason found in any KEYLESS dict.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_people.py (do_change_role), wordpress_workbench_people_bulk.py (do_change_role), workbench_permissions.py (role_change_problem, role_change_preview), workbench_people.py.</li> <li>Review note: one person gets a preview window that lists what they gain and lose, and Enter confirms. Several people get a message box with no gains or losses, and Enter cancels. The bigger change gets the smaller preview and the opposite default.</li> <li>Review note: a person with a role the site defines itself (a shop manager, say) opens the list on Subscriber, because only the five default roles are offered. One Enter and one more Enter in the preview demotes them. Custom roles should be listed, or the list should start on nothing.</li> <li>Review note: the role can also be changed on the Properties sheet’s Role page. The same guards apply there, but no gains-and-losses preview does.</li> </ul> <h3 id="show-memberships">Show Memberships…</h3> <ul> <li>One line: reads this person’s current and past Paid Memberships Pro levels into a report window. See the Memberships section; Check Membership Access, Change Membership Level and Cancel Membership are covered there too.</li> </ul> <h3 id="their-application-passwords">Their Application Passwords…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu, under More.</li> <li>Refused for someone else’s without edit_users: “WordPress shows {name}’s application passwords only to someone who may edit them, the edit_users capability, which your role on {site} does not have.”</li> <li>None: “{name} has no application passwords on {site}.”</li> <li>What opens: a list titled “Application Passwords for {name}”, prompt “Passwords:”, one line per password with when it was made and last used. Help: “{name}’s application passwords on {site}, with when each was made and last used. OK offers to revoke the one chosen; Cancel only reads.”</li> <li>OK on one: a message box titled “Revoke Application Password”: “Revoke {password} for {name} on {site}? Whatever signs in with it stops working, and it cannot be brought back.” with “Revoke” and “Cancel”. The one QUILL signs in with is refused: “QUILL signs in to {site} with this application password, so revoking it here would lock the Workbench out. Make a new password and reconnect the site first, then revoke this one.”</li> <li>Announced after: “Revoked {password} for {name} on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: recorded with no undo.</li> <li>Menu help: “This person’s application passwords, read from the site, with Revoke after you confirm. Another person’s need the edit_users capability.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs edit_users; tier 0.</li> <li>Keyless: “Checked when a device is lost or a person leaves (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_people_admin.py.</li> <li>Review note: in a list whose purpose is reading, OK means “revoke”. Cancel is the only way to just read. A listener who presses Enter to choose a password to hear about gets a revoke question.</li> <li>Review note: the row is tier 0 (reading), but it leads to a permanent revoke. Read Only Workbench access, Safe Mode and View As Role therefore do not stop the revoke.</li> </ul> <h3 id="sign-out-everywhere">Sign Out Everywhere…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu, under More.</li> <li>Announced, then the browser opens: “WordPress keeps sign-in sessions out of its REST API: there is no route that lists them or ends them. So the Workbench opens {name}’s screen in wp-admin, where Log Out Everywhere is one button.” (for yourself: “your profile” and “Log Out Everywhere Else”).</li> <li>Keyless: “Leaves QUILL for wp-admin, after a lost device (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review note: a hand-off to wp-admin with a trailing ellipsis in the label, though no dialog follows.</li> </ul> <h3 id="invite-from-a-spreadsheet">Invite from a Spreadsheet…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu, under More.</li> <li>One line: a standard Open box for a CSV of email, role and name, a preview titled “Invite from a Spreadsheet” with a line per row and the button “Add {n}”, then “Added {k} of {n} people to {site}.” Recorded with no undo. Needs create_users; tier 2.</li> </ul> <h3 id="send-password-reset">Send Password Reset</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+W; Item > Send Password Reset (Alt+I, W); context menu, under More.</li> <li>Announced, then the browser opens on the person’s wp-admin screen: “WordPress has no REST route for a password reset, so the Workbench opens {name}’s screen in wp-admin, where Send Reset Link sends them the email.”</li> <li>Refusal on the wrong row: “Choose a person in Users first.”</li> <li>Menu help: “Opens their wp-admin screen to send the reset email. Ctrl+Shift+W.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs edit_users. Because edit_users has no plain words in the refusal table, a role without it hears “Doing this (edit users) needs the Administrator role or higher. You are an Editor on {site}.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_lists.py (do_send_password_reset), workbench_create.py (password_reset_pointer).</li> <li>Review note: the label says it sends a reset; it opens a web page. It has a chord, unlike the other hand-offs, so a listener pressing Ctrl+Shift+W in a hurry leaves QUILL.</li> <li>Review note: two other texts promise more than it does. The Add a Person note says “Send Password Reset on their row opens wp-admin” (correct), but the Invite note says “Send Password Reset on their row sends them the link to choose their own” (not true).</li> <li>Review note: the refusal sentence “Doing this (edit users)” is the fallback for a capability missing from the plain-words table. edit_users needs an entry, such as “Changing other people’s accounts”.</li> </ul> <h3 id="set-new-password">Set New Password…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu, under More.</li> <li>What opens: a one-line text box titled “Set New Password for {name}”, prompt “New password:”, already holding a strong generated password, shown as plain text. Help: “A strong password is offered; change it if you like. The password is shown as text, the way wp-admin shows the one it suggests, so you can read or copy it. QUILL sends it once and never says it, logs it or keeps it.”</li> <li>Problems, said after the box closes: “Type a password, or keep the strong one offered.”, “The password starts or ends with a space, which is easy to lose. Take it out.”, “Use at least 12 characters; the one offered is 24.”</li> <li>Confirmation: a message box titled “Set New Password”: “Set a new password for {name} on {site}? Their old one stops working at once and their browser sign-ins end. Application passwords keep working. WordPress sends no email for this, so give it to them yourself. It cannot be put back.” On your own account it adds: “ This is your own account: QUILL keeps working, because it signs in with an application password.“ The buttons are “Set Password” and “Cancel”, Cancel default.</li> <li>Announced after: “New password set for {name} on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: recorded with no undo.</li> <li>Menu help: “Give them a new sign-in password: a strong one is offered, then previewed.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs edit_users (the “Doing this (edit users)” sentence); tier 2.</li> <li>Keyless: “Set when a person is locked out, rarely; never a bare key (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_people_bulk.py (do_set_password), workbench_people_bulk.py (password_problem, set_password).</li> <li>Review note: a password the check rejects closes the box first. A listener who typed their own password hears the problem and must start again from the menu, with the generated one back in the box. The check should keep the box open.</li> <li>Review note: the help says “previewed”, but it is a confirmation message box. The password is never shown again in it.</li> <li>Review note: being recorded with no undo, it blocks Ctrl+Z for everything done before it in the session.</li> </ul> <h3 id="roles-and-what-they-may-do">Roles and What They May Do</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Alt+R; Site > Roles and What They May Do (Alt+S, R); context menu, under More.</li> <li>What opens: a read-only report titled “Roles and What They May Do”, one paragraph per role (“Subscriber: Can …”, then “Everything above, and can …”), ending “WordPress’s REST API reads roles but cannot change what a role may do. A role plugin such as Members or User Role Editor does that in wp-admin.” Copy All and Close.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_lists.py (do_roles), workbench_create.py (role_rows).</li> </ul> <h3 id="remove">Remove…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu, last under More (“Remove…”); Delete on a user row; Shift+Delete on a user row. The Item menu row for Delete still reads “Move to Trash”.</li> <li>Your own account: “You cannot remove your own account from here.”</li> <li>What opens: everyone on the site is read, then a list titled “Remove {name}”, prompt “Give their posts to:”, everyone else by name. Help: “WordPress needs someone to own what this person wrote.” Nobody else: “There is nobody else to give this person’s posts to.”</li> <li>Their posts are counted, then a message box titled “Remove”: “Remove {name}? {n} posts move to {other}. This cannot be undone.” with “Remove” and “Cancel”, Cancel default.</li> <li>The last Administrator, refused when sent: “This is the site’s only Administrator, so it cannot be removed.”</li> <li>Announced after: “Removed {name} from {site}; their posts are {other}’s.”</li> <li>Several people: a list titled “Remove {n} people”; the message box “Remove {n} people from {site}? {names}. {n} posts move to {other}. This cannot be undone.” and, on a new line, “Kept: {who and why}.” Announced after: “Removed {n} people from {site}; their posts are {other}’s.” with “ Not removed: …“ and “ This cannot be undone.“</li> <li>Undo: recorded with no undo.</li> <li>Menu help: “Remove this person, with their content given to someone else. Delete.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs delete_users (“Removing people needs the Administrator role or higher. You are an Editor on {site}.”); tier 3, Full access only, when reached from the menu.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_people.py (do_remove_user), wordpress_workbench_people_bulk.py (do_remove_user, _confirm_removal), workbench_people.py (remove_preview, remove_user).</li> <li>Review note: critical. Delete and Shift+Delete on a user row run Move to Trash and Delete Permanently, which hand over to Remove. The Workbench checks a command against the row’s own menu rows, and the user row has no Trash or Delete Permanently row, so neither the tier 3 access check nor the delete_users check runs. With Read Only access, Safe Mode or View As Role, Delete on a person still opens Remove, and Yes still removes them.</li> <li>Review note: the “Give their posts to” list includes Subscribers, who cannot edit what they are given. wp-admin offers everyone too, but a hint (“Subscriber”) beside each name would help.</li> <li>Review note: the one-person confirmation does not name the site, unlike the several-people one.</li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-altenter-a-person">Properties (Alt+Enter), a person</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: the person is read again, then a sheet titled “{name} Properties - {site}”.</li> <li>General: Display name, Username (read only), Email, Nickname, First name, Last name, Website, Biography, Language (“A WordPress language code such as en_US, for their wp-admin. Empty uses the site’s language.”), Posts written (read only, counted).</li> <li>Role: Role, a choice of the five roles, which needs promote_users. A save that would lock someone out is refused with the same sentences as Change Role.</li> <li>Custom Fields when the site registers user meta.</li> <li>Saving: users have no modified time, so their edited fields stand in for the conflict check. Ctrl+Z: “Put {name} back as it was.”</li> <li>Without permission: needs edit_users to save; your own row may always be saved.</li> <li>Code: workbench_sheets.py (_STATIC “user”), wordpress_workbench_sheets.py, workbench_people.py (check_role_save).</li> <li>Review note: Language wants a code such as en_US typed by hand. wp-admin offers a list of installed languages.</li> </ul> <h3 id="new-here-ctrln-in-users-add-a-person">New Here (Ctrl+N) in Users: Add a Person</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+N; File > New Here (Alt+F, N) with Users selected.</li> <li>What opens: a sheet titled “Add a Person - {site}”. General: Username (“The name they sign in with. It cannot be changed later.”), Email, First name, Last name, Display name, Website, Language. Role: Role, Subscriber to start, whose help ends with the no-email note.</li> <li>OK refuses with: “A new person needs a username: the name they sign in with.”, “A new person needs an email address WordPress can reach them at.”, “{role} is not a role this site has.”</li> <li>Confirmation: a message box titled “Add a Person”: “Add {name} ({username}), {email}, to {site} as {Role}? WordPress’s REST API makes the account but sends no email, so there is no choice to send one. They get a long random password nobody sees; Send Password Reset on their row opens wp-admin, where one button emails them the link to choose their own.” with “Add” and “Cancel”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Added {name} to {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: recorded with no undo.</li> <li>Without permission: no requirement is checked before the sheet opens; the site refuses without create_users.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_people_bulk.py (add_person), workbench_people_bulk.py (NEW_USER_SHEET, new_user_body, new_user_preview).</li> <li>Review note: the most important fact, that no email goes and the person cannot sign in until someone sends a reset, is in the help of the second tab and in the confirmation. It is not visible on the first tab, where the listener fills everything in.</li> <li>Review note: an account just added is easy to remove, but Ctrl+Z will not, and its entry blocks every earlier undo.</li> </ul> <h3 id="your-profile-1">Your Profile…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Alt+Shift+P; Site > Your Profile (Alt+S, Y).</li> <li>What opens: your own sheet, titled “Your Profile - {site}”, with the General page only; the Role page is left out so nobody demotes themselves here.</li> <li>Refusals: “Choose a site first.” and “{site} has not said who you are yet.”</li> <li>Saving and Ctrl+Z as for a person.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_people_bulk.py (do_your_profile).</li> </ul> <h3 id="application-passwords-new-here-ctrln">Application Passwords: New Here (Ctrl+N)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+N with Application Passwords selected; File > New Here.</li> <li>What opens: a text box titled “New Application Password”, prompt “Name:”, help “A name for what will use it, such as QUILL on the laptop.” An empty name closes with nothing said.</li> <li>No confirmation; the password is made at once.</li> <li>Then a report window titled “New Password for {site}”, holding “{name}”, a blank line, the secret, a blank line, and “Copy it now: {site} shows it only once.” Help: “The new application password. Select it and copy it; once this closes, the site will not show it again.” Copy All and Close.</li> <li>Undo: not recorded.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_people.py (_new_password), workbench_people.py (create_app_password).</li> <li>Review note: Copy All copies the name and the instruction too, not just the secret. A listener pasting it into another app’s password field pastes three lines. A “Copy Password” button that copies only the secret would fix it.</li> </ul> <h3 id="application-passwords-revoke">Application Passwords: Revoke…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Delete on the row; the context menu “Revoke…” (row 2). Shift+Delete reaches a different path (see the review note).</li> <li>The password QUILL signs in with: “QUILL signs in to {site} with this application password, so revoking it here would lock the Workbench out. Make a new password and reconnect the site first, then revoke this one.”</li> <li>Confirmation: a message box titled “Revoke Application Password”: “Revoke the application password {name} on {site}? Anything signing in with it stops working, and a revoked password cannot be restored.” with “Revoke” and “Cancel”, Cancel default.</li> <li>Announced after: “Revoked {name} on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: recorded with no undo.</li> <li>Menu help: “Stop this password working, after you confirm. Delete.”</li> <li>Without permission: no capability; tier 3, Full access only, when chosen from the menu.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (do_revoke), workbench_permissions.py (revoke_refusal).</li> <li>Review note: critical. Shift+Delete on an application password row runs the generic Delete Permanently, which has no case for application passwords. It asks “Delete 1 item from {site} permanently? This cannot be undone: {name}.” and then deletes the password’s route. It skips the guard above, so Shift+Delete on the row marked “QUILL on this computer signs in with it” revokes the password the Workbench is using and locks it out mid-session.</li> <li>Review note: Delete reaches Revoke without the tier 3 access check, for the same reason as Remove on a person: the row has no Trash row for the check to find.</li> </ul> <h3 id="application-passwords-properties">Application Passwords: Properties</h3> <ul> <li>A read-only sheet: Name, Made, Last used, Last address. The button is Close. “This is read only here.” is the F1 of every field.</li> </ul> <h2 id="menus-1">Menus</h2> <p>The Workbench shows a site’s classic menus as one flat list under Sites > site > Menus and Navigation. Each menu is one row; the menu’s items are not listed anywhere in the Workbench. A menu row offers Read in Full (the default), Add Menu Item, Add New Pages Automatically, Arrange Items, Earlier Versions, Default Navigation Menu, Copy to Site and Properties. Adding an item is now careful and complete (every kind, Search or View All, under and after any item, preview, re-read, undo). Everything else you do to an existing menu’s items (rename, move, remove) is not done in the Workbench itself: Arrange Items (added 2026-11-14) hands the menu to the editor’s older Menus window, which does those jobs without previews or Ctrl+Z. Before it, the editor’s Tools > WordPress > Menus door had replaced that window with the Workbench, leaving no way to rearrange a menu at all.</p> <h3 id="the-menus-and-navigation-node">The Menus and Navigation node</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: in the tree, Sites > site > Menus and Navigation (it sits after More Taxonomies and before Appearance). There is no Go key for it: the Go menu’s Ctrl+Shift+1 to Ctrl+Shift+9 are Posts, Pages, Media, Comments, Users, Plugins, Appearance, Settings and Site Health. The editor’s Tools > WordPress > Menus… opens the Workbench at this node (the door table in workbench_tree.py maps <code>wordpress.menus</code> to <code>menus</code>).</li> <li>What the list shows: columns menu, locations, items. Each row’s detail (the details pane and Read in Full) is <code>{name}, shown in: {places}.</code>, where places is the menu’s theme locations joined with commas, or <code>none</code>.</li> <li>F1 on the node: “The site’s menus and where the theme shows them.”</li> <li>F1 on a row: “A menu. Enter reads where it is shown; Ctrl+N or Add Menu Item adds to it, and Alt+Enter has its Properties, with whether new top-level pages join it by themselves.”</li> <li>Without permission: the node itself needs nothing to list. The write rows need <code>edit_theme_options</code>, which in WordPress’s default roles only an Administrator has, so an Editor sees them greyed.</li> <li>Code: workbench_tree.py, workbench_load_structure.py (load_menus), workbench_node_help.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The items column is always empty: load_menus fills <code>"items": ""</code> for every row. A listener hears a column header with nothing under it on every row.</li> <li>The locations column shows WordPress’s location slugs exactly as <code>wp/v2/menus</code> sends them (for example <code>primary</code>), not the theme’s names for them (“Primary Menu”). Not translated anywhere I could find.</li> <li>The list reads <code>wp/v2/menus</code>, which is classic menus only. A block theme’s Navigation menus (<code>wp_navigation</code> posts) do not appear in this node at all. The only trace of them is the Default Navigation Menu command, which names one by number.</li> <li>There is no way to see a menu’s items in the Workbench itself: no child node per menu, no items in the detail, nothing in Properties. The only way is Arrange Items, which leaves the Workbench for another window.</li> <li>Rename an item, move it up, down, in or out, and remove it: not in the Workbench’s own rows. The editor’s Menus window (wordpress_menu_commands.py) has Rename, Move Up, Move Down, Move In, Move Out, Remove, Check Menu and Locations; <code>cmd_menus</code> returns early through <code>workbench_door("wordpress.menus")</code> (and <code>open_from_door</code> always returns True), so from the editor’s Tools menu that window is gone, and it is now reached only through Arrange Items or the editor’s add-this-page-to-a-menu path.</li> <li>No Go key for Menus, although it is one of the places people visit most in wp-admin after Posts and Pages.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="read-in-full-menu-row-default">Read in Full (menu row, default)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter on a menu row, or the first row of its context menu (Applications key or Shift+F10).</li> <li>What it does: moves focus to the details pane, which holds <code>{name}, shown in: {places}.</code> Nothing else.</li> <li>Announced after: nothing; focus moves.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (do_read).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The default action of a menu reads one short sentence that the list row already said. For a menu, Enter is the one key people press expecting to go inside; here it goes to a pane that cannot show the items.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="add-menu-item">Add Menu Item…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Add Menu Item…” on a menu row. Also Ctrl+N (New Here) with a menu row selected, which first asks the New Here question (see “New Here on Menus” below). Not on the Item menu.</li> <li>What opens, in order (each a separate small dialog; each choice dialog is one list box labelled by its prompt, then OK (default) and Cancel; each text dialog is one text box, then OK (default) and Cancel): <ul> <li>First QUILL reads the site in the background (“reading what {menu} can hold”).</li> <li>Dialog title “Add Menu Item to {menu}”, list “Kind of item:” (access key K). Rows are every post type and taxonomy the site shows in menus, in this order: Pages, Posts, other post types by slug, then Custom Links, then Categories, Tags, other taxonomies. F1: “What the new item links to: a page, a post, an item of any other type, a category, a tag or other term, or a custom link to any address.”</li> <li>For Custom Links: dialog “Add a Custom Link”, text box “URL:” (access key U) prefilled with <code>https://</code>; then a second dialog with the same title, text box “Link text:” (access key L).</li> <li>For anything else: dialog “Add {kind} to {menu}”, text box “Search, or leave empty to view all:” (access key S). F1 says only published items are listed. Then QUILL searches (“finding {kind}”), and a list dialog “Add {kind} to {menu}” with list “{kind}:” of titles. With an empty search on a post type that has an archive, the first row is <code>All {kind} (the archive page)</code>.</li> <li>Where it goes: if the menu has any items, dialog “Where {title} Goes”, list “Under:” (access key U) with “Top level of the menu” first, then <code>Under {item}, level {n}</code> for every item. Then, if the chosen level already has items, the same title with list “Position:” (access key P): <code>First</code>, then <code>After {item}</code> for each sibling; the last row (the end) is preselected.</li> <li>Preview: title “Add Menu Item to {menu}”; first control the read-only Summary box (access key S), then the “Each change:” list (access key E), then the button “Add” (default) and Cancel.</li> </ul></li> <li>Preview wording: summary <code>Add {title} to the menu {menu} on {site}?</code>. Lines: <code>Adds: {title}, {kind}</code> (plus <code>, to {url}</code> for a custom link); <code>Where: {under}, {after}, {n} of {total}.</code> (under is <code>under {item}</code> or <code>at the top level</code>; after is <code>first</code> or <code>after {item}</code>); and, when items move, <code>The {n} items after it there move down one.</code></li> <li>Announced after: the site’s own result: <code>Added {name} to the menu.</code> or, if the site put it elsewhere, <code>Sent {title}, but the site did not keep it where it was asked to go.</code> The list refreshes.</li> <li>Failures announced: <ul> <li>Not a menu row: <code>Choose a menu in Menus and Navigation first.</code></li> <li>Custom link with empty text: <code>A custom link needs link text, so nothing was added.</code></li> <li>Nothing found: <code>{site} has no published {kind}{ matching words}.</code></li> <li>Menu changed after the preview: <code>The menu was changed on the site after the preview. Nothing was sent.</code> or <code>The item it was to go under is no longer in the menu. Nothing was sent.</code></li> <li>Any other error: the site’s plain error text, or <code>That did not work.</code></li> </ul></li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z (Undo WordPress Change) deletes the new item and puts back the items it renumbered. Spoken: <code>Took {title} out of {menu} again.</code> Change History lists <code>Added {title} to {menu} on {site}.</code></li> <li>F1: the dialogs’ help texts quoted above; the row’s help: “A page, post, category, tag, custom link or item of any other type, found by search or from the whole list, placed under any item and after any other, then previewed. Ctrl+N with a menu selected does it too. Ctrl+Z takes it out again.”</li> <li>Cancel: Cancel or Escape at any step stops with nothing sent and nothing said. Leaving the URL as <code>https://</code>, or clearing it, also stops silently.</li> <li>Without permission: needs <code>edit_theme_options</code>, tier Write. The context-menu row is greyed. Run anyway (for example from What Can I Do Here?), the refusal is <code>Changing menus and the theme's options needs the Administrator role or higher. You are an Editor on {site}.</code> Under Workbench Access Read Only or Organize: <code>Workbench access on {site} is {Read Only or Organize}. Site > Workbench Access changes it.</code></li> <li>Keyless: “Ctrl+N does it with a menu selected, through New Here (rule 5).”</li> <li>Code: menu_item_add.py, wordpress_workbench_menu_items.py, workbench_dashboard_places.py (MENU_ROWS, KEYLESS).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Up to six dialogs in a row for one item (kind, search, pick, under, position, preview). Each is correct, but there is no single “Add Menu Item” dialog where the kind, the item and the place can be reviewed together and changed before the preview. A wrong choice at step five means Cancel and start again.</li> <li>View All reads at most 100 items (<code>LIMIT = 100</code>, one page) and never says it stopped. On a site with 300 pages, page 101 onward is silently missing from View All; only Search finds it.</li> <li>Search results that share a title (two pages both called “Contact”) are indistinguishable in the list: rows are titles only, with no parent, date or address.</li> <li>Custom Links sits in the middle of the kinds list (after the post types, before Categories). wp-admin’s panel order is the reason, but in a list box a listener has to arrow past every custom post type to find it.</li> <li>The “Where” step is skipped entirely for an empty menu and the position step for an empty level, which is good, but the preview then says <code>Where: at the top level, first, 1 of 1.</code> with no hint that the Under question was skipped.</li> <li>Ctrl+N with a menu selected bypasses the context menu’s permission check: <code>run</code> checks <code>new_here</code> (which needs nothing), then calls <code>do_add_menu_item</code> directly. An Editor walks all six dialogs and only fails when the site refuses the write. Not tested on a live site; read from wordpress_workbench_lists.py and wordpress_workbench_window.py.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="add-new-pages-automatically">Add New Pages Automatically…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Add New Pages Automatically…” on a menu row. Also the check box “Automatically add new top-level pages to this menu” on the menu’s Properties sheet.</li> <li>What opens: QUILL reads the menu’s current setting (“reading {menu}’s settings”), then a preview titled “Add New Pages Automatically”: Summary box, “Each change:” list, the button “Turn On” or “Turn Off” (default) and Cancel.</li> <li>Preview wording, turning on: summary <code>Turn On automatically add new top-level pages to this menu for {menu} on {site}?</code>; lines <code>Now: off. New pages are added to menus by hand.</code> and <code>After: on. Each new top-level page published joins the end of {menu}.</code></li> <li>Preview wording, turning off: summary <code>Turn Off automatically add new top-level pages to this menu for {menu} on {site}?</code>; lines <code>Now: on. New top-level pages are added to {menu} by themselves.</code> and <code>After: off. Pages already in the menu stay; new ones are not added.</code></li> <li>Announced after: <code>Adding new pages automatically to {menu} is on.</code> (or off). If the site did not keep it: <code>{site} did not keep the change; it is still {on or off}.</code> If it changed meanwhile: <code>The menu's setting was changed on the site meanwhile. Nothing was sent.</code></li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z turns it back; spoken <code>Adding new pages automatically to {menu} is {on or off} again.</code></li> <li>Cancel: nothing sent, nothing said.</li> <li>Without permission: needs <code>edit_theme_options</code> (Administrator); the refusal sentence is the one under Add Menu Item.</li> <li>Keyless: “Set once per menu; also a check box on Properties (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: menu_item_add.py (AUTO_ADD_LABEL, read_auto_add, set_auto_add), wordpress_workbench_menu_items.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The summary is built from the check box label lowercased, so it reads “Turn On automatically add new top-level pages to this menu for Main on Radio?”, which is ungrammatical spoken English. Something like “Turn on adding new top-level pages to Main?” would read better.</li> <li>The command is a toggle with no indication of the current state in its label. You learn the state only from the preview’s Now line. That is acceptable because the preview always comes first, but the context-menu label could say “Turn On…” or “Turn Off…”.</li> <li>Two routes to one setting: this command (previewed, re-read before and after) and the Properties check box (a sheet save: conflict check, no preview, undone by Ctrl+Z as <code>Put {menu number} back as it was.</code>). Same result, two different confirmation styles.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="arrange-items">Arrange Items…</h3> <ul> <li>Status: appeared in workbench_dashboard_places.py (MENU_ROWS), wordpress_workbench_menu_items.py (do_arrange_menu_items) and wordpress_menu_commands.py (cmd_menus gained <code>menu_id</code> and <code>profile</code>) at about 15:26 on 2026-11-14, while this pass was being written. No engineering-log entry yet. Described as the code stood then.</li> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Arrange Items…” on a menu row, after Add New Pages Automatically. No key.</li> <li>What opens: the editor’s Menus window for that menu, titled <code>{menu}, {site}</code>, purpose <code>The menu {menu} on {site}, as a tree. Items under another item are a level deeper.</code>; a list “Menu items:” (access key S) of each item’s name, what it goes to, its level and its place among its neighbours; then buttons Add Page…, Add Post…, Add Category…, Add Link…, Rename…, Move Up, Move Down, Move In, Move Out, Remove…, Check Menu (“Find items with no name, and links to drafts or deleted pages.”), Other Menu… and Locations… (“Choose where the theme shows this menu, such as the header or footer.”).</li> <li>What each does, as written in that window: Rename <code>Change what the selected item says.</code> (spoken <code>Renamed to {new}.</code>); Move Up, Move Down, Move In, Move Out (<code>{item} cannot move that way.</code> when it cannot); Remove asks <code>Remove {item} from {menu} on {site}? The page or link itself is not touched.</code> (plus <code> Its {n} items move up into its place.</code>), button <code>Remove {item}</code>, then <code>{item} removed from {menu}.</code>; the Add buttons add at the end (<code>{name} added to {menu}, at the end.</code>).</li> <li>Preview or confirmation: none except Remove. Row help: “Each change is sent when you make it.”</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z in the Workbench does not undo these; the window records nothing in the Workbench’s Change History. The editor’s Menu History (Tools > WordPress > Menu History…) is the way back.</li> <li>Without permission: needs <code>edit_theme_options</code>, tier Write, as the other menu writes.</li> <li>Keyless: “Opens the Menus window for a reshuffle, now and then (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: workbench_dashboard_places.py, wordpress_workbench_menu_items.py, wordpress_menu_commands.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>It restores the missing jobs, but as a different window with different rules: no previews, no Ctrl+Z, its own Add buttons that duplicate Add Menu Item with fewer choices (end only). A person learns two ways to add to a menu that behave differently.</li> <li>The label says “Arrange” but the window also renames, removes, adds and sets locations; “Edit Items…” or “Open in the Menus Window…” would set the right expectation.</li> <li>When the window closes, the Workbench list is not known to refresh (not verified).</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="earlier-versions-menu">Earlier Versions… (menu)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Earlier Versions…” on a menu row.</li> <li>What opens: QUILL reads the menu and records a snapshot of it now (“reading {menu}’s earlier versions”). If it has older snapshots, a list dialog “Earlier Versions of {menu}”, list “Versions:” (access key V), rows like <code>{date}, {n} items, {m} items since removed</code> or <code>{date}, {n} items, same items as now</code>. F1: “Each earlier state QUILL kept, newest first, with items since removed.” Then a preview titled “Bring Back Items to {menu}” with the button “Bring Back”.</li> <li>Preview wording: summary <code>Add back the {n} items {menu} has lost since then, at its end?</code>; one line per item, <code>Added back: {title}</code>.</li> <li>Announced after: <code>Brought back {n} items to {menu}.</code></li> <li>Failures announced: <ul> <li>Not a menu row: <code>Choose a menu first.</code></li> <li>No snapshots yet: <code>{menu} has no earlier versions yet. WordPress keeps none for a classic menu, so QUILL keeps its own each time it reads one, starting now.</code></li> <li>Chosen version lost nothing: <code>Every item from then is still in {menu}.</code></li> <li>Menu gone: <code>{menu number} is no longer on {site}.</code> (the row’s title falls back to the menu’s id, see Properties below).</li> </ul></li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z deletes the items brought back; spoken <code>Took the {n} items out of {menu} again.</code></li> <li>Cancel: nothing sent.</li> <li>Without permission: needs <code>edit_theme_options</code>.</li> <li>Keyless: “Looked for after a mistake, rarely (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_widgets.py (do_menu_versions), looks_history.py (MenuSnapshots, MenuVersion).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>It only brings back removed items, and puts them at the end of the menu at the top level, not where they were. A restored submenu comes back flattened. The preview does say “at its end”, but not “at the top level”.</li> <li>It cannot undo a rename or a move, only a removal, although the label “Earlier Versions” suggests a full restore.</li> <li>History starts only the first time QUILL reads the menu, and only on this computer. The first use always says there are no versions, which teaches the user the command does nothing.</li> <li>If the version lost nothing the brought-back count can never be zero here, but between the preview and the write another person could restore items, and the announcement would then say “Brought back 0 items” (the work re-checks what is missing). Minor.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="default-navigation-menu">Default Navigation Menu…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Default Navigation Menu…” on a menu row.</li> <li>What opens: a standard Yes/No message box titled “Default Navigation Menu” with buttons “Ask” and “Cancel”; Cancel is the default (No default).</li> <li>Confirmation wording: <code>Ask {site} which Navigation menu a block theme shows where none is chosen? If the site has no Navigation menu yet, WordPress makes one from its classic menu or its pages.</code></li> <li>Announced after: <code>Where a block theme has no menu chosen, {site} shows {title}, Navigation menu {id}.</code> Failure: <code>The site did not name a default Navigation menu.</code></li> <li>Undo: none recorded. If WordPress made a new Navigation menu, nothing in the Workbench can remove it.</li> <li>Without permission: needs <code>edit_theme_options</code>.</li> <li>Keyless: “Asked once when setting up a block theme (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_widgets.py (do_default_navigation), looks_history.py (navigation_fallback).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>It sits on a classic menu’s row but answers a question about block Navigation menus, which this node does not list. A person on a classic theme gets a question that does not apply to them.</li> <li>The answer speaks the menu’s number (<code>Navigation menu 1234</code>), which means nothing to a listener and cannot be used anywhere in the Workbench.</li> <li>The tier is Read, but it can create a menu on the site. The confirmation is honest about that; the history is not (no entry, no undo).</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="copy-to-site-menu">Copy to Site… (menu)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Copy to Site…”; Item menu Copy to Site, Ctrl+Shift+Y.</li> <li>What opens: always a list dialog “Copy to Site”, list “Site:” (help “Which of your other connected sites.”), even when only one other site is connected. Then a preview titled “Copy from {source} to {target}” with the button “Copy”, listing each planned step, pictures to upload (<code>Upload the picture {url}</code>) and <code>Left out: ...</code> lines.</li> <li>Announced after: <code>Copied to {target}; new items are drafts there.</code> or, with failures, <code>Copied to {target}, except {n}; the report says which.</code> and a report titled “Copy to {target}”.</li> <li>Failures: no other site: <code>Connect a second site first: File > WordPress > Connect a Site.</code> Nothing selected: <code>Select what to copy first.</code></li> <li>Undo: none. Change History records <code>Copied {n} from {source} to {target}.</code>, and Ctrl+Z says <code>Copied {n} from {source} to {target}. This cannot be undone.</code></li> <li>Without permission: the target site is checked as a Write; the refusal is the Workbench access sentence or the role sentence for the target.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_bold.py, workbench_site_copy.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>“Copied 3 from Radio to Studio.” is missing its noun.</li> <li>For a menu, the copy replaces the target menu’s links (workbench_site_copy.py says “its links there are replaced”). That is a destructive change on the target with no undo; check that the preview says it plainly.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-menu">Properties (menu)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+Enter on a menu row, the last context-menu row “Properties…”, or Item > Properties.</li> <li>What opens: the shared Properties sheet titled <code>{menu number} Properties - {site}</code> (for example <code>12 Properties - Radio</code>), one page “General” with: Menu name (text), Description (multi-line), Automatically add new top-level pages to this menu (check box, needs <code>edit_theme_options</code>), Shown in (list, read only).</li> <li>Field help: Menu name “The menu’s name in wp-admin.”; Description “A note about the menu; visitors do not see it.”; the check box “Checked, each new top-level page published from now on is added to the end of this menu by WordPress itself.”; Shown in “The theme’s places this menu is shown in.”</li> <li>Announced after a save: <code>Saved 1 change to {menu number} on {site}.</code> (or <code>Saved {n} changes</code>), plus <code> The site did not keep: ...</code> when a field was dropped. No preview before a menu sheet save.</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z, spoken <code>Put {menu number} back as it was.</code></li> <li>Without permission: a field the role cannot change is read only, with the refusal sentence as its F1 help.</li> <li>Code: menu_item_add.py (MENU_SHEET), workbench_sheets.py, wordpress_workbench_sheets.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The sheet title, the saved sentence and the undo sentence all name the menu by its number, not its name: WbRow.title looks for a title, name, what, plugin, theme, setting, form or site cell, and a menu row’s name is in a <code>menu</code> cell, so the title falls back to the row key (the menu id). The menu commands work around it with their own <code>_named()</code>, the sheet does not.</li> <li>This is the only place to rename a menu, and Shown in is read only: choosing which theme location shows a menu is only in the Menus window’s Locations… (through Arrange Items).</li> <li>Shown in lists location slugs, as in the list.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="new-here-on-menus-ctrln">New Here on Menus (Ctrl+N)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+N or File > New Here…, or the node context menu’s “New Here…”, with the Menus and Navigation node current.</li> <li>What opens: with a menu row selected, a list dialog “New Here”, list “Make:” with <code>A new item in {menu}</code> (first, preselected) and <code>A new menu</code>. F1 “An item in the selected menu, as Add Menu Item does, or another menu.” The first goes to Add Menu Item. The second (or Ctrl+N with no row selected) asks, in a dialog titled “New Menu”, text box “Name:” (help “Its name, as the site shows it.”).</li> <li>Announced after: <code>Made the menu {name} on {site}.</code> Empty name: <code>It needs a name, so nothing was made.</code></li> <li>Undo: none recorded. Ctrl+Z after making a menu undoes whatever change came before it.</li> <li>Without permission: New Here has no requirement, so nothing is greyed; the site refuses an Editor at write time.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_lists.py (do_new_here, _item_or_menu), workbench_create.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>A new menu is made with no preview and no undo, unlike every other write in this node. Worse, because it is not in Change History, Ctrl+Z right after it silently undoes the previous, unrelated change.</li> <li>File > New Here’s own help still says “A new user or password, where you are.”, which is out of date in every node.</li> <li>Delete a menu: not found in the Workbench.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h2 id="widgets">Widgets</h2> <p>Classic themes’ widget areas are under Sites > site > Appearance > Widgets and Sidebars, one row per area. A widget area’s context menu adds, changes, moves and removes its widgets, for every widget type the site registers: a legacy widget is filled in on the form WordPress itself draws, turned into QUILL’s own labelled controls; a block widget is edited as text. Add and Change are previewed as a visitor would see the widget; Remove asks first; every change is undone with Ctrl+Z. The widgets themselves are never rows, so every command starts from the area and asks which widget.</p> <h3 id="the-widgets-and-sidebars-node">The Widgets and Sidebars node</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Sites > site > Appearance > Widgets and Sidebars. Ctrl+Shift+7 (Go > Appearance) lands on Themes, not here, so it is Ctrl+Shift+7 then arrows in the tree. The editor’s Tools > WordPress > Manage > Widgets… opens the Workbench here.</li> <li>What the list shows: columns sidebar, widgets, status: the area’s name, how many widgets it holds, and WordPress’s status word as sent (<code>active</code> or <code>inactive</code>). Detail: <code>{name}, {n} widgets.</code></li> <li>F1 on the node: “The classic theme’s widget areas. Each row’s context menu adds, changes, moves, removes and previews its widgets, of every kind the site has.”</li> <li>F1 on a row: “A widget area and its widgets.
