Add, Change, Move and Remove a Widget are on the context menu, for every kind of widget the site has.”</li> <li>Context menu: Read in Full (default), Add a Widget…, Change a Widget…, Move a Widget…, Remove a Widget…, Preview Widgets, then Show Only and Hide submenus, then Properties….</li> <li>Code: workbench_tree.py, workbench_load_structure.py (load_sidebars), workbench_dashboard_places.py (WIDGET_ROWS).</li> <li>Naming: in every dialog title and spoken sentence below, <code>{area}</code> is the widget area’s id, not its name. WbRow.title looks for a title, name, what, plugin, theme, setting, form or site cell; an area’s name is in a <code>sidebar</code> cell, so the title falls back to the row key, WordPress’s sidebar id. So the dialogs say “Add a Widget to sidebar-1” and “Removed Text widget: Hours from sidebar-1; it is in Inactive Widgets.”, and the Properties sheet is titled <code>sidebar-1 Properties - {site}</code>. (The list row itself shows the name.)</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The id-for-name problem above is the first thing to fix: every widget sentence names an area the way only a theme developer would.</li> <li>The detail says <code>{n} widgets.</code> and nothing about what they are. To hear the widgets you must run Preview Widgets, which opens a report.</li> <li>“1 widgets” is said for an area with one widget (no singular form in load_sidebars).</li> <li>On a block theme this node is normally empty or shows only WordPress’s inactive area; nothing says “your theme uses the Site Editor instead; see Templates and Template Parts”.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="read-in-full-widget-area-default">Read in Full (widget area, default)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter, or the first context-menu row.</li> <li>What it does: focus to the details pane, which says <code>{name}, {n} widgets.</code></li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="add-a-widget">Add a Widget…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Add a Widget…” on a widget area row.</li> <li>What opens: <ul> <li>QUILL reads the site’s widget types (“reading the kinds of widget”).</li> <li>List dialog “Add a Widget to {area}”, list “Kind of widget:” (access key K); each row <code>{name}: {description}</code>, Block first. F1: “Block holds any block, as text; the rest each have their own settings.”</li> <li>For Block: dialog “Add a Block Widget to {area}”, multi-line text box “Text:” (access key T), help “The words of a paragraph block, shown in this area on every page.”</li> <li>For any other kind: QUILL reads its form (“reading {kind}’s form”) and shows it as a QUILL form titled “Add {kind}”, purpose “The widget’s own settings, as WordPress draws them.”, one labelled control per field of WordPress’s own form (text boxes, check boxes, choices), access keys assigned automatically, then OK and Cancel. A kind whose form has no fields skips this dialog.</li> <li>Preview: title “Add {kind}”, button “Add”.</li> </ul></li> <li>Preview wording: summary <code>Add this {kind} widget to {area} on {site}? It shows on every page that has this area.</code>; one line <code>Visitors see: {what WordPress renders}</code> (or <code>(nothing yet)</code>).</li> <li>Announced after: <code>Added a {kind} widget to {area} on {site}.</code>; the list refreshes.</li> <li>Failures: not an area: <code>Choose a widget area in Widgets and Sidebars first.</code>; no types: <code>{site} lists no kinds of widget.</code>; an empty block text stops silently.</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z deletes it; spoken <code>Removed the {kind} widget again.</code></li> <li>Cancel: at any step, nothing sent.</li> <li>Without permission: needs <code>edit_theme_options</code> (Administrator), tier Write.</li> <li>Keyless: “Widgets are added a few times a site’s life (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_widgets.py, widget_types.py, site_structure.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The help says Block “holds any block, as text”, but Add makes only a paragraph block from plain words (<code>paragraph_block(text)</code>). You cannot add a heading, a list or a Latest Posts block from here; only Change edits a block widget’s markup.</li> <li>The widget is always added at the end of the area. There is no position choice, unlike Add Menu Item.</li> <li>“Visitors see:” is WordPress’s rendering turned into words, which is the right idea; for a widget that renders nothing until configured, “(nothing yet)” is clear.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="change-a-widget">Change a Widget…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Change a Widget…”.</li> <li>What opens: <ul> <li>QUILL reads the area’s widgets. With more than one, list dialog “Change a Widget”, list “Widget:” (access key W), each widget as spoken (a block widget by its first block’s description, a legacy one as <code>{kind} widget: {words}</code>), in page order. With one widget it is chosen without asking.</li> <li>A block widget: dialog “Change {widget}”, multi-line text box “Block markup:” (access key B) holding the raw markup; help “The widget’s block markup. OK reads the change back before it is sent.”</li> <li>A legacy widget: its own form, titled “Change {widget}”.</li> <li>Preview: title “Change {widget}”, button “Save”.</li> </ul></li> <li>Preview wording: summary <code>Save {widget} in {area} on {site}?</code>. For a block widget the lines are a line-by-line change list (<code>Line {n} removed: ...</code>, <code>Line {n} added: ...</code>, at most a set number then <code>And {n} more line changes.</code>, or <code>No line changes.</code>). For a legacy widget: <code>Visitors saw: {before}</code> and <code>Visitors will see: {after}</code>.</li> <li>Announced after: <code>Saved {widget} in {area} on {site}.</code></li> <li>Failures: <code>{area} has no widgets.</code></li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z; spoken <code>{widget} is back as it was.</code></li> <li>Cancel: unchanged block text, or Cancel, stops silently.</li> <li>Without permission: <code>edit_theme_options</code>.</li> <li>Keyless: “Widgets change a few times a site’s life (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_widgets.py, widget_types.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>A legacy widget whose form has no fields returns no answers, and the command then stops without a word (<code>if not data: return</code>). The person hears nothing and does not know why.</li> <li>Unchanged block markup also stops silently, while Edit Block Markup for templates says <code>{title} is unchanged, so nothing was sent.</code> Inconsistent.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="move-a-widget">Move a Widget…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Move a Widget…”.</li> <li>What opens: the same widget choice (title “Move a Widget”), then list dialog “Move {widget}”, list “To:” (access key T) of the other areas; help “The widget area it moves to; it goes at the end there.”</li> <li>Preview or confirmation: none. Choosing the area and pressing OK moves it.</li> <li>Announced after: <code>Moved {widget} from {area} to {target}.</code> No other area: <code>{site} has no other widget area to move it to.</code></li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z; spoken <code>{widget} is back in {area}.</code></li> <li>Without permission: <code>edit_theme_options</code>.</li> <li>Keyless: “Rare rearranging of widget areas (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The only widget write with no preview. Moving a widget changes every page that shows either area, the same reach Add’s preview warns about.</li> <li>The undo puts it back at the end of the original area, not in its old position.</li> <li>Moving within an area (reordering) is not offered.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="remove-a-widget">Remove a Widget…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Remove a Widget…”.</li> <li>What opens: the widget choice (title “Remove a Widget”), then a Yes/No message box titled “Remove a Widget” with buttons “Remove” and “Cancel”, Cancel the default.</li> <li>Confirmation wording: <code>Take {widget} out of {area} on {site}? WordPress keeps it in Inactive Widgets, and Ctrl+Z puts it back.</code></li> <li>Announced after: <code>Removed {widget} from {area}; it is in Inactive Widgets.</code></li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z moves it back; spoken <code>{widget} is back in {area}.</code></li> <li>Without permission: <code>edit_theme_options</code>.</li> <li>Keyless: “Rare, and it takes a widget off every page (rules 4 and 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Good: honest about where it goes and how to undo it.</li> <li>The confirmation is a message box, while Add and Change use the preview window; a listener meets two confirmation styles for one family of commands.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="preview-widgets">Preview Widgets</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Preview Widgets”.</li> <li>What opens: after “rendering the widgets in {area}”, a report window titled “Widgets in {area}”: a read-only multi-line box “Report:” (access key R), then “Copy All” and “Close”. One line per widget, <code>{widget}: {what a visitor sees}</code> or <code>{widget}: (shows nothing)</code>; or <code>{area} has no widgets.</code></li> <li>F1: “Each widget in this area, then what a visitor sees of it.”</li> <li>Keyless: “Read Widgets does it now and then, from the context menu (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The keyless reason calls it “Read Widgets”; the menu calls it “Preview Widgets”. The written reason names a command that does not exist.</li> <li>This is the only way to hear the widgets at all; it would serve better as the area’s default (Enter) than Read in Full.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-widget-area">Properties (widget area)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+Enter, the last context-menu row, or Item > Properties.</li> <li>What opens: no sheet is written for widget areas, so QUILL builds one from the <code>wp/v2/sidebars</code> route’s schema: page General and, for multi-line fields, page More. Field labels are the schema’s own title or description, cut to 60 characters; fields the schema marks read-only are read only.</li> <li>Code: workbench_sheets.py (schema_sheet, _schema_field), wordpress_workbench_sheets.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Labels are WordPress’s developer descriptions (for example the route’s description of before_widget HTML), not words a site owner uses. Not verified on a live site which fields end up editable.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="wp-admin-for-this-place-widgets-and-sidebars">wp-admin for This Place (Widgets and Sidebars)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: the tree’s context menu on the node (Applications key on the tree), under More, “wp-admin for This Place…”.</li> <li>What it does: the node has one nearest screen, Appearance > Widgets, so it opens without a choice: <code>Sidebars and their widgets are in Widgets and Sidebars. Opening Appearance > Widgets on {site} in your browser.</code></li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>It tells you the Workbench does all of it, then opens wp-admin anyway. When the map says <code>workbench</code>, the sentence could ask first or explain what wp-admin adds (drag-and-drop, the Customizer’s live preview).</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h2 id="appearance-and-the-site-editor">Appearance and the Site Editor</h2> <p>Appearance is a folder node under each site holding Themes, Global Styles, Templates, Template Parts, Additional CSS, Patterns, Fonts and Widgets and Sidebars (Widgets has its own section above; theme row actions are in “Plugins and themes” below). What REST allows is done in place: a block theme’s palette colours and font sizes, its templates and template parts as block markup, and its Additional CSS, each previewed line by line and undone with Ctrl+Z. Themes are read only (WordPress’s REST API cannot switch, install or update them), patterns and fonts are lists with almost nothing to do, and style variations and Previous Looks are not in the Workbench at all: they stay in the editor’s Tools > WordPress menu.</p> <h3 id="the-appearance-folder-and-its-go-key">The Appearance folder and its Go key</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+7, Go > Appearance (<code>Appearanc&e</code>, help “Themes, templates, patterns.”). It lands on Appearance > Themes, not on the Appearance folder (GO_NODES maps <code>go_appearance</code> to <code>themes</code>).</li> <li>What the list shows: the folder has no loader, so its list is its children; the node’s text adds “ Open it with Right Arrow to see what it holds.“</li> <li>F1: “Themes, templates, patterns, fonts and widgets.”</li> <li>Code: workbench_window_menu.py (GO_NODES), workbench_tree.py, workbench_node_help.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>F1 for Appearance does not mention Global Styles, Template Parts or Additional CSS, which are three of its eight children.</li> <li>Go > Appearance landing on Themes means the key never shows you the folder; the help “Themes, templates, patterns.” suggests otherwise. For an Editor (who cannot list themes) Ctrl+Shift+7 lands on a node that only says they lack the role.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-themes-node">The Themes node</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+7; Appearance > Themes; the editor’s Tools > WordPress > Manage > Themes… door.</li> <li>What the list shows: columns theme, version, status (<code>active</code> or <code>inactive</code>). Detail: <code>{name}, version {version}, {status}. REST only reads themes: Activate and Open in Dashboard go to wp-admin.</code> After Check for Updates on WordPress.org the version cell can read <code>{installed}, update waiting: {latest}</code> (see “Plugins and themes”).</li> <li>F1 on the node: “The installed themes. WordPress’s REST API only reads them, so Open in Dashboard activates, installs and updates in wp-admin; Find a Theme on WordPress.org searches.”</li> <li>F1 on a row: “A theme. REST only reads themes: Activate and Open in Dashboard go to Appearance > Themes in wp-admin. Check for Updates on WordPress.org says whether one waits.”</li> <li>Context menu: Read in Full (default), Activate…, Find a Theme on WordPress.org…, Open in Dashboard, Check for Updates on WordPress.org…, Show Only and Hide, Properties….</li> <li>Without permission: the node needs <code>switch_themes</code>; for anyone below Administrator the node shows <code>Changing the theme needs the Administrator role or higher. You are an Editor on {site}.</code> instead of a list.</li> <li>Code: workbench_load.py (load_themes), workbench_actions.py, workbench_people.py (_NODE_NEEDS).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The refusal says “Changing the theme” when the person only tried to look. An Editor cannot even read which theme is active from the Workbench, although that is harmless information (At a Glance does name the theme).</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-theme">Properties (theme)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+Enter, last context-menu row, Item > Properties.</li> <li>What opens: sheet <code>{theme} Properties - {site}</code>, page General, every field read only: Name, Version, Status, Author, Description (multi-line), Parent theme. Each field is a written read-only field, so its F1 is <code>{label}, as the site says it.</code> (themes are also in the sheet’s no-edit set).</li> <li>Code: workbench_sheets.py, wordpress_workbench_batch_sheets.py (_NO_EDIT).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>No “Requires WordPress”, “Requires PHP”, “Tested up to”, screenshot description or tags, all of which <code>wp/v2/themes</code> sends; a person choosing between themes has only the description.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-global-styles-node">The Global Styles node</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Appearance > Global Styles.</li> <li>What the list shows: columns setting, kind, value, contrast. One row per palette colour and font size of the active block theme, merged with the site’s own overrides. Kind is <code>Colour</code> or <code>Font size</code>. For a colour the detail and the contrast cell are a sentence like <code>{name}, {colour}, {ratio} on the page background, {passes or fails}</code> (exact wording from site_styles.contrast_sentence); for a font size, <code>{name}, font size {value}.</code></li> <li>Empty cases: a classic theme gives one fact row, <code>Global Styles: The active theme does not expose WordPress global styles.</code>; a block theme without a palette or sizes, <code>{theme} exposes no palette or font sizes.</code>; and when the account cannot read the overrides, an extra row <code>User styles: This account cannot read the site's own style overrides; these rows show the active theme's defaults.</code></li> <li>F1 on the node: “The site’s global styles: colours, type and spacing.”</li> <li>F1 on a row: “A palette colour or font size in the active block theme. Change… previews the new value.”</li> <li>Context menu: Read in Full (default), Change…, Show Only and Hide, Properties….</li> <li>Code: workbench_load_structure.py (load_global_styles), site_styles.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>F1 promises spacing; the node lists colours and font sizes only. Font families, spacing, element styles (links, headings, buttons) and block styles are not listed.</li> <li>The contrast column is good and rare. It measures against the page background and text colour only; a colour used as a button background against white text is not judged.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="change-global-style">Change… (global style)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Change…” on a Global Styles row.</li> <li>What opens: dialog “Change {name}”, text box “New value:” (access key N) holding the current value. Help: for a colour “Type a colour such as #1a4d2e.”; for a font size “Type a font size with its unit, such as 1.125rem or 18px.” Then a Yes/No message box titled “Change {name}”, buttons “Change {name}” and “Cancel”, with Cancel the default.</li> <li>Confirmation wording: <code>Change {name} on {site} from {old} to {new}? Visitors see this change immediately. Undo WordPress Change can put the previous style back.</code> For a colour, a contrast sentence for the new value is added.</li> <li>Announced after: <code>{name} changed on {site}. Undo WordPress Change can restore its previous value.</code></li> <li>Failures announced: <ul> <li>Unreadable colour: <code>QUILL cannot read {value} as a colour. Type it as a hex code such as #1a4d2e, or as rgb(26, 77, 46).</code></li> <li>Unreadable size: <code>QUILL cannot read {value} as a font size. Type a number with its unit, such as 1.125rem or 18px.</code></li> <li>Same value: <code>{name} on {site} already has that value.</code></li> <li>Theme changed: <code>The active theme on {site} changed. Refresh this list first.</code></li> <li>Value changed elsewhere: <code>{name} on {site} changed since this list loaded. Refresh and try again.</code></li> <li>No record: <code>The active theme on {site} has no editable style record.</code>, or, before anything opens, <code>This theme has no site-specific style record to change.</code></li> <li>Not verified: <code>The site did not verify the new value for {name}.</code></li> </ul></li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z; spoken <code>Restored {name} on {site}.</code>, or <code>Not undone: {name} on {site} changed again on the site.</code> QUILL also keeps the previous styles in a local backup file before saving (site_styles.keep_previous).</li> <li>Cancel: nothing sent.</li> <li>Without permission: needs <code>edit_theme_options</code>, tier Write; refusal <code>Changing menus and the theme's options needs the Administrator role or higher. You are ...</code></li> <li>Keyless: no written reason found in the KEYLESS tables I read.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_bold_styles.py, site_styles.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>F1 says “Change… previews the new value”, but this is a plain message box, not the preview window every other Site Editor write uses, and Cancel is its default button. Inconsistent with Edit Additional CSS and Edit Block Markup.</li> <li>The module’s own comment says “Enter on a Global Styles row changes that colour or font size”; Enter actually reads (Read in Full is the default).</li> <li>Colours are typed as hex or rgb. Named colours, and choosing from the theme’s own palette, are not offered. A listener has no way to hear a colour except as a code plus a nearest-colour name.</li> <li>The confirmation repeats the old and new values and then a contrast sentence that repeats the new value again; long for a message box.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-global-style">Properties (global style)</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: sheet <code>{name} Properties - {site}</code>, page General: Name, Kind, Value, Contrast, all read only (each F1 <code>{label}, as the site says it.</code>; global styles are also in the no-edit set). Changing goes through Change….</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Alt+Enter cannot change the value although Properties is where values change in most lists, and nothing on the sheet points to Change….</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-templates-and-template-parts-nodes">The Templates and Template Parts nodes</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach them: Appearance > Templates; Appearance > Template Parts.</li> <li>What the list shows: columns title, source, slug. Source is <code>customized</code> (the site’s own copy) or <code>from the theme</code>. Detail: <code>{title}, {source}.</code></li> <li>F1 on Templates: “The block theme’s templates. Edit Block Markup, Earlier Versions, Reset to the Theme’s Version and New Template are on each row’s context menu.”</li> <li>F1 on Template Parts: “The block theme’s template parts, such as the header and footer, edited like templates.”</li> <li>F1 on a row: “A template or template part. Edit Block Markup changes it after a preview; Reset to the Theme’s Version takes a customized one back to the theme’s.”</li> <li>Context menu: Read in Full (default), Edit Block Markup…, Earlier Versions…, Reset to the Theme’s Version…, New Template…, Open in the Site Editor, Show Only and Hide, Properties….</li> <li>Code: workbench_load_structure.py (load_templates), workbench_dashboard_places.py (TEMPLATE_ROWS), workbench_site_editor.py, wordpress_workbench_site_editor.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>New Template is only on a row’s context menu. On an empty Templates list (a classic theme, or a fresh one) there is no row, so there is no way to make the first template; Ctrl+N (New Here) on Templates says <code>New Here makes posts, pages, custom items, categories, tags, terms, menus, people and application passwords. Choose one of those nodes first.</code></li> <li>A template part’s area (header, footer, general) is not a column, though it is what tells parts apart.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="edit-block-markup">Edit Block Markup…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Edit Block Markup…” on a template or template part.</li> <li>What opens: QUILL reads it (“reading {title}”), then dialog <code>{Template or Template part}: {title}</code>, multi-line text box “Block markup:” (access key B) with the markup, OK and Cancel. Help: “The block markup, as WordPress stores it: each block is an HTML comment that opens and closes it. Change the text, then OK reads the change back before anything is sent.” Then a preview titled <code>Save {Template}: {title}</code>, button “Save”.</li> <li>Preview wording: summary <code>Save the {template or template part} {title} on {site}?</code>, plus, for one from the theme, <code> It comes from the theme, so the site keeps its own copy; Reset to the Theme's Version goes back.</code> Lines: the line-by-line change list (<code>Line {n} removed: ...</code>, <code>Line {n} added: ...</code>, <code>And {n} more line changes.</code>).</li> <li>Announced after: <code>Saved the {noun} {title} on {site}.</code> Unchanged: <code>{title} is unchanged, so nothing was sent.</code></li> <li>Failures: <code>{title} was changed on the site after QUILL read it. Nothing was sent; read it again and make the change there.</code>; <code>The site kept a different version of {title}.</code>; <code>The site did not send the template.</code>; not a template row: <code>Choose a template or template part first.</code></li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z; a customized one gets its old text back, a theme one is reset to the theme; spoken <code>{title} is back as it was on {site}.</code></li> <li>Without permission: <code>edit_theme_options</code> (Administrator).</li> <li>Keyless: “Templates change a few times a site’s life (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Editing a whole template as raw block comments in a plain text box is honest and complete, but heavy: a header part is often hundreds of lines of nested comments. The editor’s own block tools are not offered here (no “Open in Editor” for a template).</li> <li>The conflict message says “read it again and make the change there” but does not offer to reload; your edit is lost.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="earlier-versions-template">Earlier Versions… (template)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Earlier Versions…” on a template row.</li> <li>What opens: list dialog “Earlier Versions of {title}”, list “Versions:” (access key V) with rows <code>Saved {when}</code>; help “Each earlier version the site kept, newest first. OK previews it.” Then preview “Restore {title}”, button “Restore”.</li> <li>Preview wording: summary <code>Put back the version of {title} saved {when}?</code>; lines: the change list from now to then.</li> <li>Announced after: <code>Restored {title} to its version of {when}.</code> None: <code>{title} has no earlier versions on the site.</code></li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z; spoken <code>{title} is back as it was.</code></li> <li>Without permission: <code>edit_theme_options</code>.</li> <li>Keyless: “Looked for after a mistake, rarely (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>A template still from the theme always says it has no earlier versions, without saying why (only customized copies have revisions).</li> <li>Rows are dates only; no author, no size of change.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="reset-to-the-themes-version">Reset to the Theme’s Version…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Reset to the Theme’s Version…”.</li> <li>What opens: QUILL reads the template and the theme’s own version. Then a preview titled “Reset {title}” (button “Reset”) or, for a template the theme never had, “Delete {title}” (button “Delete”).</li> <li>Preview wording, reset: summary <code>Reset {title} to the theme's version on {site}?</code>; lines: the change list, or <code>The theme's version could not be read for this preview.</code></li> <li>Preview wording, delete: summary <code>The theme has no {title}, so resetting deletes this {noun} from {site}. Pages that use it fall back to the theme's own.</code>; line <code>{n} lines of block markup are removed.</code></li> <li>Announced after: <code>{title} on {site} is the theme's version again.</code> or <code>Deleted the {noun} {title} from {site}.</code> Already the theme’s: <code>{title} already is the theme's version; nothing to reset.</code></li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z puts the site’s copy back; spoken <code>{title} is the site's own copy again.</code></li> <li>Without permission: <code>edit_theme_options</code>, tier Destructive.</li> <li>Keyless: “Removes the site’s copy: never a bare key (rules 4 and 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The row’s help says “Confirmed by name”, but the confirmation is the ordinary preview with a Reset or Delete button; nothing is typed.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="new-template">New Template…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “New Template…” on any template or template part row.</li> <li>What opens: dialog “New Template” (or “New Template Part” from a part’s row), text box “Name (slug):” (access key N). Help: “Lowercase letters, numbers and hyphens: single-post-news for one post, page-about for one page, category-news for a category. It starts from the template WordPress would use for that name today.” Then a preview titled “New Template” or “New Template part”, button “Make It”.</li> <li>Preview wording: summary <code>Make the {noun} {slug} on {site}? It starts as a copy of {base}.</code> or <code>... It starts empty.</code>; lines: the markup it starts with, as added lines.</li> <li>Announced after: <code>Made the {noun} {slug} on {site}.</code> Bad slug: <code>{slug} cannot be a template's name: use lowercase letters, numbers and hyphens.</code></li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z deletes it; spoken <code>Deleted the {noun} {slug} again.</code></li> <li>Without permission: <code>edit_theme_options</code>.</li> <li>Keyless: “A new template is made a few times a site’s life (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>For a template part the help still says it starts from the template WordPress would use, but the code never looks one up for a part: it always starts empty.</li> <li>A part is made with no area (header, footer), so it lands in General; not offered.</li> <li>The preview title capitalises only the first word: “New Template part”.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="open-in-the-site-editor">Open in the Site Editor</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Open in the Site Editor”.</li> <li>What it does: opens <code>wp-admin/site-editor.php?postType=...&postId=...</code> in the browser and says <code>Opened {title} in the Site Editor on {site}, in your browser.</code> No address: <code>{site} has no address QUILL knows.</code></li> <li>Keyless: “Leaves QUILL for the browser, now and then (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Fine as a hand-off. The Site Editor is one of the least accessible screens in wp-admin, so its help could say what it is for (“to lay it out by sight”) as the row help does.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-template">Properties (template)</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: a sheet built from the template route’s schema, labels from the schema’s descriptions cut to 60 characters, read-only fields as the schema marks them.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Field labels are developer descriptions (for example the schema’s sentence describing the slug), and a 60-character cut can end mid-word.</li> <li>Not verified whether the content field is editable here; if it is, it is a second route to change a template that skips Edit Block Markup’s line-by-line preview.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-additional-css-node">The Additional CSS node</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Appearance > Additional CSS.</li> <li>What the list shows: one row. Block theme: columns screen, reach; screen <code>Additional CSS</code>, reach <code>{n} lines of Additional CSS</code> or <code>No Additional CSS yet</code>; detail <code>{said} in {theme}'s site styles. It begins: {first line} Edit Additional CSS changes it; each save is kept in Previous Looks.</code> Classic theme: a wp-admin row, reach <code>Only in wp-admin</code>, detail <code>{theme} is a classic theme, which keeps its Additional CSS in the Customizer; no REST route reaches it. Enter opens Appearance > Customize > Additional CSS in your browser.</code></li> <li>F1 on the node: “The site’s Additional CSS. In a block theme Edit Additional CSS changes it after a preview, and each save is kept in Previous Looks; a classic theme’s opens the Customizer.”</li> <li>F1 on the block row: “The site’s Additional CSS. Edit Additional CSS changes it, after a preview.”</li> <li>Context menu, block theme: Read in Full (default), Edit Additional CSS…, Properties…. Classic theme (it is a wp-admin Screens row): Open in Dashboard (default), Go to Its Place in the Workbench, Read in Full, Properties….</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The column header “reach” holds a line count on a block theme. A listener hears “reach, 12 lines of Additional CSS”.</li> <li>On a classic theme, Enter announces only <code> Opening Appearance > Customize > Additional CSS on {site} in your browser.</code>: the row’s raw data has no <code>why</code>, so the reason sentence before “Opening” is empty.</li> <li>On a classic theme, Go to Its Place in the Workbench selects Additional CSS, the node you are already in.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="edit-additional-css">Edit Additional CSS…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Edit Additional CSS…” on the block theme’s row.</li> <li>What opens: dialog <code>Additional CSS: {site}</code>, multi-line text box “CSS:” (access key C). Help: “The site’s Additional CSS. It applies to every page of the site; OK reads the change back before anything is sent.” Then preview “Save Additional CSS”, button “Save”.</li> <li>Preview wording: summary <code>Save the Additional CSS of {site}? CSS changes how every page of the site looks. WordPress keeps the version it replaces in Previous Looks.</code>; lines: the change list.</li> <li>Announced after: <code>Saved the Additional CSS of {site}.</code> Unchanged: <code>The Additional CSS is unchanged, so nothing was sent.</code> Checked before the preview: <code>Nothing was sent. WordPress refuses markup such as <style> in CSS; take the tags out.</code>, <code>Nothing was sent. A closing brace comes before its opening one.</code>, <code>Nothing was sent. {n} opening brace(s) never closed.</code> Conflict: <code>The site's Additional CSS was changed after QUILL read it. Nothing was sent; read it again and make the change there.</code> Wrong row: <code>Choose the Additional CSS row of a block theme first.</code></li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z; spoken <code>The Additional CSS of {site} is back as it was.</code></li> <li>Without permission: <code>edit_theme_options</code>. Note WordPress itself needs <code>edit_css</code> for this route.</li> <li>Keyless: “Additional CSS changes a few times a site’s life (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Good brace and markup checks before the preview, said in one sentence.</li> <li>“Kept in Previous Looks” points at a feature the Workbench does not have; Previous Looks is only in the editor (Tools > WordPress > Manage > Previous Looks…).</li> <li>A failed check throws the typed text away; the person has to retype the whole edit.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-patterns-node">The Patterns node</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Appearance > Patterns; the editor’s Tools > WordPress > Manage > Synced Patterns… door.</li> <li>What the list shows: columns title, status, modified, from <code>wp/v2/blocks</code>. Detail: <code>{title}, a synced pattern, {status}.</code></li> <li>F1 on the node: “Synced patterns: blocks you reuse, changed in one place.” F1 on a row: “A synced pattern.”</li> <li>Context menu: Read in Full (default), Copy to Site… (Ctrl+Shift+Y), Show Only and Hide, Properties….</li> <li>Properties: built from the blocks route’s schema.</li> <li>Code: workbench_load_structure.py (load_patterns), workbench_actions.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li><code>wp/v2/blocks</code> returns unsynced patterns too, but every row says “a synced pattern” (not verified on a live site whether unsynced ones are filtered).</li> <li>No way to edit a pattern’s blocks, rename it, make one or delete one from its row. The editor’s old Synced Patterns window had Edit (a “Blocks:” text box and a “Save Synced Pattern” confirmation), but its door now opens this node instead. Regression unless the schema sheet covers it (not verified).</li> <li>Pattern categories: not found.</li> <li>Row help “A synced pattern.” is the shortest F1 in the Workbench and says nothing about what can be done.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-fonts-node">The Fonts node</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Appearance > Fonts; the editor’s Tools > WordPress > Manage > Fonts… door.</li> <li>What the list shows: columns family, slug, from <code>wp/v2/font-families</code>. Detail: <code>{name}, a font family installed on the site.</code></li> <li>F1 on the node: “The fonts the site has installed.” F1 on a row: “An installed font.”</li> <li>Context menu: Read in Full (default), Show Only and Hide, Properties….</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Install a font and delete a font: not found in the Workbench. The editor’s old Fonts window (wordpress_design_commands.py) offers both (<code>Install {name}</code>, <code>Delete {name}</code>), but its door now opens this read-only list. Regression.</li> <li>No font faces (weights and styles) are listed; the Properties sheet shows the raw family settings.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="style-variations-and-previous-looks">Style variations and Previous Looks</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach them: not in the Workbench. In the editor, Tools > WordPress > Design > Style Variations… and Site Styles…, and Tools > WordPress > Manage > Previous Looks….</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Three Workbench help strings say “kept in Previous Looks”, but there is no Previous Looks node or command in the Workbench. Either add the node (Appearance > Previous Looks, listing global-styles revisions) or say “in the editor’s Tools > WordPress > Manage > Previous Looks”.</li> <li>Switching a block theme’s style variation, one of the most common appearance jobs, has no place in the Appearance branch.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="wp-admin-for-this-place-appearance-nodes">wp-admin for This Place (Appearance nodes)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: tree context menu on the node, under More, “wp-admin for This Place…”.</li> <li>What it offers, by node (from workbench_admin_map.py): <ul> <li>Themes: a list dialog “wp-admin for This Place”, list “Screens:” (access key S), rows <code>{screen}: {reach}</code>: “Appearance > Themes: Part in the Workbench, the rest in wp-admin”, “Appearance > Themes > Add New Theme: Only in wp-admin”, “Appearance > Customize: Part in the Workbench, the rest in wp-admin”, “Tools > Theme File Editor: Only in wp-admin”.</li> <li>Templates: Appearance > Editor (opens directly).</li> <li>Global Styles: Appearance > Editor > Styles.</li> <li>Additional CSS: Appearance > Customize > Additional CSS.</li> <li>Template Parts, Patterns, Fonts and the Appearance folder: none; spoken <code>No wp-admin screen is nearer to this place than the Workbench itself. Overview > wp-admin Screens lists them all.</code></li> </ul></li> <li>Announced: <code>{why} Opening {screen} on {site} in your browser.</code></li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Template Parts finds nothing, while Templates finds the Site Editor: the lookup falls back from <code>template_parts</code> to <code>template</code>, which is not a node key.</li> <li>Customize is mapped to Themes, not to the Appearance folder, so on a classic theme the Customizer is two levels away from where people expect it.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h2 id="plugins-and-themes">Plugins and themes</h2> <p>Plugins are a real list you can act on: WordPress’s REST API activates, deactivates, installs and deletes plugins, so the Workbench does those (install from WordPress.org’s directory, after consent and a preview). Updating a plugin, automatic updates, and every theme change except reading are not on the REST API, so those rows hand you to wp-admin, saying why first. New since wp_missing.md: Check for Updates on WordPress.org compares each plugin, theme and WordPress itself with WordPress.org’s current version and marks the row “update waiting”, “up to date” or “unknown”. The wp-admin map now knows the plugins’ own screens. Both are being worked on today (see “What exists and what is in progress” at the end of this section).</p> <h3 id="the-plugins-node">The Plugins node</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+6 (Go > Plugins, <code>P&lugins</code>, help “This site’s plugins.”); Sites > site > Plugins; the editor’s Tools > WordPress > Manage > Plugins… door.</li> <li>What the list shows: columns plugin, version, status, author, sorted by name. Status is <code>active</code>, <code>inactive</code> or <code>network active</code>. Detail: <code>{name}, version {version}, {active or inactive}, by {author}. Updates and automatic updates are in wp-admin: Open in Dashboard.</code> After Check on WordPress.org, <code> WordPress.org: {facts}</code> is added; after Check for Updates, the version cell may read <code>{installed}, update waiting: {latest}</code> and the detail gains a sentence (below).</li> <li>Group By: Plugins is one of the nodes View > Group By (Ctrl+Alt+G) can split into child nodes by a column, for example by status.</li> <li>F1 on the node: “The installed plugins, active and not.”</li> <li>F1 on a row: “A plugin. Activate or Deactivate is on the context menu, and Check for Updates on WordPress.org says whether one waits; updates run in wp-admin, through Open in Dashboard.”</li> <li>Context menu: Read in Full (default), Activate or Deactivate, Check on WordPress.org…, Find a Plugin on WordPress.org…, Open in Dashboard, Check for Updates on WordPress.org…, Show Only and Hide, Properties…. (Six rows plus Properties, so no More submenu.)</li> <li>Without permission: the node needs <code>activate_plugins</code>; anyone below Administrator sees <code>Turning plugins on and off needs the Administrator role or higher. You are an Editor on {site}.</code> instead of the list.</li> <li>Code: workbench_load.py (load_plugins), workbench_actions.py, workbench_admin_actions.py (PLUGIN_ROWS), workbench_gap_actions.py (UPDATE_CHECK).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Every row’s detail ends with “Updates and automatic updates are in wp-admin: Open in Dashboard.” The same sentence on every one of forty plugins is exactly the chattiness GATE-13 warns about; say it once in the node’s F1 or the status bar.</li> <li>Delete a plugin: not found as a row action, although <code>wp/v2/plugins</code> can delete (the map’s Plugins row says “Activate, deactivate, install and delete are in Plugins”).</li> <li>The refusal for an Editor talks about turning plugins on and off when they only opened the list.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="read-in-full-plugin-default">Read in Full (plugin, default)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter, or the first context-menu row.</li> <li>What it does: focus to the details pane with the detail sentence above.</li> </ul> <h3 id="activate-or-deactivate">Activate or Deactivate</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Activate or Deactivate”. No key.</li> <li>What opens: a Yes/No message box titled “Activate” or “Deactivate”, buttons “Activate” or “Deactivate” and “Cancel”; Cancel is the default.</li> <li>Confirmation wording: <code>Activate {plugin} on {site}?</code> or <code>Deactivate {plugin} on {site}?</code>. For QUILL Accessible Forms (or its older name, BITS Accessible Forms), <code>Deactivate</code> adds <code>QUILL relies on this plugin for QUILL's Forms commands and Held as Spam for form entries. Turning it off stops that.</code> (or the older-name wording).</li> <li>Refused before asking: deactivating the Application Passwords plugin: <code>QUILL signs in to {site} through this plugin, so turning it off here would lock the Workbench out before it could turn it back on. Turn it off in wp-admin's Plugins screen if you mean to.</code></li> <li>Announced after: <code>{plugin} is now active on {site}.</code> or <code>... inactive on {site}.</code> The site is then read again in the background (types, fields and permissions), and the tree is rebuilt.</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z sets the old status back; spoken <code>{plugin} put back.</code></li> <li>Without permission: needs <code>activate_plugins</code>, tier Write; refusal <code>Turning plugins on and off needs the Administrator role or higher. You are ...</code>; under Read Only or Organize access, the access sentence.</li> <li>Keyless: no written reason found in the KEYLESS tables I read.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (do_toggle_plugin), workbench_permissions.py (deactivate_refusal, deactivate_warning).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The label “Activate or Deactivate” does not say which it will do for this row. A listener must read the status column first or wait for the message box. Label it from the row’s status (“Deactivate…” on an active plugin), and add the ellipsis since it asks.</li> <li>The plugin’s Properties sheet has an editable Status choice (active, inactive). Saving it goes through the generic sheet save, which has no preview and never calls <code>deactivate_refusal</code> or <code>deactivate_warning</code>. So Alt+Enter, Status, inactive, OK can turn off the Application Passwords plugin that the context menu refuses to touch, locking the Workbench out. Read from wordpress_workbench_sheets.py and workbench_sheets.py; not tried live. This is the most serious finding in this section.</li> <li>“{plugin} put back.” does not say which state it went back to.</li> <li>Network-active plugins on a multisite: deactivating sends <code>inactive</code> from a single site’s route; not verified what WordPress does.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="check-on-wordpressorg">Check on WordPress.org…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Check on WordPress.org…” on a plugin row.</li> <li>What opens: the first time in this window, a Yes/No message box titled “Ask WordPress.org”, buttons “Ask” and “Cancel” (Cancel default): <code>Ask WordPress.org's plugin directory? QUILL sends the words you type, or the short names (slugs) of the plugins in question, to api.wordpress.org. No site address, account or password is sent.</code> Then a report window titled <code>Plugins on {site}, from WordPress.org</code>, one line per plugin, <code>{name}: {facts}</code>, then a blank line and two limits: <code>WordPress's REST API cannot update a plugin or turn its automatic updates on or off.</code> and the no-rollback paragraph (<code>There is no rollback: WordPress's REST API cannot put a plugin back to an earlier version. ...</code>).</li> <li>The facts: <code>{name}, version {v}, by {author}, rated {x} of 5 by {n} people, {n}+ active installs, last updated {date}, tested up to WordPress {v}, needs WordPress {v} and PHP {v}.</code> plus a warning when it applies: <code>WordPress.org closed {name} on {date} ({reason}): it gets no updates there and cannot be installed from it.</code> or <code>{name} has not been updated on WordPress.org since {date}, over two years: it may be abandoned.</code> Not listed: <code>{name} is not in the WordPress.org directory (a paid, custom or retired plugin), so QUILL cannot say whether it is kept up to date.</code></li> <li>Announced: declining the consent says <code>Nothing was sent to WordPress.org.</code> The facts are then added to every plugin row’s detail for the rest of this window.</li> <li>F1 of the report: “Each plugin on this site as WordPress.org’s directory describes it, with a warning for any it has closed or not seen updated in two years.”</li> <li>Without permission: no requirement (tier Read); the Plugins node itself needs <code>activate_plugins</code>.</li> <li>Keyless: “Checked now and then, after you agree to ask (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_admin_commands.py (do_plugin_directory), dotorg.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The row command checks every plugin on the site, not the row it was chosen on; the label reads as if it is about this plugin.</li> <li>Consent is asked once per window per kind of question (plugins, themes, updates, languages): four separate “Ask WordPress.org” boxes for one source. Reasonable for privacy, but the person may agree four times in one session and is never told the agreement lasts only until the window closes.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="find-a-plugin-on-wordpressorg">Find a Plugin on WordPress.org…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Find a Plugin on WordPress.org…” on a plugin row.</li> <li>What opens: dialog “Find a Plugin on WordPress.org”, text box “Search for:” (access key S), help “Words in the plugin’s name or description. Only these words are sent.”; then (first time) the plugins consent box above; then list dialog “Plugins on WordPress.org”, list “Plugins:” (access key P), each row the plugin’s facts with its description; then a preview titled “Install {name}”, button “Install”.</li> <li>Preview wording: summary <code>Each site installs {name} ({slug}) from WordPress.org, inactive. A plugin not in the WordPress.org directory fails on that site and the rest go on. WordPress's REST API cannot update a plugin or turn its automatic updates on or off. There is no rollback: ...</code>; lines <code>{site}: installs {slug}, left inactive</code> and the plugin’s facts.</li> <li>Announced after: <code>{name} installed on 1 of 1 sites, inactive.</code> with <code> Not installed: {site}: {error}</code> on failure. None found: <code>WordPress.org has no plugin matching {words}.</code> Not on a site row: <code>Find a Plugin works on a site's Plugins list.</code></li> <li>Undo: none recorded.</li> <li>Without permission: needs <code>install_plugins</code>, tier Write; refusal <code>Doing this (install plugins) needs the Administrator role or higher. You are ...</code> (install_plugins has no plain-words entry in the refusal table).</li> <li>Keyless: “A new plugin is added a few times a year, previewed (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_admin_commands.py (do_find_plugin, _install_preview).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The consent question comes after you have typed the search, so declining wastes the typing; Check on WordPress.org asks first.</li> <li>The install is not in Change History. Ctrl+Z right after it undoes the change before it, silently. Either record it with an undo (delete the inactive plugin) or record it as “cannot be undone”.</li> <li>After installing, the plugin is inactive and the next step (Activate) is not offered; the person must find the new row and use Activate or Deactivate.</li> <li>“installed on 1 of 1 sites” is the multi-site sentence reused for one site.</li> <li>The refusal sentence reads “Doing this (install plugins)”, the only place a raw capability name is spoken.</li> <li>The command needs a plugin row to be selected; on a site with no plugins listed there is no row, so no way to find the first plugin.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="open-in-dashboard-plugin">Open in Dashboard (plugin)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Open in Dashboard” on a plugin row.</li> <li>What it does: says <code>WordPress's REST API cannot update a plugin or turn its automatic updates on or off. Opening Plugins on {site} in your browser.</code> and opens <code>wp-admin/plugins.php</code>. No address: <code>{site} has no address QUILL knows, so Plugins cannot be opened.</code></li> <li>Keyless: “Leaves QUILL for the browser, now and then (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_admin_commands.py (do_open_dashboard), workbench_no_rest.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The row help shared with themes says “for a theme, activating and installing”, on a plugin row.</li> <li>It opens the whole Plugins screen, not this plugin; the person has to find the plugin again in wp-admin’s table.</li> <li>After an update waiting is found, the detail says “Dashboard > Updates runs it”, but Open in Dashboard on a plugin row goes to Plugins, not Dashboard > Updates.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="check-for-updates-on-wordpressorg-update-waiting">Check for Updates on WordPress.org… (“update waiting”)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Check for Updates on WordPress.org…” on a plugin row, a theme row, and a Needs My Attention > Updates row. No key.</li> <li>What opens: the first time in this window, a Yes/No message box titled “Ask WordPress.org”, buttons “Ask” and “Cancel” (Cancel default): <code>Ask WordPress.org which versions are current? QUILL sends the short names (slugs) of this site's plugins and themes to api.wordpress.org, and asks for the current WordPress. No site address, installed version, account or password is sent.</code> Then it works in the background (“asking WordPress.org for current versions”); no further dialog.</li> <li>What changes: for this window, every plugin, theme and Updates row of that site is marked. The Update cell (Updates node) or the version cell (Plugins and Themes) reads one of: <ul> <li><code>Update waiting: version {latest}</code> (on Plugins and Themes the version cell becomes <code>{installed}, update waiting: {latest}</code>), detail <code>WordPress.org has version {latest}; this site has {installed}, so an update waits. Dashboard > Updates runs it.</code></li> <li><code>Up to date</code>, detail <code>WordPress.org has version {latest}, the version installed.</code></li> <li><code>Unknown: the site does not say its version</code>, detail <code>WordPress.org has version {latest}; the site does not say which it has.</code></li> <li><code>Unknown: not on WordPress.org</code>, detail <code>WordPress.org does not list it (a paid, custom or closed one), so whether an update waits is unknown.</code></li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: <code>{n} update(s) waiting on {site}: {names, up to six} and more.</code> or <code>Nothing on {site} has an update waiting on WordPress.org.</code>, then <code> {n} not on WordPress.org, so unknown.</code> when any are unknown. Wrong row: <code>Choose a plugin, a theme or a row under Needs My Attention > Updates first.</code></li> <li>Undo: nothing to undo (reads only).</li> <li>F1: the row’s help, “After you agree to ask WordPress.org, compare this site’s WordPress, plugins and themes with the versions WordPress.org has now, and mark each row update waiting, up to date or unknown. Only short names are sent. Paid plugins WordPress.org does not list say unknown.”</li> <li>Without permission: no requirement; tier Read.</li> <li>Keyless: “Asked now and then, after you agree to ask (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: workbench_update_check.py, wordpress_workbench_gaps.py (do_check_updates, with_update_facts), wordpress_workbench_window.py (rows marked on load).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>This is the cheapest real win wp_missing.md asked for, and it is here. But it checks one site per run. On Needs My Attention > Updates, which gathers every site, you must run it once per site (choosing a row of each), and the marks last only until the window closes. There is no “check every site” and no Watch that uses it.</li> <li>The Watch “Plugin updates available” (workbench_watches.py) still looks only for an update field the site itself sends, which core WordPress never sends, so the Watch stays silent even after this check finds updates.</li> <li>A plugin WordPress.org has closed is marked <code>Unknown: not on WordPress.org</code> (latest_plugins returns None for a closed plugin). A closed plugin is a security warning, not an unknown; Check on WordPress.org says “closed”, this check hides it.</li> <li>Each row’s detail keeps the earlier sentence too, so an Updates row can say “REST does not say whether an update waits.” and then “so an update waits.” in one detail.</li> <li>The command is on every plugin row, but it checks all of the site’s plugins and themes and WordPress; the label suggests it is about the row.</li> <li>Up to 100 theme lookups, one request a theme; plugins one request each. On a site with many plugins this is slow, with no progress said beyond the background task name.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-plugin">Properties (plugin)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+Enter, last context-menu row, Item > Properties.</li> <li>What opens: sheet <code>{plugin} Properties - {site}</code>, page General: Name, Version, Author, Description (multi-line), Status (a choice of <code>active</code> and <code>inactive</code>, needs <code>activate_plugins</code>), Needs WordPress, Home page (a link). All but Status read only.</li> <li>Announced after a save: <code>Saved 1 change to {plugin} on {site}.</code>; Ctrl+Z <code>Put {plugin} back as it was.</code></li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>See Activate or Deactivate: the Status choice skips the lockout refusal, the QUILL-relies-on warning and the confirmation.</li> <li>No “update waiting”, WordPress.org facts, “Needs PHP” or “Network only” on the sheet, though the row’s detail and <code>wp/v2/plugins</code> carry them.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="theme-rows-activate">Theme rows: Activate…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Activate…” on a theme row (Appearance > Themes).</li> <li>What it does: no dialog. Says <code>WordPress's REST API only reads themes: it cannot activate, update or delete one, or turn its automatic updates on. Opening Appearance > Themes on {site} in your browser.</code> and opens <code>wp-admin/themes.php?theme={stylesheet}</code>. Already active: <code>{theme} is already the theme on {site}.</code></li> <li>Without permission: needs <code>switch_themes</code>; the Themes node already refuses non-administrators.</li> <li>Keyless: no written reason found in the KEYLESS tables I read.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_admin_commands.py (do_activate_theme), workbench_no_rest.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The label has an ellipsis but opens no dialog; it opens the browser.</li> <li>The spoken reason starts with “it cannot activate” immediately after you asked to activate, which sounds like a failure. Lead with the hand-off: “Activating happens in wp-admin; opening Appearance > Themes …”.</li> <li>Nothing comes back: the Workbench does not notice the switch until a later read, when (up to five minutes later, on the next request) it announces <code>{site} changed since QUILL last looked: the theme is now {stylesheet and version}...</code> out of context.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="theme-rows-find-a-theme-on-wordpressorg">Theme rows: Find a Theme on WordPress.org…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Find a Theme on WordPress.org…”.</li> <li>What opens: dialog “Find a Theme on WordPress.org”, text box “Search for:” (access key S), prefilled with the selected theme’s name; help “Words in the theme’s name or description. Only these words are sent.” Then (first time) the consent box: <code>Search WordPress.org's theme directory? QUILL sends the words you type to api.wordpress.org, and nothing about your sites.</code> Then list dialog “Themes on WordPress.org”, list “Themes:” (access key T), help “Each theme in words. OK opens Add New for it on your site.” Each row <code>{name}, version {v}, by {author}, rated {x} of 5 by {n} people, needs WordPress {v} and PHP {v}. {description}</code>.</li> <li>What happens after OK: wp-admin’s Add New Theme screen opens, searching for the slug, saying <code>WordPress's REST API cannot install a theme. Opening Appearance > Themes > Add New on {site} in your browser.</code></li> <li>Announced: none found <code>WordPress.org has no theme matching {words}.</code></li> <li>Keyless: “Choosing a new theme happens a few times a year (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Prefilling the search with the theme you already have makes the first search find that theme. An empty box would be the better start.</li> <li>The search, the consent and the list all happen in QUILL, then the job is finished in wp-admin’s theme browser, which is visual and searches again. The hand-off is honest but it is a dead end for a screen-reader user who wanted QUILL to install it.</li> <li>No requirement is checked, although installing a theme needs <code>install_themes</code>.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="theme-rows-open-in-dashboard-and-check-for-updates">Theme rows: Open in Dashboard and Check for Updates</h3> <ul> <li>Open in Dashboard: says <code>WordPress's REST API only reads themes: it cannot activate, update or delete one, or turn its automatic updates on. Opening Appearance > Themes on {site} in your browser.</code> and opens <code>wp-admin/themes.php</code>.</li> <li>Check for Updates on WordPress.org…: as for plugins; theme rows get <code>{installed}, update waiting: {latest}</code> in their version cell.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Activate… and Open in Dashboard open the same screen with the same sentence (Activate adds <code>?theme=</code>); two rows, one job.</li> <li>Delete a theme, update a theme, theme auto-updates: wp-admin only, said in the row; no Workbench path.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="wp-admin-map-rows-for-plugins">wp-admin map rows for plugins</h3> <ul> <li>Where they show: Sites > site > Overview > wp-admin Screens lists every screen (core and, where the site’s REST index has the plugin’s namespace, the plugin’s screens), and “wp-admin for This Place…” on a node’s tree context menu (under More) offers the screens whose nearest node it is.</li> <li>The core Plugins rows (from workbench_admin_map.py): <ul> <li>“Plugins > Installed Plugins”, <code>wp-admin/plugins.php</code>, nearest Plugins, “Part in the Workbench, the rest in wp-admin”: <code>Activate, deactivate, install and delete are in Plugins; updates and automatic updates are not in REST.</code></li> <li>“Plugins > Add New Plugin”, <code>wp-admin/plugin-install.php</code>, nearest Plugins, “All of it in the Workbench”: <code>Find a Plugin on WordPress.org installs one, after a preview.</code></li> <li>“Tools > Plugin File Editor”, <code>wp-admin/plugin-editor.php</code>, nearest Plugins, “Only in wp-admin”: <code>No REST route edits plugin files, and many hosts turn this screen off.</code></li> </ul></li> <li>The plugin screens (from workbench_admin_map_plugins.py), each offered only when the site lists the plugin’s namespace: <ul> <li>Gravity Forms (<code>gf/v2</code>): Forms > Forms (nearest Forms, both), Forms > Entries (Responses, all in the Workbench), Forms > Settings (Forms, only in wp-admin).</li> <li>QUILL Accessible Forms (<code>qaf/v1</code>): Accessible Forms, <code>admin.php?page=qaf</code> (Forms, all in the Workbench). The source marks this slug unverified.</li> <li>Redirection (<code>redirection/v1</code>): Tools > Redirection (Redirects, both), Tools > Redirection > 404s (404 Log, all in the Workbench).</li> <li>WooCommerce (<code>wc/v3</code>): Products > All Products (Shop), WooCommerce > Orders (Shop Orders), Analytics > Overview (Shop Reports), WooCommerce > Settings (Store Settings), all “both”; WooCommerce > Status > Scheduled Actions and WooCommerce > Extensions (Shop, only in wp-admin).</li> <li>The Events Calendar (<code>tribe/events/v1</code>): Events, Venues, Organizers, Event Categories (all in the Workbench), Events > Settings and Events > Import (only in wp-admin).</li> <li>Yoast SEO (<code>yoast/v1</code>): Yoast SEO > Settings, nearest Settings, only in wp-admin.</li> <li>Paid Memberships Pro (<code>pmpro/v1</code>): Dashboard, Settings > Levels, Members, Orders, Discount Codes (both), Reports and Settings > Payment Gateway (only in wp-admin).</li> </ul></li> <li>A row’s detail: <code>{screen}, {path}. {reach}. {says} Nearest place in the Workbench: {place}. Enter opens it in your browser.</code></li> <li>Row actions and wording: see “The wp-admin Screens node” under Tools.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The WooCommerce Settings row now says Store Settings changes settings, which fixes the stale line wp_missing.md flagged.</li> <li>Only seven plugins are mapped. Every other installed plugin’s settings screen (a cache plugin, a security plugin, an SEO plugin other than Yoast) is not reachable through wp-admin for This Place on Plugins. A plugin row has no “Open Its Settings” action. Not found in code.</li> <li>“wp-admin for This Place” is on the tree’s node menu, under More, fourth of four. On the Plugins list you must move to the tree, open its context menu, open More, and find it. It is not on the plugin row’s own menu.</li> <li>“All of it in the Workbench” for Add New Plugin is generous: wp-admin’s screen also uploads a plugin zip, which the Workbench cannot.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="what-exists-and-what-is-in-progress">What exists and what is in progress</h3> <ul> <li>Exists in code now (read 2026-11-14 at the end of this pass): Check for Updates on WordPress.org on plugin, theme and Updates rows; marking rows <code>Update waiting: version {latest}</code>, <code>Up to date</code>, <code>Unknown: not on WordPress.org</code>, <code>Unknown: the site does not say its version</code>; the spoken summary; marks kept for the window. Plugin screens for Gravity Forms, QAF, Redirection, WooCommerce, The Events Calendar, Yoast SEO and Paid Memberships Pro in the wp-admin map.</li> <li>In progress (another worker was changing workbench_update_check.py, workbench_actions.py, workbench_admin_map_plugins.py and wordpress_workbench_gaps.py during this pass; no engineering-log entry for either yet): anything beyond the above, such as checking every site at once, a Watch that uses WordPress.org’s answer, an update column on the Plugins list, or more plugins in the map. Treat the descriptions above as a snapshot.</li> </ul> <h2 id="settings">Settings</h2> <p>Sites > site > Settings lists the settings WordPress’s REST API reads and writes (<code>wp/v2/settings</code>), each with a plain sentence of what it does on this site, using the site’s own values as the example: “Posts page: News, so visitors see your latest posts at …”. This is one of the best-explained lists in the Workbench. Changing a setting is done on its Properties sheet, with a preview and Ctrl+Z. The weak spot is that sheet: every setting, whatever its kind, is changed by typing its raw stored value into one text box.</p> <h3 id="the-settings-node">The Settings node</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+8 (Go > Settings, <code>Settin&gs</code>, help “This site’s settings.”); Sites > site > Settings; the editor’s Tools > WordPress > Site Settings… door.</li> <li>What the list shows: columns setting, value, in this order where the site sends them: Site title, Tagline, Site language, Time zone, Date format, Time format, First day of the week, Administrator email, Front page shows, Front page, Posts page, Posts per page, New posts take comments, New posts take pingbacks, Turn emoticons into pictures, Default post format, Site icon. The value is friendly: pages by title (or <code>page {n}</code>, or <code>none</code>), choices by their words (<code>Your latest posts</code>, <code>Yes</code>, <code>Monday</code>, <code>Standard</code>), the on/off setting as <code>yes</code> or <code>no</code>, the icon as <code>picture {n}</code> or <code>none</code>, empty text as <code>(empty)</code>.</li> <li>Detail: <code>{label}: {value}. {explanation}</code>, for example the date format’s <code>{format}: a date reads {example}.</code>, start of week <code>Calendars start their week on {day}.</code>, email <code>{address}: where WordPress sends notices meant for whoever runs the site. Changing it sends a confirmation there first.</code>, emoticons <code>Typed :-) becomes a picture, which a screen reader names by its alt text.</code> or <code>Typed :-) stays as text, the way a screen reader reads it best.</code></li> <li>F1 on the node: “The site’s settings the REST API can read and change.”</li> <li>F1 on a row: “A setting. Alt+Enter changes it.”</li> <li>Context menu: Read in Full (default), Language Pack Status…, Show Only and Hide, Properties….</li> <li>Without permission: the node needs <code>manage_options</code>; anyone below Administrator sees <code>Changing site settings needs the Administrator role or higher. You are an Editor on {site}.</code> instead of the list.</li> <li>Code: workbench_load.py (load_settings), site_admin.py (SETTINGS_FIELDS, CHOICES, settings_value_text), workbench_settings_help.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The explanations are excellent and should be the model for other lists.</li> <li>The refusal says “Changing” to someone who only opened the list. An Editor cannot even read the site’s time zone or date format here.</li> <li>Missing from the list because REST does not carry them (said only in the wp-admin map rows): who may register, the default role, the WordPress and site addresses, permalinks, search engine visibility, the privacy policy page, media sizes, discussion’s moderation settings.</li> <li>The Site icon reads <code>picture {number}</code>; the picture’s title is not looked up, unlike the front and posts pages.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="read-in-full-setting-default">Read in Full (setting, default)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter, or the first context-menu row.</li> <li>What it does: focus to the details pane with the explanation.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Row help says “Alt+Enter changes it”, which is right, but Enter is what people press to change a value in a list; a setting’s Enter could open the change directly.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-change-a-setting">Properties: Change a Setting</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+Enter on a setting row, the last context-menu row, or Item > Properties.</li> <li>What opens: sheet <code>{label} Properties - {site}</code>, page General: Setting (read only, the label) and Value (a text box, needs <code>manage_options</code>). Then, after OK, a preview titled “Change a Setting”, button “Change”.</li> <li>Preview wording: summary <code>Change {label} on {site}. Ctrl+Z puts it back.</code>; one line <code>{label} ({key}): {old} becomes {new}</code>, with <code>(empty)</code> for an empty value.</li> <li>Refused before the preview: a site address (<code>url</code>, <code>home</code>, <code>siteurl</code>): <code>The site address is not changed from the Workbench. QUILL signs in at the site's address, and a wrong address locks everyone out of wp-admin too. Change it in wp-admin's Settings > General, then reconnect the site.</code>; the default role set to administrator: <code>New users are never made Administrators by default: anyone who signs up would control the site. Choose Subscriber, or another role without manage_options.</code></li> <li>Announced after: <code>Saved 1 change to {label} on {site}.</code>, plus <code> The site did not keep: ...</code> when WordPress dropped it.</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z; spoken <code>Put {label} back as it was.</code></li> <li>Cancel: Cancel on the sheet or the preview sends nothing.</li> <li>Without permission: the Value field is read only, with the refusal as its F1 help.</li> <li>Code: workbench_sheets.py (the Setting sheet), wordpress_workbench_sheet_guards.py (setting_cleared), workbench_permissions.py (setting_refusal, setting_preview), workbench_edit.py (the save sends the typed text as the value).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The Value box holds the raw stored value, not the friendly one the list shows: <code>posts</code> or <code>page</code> for Front page shows, a page number for Front page and Posts page, <code>0</code> to <code>6</code> for First day of the week, <code>open</code> or <code>closed</code>, an empty string for the standard post format, and the raw on/off for emoticons. The list knows the words (CHOICES) but the sheet offers no choice list, no page picker and no check box. A listener has to know WordPress’s internal values to change most of these.</li> <li>The value is sent as typed text, without converting it to a number or a true/false (workbench_edit.py puts the string straight into the body). Whether WordPress accepts “6” for start_of_week or “false” for use_smilies was not checked live.</li> <li>The preview shows raw values too: “Front page (page_on_front): 12 becomes 34” tells a listener nothing about which pages those are.</li> <li>The site-address and default-role guards can never fire from this list, because neither setting is in SETTINGS_FIELDS. Harmless, but the protection is in code nobody reaches here.</li> <li>One setting per sheet: there is no batch sheet for settings, so changing title and tagline is two round trips.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="language-pack-status">Language Pack Status…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Language Pack Status…” on any setting row.</li> <li>What opens: the first time in this window, a Yes/No box titled “Ask WordPress.org”, buttons “Ask” and “Cancel” (Cancel default): <code>Ask WordPress.org which language packs it has? QUILL sends each WordPress version number your sites run to api.wordpress.org, and nothing else.</code> Then a report window titled “Language Pack Status”, one sentence per connected site (from dotorg.site_languages), then Copy All and Close. Report F1: “Each site’s language and whether WordPress.org has its pack. Installing a pack is done in Dashboard > Updates or Settings > General.”</li> <li>Announced: declining says <code>Nothing was sent to WordPress.org.</code></li> <li>Without permission: no requirement; it reads each site’s settings, so a site where you are not an Administrator may fail.</li> <li>Keyless: “Checked after a WordPress release, not daily (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_admin_commands.py (do_language_packs), dotorg.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>It is on one site’s setting rows but reports on every connected site. The label and placement suggest this site.</li> <li>Installing a missing pack is wp-admin only (<code>WordPress's REST API cannot install or update a language pack.</code>), but the report has no “open Settings > General” action.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="wp-admin-for-this-place-settings">wp-admin for This Place (Settings)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: tree context menu on Settings, under More, “wp-admin for This Place…”.</li> <li>What it offers: list dialog “wp-admin for This Place”, list “Screens:”, rows: “Settings > General: Part in the Workbench, the rest in wp-admin”, “Settings > Writing: Part in the Workbench, the rest in wp-admin”, “Settings > Reading: Part in the Workbench, the rest in wp-admin”, “Settings > Permalinks: Only in wp-admin”, “Settings > Privacy: Only in wp-admin”, and, where Yoast SEO is present, “Yoast SEO > Settings: Only in wp-admin”. Help: “The wp-admin screens nearest here. OK opens the one chosen.”</li> <li>Announced: <code>{why} Opening {screen} on {site} in your browser.</code>, for example <code>Title, tagline, email, language, time zone and formats are in Settings; who may register, the default role and the WordPress and site addresses are only in wp-admin. QUILL never changes the addresses: a typo locks the owner out. Opening Settings > General on {site} in your browser.</code></li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Settings > Media and Settings > Discussion are mapped to Media and Comments, so they do not appear here, where a person who knows wp-admin’s Settings menu looks for them.</li> <li>The reason sentences are long, and every one is spoken before the browser opens.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h2 id="tools">Tools</h2> <p>There is no Tools node in the Workbench’s site tree. workbench_tree.py has no <code>tools</code> node, and none of the site template’s children is called Tools. wp-admin’s Tools menu is instead spread across the tree by the wp-admin map: Site Health is its own node, privacy requests are Privacy Requests, Redirection is Redirects, import and export point at Posts, and the rest is “only in wp-admin”. The places to reach these screens are Overview > wp-admin Screens (a list of every screen) and “wp-admin for This Place…” on a node’s tree context menu. This section covers those, Privacy Requests and Administration Areas. The Workbench’s own menu-bar Tools menu (Workbench Options, REST Console, Saved Requests, Plain-Language Batch, Practice Site, WXR Files) is out of scope here.</p> <h3 id="where-wp-admins-tools-screens-land">Where wp-admin’s Tools screens land</h3> <p>From ADMIN_MAP in workbench_admin_map.py (screen, address, nearest node, reach, what is said):</p> <ul> <li>Tools > Available Tools, <code>wp-admin/tools.php</code>, nearest More from This Site, only in wp-admin: <code>The categories and tags converter and Press This are not in REST.</code></li> <li>Tools > Import, <code>wp-admin/import.php</code>, nearest Posts, only in wp-admin: <code>Importers are not in REST; Send a Folder as Drafts, in the editor, sends files.</code></li> <li>Tools > Export, <code>wp-admin/export.php</code>, nearest Posts, only in wp-admin: <code>The WXR export is not in REST; Download My Writing, in the editor, saves posts.</code></li> <li>Tools > Site Health > Status, <code>wp-admin/site-health.php</code>, nearest Site Health, all in the Workbench: <code>Every test the site offers over REST runs in Site Health.</code></li> <li>Tools > Site Health > Info, <code>wp-admin/site-health.php?tab=debug</code>, nearest Site Health > Information, only in wp-admin: <code>The server, database and constants have no REST route.</code></li> <li>Tools > Export Personal Data and Tools > Erase Personal Data, nearest Privacy Requests, only in wp-admin: <code>Privacy requests have no REST route.</code></li> <li>Tools > Theme File Editor (nearest Themes) and Tools > Plugin File Editor (nearest Plugins), only in wp-admin: <code>No REST route edits theme files, and many hosts turn this screen off.</code> (plugin files likewise).</li> <li>Tools > Redirection and Tools > Redirection > 404s (where the Redirection plugin is), nearest Redirects and 404 Log.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The Import and Export rows are stale. The Workbench’s own Tools menu now has WXR Files > Export Site as WXR… and Import WXR as Drafts… (engineering log, 2026-11-14), yet the map still says “The WXR export is not in REST; Download My Writing, in the editor, saves posts.” and marks both “Only in wp-admin”. A person following the map is sent to the browser for something QUILL now does.</li> <li>Tools > Available Tools maps to More from This Site, a list of REST namespaces; nothing there relates to the categories and tags converter. The Workbench does have Convert to Category or Tag… (Ctrl+Shift+K) on a term row, which the map does not mention.</li> <li>With no Tools node, a person who knows wp-admin (“Tools > Site Health”) has to know that Site Health is directly under the site, and that privacy is under Privacy Requests. The wp-admin Screens list is the only bridge, and it is under Overview, not where Tools would be.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-wp-admin-screens-node">The wp-admin Screens node</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Sites > site > Overview > wp-admin Screens.</li> <li>What the list shows: columns screen, place, reach; one row per screen in ADMIN_MAP that this site has (a plugin’s screens only where its REST namespace is listed). Reach is <code>All of it in the Workbench</code>, <code>Part in the Workbench, the rest in wp-admin</code>, or <code>Only in wp-admin</code>. Detail: <code>{screen}, {path}. {reach}. {says} Nearest place in the Workbench: {place}. Enter opens it in your browser.</code></li> <li>F1 on the node: “Every wp-admin screen, the place in the Workbench nearest to it, and how much of it REST reaches. Enter opens the screen in your browser.”</li> <li>F1 on a row: “A wp-admin screen. Enter opens it in your browser after saying what REST cannot do there; Go to Its Place selects the nearest node in the Workbench.”</li> <li>Context menu: Open in Dashboard (default), Go to Its Place in the Workbench, Read in Full, Show Only and Hide, Properties….</li> <li>Code: workbench_dashboard_places.py (DASHBOARD_NODES, SCREEN_ROWS), workbench_admin_map.py, workbench_admin_map_plugins.py, workbench_dashboard.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>A row’s spoken detail is long: name, raw path, reach, reason, place, instruction. The path (<code>wp-admin/options-general.php</code>) is the least useful part for a listener and comes second.</li> <li>There is no filter by reach, so finding “what can I only do in wp-admin” means Filter Builder or Show Only on the reach cell.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="open-in-dashboard-wp-admin-screens-row-default">Open in Dashboard (wp-admin Screens row, default)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter on a row, or the first context-menu row.</li> <li>What it does: says <code>{says} Opening {screen} on {site} in your browser.</code> and opens the screen’s full address.
