No address: <code>{site} has no address QUILL knows, so {screen} cannot be opened.</code></li> <li>Keyless: “Enter does it on a wp-admin Screens row.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_site_editor.py (do_open_admin_screen, _open_screen_row).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>For a screen marked “All of it in the Workbench”, Enter still leaves for the browser, after saying the Workbench does it all. The default for those rows could be Go to Its Place.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="go-to-its-place-in-the-workbench">Go to Its Place in the Workbench</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Go to Its Place in the Workbench”.</li> <li>What it does: selects the nearest node in the tree and moves focus to the tree. When the node does not exist for this site: <code>{row title} has no place in the Workbench on {site}.</code> Wrong row: <code>Choose a wp-admin screen first.</code></li> <li>Keyless: “The tree is a keystroke away; this is a shortcut to it (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Nothing is announced on success beyond what the tree says on focus, which is right by GATE-13.</li> <li>The failure sentence names the row by its internal key (for example <code>plugin_install has no place ...</code>): a wp-admin Screens row has no title-like cell, so its title falls back to its key.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-wp-admin-screens-row">Properties (wp-admin Screens row)</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: no sheet is written, and the row has no route, so a read-only sheet of its raw data: fields Node, Why, Name, labelled from the keys. Node shows the internal node key (<code>overview</code>, <code>site_health_info</code>). The sheet title is <code>{screen key} Properties - {site}</code>.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Not useful: everything here is already in the detail, and Node is an internal name. The engine requires Properties last on every menu, so it cannot be removed, but it could show the screen, path, reach and reason with proper labels.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="wp-admin-for-this-place">wp-admin for This Place…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: on the tree, the node’s context menu (Applications key or Shift+F10 on the tree, or on an empty list), under More: the node menu is Refresh, New Here…, Load More, Load All, Columns…, Group By…, then More (What Can I Do Here?, Counts by Status, Empty Trash…, wp-admin for This Place…), then Properties.</li> <li>What opens: if the node has one nearest screen, nothing: it opens it. If several, a list dialog “wp-admin for This Place”, list “Screens:” (access key S), rows <code>{screen}: {reach}</code>, help “The wp-admin screens nearest here. OK opens the one chosen.” A status or group child borrows its parent’s screens.</li> <li>Announced: <code>{says} Opening {screen} on {site} in your browser.</code> None: <code>No wp-admin screen is nearer to this place than the Workbench itself. Overview > wp-admin Screens lists them all.</code> Not on a site: <code>wp-admin for This Place works on a site's nodes.</code></li> <li>Keyless: “Leaves QUILL for wp-admin, only for what REST cannot do (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: workbench_dashboard_places.py (NODE_ROWS), wordpress_workbench_site_editor.py (do_admin_here), workbench_admin_map.py (screens_for).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Buried: fourth of four under More, on the tree’s menu only. A row’s own menu never has it. For the job it exists for (the thing REST cannot do here), it should be one level up.</li> <li>The keyless reason says “only for what REST cannot do”, but it also opens screens the map marks “All of it in the Workbench” (Menus, Widgets, Posts), with no question first.</li> <li>“Empty Trash…” and “Counts by Status” sit beside it on every node’s menu, including Menus, Settings and Site Health, where they mean nothing.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-privacy-requests-node">The Privacy Requests node</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Sites > site > Privacy Requests.</li> <li>What the list shows: columns problem, status. Normally two rows, “Export Personal Data” and “Erase Personal Data”, each with status <code>in wp-admin</code>. Detail for export: <code>Export requests are kept in wp-admin: Tools > Export Personal Data. Enter opens it in your browser, where you add, confirm and send each export. WordPress's REST API has no route for these requests, so QUILL cannot list, confirm or complete them, and cannot tell afterwards whether they were done.</code> (Erase: the same shape, “add, confirm and erase each request”.) If the site’s REST index does list a privacy-request route (a plugin’s), the rows are that route instead: <code>{site} offers {route} for requests.</code></li> <li>F1 on the node: “Where wp-admin keeps personal data export and erase requests; Enter opens it.”</li> <li>F1 on a row: “A wp-admin screen for privacy requests. Enter opens it in your browser.”</li> <li>Context menu: Open This wp-admin Screen (default), Read in Full, Show Only and Hide, Properties….</li> <li>Code: workbench_sweep.py (privacy_rows), workbench_sweep_places.py, wordpress_workbench_sweep.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="open-this-wp-admin-screen-privacy">Open This wp-admin Screen (privacy)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter, or the first context-menu row.</li> <li>What it does: opens the screen, then says <code>Opened {screen} for {site} in your browser. Requests are confirmed and done there; QUILL cannot see whether they were.</code> Wrong row: <code>Choose Export or Erase Personal Data first.</code></li> <li>Keyless: “Enter opens it; privacy requests are rare (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Honest and complete for what REST allows. It is a pure hand-off: the node lists two fixed rows, not the requests, so it cannot tell you whether any request is waiting, which is the one thing a site owner needs from it (a legal deadline applies to these).</li> <li>When a plugin route exists the rows become plain problem rows (Read in Full and Properties only) that name the route, with no way to open or act on the requests.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="administration-areas">Administration Areas</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Sites > site > Administration Areas.</li> <li>What the list shows: columns area, where, plugins; one row per area of running a site (Redirects, Privacy requests, Content audit, Media housekeeping, Search and sitemaps, Scheduled tasks, SSL and the address, Someone else is editing, Backups, Security, sign-ins and two-factor, Caching and speed, Multilingual sites, Consent and cookies, Email delivery, Newsletters and subscribers, Courses, and more), each with where QUILL handles it, the plugins that provide it, and a boundary sentence (for example <code>No cache is purged by QUILL. QUILL has no adapter for these plugins: ...</code>).</li> <li>F1 on the node: “Every part of running a site: what QUILL does, and where the rest is.”</li> <li>F1 on a row: “One part of running a site. Enter goes where QUILL or wp-admin handles it.”</li> <li>Context menu: Go There (default), Read in Full, Show Only and Hide, Properties….</li> <li>Code: workbench_coverage.py (THIRD_SWEEP), workbench_sweep_places.py, wordpress_workbench_sweep.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="go-there-administration-area">Go There (administration area)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter, or the first context-menu row.</li> <li>What it does: selects QUILL’s node for the area if there is one (focus to the tree); else the plugin’s namespace under More from This Site, if the site has it; else opens the wp-admin screen and says <code>Opened {full address} in your browser.</code></li> <li>Keyless: “Enter does it on the row.”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The browser case speaks the whole address (<code>Opened {full address} in your browser.</code>, the row’s link, which for most areas ends in <code>wp-admin/plugins.php?plugin_status=active</code>). Every other hand-off says the screen’s name.</li> <li>For Backups, Security, Caching and most others the “screen” is the Plugins list filtered to active, not the plugin’s own screen, so Go There lands on a generic list.</li> <li>This list is the closest thing to a Tools node, and it is good orientation, but it is a flat list of 20-odd areas with long boundary sentences read in full.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="import-and-export">Import and export</h3> <ul> <li>In the site tree: no node. The map’s Import and Export rows say only in wp-admin (see above). The Workbench menu bar’s Tools > WXR Files does WXR export and import (out of scope here).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Fix the map rows so wp-admin for This Place on Posts and the wp-admin Screens list point at WXR Files instead of sending people to wp-admin.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-abilities-node">The Abilities node</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Sites > site > Abilities, a child of the site after Site Health. The editor’s Tools > WordPress > Manage > AI Abilities… opens the Workbench here.</li> <li>What the list shows: columns ability, plugin, category, says; one row per ability the site’s Abilities API offers (WordPress 6.9 or later).</li> <li>F1 on the node: “What the site’s Abilities API offers, each a command you can run.”</li> <li>F1 on a row: “An ability the site offers. Enter runs it, after a preview.”</li> <li>Context menu, in order: Run… (default, Enter), Offer to Ask the Site, Properties…</li> <li>Code: workbench_tree.py, workbench_actions.py, wordpress_workbench_discovered.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="run-ability-row-default">Run… (ability row, default)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter on an ability row, or the context menu’s first row. No key besides Enter.</li> <li>What opens: the shared sheet editor titled “Run {ability} - {site}”, one field per input the ability takes. OK keeps the values; then a preview titled “Run {ability}” with the button “Run”.</li> <li>Announced after: a report window titled “{ability} - {site}” with the ability’s answer; its F1 says “What the ability answered.”</li> <li>Cancel: closing the sheet without values, or Cancel on the preview, runs nothing and says nothing.</li> <li>Without permission: the row is listed as tier Read, but the access check raises it to Write for an ability that is not read-only, and to Destructive for one the site marks destructive (refusal_for in wordpress_workbench_people.py). Under a lower Workbench Access the access sentence is said; a WordPress role the site refuses comes back as the result.</li> <li>Keyless: no written reason found (Enter is its route).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The menu cannot show in advance whether a given ability will change the site; the tier is only decided when it runs, so a Read Only window hears the refusal after choosing Run.</li> <li>The cancel path is silent, unlike most Workbench previews.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="offer-to-ask-the-site-ability-row">Offer to Ask the Site (ability row)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: an ability row’s context menu (Applications key or Shift+F10), second row, “Offer to Ask the Site”. Not in the Item menu, no key.</li> <li>What it does: turns on or off whether Ask the Site (Tools > WordPress > Manage > Ask the Site…) may suggest this ability. The choice is kept on this computer, per site, in the same per-site choices the editor’s Abilities window used. Row help: “Turn on or off whether Ask the Site may suggest this ability. Kept on this computer; QUILL still asks you before every run.”</li> <li>Confirmation: only when turning on an ability that is not read-only. Title “Offer to Ask the Site”, message “Let Ask the Site offer {ability} on {site}? It can change the site. QUILL still asks you every time before it runs.”, buttons Offer It and Cancel. Turning a read-only ability on, or any ability off, is immediate.</li> <li>Announced after: “Ask the Site may now offer {ability} on {site}.” or “Ask the Site no longer offers {ability} on {site}.”</li> <li>Failures: “Offer to Ask the Site works on one of the site’s abilities.” (wrong row); “Ask the Site is not available for {site} here.” (site not connected here).</li> <li>Undo: nothing recorded; Ctrl+Z does not reverse it. Run the command again.</li> <li>Cancel: nothing changes, nothing is said.</li> <li>Keyless: no written reason found in code.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_discovered.py (do_offer_to_ask), workbench_actions.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Before this row existed (added 2026-11-14, coordinator report), the AI Abilities door sent people to the Workbench, where nothing could turn an ability on, so Ask the Site’s refusal “Turn them on one by one in Abilities” was a dead end. It is now reachable, but only from the context menu.</li> <li>The label is a toggle that does not say its current state; the list has no column showing which abilities are offered. A person must remember, or run it and listen to the outcome to find out (which flips it).</li> <li>Consider “Offered to Ask the Site” as a column, or the label changing to “Stop Offering to Ask the Site” when on.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h2 id="site-health-and-maintenance">Site Health and maintenance</h2> <p>Upkeep is spread over several places: Sites > site > Site Health (WordPress’s own tests over REST, plus QUILL’s checks) with its Information and Security children; Needs My Attention > Site Health and Updates (gathered from every site); and the site’s Search and Upkeep, Content Audit, Redirects with its 404 Log, and Media > Housekeeping. Almost everything here reads and explains; the only writes are redirects and moving an audit finding to the Trash. The writing is careful about where each answer comes from and what REST cannot say. The weak points are lists that are long but say little (Updates), problem rows with nothing to do but read, and two places that disagree about search engine visibility.</p> <h3 id="the-site-health-node">The Site Health node</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+Shift+9 (Go > Site Health, <code>Site &Health</code>, help “This site’s health checks.”); Sites > site > Site Health; the editor’s Tools > WordPress > Site Check door.</li> <li>What the list shows: columns test, status, says, critical first, then should be improved, information, good. Status words are <code>Critical</code>, <code>Should be improved</code>, <code>Good</code> or <code>Information</code>. Rows come from QUILL’s own checks and WordPress’s tests: <code>WordPress version: {v}.</code>; <code>HTTPS: the site's address uses HTTPS.</code> or <code>HTTPS: the site's address does not use HTTPS. Visitors' browsers will call it not secure.</code> (critical); <code>Time zone: {zone}.</code> or <code>Time zone: a fixed offset of {n} hours. A city keeps daylight saving right.</code>; a permalinks line; settings lines; one line per Site Health test the site lists under <code>wp-site-health/v1/tests/</code> (its own label); for a failing authorization header, <code>What this means for QUILL: the web server drops the Authorization header before WordPress sees it, so application passwords cannot work. ...</code>; <code>{plugin}: recognised. QUILL offers {what}.</code> for The Events Calendar, WooCommerce, Yoast SEO, Rank Math and Abilities for AI; and always <code>Search engine visibility: WordPress does not share this over its API; it is under Settings, Reading in the dashboard.</code></li> <li>When tests cannot run: <code>Health checks: only an administrator can run them.</code>, <code>{test}: the site could not run this test.</code>, or <code>PHP version, updates and loopback: this site does not offer its health checks to QUILL.</code></li> <li>F1 on the node: “WordPress’s own checks of how well the site is set up.”</li> <li>F1 on a row: “A problem a check found. Enter reads what it means.”</li> <li>Context menu: Read in Full (default), Show Only and Hide, Properties….</li> <li>Code: workbench_load.py (load_health), site_admin.py (site_check), site_health.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The test column is the first sentence of the line and the says column is the whole line, so every row reads its first sentence twice.</li> <li>Good rows say “A problem a check found.” on F1, even for <code>HTTPS: the site's address uses HTTPS.</code></li> <li>Every test runs each time the node loads (each test is its own request; the loopback test waits on the site), with no cache; opening Site Health is slow on a slow host. Not measured.</li> <li>“Search engine visibility: WordPress does not share this over its API” contradicts Search and Upkeep, which does judge it (from the sitemap) and says where its answer comes from. Two answers to one question in one tree.</li> <li>Nothing to do on a problem row: no “Open in Dashboard” to the test’s own screen, no “how to fix” link, although WordPress’s test answers carry an actions link. The only hand-off is through the node’s wp-admin for This Place (Tools > Site Health > Status, marked “All of it in the Workbench”).</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="read-in-full-and-properties-site-health-row">Read in Full and Properties (Site Health row)</h3> <ul> <li>Read in Full: Enter; focus to the details pane, which holds the line.</li> <li>Properties: no route and no written sheet, so a read-only sheet of the row’s cells: Test, Problem, Status, Says, Site. Its title is <code>{row number} Properties - {site}</code> (the row has no title-like cell, so its title is its index).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Test and Problem hold the same text; Says repeats it. Five fields, two facts.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="information-site-health--information">Information (Site Health > Information)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Sites > site > Site Health > Information.</li> <li>What the list shows: columns test, status, says. First row “Server, database and constants” (an open-in-wp-admin row): <code>The Info tab (server, PHP, database and constants) has no REST route. Tools > Site Health > Info in wp-admin has it, and its Copy Site Info to Clipboard button gives your host the whole report. Enter opens it in your browser.</code> Then “Tests on REST”: <code>The tests this site offers over REST, run in the Site Health node: {names}.</code> Then the directory sizes, one row each from the site’s directory-sizes route, <code>{part}: {size}.</code> or <code>{part}: the site could not measure it ({why}).</code>, and <code>Altogether: {size}.</code>; or one row <code>Directory sizes: only an administrator can read them.</code></li> <li>F1 on the node: “What REST covers of Site Health’s Info tab: the tests and the directory sizes. The server, database and constants are only in wp-admin; their row opens Site Health Info.”</li> <li>Context menu, first row: Open in Dashboard (default), Read in Full, Show Only and Hide, Properties…. Other rows: Read in Full (default), Show Only and Hide, Properties….</li> <li>Code: workbench_health_info.py, site_space.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Open in Dashboard on that row opens the browser silently: it runs the generic open-on-site command, which speaks nothing, while Open in Dashboard on a plugin or theme row says its reason first. (The reason is in the row’s detail, already heard.)</li> <li>The directory sizes are the Workbench’s only “space used” view; the editor’s Tools > WordPress > Manage > Space Used is separate. WordPress computes these sizes on request, so this node can be the slowest in the tree; nothing warns.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="security-site-health--security">Security (Site Health > Security)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Sites > site > Site Health > Security.</li> <li>What the list shows: two rows. “XML-RPC”: status <code>Should be improved</code> when <code>xmlrpc.php</code> answers (<code>XML-RPC is on: xmlrpc.php answers and lists {n} methods, including pingbacks and methods that take a password. If nothing you use needs it (the Jetpack app and some old phone apps do), turn it off with a security plugin or at the web server. Whether a plugin has turned off only its sign-in cannot be told without trying a password, which QUILL never does.</code>), <code>Good</code> when blocked (<code>XML-RPC is off: xmlrpc.php does not answer ({why}). Nothing in QUILL needs it.</code>), else <code>XML-RPC: QUILL could not tell whether xmlrpc.php answers ({why}).</code> Then “QUILL and XML-RPC”, information: <code>QUILL never uses XML-RPC: every job it does has a REST route, and XML-RPC takes a password on every call, lets one request try many passwords, and carries pingbacks that can be aimed at other sites.</code></li> <li>F1 on the node: “Whether the site still answers on XML-RPC, which QUILL never uses, checked without a password.”</li> <li>Context menu: Read in Full (default), Show Only and Hide, Properties….</li> <li>Code: workbench_health_info.py (xmlrpc_check, security_rows).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Clear and careful. The name “Security” promises more than one check; the Administration Areas row for security plugins is where the rest is said, and nothing here links to it.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="needs-my-attention--site-health">Needs My Attention > Site Health</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+1 (Go > Needs My Attention) then Site Health in the tree; or Needs My Attention > Site Health.</li> <li>What the list shows: columns problem, site, status; only the Critical and Should be improved rows from every connected site’s Site Health list.</li> <li>F1 on the node: “Site Health problems on every site, worst first.”</li> <li>Context menu: as for a Site Health row (Read in Full, Properties).</li> <li>Code: workbench_attention.py (_health).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>It runs every site’s full Site Health (every test on every site) to keep a few rows; on several sites this is slow.</li> <li>“Worst first” holds within a site; across sites the gathered rows are not re-sorted by status (not verified on a live run).</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="needs-my-attention--updates">Needs My Attention > Updates</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Needs My Attention > Updates.</li> <li>What the list shows: columns what, site, installed, update. For each site: a WordPress row (<code>WordPress {v} on {site}. WordPress's REST API does not say whether a newer WordPress or new translations wait; Dashboard > Updates does.</code>), an “Automatic updates” row from Site Health’s background-updates test where the site offers it (<code>Automatic updates on {site}, from Site Health: {label}.</code>, or <code>Automatic updates on {site}: only an administrator can run this Site Health test.</code>), then every plugin and every theme with its installed version. The update cell is <code>Not reported over REST</code> until Check for Updates on WordPress.org marks it (see “Plugins and themes”); a plugin whose payload carries an update field says <code>Version {v} reported by the site</code>. Each row’s detail ends <code> REST does not say whether an update waits.</code> (or what the site reports) and, where the site says, <code> It updates itself automatically.</code> or <code> It does not update itself.</code>, then <code> Enter opens Dashboard > Updates in your browser.</code> A site where you cannot list plugins gives one row: <code>Your role on {site} cannot list its plugins.</code></li> <li>F1 on the node: “WordPress, plugin and theme versions; Check for Updates on WordPress.org, on any row’s context menu, says which have an update waiting. Enter opens Dashboard > Updates.”</li> <li>F1 on a row: “A version on the site and whether an update waits, once Check for Updates on WordPress.org has asked. Enter opens Dashboard > Updates in your browser.”</li> <li>Context menu: Open Dashboard Updates in the Browser (default), Read in Full, Check for Updates on WordPress.org…, Show Only and Hide, Properties….</li> <li>Code: workbench_updates.py, workbench_attention.py, workbench_update_check.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>This is the least “needs my attention” list in Needs My Attention: it lists every plugin and theme on every site, waiting or not. Even after Check for Updates, the up-to-date rows stay. It should list only what waits (after the check) or be renamed Versions.</li> <li>Check for Updates runs per site, so with five sites the person runs it five times from rows of each site; the marks are lost when the window closes.</li> <li>Open Dashboard Updates in the Browser opens silently (the generic open-on-site command), while the detail already said it.</li> <li>The Properties sheet for a plugin or theme row is the raw REST payload (fields labelled from their keys, for example “Plugin uri”, “Requires php”, “Textdomain”); for the WordPress row, the four cells.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="open-dashboard-updates-in-the-browser-updates-row-default">Open Dashboard Updates in the Browser (Updates row, default)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter on an Updates row.</li> <li>What it does: opens <code>wp-admin/update-core.php</code> for the row’s site. Nothing is spoken. If the row has no link (the site’s address unknown), the command falls back to the site’s own address, and says <code>This has no address on the site yet.</code> only when that is empty too.</li> <li>Keyless: it is Enter.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Updates are the one maintenance job a site owner must do, and here it is a pure hand-off to wp-admin’s Updates screen, which is a long form of check boxes. Said honestly in every row; still a dead end.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-search-and-upkeep-node">The Search and Upkeep node</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Sites > site > Search and Upkeep.</li> <li>What the list shows: columns what, state, source. Rows: search engine visibility (from <code>blog_public</code> if the site sends it, else judged from <code>wp-sitemap.xml</code>: <code>{site} lets search engines index it.</code>, <code>{site} asks search engines not to index it.</code>, <code>{site} serves WordPress's sitemap, so it probably lets search engines index it.</code>, <code>{site} serves no WordPress sitemap. That happens when it asks search engines not ...</code>, or <code>QUILL could not tell whether {site} asks not to be indexed.</code>); the sitemap (<code>{address} lists {n} sitemaps: {names}.</code>, or <code>{site} did not serve {address}...</code>); missed schedules (<code>Missed schedule: {title}</code>, <code>{title} was due on {date} and is still waiting. WordPress publishes on a visit through WP-Cron; Approve and Schedule on its row in Posts or Pages publishes it now.</code>, or <code>Nothing scheduled is overdue.</code>); the certificate (<code>{site}'s certificate is good until {date}, {n} days from now.</code>, <code>{site}'s certificate expired {n} days ago.</code>, <code>{site} is connected over plain HTTP, so there is no certificate to read.</code>, or <code>QUILL's connection to {site} checked its certificate, which is valid today. Its expiry date was not handed over, so no warning can be given ahead of time.</code>); and editing locks (<code>Being edited: {title}</code>, <code>{person} has {title} open in WordPress's editor now. ...</code>, <code>Changed in the last 15 minutes: {title}</code>, or <code>No one is seen editing a post or page now.</code>). Each detail ends with <code> Where this comes from: {source}</code>.</li> <li>F1 on the node: “Search visibility, the sitemap, missed schedules, the certificate and who is editing, each with where QUILL’s answer comes from.”</li> <li>F1 on a row: “One upkeep check, ending with where its answer comes from.”</li> <li>Context menu: Read in Full (default), Open Where It Is Checked, Show Only and Hide, Properties….</li> <li>Code: workbench_upkeep.py, workbench_sweep_places.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Strong: every row says how it knows. The certificate warning inside 30 days is the kind of thing a site owner never sees until it is too late.</li> <li>A missed schedule’s detail says <code>Approve and Schedule on its row in Posts or Pages publishes it now.</code>, but that command is not on the upkeep row; you have to find the post again elsewhere.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="open-where-it-is-checked">Open Where It Is Checked</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context-menu label “Open Where It Is Checked” on an upkeep row.</li> <li>What it does: opens the row’s page or wp-admin screen (for visibility, Settings > Reading) in the browser. Nothing is spoken. A row with no link of its own (the certificate, for example) opens the site’s home page instead, silently; only when the site’s address is unknown too does it say <code>This has no address on the site yet.</code></li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>On rows without their own link, “Open Where It Is Checked” opening the home page is a silent wrong answer.</li> <li>Same command id as “Open on the Site” (Shift+Enter) elsewhere, under another label; Shift+Enter does the same here, which is not said.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="content-audit-and-media--housekeeping">Content Audit and Media > Housekeeping</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach them: Sites > site > Content Audit; Sites > site > Media > Housekeeping.</li> <li>What the lists show: columns problem, status. Content Audit: “Pages nobody links to, pages not changed in a year, duplicate titles and text, very long or short pages, hard reading, headings that skip a level.” (node F1). Housekeeping: “Files used nowhere, files uploaded twice, very large files, video without captions, audio without a transcript; usage shows before Move to Trash.” (node F1).</li> <li>F1 on a row: “Something the audit found. Enter reads it; Show Where It Is Used comes before Move to Trash.”</li> <li>Context menu: Read in Full (default), Show Where It Is Used, Move to Trash…, Open on the Site, Show Only and Hide, Properties….</li> <li>Code: workbench_sweep.py, workbench_sweep_places.py, wordpress_workbench_sweep.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="show-where-it-is-used">Show Where It Is Used</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: a report window titled <code>Where {title} Is Used</code>, one line per post or page, <code>{place} uses it</code> (or <code>links to it</code>), or <code>Nothing in the posts and pages read uses it.</code> F1: “Each post or page that uses or links to it, one a line.”</li> <li>Keyless: “Read before tidying up, a few times a year (rule 9).”</li> </ul> <h3 id="move-to-trash-audit-finding">Move to Trash… (audit finding)</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: a preview titled <code>Move {title} to the Trash</code>, button “Move to Trash”. Summary <code>Move {title} on {site} to the Trash, where Restore brings it back.</code>; for media, <code>Move {title} on {site} to the Trash. WordPress keeps media in a Trash only where the site turns that on; where it does not, nothing is deleted and QUILL says so.</code> Lines: where it is used, as above.</li> <li>Announced after: <code>Moved {title} to the Trash on {site}. Restore from Trash brings it back.</code> For media on a site without a media Trash: <code>{site} keeps no Trash for media, so {title} was not deleted. Delete Permanently on its row in Media does that, after you confirm.</code></li> <li>Undo: none recorded; Restore from Trash on the item’s own row (in its Trash node) brings it back.</li> <li>Without permission: needs <code>delete_posts</code>, tier Organize.</li> <li>Keyless: “Tidying after an audit is occasional, and previewed (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Ctrl+Z does not undo it, although the item is only in the Trash and every other Trash move in the Workbench is undoable. Ctrl+Z here undoes the change before it.</li> <li>Good that usage is shown in the preview itself, not only on request.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-redirects-node-and-its-404-log">The Redirects node and its 404 Log</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach them: Sites > site > Redirects; Redirects > 404 Log.</li> <li>What the lists show: Redirects: columns source, target, code, hits, state, from the Redirection plugin’s <code>redirection/v1</code> routes. 404 Log: columns address, when, from.</li> <li>F1 on Redirects: “The Redirection plugin’s redirects: add with Ctrl+N, change, test, turn off.” On 404 Log: “Addresses visitors asked for that were not found; redirect any of them.” Row F1: “One redirect: from, to, its code and hits. Test an Address follows it.” and “An address that was not found. Redirect This Address sends it somewhere.”</li> <li>Redirect row menu: Read in Full (default), Test an Address…, Add Redirect…, Change Redirect…, Turn On or Off, Delete Redirect…, Open on the Site, Show Only and Hide, Properties…. 404 row menu: Read in Full (default), Redirect This Address…, Test an Address…, Show Only and Hide, Properties….</li> <li>Without the plugin: <code>{site} does not have the Redirection plugin's REST namespace, so QUILL cannot change its redirects. Administration Areas says where they are kept.</code></li> <li>Code: workbench_redirects.py, workbench_sweep_places.py, wordpress_workbench_sweep.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="add-redirect-redirect-this-address-and-change-redirect">Add Redirect…, Redirect This Address… and Change Redirect…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach them: Ctrl+N (New Here) on Redirects, or the context-menu labels.</li> <li>What opens, in order, each its own dialog titled “Add Redirect”, “Redirect This Address” or “Change Redirect”: text box “Old address, from this site (such as /old-page/):” (help “The address visitors arrive at. A path on this site.”); text box “New address it leads to:” (help “Where visitors are sent instead: a path on this site or a full address.”); list “Kind of redirect:” with <code>301, moved for good</code>, <code>302, found elsewhere for now</code>, <code>307, for now</code>, <code>308, for good</code> (help “301 tells search engines the move is for good; 302 and 307 are for now.”); when the plugin has more than one group, list “Group to keep it in:” (help “The Redirection plugin keeps every redirect in a group. Redirections is the usual one.”); then a preview titled “Redirect Preview”, button “Save Redirect”.</li> <li>Preview wording: summary <code>On {site}: add a redirect.</code> or <code>On {site}: change this redirect.</code>; lines <code>From: {old}</code>, <code>To: {new}</code>, <code>Kind: {code}</code>.</li> <li>Announced after: <code>Saved on {site}: {old} now leads to {new}.</code> No group: <code>The Redirection plugin on {site} has no group to keep a redirect in. Add one under Tools, Redirection, Groups, then try again.</code></li> <li>Undo: none recorded.</li> <li>Without permission: needs <code>manage_options</code>, tier Write.</li> <li>Keyless: Add, “Ctrl+N (New Here) on Redirects reaches it; no chord of its own.”; Change, “Redirects change rarely (rule 9).”; Redirect This Address, “Reviewing the 404 log is a monthly job (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>None of the three prompts has an access key (no <code>&</code> in “Old address…”, “New address it leads to:”, “Kind of redirect:”, “Group to keep it in:”), unlike every other Workbench dialog.</li> <li>A redirect write is not in Change History: Ctrl+Z after saving undoes something else.</li> <li>“Kind: 301” in the preview drops the words the list used (“moved for good”).</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="turn-on-or-off-and-delete-redirect">Turn On or Off and Delete Redirect…</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: a Yes/No message box. Toggle: title and button “Turn Off” or “Turn On”, <code>{Turn Off or Turn On} the redirect from {source} on {site}?</code>. Delete: title and button “Delete Redirect”, <code>Delete the redirect from {source} to {target} on {site}? The Redirection plugin keeps no Trash, so it is gone for good.</code></li> <li>Announced after: <code>The redirect from {source} is off.</code> (or on); <code>Deleted the redirect from {source} on {site}.</code></li> <li>Undo: none.</li> <li>Keyless: toggle “Redirects are turned off rarely (rule 9).”; delete “A destructive, rare job: never a bare key (rule 4).”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Delete Redirect’s row help says “Gone for good, after you confirm by name”, but it is an ordinary Yes/No box; nothing is typed, unlike Delete Permanently elsewhere.</li> <li>“Turn On or Off” does not say which it will do.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="test-an-address">Test an Address…</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: dialog “Test an Address”, text box “Address on this site to follow:” (no access key), prefilled from the row; help “QUILL follows the redirects it read from this site. Nothing is sent.” Then a report titled “Test an Address”, hop by hop.</li> <li>Keyless: “Asked when a link is reported broken, now and then (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Useful and safe. The help’s “Nothing is sent” is true of the test; the first run does read every redirect from the site.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="space-used">Space used</h3> <ul> <li>Where: the directory sizes in Site Health > Information (above). There is no space node or upkeep row for it; the editor’s Tools > WordPress > Manage > Space Used is the other place.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>wordpress.md marks Space Used in Site Check “in progress”; in the Workbench it is a set of Information rows, readable only by an administrator.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h2 id="snapshots-and-moving-sites">Snapshots and moving sites</h2> <p>These are the commands that copy a whole site, or part of one, somewhere else: to a folder on this computer (Take a Snapshot), back onto the site (Put Back from a Snapshot), to another connected site (Copy to Site), side by side with another site (Compare Sites), and to or from a WordPress export file (Tools > WXR Files). All of them run their network work in the background (the window’s two-worker task pool, shared with list loading) and say one sentence when done. Snapshot, Put Back, Compare, Copy and both WXR commands are window-menu commands, not row actions, except Copy to Site, which is also on several row menus. Every finding below was read from the code on 2026-11-14; nothing here has been tried against a live site.</p> <h3 id="take-a-snapshot">Take a Snapshot</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Site menu, Take a Snapshot (Alt+S, N); chord Ctrl+Alt+S. Not on any context menu.</li> <li>Which site: the site of the selected node, otherwise the current site. With no site at all it says “Connect a site first: File > WordPress > Connect a Site, in the editor.”</li> <li>What opens: a chooser titled “Take a Snapshot”. <ul> <li>Tab order: list labelled “Save:” (access key S), then OK (the default button, no access key), then Cancel.</li> <li>Choices: “Everything but the media files” (selected first) and “Everything, with the media files too”.</li> <li>Enter, double-click or OK takes the selected choice.</li> <li>What is saved either way: every post, page, category, tag, menu, menu item, synced pattern, media record and the settings <code>wp/v2/settings</code> gives. The second choice also downloads each media file into a “media files” folder.</li> </ul></li> <li>Where it goes: the Workbench data folder, then <code>wordpress_snapshots</code>, then the site’s profile id (for example <code>wp-1a2b3c4d5e6f</code>), then a folder named for the time, such as <code>2026-11-14 101500</code>. It holds one JSON file per part plus <code>snapshot.json</code>, which records what was taken, what the site refused, and what is never taken.</li> <li>Preview or confirmation: none. Nothing is written to the site, so that is reasonable.</li> <li>Announced after: “Snapshot of {site} saved: {counts}. In {full folder path}. Left out: {left-out list joined by semicolons}.” <ul> <li>Counts look like “12 posts, 4 pages, 3 categories, 10 tags, 2 menus, 15 menu items, 1 patterns, 40 media, 31 settings”. The count words never go singular.</li> <li>The left-out list always includes “people, passwords, plugins, themes, revisions and comments: never taken”. Without media files it also includes “the media files themselves (not asked for; records kept)”. It adds a line for each part the site refused (“{part}: the site would not give it ({why})”) and for each media file that failed.</li> </ul></li> <li>Failure: a part the site refuses is recorded and named in the spoken sentence, not treated as empty. Any other error (a disk error, say) speaks the error text, or “That did not work.”</li> <li>Undo: not needed; nothing changed on the site.</li> <li>F1: the chooser’s help is “Every post, page, pattern, term, menu, setting and media record of {site}, to this computer. The media files themselves only if you choose; people, passwords, plugins, themes, revisions and comments are never saved.” On the list it adds “Press Enter to choose the selected one.”</li> <li>Cancel: Escape or Cancel closes the chooser. Nothing is said.</li> <li>Without permission: no Workbench Access or role check, which is right for a read. What the account cannot read goes into the left-out list instead.</li> <li>Code: workbench_snapshot.py (take_snapshot, snapshot_left_out, snapshot_counts_words), wordpress_workbench_bold.py (do_snapshot), wordpress_dialogs.py (ask_choice).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: the completion sentence is very long to hear. It reads out a full Windows path with a profile id, then the whole left-out list. A screen-reader user gets a 30-plus-word path read character-ish, and no way to act on it. Offer “Open the Snapshot Folder” or a report instead.</li> <li>Review note: custom post types are not taken. Only <code>wp/v2/posts</code> and <code>wp/v2/pages</code> are read, so Events, Products, Podcast episodes and the rest are left out. So are templates, template parts, global styles, block-theme Navigation menus (<code>wp_navigation</code>) and form-plugin data. None of these appears in the left-out list, which names only “people, passwords, plugins, themes, revisions and comments”. The person is told a snapshot is complete when it is not.</li> <li>Review note: the menu help says “Everything but media, saved here.” That is wrong both ways: media records are always saved, and much more than media is left out (see the note above).</li> <li>Review note: “1 patterns”, “1 media” and the like: the count words are never made singular.</li> <li>Review note: there is no way to list, open, rename or delete snapshots from the Workbench. They pile up, with media files possibly large, in the data folder.</li> <li>Review note: Ctrl+Alt+S (take a snapshot) is one modifier away from Ctrl+Alt+Shift+S (Put Back, which writes to the site) and from Ctrl+Shift+S (mark as Spam). Put Back is protected by a picker and a preview, so the risk is a startled moment rather than data loss. On keyboards where Ctrl+Alt is AltGr, these chords may also collide with typing in the filter box.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="put-back-from-a-snapshot">Put Back from a Snapshot</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Site menu, Put Back from a Snapshot (Alt+S, K); chord Ctrl+Alt+Shift+S. Not on any context menu.</li> <li>Before anything opens: <ul> <li>No site: “Connect a site first: File > WordPress > Connect a Site, in the editor.”</li> <li>No snapshot of this site: “There is no snapshot of {site} yet: Site > Take a Snapshot makes one.”</li> </ul></li> <li>What opens, step 1: a chooser titled “Put Back from a Snapshot”. <ul> <li>List labelled “Snapshot:” (access key S), then OK (default), then Cancel.</li> <li>Rows are the snapshot folder names, newest first, such as “2026-11-14 101500”.</li> <li>Help: “The snapshots of this site, newest first. The next step previews exactly what would be put back.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Step 2, only when the snapshot was taken on the Practice Site and this site is real: the practice crossing question, titled “Put Back from a Snapshot: from the Practice Site to a Real Site”. <ul> <li>Wording: “Put Back from a Snapshot would send what is on {practice}, the practice site on this computer, to {real}: a real site that other people can see. Work on the practice site never goes anywhere else unless you choose it here. Send it to {real}?”</li> <li>Buttons: “Send to {site}”, and “Cancel”, which is the default.</li> </ul></li> <li>Step 3: the site is read in the background (“comparing with the snapshot”). If nothing differs: “{site} already matches the snapshot of {name}.”</li> <li>Step 4: a preview titled “Put Back {snapshot name}”. <ul> <li>Tab order: “Summary:” (read-only multi-line text), then “Each change:” (a list box with the first line selected), then “Put Back” (the default button), then Cancel.</li> <li>Summary: “{n} changes on {site}. Posts, pages, patterns, categories and tags changed since the snapshot are set back to it; WordPress keeps a revision of each post and page it changes, so this can be undone from Revisions. Ones deleted since are made again as drafts, at new addresses. Menus, settings, people, plugins and media files are not put back.”</li> <li>Each line is either “Put back the {kind} {title}: it changed since the snapshot.” or “Make the {kind} {title} again, as a draft: it was deleted.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: “Put back {done} of {total} on {site}.” If any line failed, a report titled “Put Back from a Snapshot” opens. It has one line per item: “Put back: {kind} {title}.”, “Made again: {kind} {title}.” or “Not put back: {kind} {title}: {why}”. The list then reloads.</li> <li>Failure while planning: if the site refuses to list any one of the five kinds, the whole plan stops and only the site’s error sentence is spoken. Nothing is previewed.</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z says “Put back the snapshot {name}. This cannot be undone.” The change is recorded with no undo, even though the preview says the change “can be undone from Revisions” (that means one post at a time, in wp-admin).</li> <li>F1: the chooser help quoted above. The preview dialog’s help is “What the change will do, before anything is sent. The button names the change; Cancel sends nothing.”</li> <li>Cancel: Escape at any step sends nothing and says nothing.</li> <li>Without permission: not found in code. The command never calls <code>write_refusal</code>, and <code>refusal_for</code> returns nothing for a command that is not a row action. Workbench Access “Read Only”, Safe Mode and View As Role therefore do not stop it. Only WordPress itself refuses, line by line, and the report shows “Not put back: …”.</li> <li>Code: workbench_snapshot.py (restore_plan, run_restore, RESTORE_TERMS, snapshot_source), wordpress_workbench_bold.py (do_restore_snapshot), wordpress_workbench_bold_practice.py (practice_crossing_ok), workbench_practice.py (crossing_warning), wordpress_workbench_dialogs.py (PreviewDialog).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note (critical): Put Back writes to the site with no Workbench Access, Safe Mode or View As Role check. A person who set a site to Read Only, or who is in Safe Mode, can still overwrite posts and pages from this menu.</li> <li>Review note (critical): it puts back far less than “set back to it” promises. For posts, pages and patterns only the title, content and excerpt are compared and sent; for terms, only the name and description. Status, slug, date, author, categories, tags, featured image, parent and menu order are never compared or restored. A post unpublished since the snapshot stays unpublished, and the preview does not say so.</li> <li>Review note (critical): an item deleted since the snapshot is “made again as a draft, at a new address” with only title, content and excerpt. Its categories, tags, featured image and parent are lost. A deleted category made again gets a new id, and no post is re-filed under it.</li> <li>Review note: items moved to the Trash since the snapshot are probably treated as deleted, because WordPress’s <code>status=any</code> leaves out trashed items. Put Back then makes a duplicate draft while the original still sits in the Trash. A Restore from Trash would have been the right move. Worth checking on the live site.</li> <li>Review note: the chooser shows only a timestamp. There are no counts, no “with media files”, no “taken on the practice site”, and no way to read what a snapshot holds before choosing it.</li> <li>Review note: it is all or nothing. The preview is a read-only list, with no check boxes to put back only some items, unlike Split in By Name, which uses check boxes.</li> <li>Review note: the preview’s default button is “Put Back”, so Enter in the list sends the change. The confirmation dialogs elsewhere (crossing, Reset, Check Every Link) default to Cancel. Two different safety habits sit in the same window.</li> <li>Review note: the plan reads every post and page of the site (up to 1000 pages of 100) with no progress or count. A big site sits silent for a long time.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="copy-to-site-2">Copy to Site</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: <ul> <li>Item menu, Copy to Site (Alt+I, O); chord Ctrl+Shift+Y.</li> <li>Context menu “Copy to Site…” on item (post, page, custom item), media, term, pattern, menu, form and Gravity Forms form rows.</li> <li>Row help: “To another of your sites, after a preview. Ctrl+Shift+Y.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Before anything opens: <ul> <li>Nothing selected: “Select what to copy first.”</li> <li>Only one site connected: “Connect a second site first: File > WordPress > Connect a Site.”</li> </ul></li> <li>What opens, step 1: a chooser titled “Copy to Site”. <ul> <li>List labelled “Site:” (access key S), then OK (default), then Cancel.</li> <li>Rows are every other connected site (not limited by the Across Sites scope).</li> <li>Help: “Which of your other connected sites.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Step 2, only from the Practice Site to a real site: the crossing question, titled “Copy to Site: from the Practice Site to a Real Site”. Wording as under Put Back, with “Copy to Site” as the action. Buttons “Send to {site}” and “Cancel” (the default).</li> <li>Step 3: the target’s permission is checked for the first selected row’s kind (see Without permission).</li> <li>Step 4: the plan is read in the background (“planning the copy to {target}”), then a preview titled “Copy from {source} to {target}” opens. <ul> <li>Summary: “Copy from {source} to {target}: {n} made, {n} replaced, {n} skipped, and {n} pictures uploaded. New items are drafts; a replaced item keeps its status on {target}. Links to {source} are pointed at {target}.”</li> <li>Each change list: <ul> <li>one line per item: “Make {kind} {title}.” or “Replace {kind} {title}: {why}” or “Skip …”;</li> <li>then “Upload the picture {url}” for each picture;</li> <li>then “Left out: {line}”, always including “Not copied: comments, revisions, custom fields, publishing (a new item is a draft, a private one included), sticky and template choices, and taxonomies other than categories and tags.” It adds a line for private items and for plugin blocks the target may lack.</li> </ul></li> <li>Button: “Copy” (the default), and Cancel.</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: <ul> <li>Success: “Copied to {target}; new items are drafts there.”</li> <li>Partial: “Copied to {target}, except {n}; the report says which.”, then a report titled “Copy to {target}” with one outcome line per item, the failed pictures, and the left-out section.</li> </ul></li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z says “Copied {n} from {source} to {target}. This cannot be undone.” No undo is recorded. A replaced item on the target can be recovered only from its WordPress revisions.</li> <li>F1: the context-menu row help above. The preview dialog’s generic help.</li> <li>Cancel: at any step, sends nothing, says nothing.</li> <li>Without permission: <code>write_refusal</code> on the target, using the first selected row’s kind. You hear one of: <ul> <li>“Workbench access on {site} is Read Only. Site > Workbench Access changes it.” (or Organize);</li> <li>“Safe Mode is on, so the Workbench only reads.”;</li> <li>“You are viewing {site} as {Role}, which only reads. Site > View As Role ends it.”;</li> <li>the role sentence: “{What} needs the {Role} role or higher. You are {a role} on {site}.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Code: workbench_site_copy.py (plan_copy, run_copy, CopyPlan.summary, CopyLine.sentence), workbench_site_copy_order.py (terms and files first), workbench_carry.py (COPY_LEFT_OUT, copy_left_out), wordpress_workbench_bold.py (do_copy_to_site).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: the success sentence says “new items are drafts there”, even when every line was a Replace. A replaced published page goes live at once, and the spoken outcome hides that. Say how many were replaced live.</li> <li>Review note: the permission check uses only the first selected row’s kind. A mixed selection (a post plus a menu) is checked against post rights only.</li> <li>Review note: “Connect a second site first: File > WordPress > Connect a Site.” The Workbench’s own File menu has no WordPress submenu. The path exists only in the editor, and unlike <code>_site_now</code>’s sentence this one does not say “in the editor”.</li> <li>Review note: there is no undo, and this is a write to another site. Ctrl+Z also cannot reach any earlier undoable change while this entry is newest, because <code>do_undo</code> only looks at the newest entry (see the Needs My Attention section’s note on the undo stack).</li> <li>Review note: the module docstring says custom terms travel; COPY_LEFT_OUT says “taxonomies other than categories and tags” do not. The preview is the one the person hears, so it is probably right, but the two disagree.</li> <li>Review note: “Left out:” lines are mixed into the same “Each change” list as the actions, so arrowing through the plan reads things that will not happen as if they were steps.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="compare-sites">Compare Sites</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Site menu, Compare Sites (Alt+S, I); chord Ctrl+Alt+K. Menu help “What differs from another.”</li> <li>What opens: the same “Compare Sites” chooser (“Site:” list of every other site, OK default, Cancel; help “Which of your other connected sites.”). The first site is the current one.</li> <li>Then: background “comparing sites”, then a report titled “{site} and {other}”. <ul> <li>Tab order: “Report:” (read-only text), then “Copy All” (access key A), then Close.</li> <li>One line per difference, in the form “{kind} {slug}: {words}”. Examples: <ul> <li>“post about-us: titles differ, text differs”;</li> <li>“page contact: only on {other}”;</li> <li>“post news: publish here, draft there”;</li> <li>“category events: descriptions differ”.</li> </ul></li> <li>When nothing differs: “Their posts, pages, patterns, categories, tags and menus are the same.”</li> <li>Always last: “Not compared: media files, revisions, menu links, settings, people, plugins and themes.”</li> <li>Limits are said: “only the first 2000 on {site} compared”, and “not compared: {site} said {why}” when a kind could not be read.</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: nothing spoken; the report opens. That is right under GATE-13, since the dialog title and text are read anyway.</li> <li>Undo: nothing to undo.</li> <li>F1: “What differs between the two sites, by kind and address (slug), and what is not compared.” On the text it adds “Read it line by line; copy any part with Ctrl+C.”</li> <li>Cancel: Escape at the chooser.</li> <li>Without permission: none needed; what cannot be read is said in the report.</li> <li>Code: workbench_snapshot.py (compare_sites, COMPARE_KINDS, COMPARE_OMITS), wordpress_workbench_bold.py (do_compare_sites).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: status words are WordPress’s raw values (“publish here, future there”), not the plain words used elsewhere (“published”, “scheduled”).</li> <li>Review note: custom post types are not compared, and the omissions line does not name them.</li> <li>Review note: it is a dead end. Nothing in the report leads to Copy to Site or Make Them the Same for a line, so acting on a difference means finding the item again by hand.</li> <li>Review note: the report is one flat list with no per-kind headings or counts at the top. On a big pair of sites the person must arrow through every line to learn the scale.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="export-site-as-wxr">Export Site as WXR</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Tools menu, WXR Files submenu, Export Site as WXR (Alt+T, W, E). Keyless; the written reason (KEYLESS) is “Moving hosts or keeping a copy: a few times a site’s life (rule 9).”</li> <li>Submenu help: “WordPress export files (WXR), written and read on this computer.” Row help: “This site, or part of it, as a WordPress export file any WordPress imports.”</li> <li>What opens, step 1: a chooser titled “Export Site as WXR”, list labelled “Export:” (access key E). The choices: <ul> <li>“Everything: posts, pages, patterns, other types, files, menus, comments”</li> <li>“Posts only”</li> <li>“Pages only”</li> <li>“Posts and pages in a date range…”</li> <li>“Posts in one category…”</li> <li>Help: “What of {site} goes in the file. A part takes only the people, terms and files its items use, as WordPress’s own exporter does; comments and menus go only with Everything.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Date range: two text questions in a row, both titled “Export Site as WXR”. <ul> <li>“From (year-month-day, or blank):” (access key F), then “To (year-month-day, or blank):” (access key T).</li> <li>Help: “A day written as 2026-01-31. Leave From blank to start at the first post, To blank to end today. Both days are included.”</li> <li>A bad day says “{text} is not a day written as year-month-day, such as 2026-01-31.” and the export stops.</li> </ul></li> <li>One category: the categories are read in the background (“reading {site}’s categories”), then a chooser “Category:” with rows like “News (12 posts)”. Help: “The posts filed under this category go in the file.” With none: “{site} has no categories QUILL can read.”</li> <li>Then a standard Save dialog titled “Export Site as WXR”. The filter is “WordPress export files (*.xml)”, and it asks before overwriting. A name with no extension gets “.xml”.</li> <li>Announced after: “{Wrote sentence} {check sentence}”. <ul> <li>Example: “Wrote site.xml: 12 post, 3 page, 40 attachment, 2 wp_block, 5 comments, 2 authors, 9 terms. site.xml is a well-formed WXR 1.2 file with 62 items.”</li> <li>A report titled “Export Site as WXR” then opens: “{site}: {scope words}.”, the same sentence, then “Left out:” with one line per thing. The lines always include “what only WordPress’s own exporter carries: custom fields REST does not show, comment, term and user meta, post passwords, and serialised plugin settings”.</li> <li>If the check failed, a second sentence: “The file did not pass its check; it is kept so you can look at it.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Undo: nothing to undo; the file stays.</li> <li>F1: the chooser help quoted. The report’s help is “What went into the WXR file, the check of it, then what was left out.”</li> <li>Cancel: any step stops quietly, the Save dialog included.</li> <li>Without permission: reads only. Items the account cannot read are named in the left-out list (“{what} that are not published: the account may not read drafts, private or scheduled ones”).</li> <li>Code: wxr_export.py (export_wxr, Scope, ExportReport), wxr_read.py (validate_wxr), workbench_site_files_actions.py (WXR_SUB, KEYLESS), wordpress_workbench_wxr.py (do_export_wxr).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: the counts are spoken with WordPress post-type slugs and never go plural: “12 post, 40 attachment, 2 wp_block”. Use the plain names (“posts”, “files”, “synced patterns”).</li> <li>Review note: the outcome is spoken and then the report opens with the same sentence. The dialog’s arrival cuts the speech off, and the person hears it twice. Speak nothing, or open nothing (GATE-13).</li> <li>Review note: one bad date ends the whole export, and From and To are not checked against each other, so From after To gives an empty file. Ask again on the same field instead.</li> <li>Review note: the Save dialog suggests no file name (such as “{site} {date}.xml”), so the person types one every time.</li> <li>Review note: an Everything export writes commenters’ emails and IP addresses (<code>wp:comment_author_email</code>, <code>wp:comment_author_IP</code>) and authors’ emails to a plain file. That matches WordPress, but neither the chooser nor the report says the file holds personal data.</li> <li>Review note: no Practice Site crossing guard. That is correct for the export itself, but see Import below.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="import-wxr-as-drafts">Import WXR as Drafts</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Tools menu, WXR Files, Import WXR as Drafts (Alt+T, W, I). Keyless; the reason is “Moving content in happens once per move (rule 9).” Row help: “A WordPress export file from any site, previewed, then made here as drafts.”</li> <li>What opens, step 1: a standard Open dialog titled “Import WXR as Drafts”, with the same .xml filter; the file must exist.</li> <li>Step 2: the file is read and matched in the background (“reading {file name}”). With nothing usable: “{file} has nothing {site} can take as a draft.”</li> <li>Step 3, when authors do not match and there is more than one person on the site: a chooser titled “Import WXR as Drafts”. <ul> <li>List labelled “Give unmatched authors’ items to:” (access key G), with the site’s people.</li> <li>Help: “{n} authors in the file have no account on {site} with the same login or email: {first five}.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Step 4: a preview titled “Import WXR as Drafts”. <ul> <li>Summary: “Make {n} items from {file} on {site}, all as drafts? Ctrl+Z moves them to the Trash afterwards.”</li> <li>Each change: “From {title} ({url}), WXR {version}.”; “Author {login} is {person} here.” or “Author {login} is not on this site; given to {person}.”; “New {category/tag/term}: {name}.”; one “{Type}: {title}.” per item; “{n} files to download from {hosts} …”; and “Left out: …” lines.</li> <li>Button: “Import as Drafts” (the default), and Cancel.</li> </ul></li> <li>Step 5, only when the file names attachments: a chooser titled “Files in the Export”. <ul> <li>List labelled “Files:”, with the second choice selected first.</li> <li>Choices: “Download the {n} files from {hosts} and upload them to {site}” and “Skip the files; the text keeps pointing at the old site”.</li> <li>Help: “Downloading asks {hosts} for each file by the address the export names, over HTTPS, up to 64 MB each, sending no password. Skipping leaves the pictures where they are.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: “Imported {count} of {n} items from {file} into {site} as drafts, with {uploaded} files. Ctrl+Z moves the drafts to the Trash.” Then a report titled “Import WXR as Drafts” with every term, file and item line (“Made the category News.”, “Uploaded photo.jpg.”, “Not made: {title}: {why}”, and so on). The list then reloads.</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z (when at least one draft was made) speaks “Moved {n} imported drafts to the Trash.” It adds “Not moved: …” for any it could not move, and “The {n} uploaded files and {n} new terms stay; remove them from Media and the term lists if you do not want them.”</li> <li>F1: the help strings above. The report’s help is “What happened to each term, file and item, then what was left out.”</li> <li>Cancel: Escape at any step, the Files question included, sends nothing.</li> <li>Without permission: not found in code. No <code>write_refusal</code> is called, so Read Only access, Safe Mode and View As Role do not stop the import; only WordPress refuses, item by item.</li> <li>Code: wxr_read.py (read_wxr), wxr_import.py (plan_import, send_import, undo_import, ImportPlan.lines), wordpress_workbench_wxr.py (do_import_wxr, _import_planned, _send_import).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note (critical): the import skips Workbench Access, Safe Mode and View As Role, as Put Back does. It can make hundreds of drafts, terms and uploads on a site marked Read Only.</li> <li>Review note: the order of questions is backwards. The person presses “Import as Drafts” and is then asked about files, and Cancel there throws away the yes they just gave. Ask about files before the preview, and show the choice in the preview.</li> <li>Review note: the spoken result always ends “Ctrl+Z moves the drafts to the Trash”, even when nothing was made. In that case no undo is recorded, and Ctrl+Z says “… This cannot be undone.” The two sentences contradict each other.</li> <li>Review note: no Practice Site guard. A WXR exported from the Practice Site can be imported into a real site with no crossing question, which is the very path the guard exists to stop.</li> <li>Review note: undo leaves the new terms and uploaded files behind. It says so, but the person must then clean up by hand across two lists.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h2 id="by-name">By Name</h2> <p>By Name is a node under each site (Sites > {site} > By Name) that lists everything on the site by its name or value: terms, custom field values, authors, media, synced patterns, links, blocks and shortcodes, forms and template parts. Each row is one value and where it is used, so a change made from the row reaches every post and page that uses it, after a preview item by item. The lists come from one scan of the site, kept for five minutes. Check Every Link, the newest command, lives on these rows.</p> <h3 id="the-by-name-node-and-its-children">The By Name node and its children</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: <ul> <li>In the tree: Sites, a site, then By Name; Right Arrow opens the children.</li> <li>No Go chord and no menu row.</li> <li>The By Name node itself has no loader, so selecting it shows an empty list with its help in the details pane.</li> </ul></li> <li>Children, in order (each loads “scan:{kind}”, columns “Value” and “Used”): <ul> <li>Every Term</li> <li>Field Values</li> <li>Authors</li> <li>Media Usage</li> <li>Patterns Used</li> <li>Links</li> <li>Blocks and Shortcodes</li> <li>Forms Used</li> <li>Template Parts</li> </ul></li> <li>What each row says: “Value” is the name (“News (category)”, “Venue: Main Hall”, “Sam”, “logo.png”, an address); “Used” is “{n} posts” or “{n} places”. The detail is “{name}: in {n} places. Used on: {first 20 titles}…”.</li> <li>A site that refused a list shows a first row “Not read on {site}”. Its detail: “On {site}, QUILL could not read {routes and reasons}. What is listed from {site} may be incomplete, and nothing there is counted as unused or missing.”</li> <li>F1 (node help): <ul> <li>By Name: “Everything on this site by name: terms, fields, authors, media, links, blocks.”</li> <li>Every Term: “Every term of every taxonomy, with how many posts each has.”</li> <li>Field Values: “Every custom field value, and how many items have it.”</li> <li>Authors: “Every author, with what they have written by status.”</li> <li>Media Usage: “Every file in the library, and where it is used.”</li> <li>Patterns Used: “Synced patterns, and the pages that use them.”</li> <li>Links: “Every link in the content, and the pages it is on. Check Every Link, on a row’s context menu, checks them all in the background.”</li> <li>Blocks and Shortcodes: “Blocks and shortcodes, and the pages that use them.”</li> <li>Forms Used: “Forms placed in pages, and where.”</li> <li>Template Parts: “A block theme’s template parts, and the templates that place them.”</li> </ul></li> <li>F1 (row help, kind “value”): “One value, wherever it is used. Enter lists where. Under Links, Check Every Link checks the whole site’s links in the background.”</li> <li>Code: workbench_places.py (BY_NAME_NODES, places_rows “value”), workbench_across.py (SITE_KINDS, scan_site, kind_rows), wordpress_workbench_across.py (site_scan, scan_rows), workbench_node_help.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: F5 does not re-read. The scan is kept for five minutes (<code>_SCAN_SECONDS = 300</code>), and <code>refresh</code> does not clear it; only a By Name change does. After editing on the site, F5 shows the old answer, silently.</li> <li>Review note: the scan reads at most 10 pages of 100 (1000 posts and 1000 pages, and the same for terms, media and people), and a cap is never said. On a bigger site, Rename Everywhere and Replace Everywhere change “everywhere” except the items past 1000, and the preview looks complete. The unread note covers refusals, not truncation.</li> <li>Review note: the By Name node’s F1 lists “terms, fields, authors, media, links, blocks” and leaves out patterns, forms and template parts, which are three of its nine children.</li> <li>Review note: only posts and pages are scanned (<code>wp/v2/posts</code>, <code>wp/v2/pages</code>). Custom types that use a term, field or link are not counted, so a Rename can leave them behind. Check Every Link, by contrast, includes custom types.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-value-rows-context-menu">The value row’s context menu</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Applications key or Shift+F10 on a row.</li> <li>The menu has eight actions plus Properties, so it splits: <ul> <li>Top: Where It Is Used, Rename Everywhere…, Split…, Merge Into…, Reassign…, Replace Everywhere…</li> <li>Then “More”, which holds Find Items Missing This Field and Check Every Link…</li> <li>Then the window’s own “Show Only” and “Hide” cell-filter submenus.</li> <li>Last: Properties…</li> </ul></li> <li>Every action is offered on every By Name kind. An action a kind does not allow stays enabled and, when chosen, speaks the reason from the scope matrix. Examples: <ul> <li>Rename on a Links row: “Replace changes the address on every page.”</li> <li>Merge on an author: “WordPress joins two people only when one is deleted, with reassignment, in People.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Keyless: every value command has a written reason in KEYLESS (workbench_places.py): <ul> <li>Where It Is Used: “The rows’ Enter, so it needs no chord of its own.”</li> <li>Rename Everywhere: “Renaming everywhere is occasional and previewed (rule 9).”</li> <li>Split: “Splitting a term or value is occasional and previewed (rule 9).”</li> <li>Merge Into: “Merging is occasional and previewed (rule 9).”</li> <li>Reassign: “Reassigning is occasional and previewed (rule 9).”</li> <li>Replace Everywhere: “Replacing everywhere is occasional and previewed (rule 9).”</li> <li>Find Items Missing This Field: “A check made now and then, on field rows only (rule 9).”</li> <li>Check Every Link: “A whole-site run after a reorganisation, rarely (rule 9).” (in workbench_gap_actions.py)</li> </ul></li> <li>Code: workbench_places.py, workbench_gap_actions.py (CHECK_LINKS), quill/core/workbench/actions.py (MENU_TOP_LIMIT 8, menu_for), workbench_frame_actions.py (_popup, can_run), workbench_scope_matrix.py (by_name_support).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note (critical for discoverability): Check Every Link, the only command built for Links rows, is buried under More. Meanwhile Rename, Split, Merge and Reassign, which all refuse on a Links row, sit on top. On every other kind, Check Every Link sits under More and refuses. The menu does not adapt to the kind it is on.</li> <li>Review note: refused actions are enabled rather than disabled or hidden, so a screen-reader user tries each one to learn which work. The scope matrix already knows (<code>by_name_support</code>). Use it in <code>supports</code> so they read “unavailable”, or leave them out.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="where-it-is-used-enter">Where It Is Used (Enter)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter on a value row (the default), or the context menu. Row help: “Each page that uses it.”</li> <li>What opens: a report titled “Where {value} Is Used”. <ul> <li>Text: one title per line, or “Nowhere in the posts and pages read.”</li> <li>Buttons: Copy All, Close.</li> </ul></li> <li>F1: “Each post or page that uses it, one a line.”</li> <li>Review note: it is a read-only text report, not a list, so there is no way to open one of those posts from it. Compare the Broken Links window, which has “Open in Editor”.</li> </ul> <h3 id="rename-everywhere">Rename Everywhere</h3> <ul> <li>Allowed on: terms (name and slug), field values, template parts (title). Refused on the rest with the matrix reason.</li> <li>Tier and permission: WRITE. Workbench Access must be Full. Terms need manage_categories, template parts edit_theme_options, everything else edit_others_posts. A refusal speaks the access or role sentence.</li> <li>What opens, by kind: <ul> <li>Terms: “New name:” (access key N), filled with the current name; then “New slug (empty keeps it):” (access key S). Both titled “Rename Everywhere”.</li> <li>Template parts: “New title:”.</li> <li>Fields: “New value for {key}:”.</li> <li>An empty answer, or an unchanged one, stops quietly.</li> </ul></li> <li>Preview: titled “Rename Everywhere…”, with the ellipsis in the title. <ul> <li>Summary: “{value} is renamed. {n} changes on {site}.” plus the unread note if any.</li> <li>Lines: “{site}: {title}: {words}”, up to 200, then “and {n} more”, then any “Not read on …” lines.</li> <li>Button: “Rename Everywhere” (the default), and Cancel.</li> </ul></li> <li>Nothing to change: “Nothing the scan read uses {value}.”</li> <li>Announced after: “Rename Everywhere: {good} of {n} changed.” If any failed: “ {n} not: {first failure}.“ and a report “Rename Everywhere: Each Item” with “Done. …” and “Not done. …” lines. Items changed on the site since the scan are left alone and reported (the conflict stamp).</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z sends each item’s “before” back: “Put {n} back as they were.”</li> <li>Cancel: any step, nothing sent.</li> <li>Code: workbench_by_name.py (plan_term_rename, plan_part_rename, plan_field, apply_edits, undo_edits, outcome_report), wordpress_workbench_by_name.py (do_rename_value, _bn_send).</li> <li>Review note: the preview’s title keeps the menu’s ellipsis (“Rename Everywhere…”). Every other preview in the window has a plain title. It is read as the dialog title on every open.</li> </ul> <h3 id="split">Split</h3> <ul> <li>Allowed on: terms (chosen items move to a new term), field values (chosen items get a new value), authors (chosen items go to another person).</li> <li>What opens: <ul> <li>A check-box list titled “Split”, labelled “Items that move”. <ul> <li>The group’s access key is stripped (<code>prompt.replace("&", "")</code>).</li> <li>Help: “Check each item that moves. The ones left unchecked stay as they are. Space checks or unchecks.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Then, by kind: terms get “New term:”; fields get “New value for {key}:”; authors get a chooser “Give them to:” with help “The new author.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Preview: “Split…”. Summary “{n} items move to the new term {name}.” (or “{n} items change.” or “{n} items go to {person}.”). Button “Split”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Split: {good} of {n} changed.”</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z puts items back, and for terms deletes the new term: “Put {n} back as they were. {name} was deleted again.”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: pressing OK with nothing checked does nothing and says nothing (<code>if not picked: return None</code>). Say “Nothing checked, so nothing moves.”</li> <li>Review note: the check-box group has no access key. It is the only control, so that is minor.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="merge-into-1">Merge Into</h3> <ul> <li>Allowed on: terms (every item moves to another term, then this term is deleted for good), field values (every item takes another value already in use).</li> <li>What opens: a chooser titled “Merge Into”, labelled “Merge into:”. <ul> <li>For terms, the rows are the other terms of the same taxonomy. Help: “The term every item moves to. This term is then deleted.”</li> <li>For fields, the rows are the other values of that field. Help: “The value every item with this one takes.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Preview: “Merge Into…”. Button “Merge Into”. <ul> <li>Terms summary: “Every item moves to {target}; this term is deleted. {n} changes on {site}. This cannot be undone.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: “Merge Into: {good} of {n} changed. The old term was deleted.” or “… The old term was kept, because not every item moved.”</li> <li>Undo: terms, none; Ctrl+Z says “{said} This cannot be undone.” Fields are undoable.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: tier mismatch. This row is Tier WRITE (<code>merge_value</code>), while Merge Into on a term row is Tier DESTRUCTIVE (workbench_actions.py), for the same irreversible term deletion. Under Organize access both are refused, but the tiers should agree, and DESTRUCTIVE elsewhere means confirm by name.</li> <li>Review note: the chooser lists every other term with no post counts, so the person cannot tell “News (300 posts)” from “news (1 post)”. The By Name row itself shows the counts.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="reassign">Reassign</h3> <ul> <li>Allowed on: terms (items move to another existing term, which stays) and authors (every item goes to another person).</li> <li>What opens: a chooser titled “Reassign”. Terms: “Move items to:”, help “The term every item moves to. This term stays, with no items.” Authors: “Give everything to:”, help “The new author.”</li> <li>Preview “Reassign…”, summary “Every item moves to {target}.” or “Everything goes to {person}.”, button “Reassign”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Reassign: {good} of {n} changed.” Undo: Ctrl+Z puts them back.</li> </ul> <h3 id="replace-everywhere">Replace Everywhere</h3> <ul> <li>Allowed on: media (another library file), synced patterns (another pattern), links (another address), blocks (shortcodes only), forms (another form by number).</li> <li>What opens, titled “Replace Everywhere”: <ul> <li>Media and patterns: a chooser “Use instead:”, help “What every page uses instead.”</li> <li>Links: “New address:”, help “The address every link to this one gets instead.”</li> <li>Blocks: “New shortcode name:”, help “Another shortcode’s name, such as gallery.”</li> <li>Forms: “Other form, such as Form 7:”, help “The form every page places instead.”</li> </ul></li> <li>If the plan finds nothing to change, its reason is spoken.</li> <li>Preview “Replace Everywhere…”, summary “Every page using {key} uses {new}. {n} changes on {site}.”, button “Replace Everywhere”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Replace Everywhere: {good} of {n} changed.” Undo: Ctrl+Z puts every item’s content back.</li> <li>Review note: the media chooser lists bare file names from the library (“IMG_2041.jpg”), with no title or alt text. For a screen-reader user choosing a replacement picture by file name alone is guesswork.</li> </ul> <h3 id="find-items-missing-this-field">Find Items Missing This Field</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: the context menu’s More submenu, on a Field Values row. Elsewhere it says “Find Items Missing This Field works on a row of By Name > Field Values.”</li> <li>What opens: a report titled “Items Missing {key}”, listing one title per line, or “Every post and page read has a value for {key}.”, plus the unread note.</li> <li>F1: “Each post or page with no value for the field, one a line.”</li> <li>Review note: like Where It Is Used, it is a dead-end text report with no Open.</li> </ul> <h3 id="check-every-link">Check Every Link</h3> <p>State on 2026-11-14 (code checked at the end of this pass): the row action, the handler, the core module, the status-bar cell, the node and row help, and two tests exist. Not yet present: an entry in the engineering log (docs/design/wordpress.md has no Check Every Link entry), any user guide, keyboard reference or command catalogue text (nothing under docs/ names it), and a live-site run. Those parts are in progress.</p> <ul> <li>How to reach it: <ul> <li>Context menu on a By Name > Links row, under More: “Check Every Link…”. Applications key, then down to More, Right Arrow, then down past Find Items Missing This Field.</li> <li>Help: “Every link in every published post, page and item on this site, checked by the site in the background, with a running count in the status bar. Choose it again, or press Enter on the status bar’s Link check, to stop. The broken links are listed with what holds each.”</li> <li>Keyless (reason quoted above).
