Tier READ, no capability needed.</li> </ul></li> <li>On the wrong row: “Check Every Link works on a row of By Name > Links.”</li> <li>What opens: a Yes/Cancel question titled “Check Every Link”. <ul> <li>Wording: “Check every link in {site}’s published posts, pages and items? The site fetches each address itself, which can take some minutes; it runs in the background, the status bar counts, and choosing this again stops it.”</li> <li>Buttons: “Check”, and “Cancel” (the default).</li> </ul></li> <li>While it runs: the status bar’s “Link check” cell. <ul> <li>Reach it with F6 from the details pane, or Shift+F6 from the tree.</li> <li>First “Reading {site}’s published items”, then “{checked} of {total} links on {site}, {broken} broken. Enter stops”. It repaints every fifth link.</li> <li>Cell help: “Check Every Link’s count while it runs. Enter stops it; what it found is kept.”</li> <li>Its context menu: “Stop Checking Links”.</li> <li>When no check runs the cell is empty.</li> </ul></li> <li>Stopping: choose Check Every Link again on any value row, press Enter on the cell, or use its menu. <ul> <li>Spoken: “Stopping the link check; what it found so far is kept.”</li> <li>If none runs: “No link check is running.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: <ul> <li>No link-check route on the site: “{site} does not offer WordPress’s link-check route, so its links cannot be checked from here. Check This Page still works.”</li> <li>Otherwise one of: <ul> <li>“{site}’s {n} published items link to no other pages.”</li> <li>“All {n} links in {site}’s {n} published items work.”</li> <li>“{broken} of {n} links on {site} lead nowhere.”</li> <li>“Stopped after {checked} of {n} links on {site}; {broken} links lead nowhere so far.”</li> </ul></li> </ul></li> <li>Then, if anything is broken: a window titled “Broken Links on {site}”. <ul> <li>“Broken links:” list. Each row: “{url}, in {title} ({kind}), … and {n} more: {why}.”</li> <li>“Details:” text: the url, why, then each holder “{title} ({kind}): {link}”.</li> <li>Buttons: “Open in Editor” (access key O; help “Open the first item that holds this link in the editor, to fix it.”) and Close.</li> </ul></li> <li>Undo: nothing changes on the site.</li> <li>F1: the node and row help quoted under By Name, and the cell help.</li> <li>Cancel: Cancel on the question starts nothing.</li> <li>Without permission: item types the account cannot read (401, 403 or 404) are skipped silently.</li> <li>Code: workbench_link_check.py (has_link_route, collect_links, check_links, outcome, BrokenLink.spoken), workbench_gap_actions.py (CHECK_LINKS, KEYLESS), wordpress_workbench_gaps.py (do_check_every_link, stop_link_check, link_check_status, _show_broken), wordpress_workbench_window_status.py (the Link check cell), link_targets.py (read_target).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note (critical for discoverability): see the menu note above. The command is under More, and only one of nine By Name kinds can use it. A Links node command, or a node context-menu row, would be easier to find than a row command that ignores its row.</li> <li>Review note: the label never changes. While a check runs, the same “Check Every Link…” row, with its ellipsis promising a dialog, silently means Stop. Show “Stop Checking Links” while one runs.</li> <li>Review note: one check at a time across all sites, and choosing it on another site’s Links row stops the running check instead of starting one. The person hears “Stopping the link check…” when they meant “check this site”.</li> <li>Review note: skipped types are silent. A custom type the account cannot read is dropped with no line, so “All 300 links … work” can be said for a partial read. By Name’s unread rows exist for exactly this.</li> <li>Review note: the result is spoken and then the Broken Links window opens with the same sentence as its purpose. The window title interrupts the speech (GATE-13, said twice).</li> <li>Review note: “Open in Editor” opens only the first item that holds the link. A link on 22 pages needs 22 trips, with no way to choose which holder; the details pane lists them but they cannot be opened. Replace Everywhere is the bulk fix and is not offered from this window.</li> <li>Review note: the check runs on the window’s two-worker pool, which list loading also uses. A run that takes minutes takes one worker, and with any other long job (a Practice Site start, a big Put Back plan) no node in the window loads until one finishes. See the Practice Site note.</li> <li>Review note: it competes with Needs My Attention > Broken Links (a sample of the first 40 links). The two are not linked: the Broken Links node does not offer “check everything”, and this command’s results do not feed that node.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h2 id="across-sites">Across Sites</h2> <p>Across Sites puts the same name side by side on every connected site, or on a Site Set: tags and categories, plugins, themes, people, settings, menus, synced patterns and application passwords, plus the Fleet, which is every site in one list. Each row says where the copies differ and which sites lack it, and its context menu can make them the same, promote a plugin to a multisite network, revoke a leaked password everywhere, compare global styles, or install a missing plugin. Site Sets and Across Sites Scope decide which sites are included. The Calendar is a separate account-level node that also spans every site, so it is covered here too.</p> <h3 id="the-across-sites-node">The Across Sites node</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: <ul> <li>Go menu, Across Sites (Alt+G, O); chord Ctrl+0. Go help: “The same name on every site.”</li> <li>It is an account-level node in the tree.</li> <li>The node has no loader: it shows an empty list with its help in the details pane, and Right Arrow opens its children.</li> </ul></li> <li>Children (each loads “across:{kind}”; columns Name, Sites, Versions, Missing): <ul> <li>Tags and Categories</li> <li>Plugins</li> <li>Themes</li> <li>People</li> <li>Settings</li> <li>Menus</li> <li>Synced Patterns</li> <li>Application Passwords</li> <li>Fleet (columns Site, State, Health, Backups, Security, Updates)</li> </ul></li> <li>Each row: <ul> <li>Name: the name, or the email for people.</li> <li>Sites: how many sites have it.</li> <li>Versions: how many variants.</li> <li>Missing: the sites without it.</li> <li>Detail: “{sentence}. Variants: {variant}: {sites}; … . Missing from: {sites}. Not read on: {sites}.” Plugin and theme rows then add the across_note, such as “ Activating, deactivating and installing from WordPress.org work here; updating and automatic updates are not on WordPress’s REST API, so Open in Dashboard goes to each site’s Plugins screen.“</li> <li>A site that refused the list is a first row, and is never counted as missing.</li> </ul></li> <li>F1 (node help): <ul> <li>Across Sites: “The same name on every site, or a Site Set: where copies differ, what is missing.”</li> <li>Tags and Categories: “Each tag and category by name, on every site: differing slugs, sites without.”</li> <li>Plugins: “Each plugin by name, on every site: versions, active or not, missing.”</li> <li>Themes: “Each theme by name, on every site.”</li> <li>People: “Each person, by email, on every site, with their role on each.”</li> <li>Settings: “Each setting, on every site, and where the values drift.”</li> <li>Application Passwords: “Each application password by name and person, on every site.”</li> <li>Menus: “Each menu by name, on every site: its items, and the sites without it.”</li> <li>Synced Patterns: “Each synced pattern by title, on every site: its content, where missing.”</li> <li>Fleet: “Every site in one list: health, backups, security and updates, each known, unknown, unavailable or stale.”</li> </ul></li> <li>F1 (row help): <ul> <li>across: “One name across sites. Alt+Enter shows its variants and the sites missing it.”</li> <li>fleet: “One site in the fleet. Enter selects it in the tree; Alt+Enter reads each state.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Code: workbench_places.py (ACROSS_NODES, places_rows “across” and “fleet”), workbench_across.py (ACROSS_KINDS, gather_across, across_rows), workbench_fleet.py, wordpress_workbench_across.py (across_list, across_sheet), workbench_node_help.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: Ctrl+0 lands on an empty container. The person hears the node and an empty list, and must know to press Right Arrow. Ctrl+1 (Needs My Attention) avoids this by landing on Inbox, a child. Ctrl+0 could land on Tags and Categories, or the container could list its children as rows.</li> <li>Review note: a row’s detail is one long run-on sentence of variants, missing sites and, for plugins and themes, the same REST disclaimer on every row. Arrowing down a 40-plugin list repeats the disclaimer 40 times if the details pane is read. Put the disclaimer in the node’s help once.</li> <li>Review note: the Plugins variant is “version {v}, {status}”, which mixes two facts into one variant. See Make Them the Same.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="across-row-context-menu">Across row context menu</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Applications key or Shift+F10 on an across row. The rows (seven actions plus Properties, so no More): <ul> <li>Read in Full (default, Enter): moves focus to the details pane.</li> <li>Make Them the Same…</li> <li>Promote to Network…</li> <li>Revoke Everywhere…</li> <li>Compare Global Styles</li> <li>Install on Sites Missing It…</li> <li>Open in Dashboard</li> <li>Then Show Only and Hide submenus, then Properties…</li> </ul></li> <li>Keyless reasons (workbench_places.py KEYLESS and workbench_admin_actions.py): <ul> <li>Make Them the Same: “Aligning sites is occasional and previewed site by site (rule 9).”</li> <li>Promote to Network: “Done once per plugin on a network, rarely (rule 9).”</li> <li>Compare Global Styles: “Comparing styles across sites is occasional (rule 9).”</li> <li>Revoke Everywhere: “Revoking a leaked password everywhere is rare (rule 9).”</li> <li>Install on Sites Missing It: “Filling the gaps across sites is occasional and previewed.” (workbench_admin_actions.py; it cites no rule number, unlike its neighbours).</li> <li>Open in Dashboard: “Leaves QUILL for the browser, now and then (rule 9).”</li> </ul></li> <li>Every action is offered on every across kind and refuses with the matrix reason on kinds it does not serve. Examples: <ul> <li>“Revoke Everywhere works on a row of Across Sites > Application Passwords.”</li> <li>“Install on Sites Missing It works on Across Sites > Plugins and Themes.”</li> <li>“Promote to Network works on a plugin, in Across Sites > Plugins. {reason}”</li> </ul></li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note (critical): across rows carry no site (<code>WbRow("across", "", ...)</code>), so the window’s <code>refusal_for</code> returns nothing for every across action. Each handler must check access itself: <ul> <li>Make Them the Same and Revoke Everywhere check per site.</li> <li>Install on Sites Missing It checks nothing.</li> <li>Promote to Network checks only <code>activate_plugins</code>, not Workbench Access.</li> <li>Under Read Only access, Safe Mode or View As Role, those last two still write.</li> </ul></li> <li>Review note: Revoke Everywhere is irreversible (“a revoked password cannot be restored”) but is Tier WRITE, the same tier as Make Them the Same. It should be DESTRUCTIVE, like Delete Permanently, with a confirmation by name.</li> <li>Review note: five of seven actions refuse on most kinds. Hide the rows a kind cannot use; the scope matrix already says which.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="make-them-the-same">Make Them the Same</h3> <ul> <li>Allowed on: tags and categories (name, slug, description; made where missing), plugins (active or not), settings (the value), people (the role), synced patterns (content; made where missing), themes (opens each site’s Themes screen), menus (items copied as links). Application passwords are refused: “A password is made on each site and shown once; it cannot be copied.”</li> <li>What opens, step 1: a chooser titled “Make {name} the Same”. <ul> <li>Labelled “Which version wins:” (access key W). The rows are the variant words, such as “slug news” or “version 2.3, active”.</li> <li>Help: “Every site in scope is changed to match the version you choose.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Step 2, when every holder of the winner is the Practice Site and a real site would change: the crossing question, titled “Make {name} the Same: from the Practice Site to a Real Site”.</li> <li>Step 3: each target site is checked. Refused sites become “Not changed. {site}: {name}: {reason}” lines. A site the list could not be read on is never written to: “not read: {why}; nothing is sent there”.</li> <li>Step 4, the preview, titled “Make {name} the Same”: <ul> <li>Ordinary kinds: <ul> <li>Summary: “{n} sites get {name} as {winner}.”</li> <li>Lines: “{site}: {name}: {words}”, for example “slug news-old becomes news”, “inactive becomes active”, “role author becomes editor”, “made, as the winner has it”. Then the “Not changed. …” lines.</li> <li>Button: “Make Them the Same”.</li> </ul></li> <li>Themes: <ul> <li>Summary: “WordPress’s REST API cannot activate a theme, so QUILL opens each site’s Themes screen in your browser, to activate {name} there.”</li> <li>Lines: “{site}: opens Appearance > Themes for {stylesheet}”.</li> <li>Button: “Open Them”.</li> </ul></li> <li>Menus: <ul> <li>Summary: “{n} sites get the {name} menu’s items as links, pointed at each site’s own address. Their items are replaced, and this cannot be undone.”</li> <li>Lines: “{site}: replaces its items” or “{site}: makes the menu”.</li> <li>Button: “Make Them the Same”.</li> </ul></li> </ul></li> <li>Nothing to send: “Nothing to send: {first refusal}.” or “Every site already matches.”</li> <li>Announced after: <ul> <li>Ordinary kinds: “{name}: {good} of {n} sites changed to {winner}.”</li> <li>Themes: “Opened {n} Themes screens; activate {name} on each.”</li> <li>Menus: “{name}: {good} of {n} sites now have the same menu.”</li> <li>If any site failed, a report “Make {name} the Same: Each Site” with “Done.” and “Not done.” lines.</li> </ul></li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z puts back every edit that has a “before” (“Put {n} back as they were.”). Made-where-missing items and menus cannot be undone.</li> <li>Code: workbench_across.py (same_everywhere_plan), workbench_across_ops.py (MAKE_SAME_KINDS, make_same_edits, _edit_for, menu_copy, theme_screens), wordpress_workbench_across.py (do_make_same, _make_themes_same, _make_menus_same, _across_refusal).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note (critical): for plugins, the variant includes the version, but the edit sends only <code>{"status": ...}</code>. Choose “version 2.3, active” when another site has “version 2.1, active”: the plan says “changes from version 2.1, active to version 2.3, active”, the request changes nothing, and the outcome says “2 of 2 sites changed to version 2.3, active”. That is a false success. Either compare plugin status only, or say plainly that versions are not changed and point to Open in Dashboard.</li> <li>Review note: Make Them the Same on Settings can push values such as <code>siteurl</code>, <code>home</code> or <code>admin_email</code> from one site to the others. Those are in <code>wp/v2/settings</code>. The preview lists them, but nothing warns that some settings are site-identity values that must never be the same.</li> <li>Review note: the winner chooser shows only variant words, not which sites hold each variant or how many. The person must read the row’s Properties first to know which is the majority.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="promote-to-network">Promote to Network</h3> <ul> <li>Allowed on: Across Sites > Plugins rows only.</li> <li>What happens: each site holding the plugin is checked in the background (“checking for a network”). <ul> <li>Not a network: “{site} is not a multisite network: its plugin schema has no network-active …” (the full sentence is in workbench_across_ops.py network_refusal).</li> <li>Lacking rights: “Promote to Network needs a super admin of {site}’s network.”</li> <li>If none qualifies, that first reason is spoken and nothing opens.</li> </ul></li> <li>Practice guard (together): titled “Promote to Network: the Practice Site and Real Sites Together”. Wording: “Promote to Network would change {real}, a real site that other people can see, in the same step as {practice}, the practice site on this computer. Practice and real sites are changed together only if you choose it here. Change {real} too?” Buttons “Change {site} Too” (or “Change Them Too”) and “Cancel” (the default).</li> <li>Preview, titled “Promote to Network”: <ul> <li>Summary: “{name} runs on every site of each network below, and no single site can deactivate it. WordPress refuses unless you are a super admin.”</li> <li>Lines: “{site}: becomes network-active” plus “Not changed. {why}”.</li> <li>Button: “Promote”.</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: “{name}: network-active on {good} of {n} networks.” A failure report opens if any failed.</li> <li>Undo: none. Ctrl+Z says “{said} This cannot be undone.”</li> <li>Without permission: only <code>activate_plugins</code> is checked. Workbench Access, Safe Mode and View As Role are not (see the access note above).</li> </ul> <h3 id="revoke-everywhere">Revoke Everywhere</h3> <ul> <li>Allowed on: Across Sites > Application Passwords rows. The key is “{password name}, {person}”.</li> <li>Checks: each site’s <code>write_refusal</code>, plus <code>edit_users</code> when the password is not yours. Refused sites become “Not changed.” lines. <ul> <li>Nothing to revoke: “Nothing to revoke: {first refusal}.” or “No site has it.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Practice guard (together), as above, with “Revoke Everywhere”.</li> <li>Preview, titled “Revoke Everywhere”: <ul> <li>Summary: “{name} is revoked on {n} sites. Anything signing in with it stops working, and this cannot be undone: a revoked password cannot be restored.”</li> <li>Lines: “{site}: revokes {name}”.</li> <li>Button: “Revoke” (the default).</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: “{name}: revoked on {good} of {n} sites. It cannot be undone.”</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z then says “{name}: revoked on {good} of {n} sites. It cannot be undone. This cannot be undone.”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: the Ctrl+Z sentence is doubled: “It cannot be undone. This cannot be undone.” <code>do_undo</code> appends its own “This cannot be undone.” to a summary that already says it.</li> <li>Review note: a destructive, irreversible action whose preview defaults to “Revoke” on Enter. Every other irreversible action in the window (Delete Permanently, Reset) asks with Cancel as the default.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="compare-global-styles">Compare Global Styles</h3> <ul> <li>Allowed on: Themes rows. On other kinds it speaks “Only themes have global styles.”</li> <li>What happens: every site in scope is read in the background (“comparing global styles”).</li> <li>Announced after: “{n} of {m} styles differ across {k} sites; {s} are the same.” or “No site’s global styles could be read.” Then a report “Compare Global Styles” with the summary, each unreadable site, and “{style}: {site} {value}; {site} {value}” for each style that differs.</li> <li>F1: “Each style that differs, with each site’s value; unread sites say why.”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: it compares every site’s styles in effect, not the selected theme’s. The row the person chose makes no difference beyond being a theme row.</li> <li>Review note: spoken, and then a report with the same sentence opens (GATE-13).</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="install-on-sites-missing-it">Install on Sites Missing It</h3> <ul> <li>Allowed on: Plugins and Themes rows. Otherwise “Install on Sites Missing It works on Across Sites > Plugins and Themes.” <ul> <li>Nothing missing: “Every site in scope already has {name}.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Practice guard: when only the Practice Site holds it, the crossing question titled “Install on Sites Missing It: from the Practice Site to a Real Site”.</li> <li>Themes: a preview titled “Install on Sites Missing It”. <ul> <li>Summary: “WordPress’s REST API cannot install a theme, so QUILL opens Appearance > Themes > Add New for {name} on {n} sites in your browser.”</li> <li>Lines: “{site}: opens Add New, searching for {slug}”. Button “Open Them”.</li> <li>Nothing is spoken after; the browser tabs open.</li> </ul></li> <li>Plugins: <ul> <li>Without a WordPress.org slug: “{name} does not say its WordPress.org name, so it cannot be installed.”</li> <li>Otherwise, on first use in the window, the WordPress.org consent question (Yes button “Ask”, default Cancel). Declining speaks “Nothing was sent to WordPress.org.” and the preview still opens, with “Not checked on WordPress.org.” as a line.</li> <li>Preview “Install on Sites Missing It”: “Each site installs {name} ({slug}) from WordPress.org, inactive. A plugin not in the WordPress.org directory fails on that site and the rest go on.” plus the update limits. Lines “{site}: installs {slug}, left inactive”. Button “Install”.</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: “{name} installed on {made} of {n} sites, inactive.” plus “ Not installed: {first three}“ when some failed.</li> <li>Undo: none recorded; nothing goes into Change History at all.</li> <li>Without permission: not found in code. No access or capability check on the target sites. WordPress refuses per site, and the refusal shows in “Not installed”.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note (critical): installs plugins with no Workbench Access, Safe Mode or View As Role check, and no <code>install_plugins</code> check.</li> <li>Review note: plugin installs are not recorded in Change History, so they do not appear in Edit > Change History either. Every other site change in this section is recorded.</li> <li>Review note: declining WordPress.org consent still goes straight to an install preview, which installs from WordPress.org anyway. Saying no to “ask WordPress.org” and then pressing Install sends the request to the site, which itself asks WordPress.org. The consent wording should make clear what “no” covers.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="open-in-dashboard">Open in Dashboard</h3> <ul> <li>On an across row, when more than one site applies: a chooser “Which site:”, help “The site whose wp-admin screen opens in your browser.”; the browser then opens that screen. On a row with no screen: “Open in Dashboard works on a plugin, a theme, or Across Sites row.”</li> <li>Review note: one site at a time. “Open it on every site missing it” is what Install already does for themes, but Open in Dashboard has no “all” choice.</li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-on-an-across-row-altenter">Properties on an across row (Alt+Enter)</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: a read-only sheet with three pages. <ul> <li>General: “About” (the detail).</li> <li>Variants: one “{variant}: {sites}” a line.</li> <li>Missing From: the missing sites and “Not read on {site}: {reasons}”, or “Every site in scope has it.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Review note: the sheet’s only purpose is reading; it adds no actions. Fine, but the row’s detail already holds the same text.</li> </ul> <h3 id="site-sets">Site Sets</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Site menu, Site Sets (Alt+S, T); chord Ctrl+Alt+T. Menu help “Name a set of your sites.”</li> <li>What opens, step 1: a text question titled “Site Sets”, labelled “Name of the set:” (access key N). Help: “Such as Client sites. A name you have used before changes that set.” Empty stops quietly.</li> <li>Step 2: a check-box list titled “Site Set: {name}”, labelled “Sites in the set” (access key stripped). Every connected site is listed; members are already checked. Help: “Check each site that belongs to the set. None checked removes the set. Space checks or unchecks.”</li> <li>Announced after: “{name}: {n} sites.” or “Removed {name}.”</li> <li>Stored: <code>workbench_options.json</code> beside the site list, by site display name.</li> <li>Undo: none (a local setting; not recorded).</li> <li>Cancel: either step, nothing saved.</li> <li>Code: workbench_options.py (site_sets), wordpress_workbench_across.py (do_site_sets).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: the person types a set name blind. Existing sets are never listed, so changing “Client sites” means remembering its exact spelling, and a typo silently makes a second set. Start with a chooser of existing sets plus “New Set…”.</li> <li>Review note: there is no rename. A set is deleted only by unchecking every site, which is not discoverable from the first dialog.</li> <li>Review note: members are stored by display name. Renaming a site, or a Practice Site picking up “ (practice)“, drops it from every set with no word said.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="across-sites-scope">Across Sites Scope</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Site menu, Across Sites Scope (Alt+S, O); chord Ctrl+Alt+Y. Menu help “Every site, or one set.”</li> <li>What opens: a chooser titled “Across Sites Scope”, labelled “Across:” (access key A). Rows: “Every site”, then each set name. Help: “Which sites Across Sites and the Fleet cover. Site Sets makes a set.”</li> <li>Announced after: “Across Sites covers every site.” or “Across Sites covers {set}.” The current list then reloads.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_across.py (do_scope, scope_sites).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note (critical for trust): the scope is invisible and short-lived. <ul> <li>It is not in Where Am I (Alt+1), not in the status bar, and not in the node help.</li> <li>It is kept only on the provider object (<code>self.scope</code>), so closing the window returns it to every site.</li> <li>The chooser always starts on “Every site”, whatever the current scope.</li> <li>A person who set “Client sites” yesterday and presses Make Them the Same today is changing every site.</li> </ul></li> <li>Review note: the scope covers Across Sites, the Fleet and Compare Global Styles only. The Calendar, Needs My Attention, Watches and Copy to Site’s site list ignore it. The help names “Across Sites and the Fleet”, which is accurate but easy to miss.</li> <li>Review note: if the chosen set is removed, the scope silently becomes every site (<code>members is None</code>).</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="calendar">Calendar</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Go menu, Calendar (Alt+G, D); chord Ctrl+3 (kept here because this window has no AI menu; the comment in workbench_window_menu.py records the 2026-11-13 decision). Go help “Every site’s content, by day.”</li> <li>What it lists: one row per day from 14 days ago onward, with every scheduled post and page however far ahead, from every connected site (not the Across scope), custom types included. <ul> <li>Columns: Day, Scheduled, Published.</li> <li>Day text: “Tuesday 12 November”. Detail: “Tuesday 12 November: 2 scheduled, 1 published. Enter lists them.”</li> <li>A site or type that would not answer is a first row: “Could not ask {site}, {kind}: {why}”.</li> </ul></li> <li>Day nodes: a dynamic “Day” child appears under Calendar for each day, labelled with the same day words. After a load that found new days, the tree is rebuilt.</li> <li>Row menu (kind “day”): “Open This Day” (default, help “List what the day holds.”), then Properties. Enter selects that day’s node in the tree and moves focus there. Its list has item rows (Title, Site, Status, Date) with the full item menu, including Move to Another Day… (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+D) and Copy to Site.</li> <li>F1: <ul> <li>Calendar node: “Every site’s scheduled and published content, a day to a node.”</li> <li>Day node: “What was published or is scheduled on one day, on every site.”</li> <li>Day row: “A day with scheduled or published content. Enter lists it.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Code: workbench_bold.py core (calendar_days, day_words), wordpress_workbench_bold.py (calendar_rows, day_rows, day_children, do_open_day, _days_changed).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: days run oldest first, so the list opens on two weeks ago. Today and the coming week, the part people plan with, are in the middle. Start at today, or select today’s row.</li> <li>Review note: the day words have no year, so late December and early January read the same pattern with no year cue.</li> <li>Review note: each status and type is capped at 300 (<code>max_pages=3</code>), and no row says so.</li> <li>Review note: rebuilding the tree after each Calendar load (<code>wx.CallAfter(self._days_changed)</code>) may make the screen reader re-announce the tree or move its virtual position. Worth a JAWS and NVDA check.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="fleet">Fleet</h3> <ul> <li>Under Across Sites. Rows: one per site in scope, with WordPress version, comments waiting, and Health, Backups, Security and Updates, each “known”, “unknown”, “unavailable” or “stale”.</li> <li>A site that does not answer: “not answering: {why}”, and each column says “unknown: the site did not answer”. A site that answered before keeps its last facts, marked “stale, as read {n minutes ago}; not answering now: {why}”.</li> <li>Row menu: “Open This Site” (default, “Select it in the tree.”), Properties.</li> <li>Review note: it is read only and that is said (“The Fleet only reads; it changes nothing.”). It is a good summary view, but nothing links a “critical” health state to Needs My Attention > Site Health.</li> </ul> <h2 id="needs-my-attention-and-watches">Needs My Attention and Watches</h2> <p>Needs My Attention is the account-level node that gathers, from every connected site, what is waiting on the person: comments to moderate, items waiting for review, scheduled items, their drafts, updates, health problems, missing alt text, broken links, spam, form responses and membership orders, plus Inbox, which mixes four of them. Watches are the background half: each kind is off until checked in Tools > Workbench Options, then the editor’s two-minute timer asks the sites at most every fifteen minutes and speaks what changed. Watches are a separate node under Needs My Attention that shows each Watch’s state.</p> <h3 id="needs-my-attention-and-inbox">Needs My Attention and Inbox</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: <ul> <li>Go menu, Needs My Attention (Alt+G, N); chord Ctrl+1. Go help “What needs you, on every site.”</li> <li>Ctrl+1 lands on the Inbox node, the first child, not on the container.</li> <li>The status bar’s “Waiting” cell (when comments wait) goes to Comments Waiting on Enter. Help: “Comments waiting on your sites, when there are any. Enter goes to them.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Children, in order: <ul> <li>Inbox (columns What, Site, When)</li> <li>Comments Waiting</li> <li>Pending Review</li> <li>Scheduled</li> <li>Drafts</li> <li>Updates</li> <li>Site Health</li> <li>Accessibility</li> <li>Broken Links</li> <li>Held as Spam</li> <li>Form Responses</li> <li>Membership Orders</li> <li>Watches</li> </ul></li> <li>What Inbox holds: <ul> <li>Comments waiting, prefixed “Comment: {title}”.</li> <li>Pending review, prefixed “Pending review: …”.</li> <li>Held as spam, prefixed “Held as spam: …”.</li> <li>Form responses, prefixed “Form response: …”.</li> <li>All newest first.</li> <li>A site that fails is a first row: “Could not ask {site}”, with detail “{site} did not answer: {why}”.</li> </ul></li> <li>Row menus: each Inbox row keeps its own kind’s menu (a comment row gets Approve and Spam, an item row Open in Editor and Publish), even though the node’s row type is “mixed”.</li> <li>F1: <ul> <li>Needs My Attention: “What waits on you, gathered from every connected site.”</li> <li>Inbox: “Everything below Needs My Attention, newest first, from every site.”</li> <li>Comments Waiting: “Comments held for moderation on every site. Ctrl+Shift+A approves.”</li> <li>Scheduled: “Posts and pages that will publish themselves, with their dates.”</li> <li>Drafts: “Your own drafts, on every site.”</li> <li>Held as Spam: “Comments and form entries a site doubted. Ctrl+Shift+A releases one.”</li> <li>Broken Links: “Dead web links found in a bounded sample of published posts and pages on every site.”</li> <li>mixed row: “Something that needs you. Enter reads it in full.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Without permission: a site with no sites connected shows the “no instance” text. What a role cannot read is a site error row.</li> <li>Undo: per row action (comments: Ctrl+Z puts the old status back).</li> <li>Code: workbench_attention.py (ATTENTION, gather, _inbox, unreachable_row), workbench_tree.py (the attention nodes), workbench_window_menu.py (GO_NODES), wordpress_workbench_window_status.py (Waiting cell).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: Inbox’s F1 says “Everything below Needs My Attention”. It actually holds four of the twelve: waiting comments, pending review, spam and form responses. Scheduled, drafts, updates, health, accessibility, broken links and membership orders are not in it.</li> <li>Review note: a stopped Practice Site stays connected. Every Needs My Attention node then starts with “Could not ask Practice Site” every time QUILL starts (the site stops when QUILL closes). Mark a practice site as stopped rather than failed, or leave it out of the account-level lists while it is not running.</li> <li>Review note: “Could not ask {site}” rows have kind “fact”, so their F1 says “A fact about the site. Enter reads it in the details pane.” That is accurate enough, but it would help to say what to do (check the connection, or Site Properties).</li> <li>Review note: Broken Links here is a sample (first 40 unique links, said in its row). Its F1 does not mention Check Every Link under By Name > Links, the full version.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="watches-node">Watches node</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Go menu, Watches (Alt+G, W); chord Ctrl+5. Go help “What the Workbench watches for you.” In the tree it is the last child of Needs My Attention.</li> <li>What it lists (columns Watch, State; rows are facts): <ul> <li>One row per Watch kind, “on” or “off”: <ul> <li>“New comments waiting”</li> <li>“New form responses”</li> <li>“Plugin updates available”</li> <li>“Scheduled posts going live”</li> <li>“A site not answering, and answering again”</li> <li>“Items with a reviewer leaving the queue”</li> <li>“Site Health status changes”</li> <li>“Form spam questions, once a week”</li> </ul></li> <li>“How often”: “at most every {n} minutes, while QUILL is open; Tools > Workbench Options turns each on or off”.</li> <li>When any Watch is on: <ul> <li>“Last round”: “none yet”, or “{day month, HH:MM}, {n} minutes ago”. When stale it adds “; that is stale: QUILL was closed, asleep or offline since”.</li> <li>Then one row per site: “not answering since {when}”, “answering; last heard {when}” or “not checked yet”.</li> </ul></li> </ul></li> <li>Row menu: kind “fact”, so Read in Full (Enter, moves to details) and Properties only.</li> <li>F1: <ul> <li>Node: “What you asked the Workbench to keep an eye on.”</li> <li>Row: “A fact about the site. Enter reads it in the details pane.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_bold.py (fixed_rows, family “watches”), workbench_watches.py (KINDS, status_facts), workbench_tree.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: the rows read “on” and “off” but cannot be changed where they are. Space or Enter on “New comments waiting off” does nothing useful. Let Enter (or a context-menu row) turn a Watch on or off, or open Workbench Options with that Watch focused.</li> <li>Review note: the row F1 (“A fact about the site”) is wrong for a Watch row, which is about the account.</li> <li>Review note: nothing the Watches said is kept. There is no “what changed” list on this node, so a sentence missed while away from the computer is gone. The node shows when the last round ran, but not what it found.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="workbench-options-choosing-watches">Workbench Options (choosing Watches)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Tools menu, Workbench Options (Alt+T, O); chord Ctrl+Shift+O. Menu help “Choose which Watches the Workbench announces.”</li> <li>What opens: a check-box list titled “Workbench Options”, labelled “Options” (the access key is stripped). Eight check boxes, one per kind: “Watch: New comments waiting”, “Watch: New form responses”, “Watch: Plugin updates available”, “Watch: Scheduled posts going live”, “Watch: A site not answering, and answering again”, “Watch: Items with a reviewer leaving the queue”, “Watch: Site Health status changes”, “Watch: Form spam questions, once a week”. Then OK (the default) and Cancel.</li> <li>Help (F1): “Every Watch is off until you check it: nothing is asked of your sites for Watches before then. A checked Watch asks each connected site over the internet at most every fifteen minutes while the editor is open, and says only what changed; its first look is silent. Space checks or unchecks.”</li> <li>Announced after: “Workbench options saved.”</li> <li>Stored: <code>workbench_options.json</code> (watches, watch_minutes, site_sets), shared by QUILL and QUILL Lite.</li> <li>Undo: none (a local setting).</li> <li>Cancel: nothing saved.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_options.py (do_options), workbench_options.py, wordpress_workbench_dialogs.py (ask_checks).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: the consent is only in F1. Checking a box starts network requests to every site, but the visible label says only “Watch: New comments waiting”, and the screen reader reads the help only if F1 is pressed.</li> <li>Review note: the interval is not a choice. <code>watch_minutes</code> (minimum 15) exists in the options file and is shown on the Watches node, but no dialog sets it.</li> <li>Review note: “Workbench options saved.” does not say what is now on. Saying “3 Watches on: comments, form responses, site down.” would confirm the change without a trip to the Watches node.</li> <li>Review note: the window is titled “Workbench Options” but holds only Watches. Site Sets, which share the same file, live in another dialog.</li> <li>Review note: the help says “while the editor is open”; the Watches node says “while QUILL is open”. Watches run from the editor’s timer, in QUILL and in QUILL Lite, whether or not the Workbench is open. One wording, please.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="what-watches-announce-and-when">What Watches announce, and when</h3> <ul> <li>When: the editor’s two-minute WordPress timer calls <code>watch_round</code>. A round runs only if a Watch is on, at least one site is connected, no round is already running, and at least max(15, watch_minutes) minutes have passed since the last. The round’s time is saved before any site is asked, so QUILL and QUILL Lite open together do not both run it.</li> <li>The first look at each Watch on each site is silent. A change is said once.</li> <li>Spoken sentences (each through the editor’s announce, which speaks and brailles, one announce per sentence): <ul> <li>Comments: “{n} new comment waiting on {site}.” or “{n} new comments waiting on {site}.”</li> <li>Scheduled going live: “Now published on {site}: {titles}.”</li> <li>Form responses: “A new form response on {site}.”</li> <li>Plugin updates: “Updates available on {site}: {plugin names}.”</li> <li>Site down: “{site} is not answering. Watches will try again next round.”</li> <li>Site back: “{site} answers again.”</li> <li>Reviews: “{title} on {site} was published.”, “… was approved and scheduled.”, “… was sent back to its writer.”, “… was moved to the Trash.” or “… was published privately.”</li> <li>Site Health: “Site Health on {site}: {test} is now critical; {test} is now good.”</li> <li>Form spam (weekly): “Form spam questions on {site}, week {2026-W46}: {n} asked, {n} answered, {n} answered wrongly, {n} sent anyway.” It adds “ Up from {n} asked the week before.“, “ Down from …” or “ The same as the week before.“</li> </ul></li> <li>Without permission: Site Health is silent for anyone but an administrator. A list the account may not read is silent.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_watch_commands.py (watch_round), workbench_watches.py (check, _site_round, _health, _reviews, due, MIN_MINUTES), workbench_qaf_spam_edit.py (weekly_watch), workbench_editorial.py (queue_status).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note (critical): one failed read silences every other Watch on that site. <code>_site_round</code> runs the comments check first, and any <code>WordPressApiError</code> (for example an Author account refused on held comments) is caught around the whole site (<code>except WordPressApiError: continue</code>). Form responses, updates, health and reviews on that site are then never said, every round, and nothing tells the person.</li> <li>Review note: “Plugin updates available” depends on an <code>update</code> or <code>new_version</code> field in <code>wp/v2/plugins</code>. Core WordPress does not send it (the Fleet’s own docstring says core REST “does not report waiting updates”). On most sites this Watch will never speak, and its label promises otherwise.</li> <li>Review note: a round can produce a burst. Eight kinds times several sites, each sentence a separate announce, arrive at once and may interrupt each other or whatever the person is reading in the editor. Combine a round into one sentence (“3 changes: …”) with the details on the Watches node.</li> <li>Review note: “A new form response on {site}.” is said whatever the count. Ten new responses sound like one. Watches only track the newest id, so the count is not known; say “New form responses”.</li> <li>Review note: comments waiting are read one page of 100 (<code>max_pages=1</code>). On a site with a large backlog, new comments past the first 100 are not counted.</li> <li>Review note: the Practice Site stops whenever QUILL closes. With “A site not answering” on, the next session can say “Practice Site is not answering…” and later “Practice Site answers again.” for something the person never meant to watch.</li> <li>Review note: ISO week codes (“2026-W46”) are spoken as is in the form-spam sentence. “the week of 9 November” would be friendlier.</li> <li>Review note: Watches ignore Across Sites Scope and Site Sets; every connected site is asked.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h2 id="practice-site">Practice Site</h2> <p>The Practice Site is a WordPress site that runs on this computer only (WordPress Playground on the bundled Node, at <a href="http://127.0.0.1:9400">http://127.0.0.1:9400</a>), for learning with nothing at stake. It has a node that reports its state and a Tools dialog that starts, stops, connects and resets it. Every command that would carry practice work to a real site asks first, by name, with Cancel as the default.</p> <h3 id="practice-site-node">Practice Site node</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Go menu, Practice Site (Alt+G, R); chord Ctrl+6. Go help “The WordPress site on this computer.” Also in the tree as an account-level node.</li> <li>What it lists (columns Fact, Value): <ul> <li>“Playground’s tool”: the path, or “Missing”.</li> <li>“Node.js”: the path, or “Missing”.</li> <li>“WordPress core”: the path (the unpacked folder, or the pinned archive the builds ship), or “Missing”.</li> <li>“Running”: “yes, at <a href="http://127.0.0.1:9400">http://127.0.0.1:9400</a>” or “no”.</li> <li>“What to do”: what is missing, or “Tools > Practice Site starts it.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Row menu: kind “fact”, so Read in Full and Properties only. Nothing on the node starts or stops the site.</li> <li>F1: <ul> <li>Node: “A practice WordPress site on this computer, where nothing is at stake.”</li> <li>Rows: “A fact about the site. Enter reads it in the details pane.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_bold.py (fixed_rows, the practice branch), workbench_practice.py (find_cli, missing_words, site_url).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: the node reports but cannot act. Its “What to do” row says “Tools > Practice Site starts it.” Enter on that row, or a node context-menu row, should simply open the Practice Site dialog.</li> <li>Review note: the first three rows are file paths, which are long to hear and matter only when something is missing. “Ready” or the missing piece would be enough, with paths in Properties.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-practice-site-dialog">The Practice Site dialog</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Tools menu, Practice Site (Alt+T, P); chord Ctrl+Alt+P. Menu help “Start, stop, connect or reset it.”</li> <li>Before it opens: <ul> <li>If the site had stopped by itself: “The Practice Site stopped. Its saved data is still there.”</li> <li>If it is not running and a piece is missing, that sentence is spoken and nothing opens. The sentences: <ul> <li>“The Practice Site cannot start. WordPress Playground, the part that runs it, is missing from this copy of the app.”</li> <li>“The Practice Site cannot start. Node.js, which WordPress Playground needs, is missing from this copy of the app.”</li> <li>“The Practice Site cannot start. Its WordPress files are missing from this copy of the app.”</li> <li>Each ends “To fix this, install the app again. If you use the portable version, download it again and unzip it to a new folder.” (quill/core/missing_parts.py, 2026-11-23; they used to name the build script.)</li> </ul></li> </ul></li> <li>What opens: a chooser titled “Practice Site”, labelled “Do:” (access key D). OK is the default. <ul> <li>Help: “A WordPress site on this computer only, for learning with nothing at stake. Reset puts it back as new. Press Enter to choose the selected one.”</li> <li>The choices depend on state: <ul> <li>Not running: “Start the Practice Site”, “Reset the Practice Site”.</li> <li>Running: “Connect It as Practice Site”, “Stop the Practice Site”.</li> </ul></li> </ul></li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_bold_practice.py (do_practice, _connect_practice, RESET_WARNING), workbench_practice.py, workbench_practice_process.py (start_process, wait_for_site, stop_process, the port check).</li> </ul> <h3 id="start-the-practice-site">Start the Practice Site</h3> <ul> <li>What happens: in the background (“starting the Practice Site”), Playground starts on 127.0.0.1 port 9400 only, tied to QUILL so it stops when QUILL closes. QUILL waits up to five minutes for WordPress to answer; a first start took nearly three minutes in the 2026-11-13 run.</li> <li>Announced after: <ul> <li>Success: “The Practice Site is running at <a href="http://127.0.0.1:9400">http://127.0.0.1:9400</a>. Connect it in a moment.”</li> <li>It ended while starting: “The Practice Site stopped as it was starting. Start it again or reset it.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Failures, spoken as the error: <ul> <li>Port taken: “Something on this computer is already using port 9400, which the Practice Site needs. If another copy of QUILL or QUILL Lite has its Practice Site running, stop it there first, then start this one again.”</li> <li>Died early: “The Practice Site stopped before WordPress was ready. It said: {last line} Start it again, or reset it if this keeps happening.”</li> <li>Timed out: “The Practice Site did not become ready within 300 seconds, so QUILL stopped it. Start it again; if it still does not start, reset it.”</li> <li>Window closed first: “The Workbench closed before the Practice Site started.”</li> <li>Core files: “The bundled WordPress core failed its SHA-256 check. Re-stage the Practice Site.” or “… could not be unpacked. Reset the Practice Site.” or “… is incomplete. Re-stage the Practice Site.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note (critical): a start of up to five minutes is silent. No “Starting; this can take three minutes the first time” is said at the start, and nothing is said during it. The person cannot tell a slow start from a hung one. Say the expected wait up front, and give a status-bar cell like Check Every Link’s.</li> <li>Review note: the start holds one of the window’s two background workers for up to five minutes. With a link check running, no node can load until one finishes (see Check Every Link).</li> <li>Review note: “Connect it in a moment.” means opening the dialog again and choosing Connect. Starting and connecting are two trips for what is always wanted together. Connect when the start finishes, or offer “Start and Connect”.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="connect-it-as-practice-site">Connect It as Practice Site</h3> <ul> <li>Only offered while it runs.</li> <li>What happens: QUILL reads the one-time password file the site wrote, stores the password in Windows Credential Manager, adds or updates a site profile named “Practice Site” (“Practice Site (local)”, “Practice Site (local 2)” if taken), deletes the password file, and rebuilds the tree.</li> <li>Announced after: <ul> <li>“{name} is connected. It is under Sites.”</li> <li>“{name} is connected. It is under Sites, but the one-time password file could not be removed. Reset the practice site to remove it.”</li> <li>No password file yet, but already connected: “{name} is already connected. It is under Sites.”</li> <li>No password file and not connected: “The practice site is still starting. Try Connect again in a moment.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Without permission: none needed; local only.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: Connect stays on the menu after connecting. Choosing it again says “already connected”, which is fine, but the dialog could say “Connected” in the choice instead.</li> <li>Review note: the site always shows under Sites as “(practice)” (practice_label), which is the right protection; every preview and history line carries it.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="stop-the-practice-site">Stop the Practice Site</h3> <ul> <li>Only offered while it runs. It stops Playground and every process under it.</li> <li>Announced after: “The practice site has stopped.”</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: “practice site” is lower case here and in Reset’s sentence, but “Practice Site” in the menu, the dialog title, Start’s sentence and the node. A small thing, but braille shows it.</li> <li>Review note: Stop runs on the UI thread (<code>stop_process</code> is called directly, not through <code>background</code>). On Windows this may run taskkill and wait, which freezes the window briefly with no announcement.</li> <li>Review note: the profile stays connected after Stop, so every account-level node (Needs My Attention, Calendar) lists “Could not ask Practice Site” until it is started again or forgotten.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="reset-the-practice-site">Reset the Practice Site</h3> <ul> <li>Only offered while it is stopped.</li> <li>What opens: a Yes/Cancel question titled “Reset the Practice Site”. <ul> <li>Wording: “Reset the Practice Site? This permanently deletes everything on it: every post, page, upload, comment, user, plugin and setting you made or changed there. QUILL also forgets its Practice Site connection and removes its stored password from Windows Credential Manager. Your real WordPress sites are not touched. This cannot be undone. The next start builds a brand-new site with the starter content.”</li> <li>Buttons: “Reset”, and “Cancel” (the default).</li> </ul></li> <li>What happens: in the background (“resetting the Practice Site”), the folder is emptied, the stored password is removed, the profile is removed from the site list, and the tree is rebuilt.</li> <li>Announced after: “The practice site is new again. Start it to use it.”</li> <li>Undo: none, and the warning says so plainly. This is the model the other irreversible actions (Revoke Everywhere, Merge Into) should follow.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: Reset is unreachable when a piece is missing. The dialog does not open at all if the tool, Node or core files are absent, so a person cannot clear an old practice site’s data and its stored password on a build that no longer has the tool. Reset needs only the folder.</li> <li>Review note: a snapshot of the Practice Site survives a Reset (snapshots are keyed by the profile id, which Reset forgets). Those snapshots can no longer be reached from Put Back, because Put Back looks under the current profile’s id. They are orphaned on disk.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="the-practice-crossing-question-used-by-other-commands">The practice crossing question (used by other commands)</h3> <ul> <li>Asked by: Copy to Site, Make Them the Same, Install on Sites Missing It, Put Back from a Snapshot (a snapshot taken on the Practice Site, onto a real site), and in its together form by Promote to Network and Revoke Everywhere.</li> <li>Wording (crossing): “{action} would send what is on {practice}, the practice site on this computer, to {real}: a real site that other people can see. Work on the practice site never goes anywhere else unless you choose it here. Send it to {real}?” Titled “{action}: from the Practice Site to a Real Site”. Buttons “Send to {site}” (or “Send to Them”) and “Cancel” (the default).</li> <li>Wording (together): quoted under Promote to Network. Titled “{action}: the Practice Site and Real Sites Together”. Buttons “Change {site} Too” (or “Change Them Too”) and “Cancel”.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_bold_practice.py (practice_crossing_ok), workbench_practice.py (crossing_warning, together_warning, is_practice_address, practice_label).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note: WXR is a gap in the guard. Export from the Practice Site, then Import WXR as Drafts into a real site, carries practice work across with no question.</li> <li>Review note: the question comes before the command’s own preview, so the person agrees to “send it” before seeing what “it” is. That order is deliberate in the code (“every cross-site write calls this before its own preview”). A sentence in the preview (“This comes from the Practice Site”) would keep the reminder in view at the moment of the real decision.</li> <li>Review note: on the Cancel default, Enter cancels. That is the right default for this question, and it is the opposite of every preview’s default, so the same Enter key means “go ahead” one dialog later.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="undo-across-this-section-ctrlz-edit--undo-wordpress-change">Undo across this section (Ctrl+Z, Edit > Undo WordPress Change)</h3> <ul> <li>How it works: <code>do_undo</code> looks only at the newest Change History entry. If that entry has no undo, it speaks “{summary} This cannot be undone.” and stops.</li> <li>Recorded with no undo: Copy to Site, Put Back from a Snapshot, Make Them the Same for menus, Promote to Network, Revoke Everywhere, a By Name Merge of terms, and an import that made nothing. Install on Sites Missing It is not recorded at all.</li> <li>Code: quill/core/workbench/history.py (ChangeHistory), wordpress_workbench_sheets.py (do_undo, do_history).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Review note (critical): a non-undoable entry blocks the whole stack. After any of the commands above, Ctrl+Z can no longer reach the undoable change made just before it (a Trash, a Rename Everywhere). The person hears “… This cannot be undone.” and may conclude the earlier change cannot be undone either. Skip past non-undoable entries, or say “The newest change cannot be undone; Change History lists the earlier ones.”</li> <li>Review note: summaries that already say “It cannot be undone.” get “ This cannot be undone.“ added (Revoke Everywhere).</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h2 id="forms-qaf">Forms (QAF)</h2> <p>QUILL Accessible Forms (QAF) lives in two places. The first is the editor, in both QUILL and QUILL Lite. There a form is a plain-text .qaf document written in the QAF form language: you write it, check it, fill it in yourself, export it, publish it to a WordPress site that runs the QAF plugin, place it on a page, and read the responses. All of that runs from the shared <code>FormsCommands</code> class, through one menu table (<code>quill/core/forms/menu.py</code>). The second place is the WordPress Workbench, which shows each site’s QAF forms and responses as rows. There you change a form’s status and settings, delete a form, mark responses Spam or Not Spam, search responses, and manage QAF’s spam questions. Writing, checking, Fill It In Yourself and the exports are local. Everything that reaches a site or an AI is off in Safe Mode, and the editor says “{command} is off in Safe Mode, because it would reach your site or an AI. Writing, checking, Fill It In Yourself and saving in other formats still work.” The two Responses surfaces do not offer the same things, and that is the main review finding below. The Gravity Forms copy commands are named here only; see the Gravity Forms section for them.</p> <h3 id="forms-in-the-menus-and-the-one-forms-key">Forms in the menus, and the one Forms key</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: File > New Form; Insert > Form Field; View > Form Outline; Format > Form Rules; Tools > Forms, which holds everything else in four groups. <ul> <li>Check This Form, Fill It In Yourself, Form Properties, Form Payments Setup, Insert Payment Field, Verify Payment Setup.</li> <li>Publish Form, Insert Form Into Page, Test on Site, Responses.</li> <li>Save As Web Page, Save As Word Document, Save As Large Print, Save As Braille-Ready Text.</li> <li>Draft a Form, Plain Language, Suggest Autofill Hints.</li> </ul></li> <li>Access letters inside Tools > Forms (from the table, the same in Lite): &Check, &Fill, Form &Properties, Form Payments &Setup, Insert Payment Fi&eld, &Verify, P&ublish, &Insert Form Into Page, &Test, &Responses, Save As &Web Page, Save As Wor&d, Save As &Large Print, Save As &Braille-Ready Text, Dr&aft, Plain Lan&guage, Suggest Autofill &Hints.</li> <li>The one Forms key is Responses, on the WordPress layer: WordPress key, then F (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Grave, then F). Per <code>forms/menu.py</code>, it was “decided 2026-11-13” and given “by frequency (rule 3)”.</li> <li>Keyless: every other Forms row. Reason in <code>forms/menu.py</code>: “Every other Forms command keeps no chord, each for rule 9: writing a form is occasional, and its menu letters and the Command Palette are its way in.”</li> <li>Code: core/forms/menu.py, main_frame_forms.py, lite_window_forms.py, forms_commands.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>With 17 rows in four groups, Tools > Forms is long. The editing commands (Check, Fill In, Properties) are mixed in with payments.</li> <li>Payments has three rows here, and a fourth door through the payment kind in Insert Form Field.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="new-form">New Form</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: File > New Form (Alt+F, M in Lite; QUILL’s letter comes from the route pass, M in the table). Command Palette “New Form”. No key (rule 9). Enabled in every document.</li> <li>What opens: a “New Form” dialog. Its purpose, read by F1 on the dialog: “Start a form from a template. QUILL writes it as plain text in the QAF form language, saves it, and opens it; change anything afterwards.” Controls in tab order: <ul> <li>“&Title:” edit box (help “The form’s name, as visitors will see it.”).</li> <li>“Te&mplate:” choice (help “What to start from. Each template is a few questions you can change.”).</li> <li>The Next button (default), then Cancel.</li> </ul></li> <li>Templates, each listed as “Name: description”: <ul> <li>Blank: “One question to start from.”</li> <li>Contact form: “Name, email address and a message.”</li> <li>Event registration: “Name, email, how many are coming, and access arrangements, asked only when needed.”</li> <li>Membership renewal: “Contact details, membership level, and how they would like the newsletter.”</li> <li>Survey: “A rating, a yes or no, and room to say more.”</li> <li>Accessibility feedback: “Where the problem was, what happened, what they use, and whether to reply.”</li> <li>Volunteer sign-up: “Contact details, the days they can help, and what they would like to do.”</li> </ul></li> <li>After Next: the standard Save As window, titled “Save the New Form”. The file type is “Forms (*.qaf)” and the suggested name comes from the title. It asks before overwriting. A name typed without .qaf gets .qaf added.</li> <li>Announced after: “Saved {file}, a {template name in lower case} with {n} questions.” (“question.” when there is one). The new form opens in the editor. On a write error: “QUILL could not save {file}: {reason}.”</li> <li>Undo: none needed. The file is written to disk and opened. Nothing in the current document changes.</li> <li>F1: the dialog’s purpose and each field’s help (SetHelpText in <code>BlockFormDialog</code>).</li> <li>Cancel: Cancel in either step stops silently. Nothing is written.</li> <li>Code: forms_commands.py (<code>cmd_new_form</code>), forms_templates.py, forms_dialogs.py (<code>ask_save_path</code>), wordpress_block_dialogs.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The announcement uses “a” before every template name: “a event registration with 4 questions” and “a accessibility feedback with …”. It is also odd for “a blank with 1 question”.</li> <li>The dialog’s purpose is reachable only through F1. Nothing on screen states it.</li> <li>Cancelling silently is correct here.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="insert--form-field">Insert > Form Field</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Insert > Form Field (Alt+I, F). Palette “Form Field”. No key (rule 9). Dimmed unless a form document is in front.</li> <li>What opens, step 1: an “Insert Form Field” list window. Purpose: “Every kind of question a form can ask, with Section and Page Break first. Type to narrow the list, then choose one; the next window asks only what it needs.” Controls in tab order: <ul> <li>“&Search:” box. Help: “Type words, such as date or choose, and press Enter to narrow the list.”</li> <li>“&Kind of question:” list. Each row reads “Kind: description”, for example “Email: An email address, checked as it is typed.”</li> <li>“&Details:” read-only box.</li> <li>The C&hoose button, then Close.</li> <li>Enter on the list chooses.</li> </ul></li> <li>Step 2: a dialog titled “Insert {kind}” with an Insert button (default) and Cancel. <ul> <li>For a Page Break: only “Page &title (optional):”.</li> <li>For a Section: only “&Label:”.</li> <li>For other kinds: “&Label:”, “&Id (optional):”, “&Required” check box, “&Help text:” (multi-line).</li> <li>For a choice kind, add “&Choices, one per line:” and a “Choice li&brary:” choice (“None”, then each library as “{label}, {n} choices”).</li> </ul></li> <li>Choosing the payment kind goes straight to Insert Payment Field.</li> <li>Announced after: <ul> <li>“Inserted {name}, {kind in lower case}.” The caret moves to the new question.</li> <li>A page break says “Page break inserted. Page {n} starts here.”</li> <li>The new item goes after the item at the caret. If the caret is on a section, a question goes inside it.</li> </ul></li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z in the editor takes the whole insert back (<code>apply</code> rewrites the text as one replace).</li> <li>F1: SetHelpText on the dialog and on every field.</li> <li>Cancel: silent at either step.</li> <li>Code: forms_commands.py (<code>cmd_insert_field</code>, <code>_insert</code>), forms_dialogs.py (<code>pick_from_list</code>), forms_field_types.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The payment kind is offered in the list even where Form Payments is turned off in QUILL. It then answers with the Features refusal.</li> <li>When choices and a library are both left empty on a choice kind, a placeholder “Choice 1” is added without saying so.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="view--form-outline">View > Form Outline</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: View > Form Outline (Alt+V, F). Palette “Form Outline”. No key. Dimmed outside a form document.</li> <li>What opens: a list window titled “Form Outline”. Purpose: “Every page, section and question in this form, in order. Each change here is one step that Undo in the editor takes back. Alt+Shift and the arrows move the selected question: up and down, or into and out of a section.” Tab order: the “&Questions:” list, then the buttons: <ul> <li>&Go to It (also Enter).</li> <li>Move &Up and Move &Down (also Alt+Shift+Up and Alt+Shift+Down).</li> <li>Move &In and Move Ou&t (also Alt+Shift+Right and Alt+Shift+Left).</li> <li>&Rename…, &Change Id…, Change T&ype…, Du&plicate, Re&move….</li> <li>Close.</li> </ul></li> <li>Confirmations: <ul> <li>Remove: “Remove {name} from this form? Undo in the editor brings it back.”, with Remove and Cancel buttons. No is the default.</li> <li>Change Type asks only when something is adjusted or dropped. It shows the plan’s summary and review lines, with a “Change Type” button.</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: <ul> <li>Moves: “Moved up.” / “Moved down.” / “Moved to page {n}.” / “Moved into {section}.” / “Moved out of {section}.”</li> <li>Duplicate: “Duplicated. The copy is straight after it.”</li> <li>Rename: “Renamed to {label}.”</li> <li>Change Id: “The id is now {id}.”</li> <li>Change Type: “Changed to {type}.”</li> <li>Refusals: “It is already first.” / “It is already last.” / “There is no section just above it to move into.” / “A section cannot go inside another section here.” / “It is not inside a section.” / “That is a page. Move or change its questions instead.” / “An id is letters, digits and underscores, and does not start with a digit.” / “The id {id} is already used in this form.” / “A section cannot become a question.” / “This field needs {…} before it can become {type}.”</li> <li>Cancelling a sub-dialog: “Nothing changed.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Undo: every change is one Ctrl+Z step in the editor, after the window closes.</li> <li>F1: the window’s purpose, the list’s help (“Each page, section and question, with what it needs. Press Enter to go to it; Alt+Shift and the arrow keys move it.”) and each button’s help.</li> <li>Cancel: Close or Escape leaves without changing anything.</li> <li>Code: forms_outline_commands.py, forms_dialogs.py (<code>show_outline_window</code>, <code>OUTLINE_KEYS</code>), forms_doc.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Cancelling Rename, Change Id or Change Type speaks “Nothing changed.” That is a spoken result for a cancel, where every other Cancel in Forms is silent. This is mild chattiness.</li> <li>An Alt+Shift+arrow move on a disabled action only beeps (<code>wx.Bell</code>). Nothing is spoken.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="format--form-rules">Format > Form Rules</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Format > Form Rules (Alt+O, M in Lite). Palette “Form Rules”. No key. Dimmed outside a form document.</li> <li>What opens: a list window titled “Form Rules”. Purpose: “Every rule in this form as a sentence: what shows or hides a question, what is worked out, and what is required when. Add Rule builds one from three choices.” Tab order: <ul> <li>The “&Rules:” list. Each row is a sentence such as “Show {name} when {condition}.”</li> <li>The buttons &Add Rule…, &Edit…, Re&move…, &Go to It, then Close.</li> <li>Enter goes to the question.</li> </ul></li> <li>Add Rule: a dialog titled “Add Rule”, with an Add Rule button (default) and Cancel. Fields: <ul> <li>“&When this question:” choice.</li> <li>“&Is:” choice: is, is not, is filled in, is empty, contains, is more than, is less than.</li> <li>“This &answer:” edit box.</li> <li>“Or write the condition &yourself:” edit box.</li> <li>“&Then:” choice: Show, Hide, Require, Make optional, Enable, Disable, Skip to.</li> <li>“To this &question or page:” choice.</li> </ul></li> <li>Edit: a dialog titled “Edit Rule” with “&Condition:” (“Leave it empty to remove the rule.”).</li> <li>Remove confirmation: “Remove this rule? {sentence} Undo in the editor brings it back.”, with a Remove Rule button.</li> <li>Announced after: <ul> <li>Add: “{Action} {name} when {sentence}.”</li> <li>Edit: “Rule changed.” (or “Rule removed.” when emptied).</li> <li>Remove: “Rule removed.”</li> <li>A bad condition: “That rule cannot be read: {problem}”.</li> <li>A form with no questions: “This form has no questions for a rule to use yet.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Undo: one Ctrl+Z step each, in the editor.</li> <li>F1: the purpose, the list’s help (“Each rule as a sentence. Press Enter to go to the question it is on.”) and each field’s help.</li> <li>Cancel: silent.</li> <li>Code: forms_outline_commands.py (<code>cmd_rules</code>, <code>_add_rule</code>), forms_rules.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Enter or Go to It on a free-standing rule row (the kind stored in the form’s rules list) does nothing at all, silently (<code>go</code> returns None when the row’s kind is “rule”). The list help promises “Press Enter to go to the question it is on.”</li> <li>The Add Rule dialog’s Then default is “show” and its target default is the last question. A rule added with every default is “Show {last question} when {first question} is “““. That is valid, but probably not what was meant.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="tools--forms--check-this-form">Tools > Forms > Check This Form</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Tools > Forms > Check This Form (Alt+T, then the Forms submenu, then C). No key. Dimmed outside a form document.</li> <li>What opens: when there are findings, a list window titled “Check This Form”. Purpose: “{n} things to fix in this form, most serious first. Go to It takes you to the line; Check Again after you change it.” Tab order: the “&Findings:” list, &Go to It, Check &Again, Close. Enter goes to the finding.</li> <li>Announced after: <ul> <li>With no findings, no window opens and it says “Check This Form found nothing to fix.”</li> <li>A finding about the whole form: “That finding is about the whole form, not one line.”</li> <li>Findings include reading problems, “QUILL keeps this as written and cannot read it: {line}”, structural problems, accessibility findings and divergences.</li> </ul></li> <li>Undo: not applicable. It reads only.</li> <li>F1: the purpose, the list’s help (“Each finding says what to fix. Press Enter to go to it.”) and the button help.</li> <li>Code: forms_commands.py (<code>cmd_check</code>), forms_check.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Check Again is a dead end. The window is modal, so you cannot change the form while it is open, and “Check Again after you change it” can never show anything new. When Check Again does empty the list, nothing is spoken.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="tools--forms--fill-it-in-yourself">Tools > Forms > Fill It In Yourself</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Tools > Forms > Fill It In Yourself (F in the submenu). No key. Dimmed outside a form document.</li> <li>What opens: a dialog titled “Fill It In Yourself: {form title or Untitled form}”. Purpose: “Your form as a visitor meets it. Tab through the questions and answer them; rules show and hide questions as you go, and worked-out answers update. Submit sends nothing: it shows what would have been sent.”</li> <li>Controls in tab order: <ul> <li>“&Language:” choice, only when the form has more than one language.</li> <li>A page heading (“Page 1 of 2: {title}”).</li> <li>The questions as native controls: an edit box, a check box, radio buttons in a labelled group, a drop-down list (first entry “(no answer)”), or a read-only box for worked-out answers. A question that is required has “ (required)“ in its name.</li> <li>The buttons &Back, &Next, &Submit (Submit is enabled only on the last page), then Close.</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced before it opens: <ul> <li>“This form has no questions to fill in yet.”</li> <li>When some of the form cannot be read: “QUILL could not read all of this form, so some questions may be missing. Check This Form says where.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced while filling in: <ul> <li>“{names} appears.” / “{names} are hidden.” when a rule shows or hides questions.</li> <li>The new heading after Back or Next.</li> <li>“{n} questions need an answer: {names}.” when Next finds required questions empty. Focus moves to the first of them.</li> <li>A question that only works on the site reads “QUILL cannot show a {type} question here. It works on the site.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Submit opens a report, “What Would Be Sent”, starting “Nothing was sent. This is what the form would send:”, then question-and-answer lines and “Still needs an answer: …” when relevant. The report has a “&Report:” box, a Copy &All button and Close.</li> <li>Undo: not applicable. The document is never changed.</li> <li>F1: the purpose, plus per-control help (the question’s own help text, or “{name}: answer it as a visitor would.”).</li> <li>Code: forms_commands.py (<code>cmd_fill_in</code>), forms_fill_window.py, forms_fill.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Submit does not check the last page’s required questions the way Next does. It only lists them at the bottom of the report.</li> <li>Next both moves focus (which the screen reader reads) and announces the page heading. Those two can talk over each other. The heading is not otherwise spoken, so the announcement is allowed under GATE-13, but listen for collisions.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="tools--forms--form-properties">Tools > Forms > Form Properties</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Tools > Forms > Form Properties (P in the submenu). No key. Dimmed outside a form document.</li> <li>What opens: a “Form Properties” dialog. Insert Form Field places items at the caret.</li> <li>The Lite gate bypass is described under QUILL Lite differences.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="tools--forms--publish-form">Tools > Forms > Publish Form</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Tools > Forms > P&ublish Form. No key. Dimmed outside a form document. Off in Safe Mode.</li> <li>Before anything is sent: <ul> <li>Readability: “Publish Form stopped: this form has {n} problems to fix first. Install and activate the plugin there, then try again.”</li> </ul></li> <li>What opens, a new form: <ul> <li>A “&Publish as:” choice: “Live: people can fill it in wherever it is placed” or “Draft: on the site, but nobody can fill it in yet”.</li> <li>Then the confirmation “Publish {title} to {site} as a new form?”, with a Publish button.</li> </ul></li> <li>What opens, an already published form: <ul> <li>“Update {title} on {site}? The task names (“reading {site}”, “publishing to {site}”) are not announced, so on a slow site there is silence between the button and the result.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="tools--forms--insert-form-into-page">Tools > Forms > Insert Form Into Page</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Tools > Forms > &Insert Form Into Page. Announces “{n} responses moved to the Trash on {site}.”</li> <li>Say New Count: announces “QUILL will now (or no longer) say how many new responses there are when you first use {site} in a session.”</li> <li>Refresh Payment Status: asks “Read the latest responses and payment status from {site}? This reads the site’s webhook-confirmed state; it never captures a payment.”, with a Refresh button. It closes the window and reopens it.</li> </ul></li> <li>Also spoken, once per site per session: the first time a site is used, “{site}: {n} new responses to {form}.” when there are new responses (<code>announce_new_responses_once</code>).</li> <li>Undo: nothing on the site. Deleted responses go to QAF’s Trash, which can be restored only in wp-admin.</li> <li>F1: the window purpose, the list help (“Each response: its number, when it was sent, who sent it and the form. Press Enter to read one.”) and each button’s help.</li> <li>Code: forms_responses_commands.py, forms_ai_commands.py (<code>summarise_responses</code>), forms_site_base.py, core/forms/responses.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Access-key collision. The list label “&Responses:” and the button “&Read” both claim R in one window. Under GATE-14, Windows cycles between the two instead of pressing, so Alt+R may never press Read. The dialogs are built at runtime, so the static gate may not see it. Verify.</li> <li>Refresh Payment Status is the first button in the bar, and is shown for every form, payment or not. The first Tab stop after the list is a payments command most forms never need.</li> <li>Delete takes the rows out of the list before the site has answered. If the delete then fails, only the failure sentence is spoken, and the rows stay gone from the window until it is reopened.</li> <li>Mark is silent. Whether the reader re-reads the changed row after <code>set_rows</code> is not certain. Listen for it.</li> <li>From a published form document, Responses always opens that form. To read another form’s responses you must first switch to a document that is not a form. Nothing says so.</li> <li>This window and the Workbench’s Responses do different jobs. The editor has Read, Find, Mark, Export, Summarise and Delete, but no Spam or Not Spam. The Workbench has Spam, Not Spam, Search on the Site and Saved Views, but no Delete and no Export of QAF responses. Someone handling spam and exports has to use both.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="tools--forms--save-as-web-page-word-document-large-print-braille-ready-text">Tools > Forms > Save As Web Page, Word Document, Large Print, Braille-Ready Text</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach them: Tools > Forms > Save As &Web Page / Save As Wor&d Document / Save As &Large Print / Save As &Braille-Ready Text. No keys. Dimmed outside a form document. All local, and they work in Safe Mode.</li> <li>Before each export, the same readability check as Publish Form (“{command} stopped: this form has {n} problems to fix first. …”).</li> <li>Web Page: <ul> <li>A “Save As Web Page” question, “&Send answers to:”. Help: “The web address the form sends its answers to, starting https://. Leave it empty for a form to print or read, which sends nothing.”</li> <li>A bad address: “That is not a web address. Start it with https://, or leave it empty.”</li> <li>Then a Save As. Announces “Saved {file}, a web page with {n} questions. It sends its answers to {address}.” (or “It sends its answers nowhere”).</li> </ul></li> <li>Word Document announces “Saved {file}, a Word form with {n} questions, lines to write on and boxes to check.”</li> <li>Large Print announces “Saved {file}, a large-print Word document, 18 point and up, with {n} questions, lines to write on and boxes to check.”</li> <li>Braille-Ready Text: <ul> <li>When the braille pack is installed, a “&Save as:” choice: “Braille-ready text, 40 cells a line (.txt)” or “Braille file, translated to UEB contracted (.brf)”.</li> <li>Announces “Saved {file}, braille-ready text with {n} questions, one a block.” or “Saved {file}, a braille file in UEB contracted, with {n} questions.”</li> <li>Failures: “The braille translation came back empty. Nothing was saved.” or “The braille translation did not work: {reason}. Nothing was saved.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Any write failure: “QUILL could not save {file}: {reason}.”</li> <li>Undo: not applicable. A file is written. The document is unchanged.</li> <li>Code: forms_export_commands.py, core/forms/interop_html.py, core/forms/export_word.py.</li> </ul> <h3 id="tools--forms--draft-a-form-plain-language-suggest-autofill-hints">Tools > Forms > Draft a Form, Plain Language, Suggest Autofill Hints</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach them: Tools > Forms > Dr&aft a Form / Plain Lan&guage / Suggest Autofill &Hints. No keys. Draft a Form works from any document. The other two are dimmed outside a form document. All three are off in Safe Mode.</li> <li>Draft a Form: <ul> <li>A “Draft a Form” question, “&Describe the form:” (multi-line). Help: “A sentence or two: who fills it in, and what you need to know. Only this description goes to your chosen AI. You read the draft before it is used.”</li> <li>Then it announces “Draft a Form sends your description, and nothing else, to your chosen AI.” and sends.</li> <li>The answer is shown in a report, “Draft a Form: the proposal”.</li> <li>Then the confirmation “Use this draft of {n} questions? It will {replace the form in front, or save it as a new form}.[ It has {n} reading problems to fix.]”, with a “Use It” button.</li> </ul></li> <li>Draft a Form, announced after: <ul> <li>Replacing: “The draft is in, {n} questions.
