Undo takes it out.”</li> <li>Saving: a “Save the Drafted Form” Save As, then “Saved {file}, a drafted form with {n} questions.”</li> <li>Unreadable: “The AI’s draft could not be read as a form. Nothing was changed.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Plain Language and Suggest Autofill Hints: <ul> <li>Each announces what it sends. Plain Language: “Plain Language sends the labels and help text of {n} questions to your chosen AI. No answers are sent.” Suggest Autofill Hints: “Suggest Autofill Hints sends the labels and types of {n} questions to your chosen AI. No answers are sent.”</li> <li>Each shows “{command}: the proposal”, then asks “Apply {n} rewordings (or suggestions)? It is one step, and Undo takes it all out.”, with an Apply button.</li> <li>After: “{n} rewordings applied. Undo takes them all out.” or “{n} suggestions applied. …”.</li> <li>Nothing to do: “Your AI found nothing to make plainer. Nothing was changed.” / “Your AI suggested nothing usable. Nothing was changed.” / “Every question that takes an autofill hint has one, and every format rule has help. Nothing to suggest.”</li> <li>Switched documents while waiting: “{command}: the form is no longer in front, so nothing changed.”</li> <li>AI failure: “{command}: the AI did not answer. {reason}.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Undo: one Ctrl+Z step for each applied change.</li> <li>Code: forms_ai_commands.py, core/forms/ai_prompts.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Draft a Form sends as soon as you press OK. Only a spoken sentence comes first, with no confirmation, which is acceptable because only your own description goes. Summarise asks every time, as it should, because it sends people’s answers.</li> <li>“The draft is in … Undo takes it out” replaces the whole form, including any rules or properties you wrote. The confirmation says “replace the form in front”, which is honest, but it is a large single step.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="workbench-the-forms-node-and-its-rows">Workbench: the Forms node and its rows</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: in the WordPress Workbench, open a site in the tree, then Forms. Under it are Responses and Held as Spam (Held as Spam is not on the site’s home page, <code>home=False</code>). The Attention area also has Form Responses and a mixed Held as Spam (comments and form entries).</li> <li>Rows: <ul> <li>The Forms list has one row per form, with the columns form, plugin, entries and status.</li> <li>Responses and Held as Spam have one row per response, with the columns form, plugin, status and when. The status words are “Received”, “Held as spam” and “Trash”.</li> <li>QAF responses are read for each form, newest first, at most 3 pages a form and at most 300 in all (<code>_MAX_ENTRIES</code>).</li> </ul></li> <li>Details pane: <ul> <li>A form: “{title}, {n} responses, {status}.”</li> <li>A response: “{form}, QUILL Accessible Forms, {site},[ held as spam] {date}.” plus up to three answers. For a held QAF response it adds QAF’s reason, for example “Held because: {reason} ({check} check, score {n} of {max}).”</li> </ul></li> <li>A site with no form plugin answers: “{site} has no forms plugin QUILL can read. QUILL Accessible Forms and Gravity Forms are found by their REST routes; this site lists neither.”</li> <li>F1 (node help): <ul> <li>Forms: “The site’s forms, from every form plugin QUILL reads.”</li> <li>Responses: “Responses to the site’s forms.”</li> <li>Held as Spam: “Form responses held as spam.”</li> <li>Attention > Form Responses: “New form responses, from every site with a form plugin QUILL reads.”</li> <li>Attention > Held as Spam: “Comments and form entries a site doubted. Ctrl+Shift+A releases one.”</li> <li>The form row: “A form. Enter lists its responses.”</li> <li>The response row: “A form response. Enter reads it in full. On Gravity Forms, the context menu also has Notes, Star, Mark Read and Resend Notifications, and Properties changes its answers.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Code: core/wordpress/workbench_forms.py, workbench_tree.py, workbench_node_help.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Four nodes say “Responses” or “Held as Spam” in two places each. Forms > Responses and Attention > Form Responses are the same thing under two names. Forms > Held as Spam and Attention > Held as Spam share a name and differ in scope (the Attention one also has comments).</li> <li>The 300-row and 3-page caps cut the list silently. A busy form shows fewer responses than its count, and nothing says the list is partial.</li> <li>The response row’s help mentions only Gravity Forms extras. It never says that Ctrl+Shift+S marks spam and Ctrl+Shift+A releases.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="workbench-show-its-responses-on-a-form-row">Workbench: Show Its Responses (on a form row)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter on a form row (the row’s default). Also the context menu (Applications key or Shift+F10) > Show Its Responses.</li> <li>What it does: selects the site’s Forms > Responses node, filtered to <code>form:"{title}"</code>.</li> <li>F1: ActionRow help “The form’s responses.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (<code>do_show_entries</code>).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The filter goes by the form’s title, not its id. Two forms with the same title, or a title that is part of another title, may share one filtered list. This is plausible from the code; verify how the filter matches.</li> <li>It shows only Received responses. Held-as-spam responses to the same form are in a different node, and nothing tells you they exist.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="workbench-change-form-status-qaf-form-row">Workbench: Change Form Status (QAF form row)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: the form row’s context menu > Change Form Status…. Keyless: “A form opens or closes a few times a year (rule 9).”</li> <li>What opens: a choice titled “Change Form Status - {title}”, with “&New status:”. The rows are the two statuses the form is not in now: “a draft, not shown to visitors”, “open, taking responses”, “closed, shown but not taking responses”. Help: “Whether the form takes responses.”</li> <li>Then a preview titled “Change Form Status”. It has a “&Summary:” box (“{title} on {site} becomes {new}.”) and an “&Each change:” list, for example “{title} on {site}: open, taking responses becomes closed, shown but not taking responses.”, “Anyone who opens a page with it can no longer send a response.” and “Its questions and past responses are not changed.” Buttons: Change Status (default), then Cancel.</li> <li>Announced after: “{title} on {site} is {phrase}.” The list refreshes.</li> <li>Failure: the site’s own sentence (the Workbench’s failure handler), or “That did not work.”</li> <li>Undo: none. Nothing is recorded in Change History, so Ctrl+Z does not undo it.</li> <li>Without permission: the row is tier WRITE, with no capability named. Under Read Only or Organize access it says “Workbench access on {site} is {words}. Site > Workbench Access changes it.”, and in Safe Mode “Safe Mode is on, so the Workbench only reads.” A WordPress role QAF refuses is reported only as the site’s error after sending.</li> <li>A Gravity Forms row: “That is a QUILL Accessible Forms command; choose one of its forms.”</li> <li>F1: ActionRow help “Draft, open or closed, after a preview. QUILL Accessible Forms only.”; the preview’s help.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_forms_site.py, core/wordpress/workbench_forms_site.py, workbench_plugin_actions.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>The preview’s summary uses the raw status word (“becomes inactive”, “becomes active”) while the lines and the result use the friendly phrase (“closed, shown but not taking responses”). Two vocabularies in one preview.</li> <li>There is no undo, and no Change History entry. A Ctrl+Z pressed afterwards undoes whatever was recorded before, which may be an earlier spam mark, not this status change. This is plausible from the <code>_record</code> and history design; verify.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="workbench-form-settings-qaf-form-row">Workbench: Form Settings (QAF form row)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: the context menu > Form Settings…. Keyless: “A form’s settings are set when it is made (rule 9).”</li> <li>What opens: <ul> <li>The settings are read in the background first.</li> <li>Then a choice titled “Form Settings - {title}”, with “&Settings (Enter changes one):”. Each row is “{setting}: {value}”. Lists and objects are left out (“Lists such as conditional logic are changed in the form builder.”).</li> <li>Then a question with the setting’s name as its label. Help: “Its new value: on or off for a switch, a number for a count.”</li> <li>Then a preview titled “Form Settings”, with lines “{title} on {site}: {setting} is {old} and becomes {new}.” and “Every other setting is sent back as it is. Visitors see the change at once.” The button is Change Setting.</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: <ul> <li>“Changed {setting} on {title}.”</li> <li>Errors: “This setting is on or off. Nothing was changed.” / “This setting is a whole number. Nothing was changed.” / “That is its value already. Nothing was changed.” / “{title} returned no settings QUILL can read.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Undo: none. Not in Change History.</li> <li>Without permission: as Change Form Status (tier WRITE, no capability named).</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_forms_site.py (<code>do_form_settings</code>), core/wordpress/workbench_forms_site.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Settings are shown by their raw keys with underscores turned into spaces (for example “save continue enabled”). They are not friendly labels.</li> <li>Only one setting can be changed per trip. Changing three means three round trips through the menu.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="workbench-saved-views-and-search-responses-on-the-site-qaf-form-row">Workbench: Saved Views and Search Responses on the Site (QAF form row)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach them: the context menu > Saved Views / Search Responses on the Site…. Keyless. Saved Views: “Saved views are read now and then (rule 9).” Search: “The filter box finds within loaded rows; this asks the site.”</li> <li>Saved Views: <ul> <li>A report titled “Saved Views - {site}”, one line per view: “{name}: {kind} view of {form}, {status}”.</li> <li>With none: “This site has no saved views of its responses.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Search Responses: <ul> <li>A question titled “Search Responses - {title}”, with “&Words:” (help “Found in any answer, on the site.”).</li> <li>Then a report titled “Responses with {words} - {title}”, with lines “Response {id}, {date}: {answers}”, or “No response has those words.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Undo: not applicable. Both only read.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_forms_site.py, core/wordpress/workbench_forms_site.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Saved Views is offered on one form’s row but lists every view on the site. Views of other forms are named “form {id}”, because only this form’s title is known.</li> <li>Search results are a read-only report, not rows. You cannot mark a found response as spam, or open it, from there. It searches Received responses only (<code>status: active</code>), so held spam is never found.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="workbench-delete-form-qaf-form-row">Workbench: Delete Form (QAF form row)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: the context menu > Delete Form…. Keyless: “Deleting a form deletes its responses: never a bare key (rule 4).” Shift+Delete, the Workbench’s Delete Permanently key, is not on form rows.</li> <li>What opens: a preview titled “Delete Form”. Summary: “Delete {title} and its responses from {site}.” Lines: <ul> <li>“{title} is deleted from {site}, for good: the plugin keeps no Trash for forms.”</li> <li>“Its {n} responses, their notes and uploaded files are deleted with it.”</li> <li>“Pages that show it will show nothing in its place. Export the responses first (Tools > Forms > Responses) if you need them.”</li> <li>“To go ahead, you will type the form’s name, {title}, exactly.”</li> <li>Button: “Go On to Confirm”.</li> </ul></li> <li>Then a question titled “Delete Form”: “&Type the form’s name, {title}, to delete it:”. Help: “The form is deleted only when this is its name exactly. Cancel deletes nothing.”</li> <li>Announced after: “Deleted {title} and its responses from {site}.” On a mismatch: “The name typed did not match, so nothing was deleted.”</li> <li>Undo: none, and the preview says so.</li> <li>Without permission: tier DESTRUCTIVE, no capability named. Under anything but Full access, the access sentence.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_forms_site.py (<code>do_delete_form</code>).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Pressing Cancel at the name question also says “The name typed did not match, so nothing was deleted.” That reads as an error for a deliberate cancel.</li> <li>“Export the responses first (Tools > Forms > Responses)” sends a Workbench user to the editor’s menu, because the Workbench cannot export QAF responses itself.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="workbench-not-spam-and-spam-on-a-response-row">Workbench: Not Spam and Spam (on a response row)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: the response row’s context menu > Not Spam / Spam. Also Item > Approve, or Not Spam (Ctrl+Shift+A) and Item > Spam (Ctrl+Shift+S), on one row or a selection.</li> <li>What it does: <ul> <li>QAF: <code>POST qaf/v1/entries/{id}/spam</code> or <code>.../not-spam</code>, which tells QAF’s spam providers and sends a released response’s notifications. An older QAF answers 404 or 405, and only the status is set, “which moves it but tells nobody”.</li> <li>Gravity Forms: the whole entry is sent back with its status changed.</li> <li>There is no preview or confirmation (tier ORGANIZE).</li> </ul></li> <li>Announced after: <ul> <li>“Not spam: {n} items on {site}, and the site was told.” when every selected row was held as spam.</li> <li>Otherwise “{N} items approved on {site}.” or “{N} items marked as spam on {site}.”</li> <li>Partial failure appends “ Not changed: {reasons}“.</li> <li>With no response or comment selected: “Choose a comment or a form entry.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z (Edit > Undo WordPress Change) marks the responses back the other way, and is recorded in Change History (Ctrl+Shift+H).</li> <li>Without permission: needs “forms”, which maps to <code>edit_posts</code>. Under Read Only access: “Workbench access on {site} is {words}. Site > Workbench Access changes it.” For a role below Contributor: “Editing posts needs the Contributor role or higher. You are a Subscriber on {site}.”</li> <li>F1: ActionRow help “Back to the form’s entries. Ctrl+Shift+A.” and “Hold it as spam. Ctrl+Shift+S.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (<code>_moderate</code>, <code>do_approve</code>, <code>do_spam</code>), core/wordpress/workbench_forms.py (<code>set_entry_spam</code>), core/wordpress/workbench_qaf_spam.py (<code>mark_entry</code>).</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>“and the site was told” is said even when an older QAF only moved the response and told nobody. <code>mark_entry</code> returns the providers it told, and nothing reads that list.</li> <li>The wording is borrowed from comments. Marking a response spam says “1 item marked as spam”, and Not Spam on a response that was not held says “1 item approved”. Responses are never “approved”.</li> <li>Item > Unapprove (Ctrl+Shift+U) on a response acts as Not Spam but announces “1 item unapproved on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo reverses every selected response, including any whose change failed. After Not Spam on a response that was already Received, Ctrl+Z marks it as spam. Plausible from <code>_moderate</code>; verify.</li> <li>The permission refusal talks about “Editing posts” when the person is marking a form response. That is accurate to the capability, but confusing.</li> <li>QAF responses also show Notes… and Resend Notifications… in the context menu (the Gravity rows are shared). Both refuse with “That is a Gravity Forms command. Choose a Gravity Forms form, entry or feed; QUILL Accessible Forms keeps no notes, feeds or notifications.” Star or Unstar and Mark Read or Unread do work on QAF responses (“Starred {n} entries on {site}.” and similar, with undo). The node help says these four are Gravity Forms only.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="workbench-the-site-rows-qaf-spam-commands">Workbench: the site row’s QAF spam commands</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach them: a site row’s context menu > Form Spam Questions… / Form Spam by Week. Both keyless. Questions: “A site’s own questions are written once and changed now and then, from the site’s context menu (rule 9); the settings beside them are on Alt+Shift+Enter.” Weeks: “Read now and then, when spam is in question (rule 9); the weekly Watch says each new week’s numbers without being asked.”</li> <li>Form Spam Questions: <ul> <li>Read in the background, then a choice titled “Form Spam Questions - {site}” with “&Questions (Enter on one changes or removes it):”. The first row is “Add a question…”, then “{question} Right answers: {answers}.” for each.</li> <li>Add: “&Question:”, then “&Right answers (a semicolon between):”, then a preview titled “Add a Spam Question” with an Add Question button. Announces “Added the question. The site asks it now: {question}” or “The site took the question, but reading the bank back, it is not there.”</li> <li>On an existing question: “&What to do:” with “Change its words…”, “Change its answers…” or “Remove it…”. Each change has its own preview: “Change a Spam Question” (“Was: …”, “Becomes: …”) or “Remove a Spam Question”. Announces “Changed the question, and read it back from the site.” or “Removed the question; the site no longer asks it.” (or the matching read-back failure).</li> <li>A site not on qaf/v1 gets the namespace note, for example “This site runs the older BITS Accessible Forms (baf/v1). Its spam settings can be read here, but are changed in wp-admin until the site has QUILL Accessible Forms.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Form Spam by Week: <ul> <li>A report titled “Form Spam by Week - {site}”: the namespace note, then “This week, {week}: {n} asked, {n} answered, {n} answered wrongly, {n} sent anyway.” and the earlier weeks. With none: “No weeks yet.”</li> <li>The weekly Watch says, without being asked, “Form spam questions on {site}, week {line} Up from {n} asked the week before.” (or Down).</li> </ul></li> <li>The site’s Properties, Spam page (Alt+Shift+Enter per the row help): <ul> <li>Shows QAF’s checks as check boxes, the limits as numbers, “Ask a question” as a choice, the question kinds as check boxes, “Offer Send Anyway after this many wrong answers”, and a write-only “New OOPSpam key” field.</li> <li>OK or Apply shows a preview titled “Change Form Spam Settings”, with a Save Settings button.</li> <li>Then it announces “Saved {n} spam settings, and read them back from the site.” or “Saved …, but reading them back, {problems}. Check Settings, Spam in wp-admin.”</li> <li>A conflict: “The spam settings were changed on the site since they were read, so nothing was sent. Open the site’s Properties again to see them as they are now.”</li> <li>Without permission, the fields are read only, with the reason as their F1 help: “Changing the spam settings needs the site’s Manage Options capability, or QUILL Accessible Forms’ own Manage Settings.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Undo: none for questions or settings. Not in Change History.</li> <li>Without permission: both rows are tier WRITE (Questions) or READ (Weeks), with no capability named. The site’s own refusal is spoken by the failure handler.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_qaf_spam.py, core/wordpress/workbench_qaf_spam_edit.py, core/wordpress/workbench_qaf_spam.py.</li> <li>Review notes: <ul> <li>Cancelling the Question prompt of Add a question announces “A question needs its words and at least one right answer. Nothing was added.” That is a reproach for a deliberate Cancel.</li> <li>The Spam page’s save remembers the revision read when the sheet opened. A second Apply in the same sheet, after a first that succeeded, sends the old revision and is likely to be refused as a conflict caused by your own first save. Plausible from <code>site_sheet_with_spam</code>; verify.</li> <li><code>on_save</code> returns True as soon as the background save starts. The sheet can close before a conflict is reported, and the edits typed into it are lost with it.</li> <li>These two commands sit on every site row, including sites with no QAF. There they answer “This site has no QUILL Accessible Forms.” only after a background read.</li> </ul></li> </ul> <h3 id="workbench-copy-commands-between-qaf-and-gravity-forms">Workbench: Copy commands between QAF and Gravity Forms</h3> <ul> <li>Copy as a Gravity Form… is on a QAF form row (“A new Gravity Forms form from this one, after a preview of what cannot carry over”), on a site that has Gravity Forms too. Copy as a QUILL Accessible Form… is on a Gravity Forms form row.</li> <li>Copy to Site… and Properties… (Alt+Enter) are the Workbench’s shared rows on a form row.</li> <li>See the Gravity Forms section for how the copy commands work.</li> <li>Code: core/wordpress/workbench_gravity_actions.py (<code>GRAVITY_QAF_ROWS</code>, <code>GRAVITY_BUILD_ROWS</code>).</li> </ul> <h2 id="gravity-forms">Gravity Forms</h2> <p>Gravity Forms lives under each site’s Forms node, beside QUILL Accessible Forms (QAF). QUILL finds it only when the site’s REST index lists the gf/v2 namespace. You can work through responses with four Ctrl+Alt chords (Notes, Star, Read, Resend), mark them spam or not spam, and change a response’s answers in its Properties sheet. On forms, you can read feeds and results, send a test entry, change one field’s control, edit or build a whole form in QUILL’s own form editor, and copy a form between Gravity Forms and QAF. Every change that reaches the site is previewed. Most changes have a Ctrl+Z. The commands without a chord sit under Item, Gravity Forms (Alt+I, then F), and each has an access letter. Note for the review: in git, every Gravity Forms module and its tests are still untracked files (status “??”), the entries mixin included.</p> <h3 id="where-gravity-forms-appears-in-the-tree">Where Gravity Forms appears in the tree</h3> <ul> <li>Sites > site > Forms: one row per form from every form plugin QUILL reads. Columns: form, plugin, entries, status. A Gravity Forms row has the row type “gravity_form” and the plugin cell “Gravity Forms”. Its status cell is always “active”, whatever the form’s real state (hard-coded in workbench_forms.py, _gravity_rows).</li> <li>Sites > site > Forms > Responses: entries from every form plugin, newest first. Columns: form, plugin, status (“Received”, “Held as spam” or “Trash”), when. For Gravity Forms, at most 300 rows, read one page unless the Filter Builder sent conditions to the site.</li> <li>Sites > site > Forms > Held as Spam: the same columns. Entries whose status is spam.</li> <li>Sites > site > Forms > Feeds: every add-on feed of every Gravity Forms form. Columns: name, form, addon, status (“Active” or “Inactive”). The details pane says, for example, “Newsletter, a Mailchimp feed on Contact, active. It runs when this form is submitted.”</li> <li>Sites > site > Forms > Results: quiz, poll and survey results. One row per answer. Columns: form, question, answer, count, share (“40 percent”). The details pane holds the whole question as one sentence: “Favourite colour, 12 responses: Red 5, 42 percent; Blue 7, 58 percent.” While Gravity Forms is still counting, the only row says: “Gravity Forms is still counting the results of {form}. Press F5 in a moment.”</li> <li>A site with no forms plugin: the Forms node’s error is “{site} has no forms plugin QUILL can read. QUILL Accessible Forms and Gravity Forms are found by their REST routes; this site lists neither.”</li> <li>Node F1 (workbench_node_help.py): Forms “The site’s forms, from every form plugin QUILL reads.”; Responses “Responses to the site’s forms.”; Held as Spam “Form responses held as spam.”; Feeds “Each Gravity Forms form’s add-on feeds, such as Mailchimp, Stripe or Zapier, and whether each is active. A form’s Show Its Feeds lands here filtered to it.”; Results “Quiz, poll and survey results from Gravity Forms: a row per answer with its count and share, and the question’s results as one sentence in the details pane.”</li> <li>Review notes: a response row’s title is “{form title} entry”, so every response to one form has the same name. “Added a note to Contact entry on Example Site” never says which entry. The entry number is in the Properties sheet but not in any spoken outcome.</li> <li>Review notes: the list shows no column that tells two responses apart (no submitter name or email). Only the details pane carries the first three answers.</li> </ul> <h3 id="context-menus-on-gravity-forms-rows-order-as-shown">Context menus on Gravity Forms rows (order as shown)</h3> <ul> <li>A response (form_entry), 7 rows plus Properties, so there is no More submenu: Read in Full (default, Enter), Not Spam, Spam, Notes…, Star or Unstar, Mark Read or Unread, Resend Notifications…, Properties….</li> <li>A form (gravity_form), 9 rows plus Properties, so 6 show and 3 go under More: Show Its Responses (default), Change a Field Type…, Show Its Feeds, Show Its Results, Test This Form…, Edit Form…, Properties…, then More: New Gravity Form…, Copy as a QUILL Accessible Form…, Copy to Site….</li> <li>A feed (gravity_feed): Read in Full (default), Activate or Deactivate…, Rename…, Delete Permanently…, Properties….</li> <li>A result (gravity_result): Read in Full (default), Properties….</li> <li>A QAF form row on a site that has Gravity Forms too also offers “Copy as a Gravity Form…”.</li> <li>Review notes: New Gravity Form is under More on every form row, but it acts on the site, not on the form you are on. On the form menu it is the only row that ignores the selection.</li> <li>Review notes: the form row’s F1 sentence says “Enter lists responses; the context menu shows its feeds and results, tests it, and changes a field type.” It does not mention Edit Form, New Gravity Form or the two Copy commands.</li> </ul> <h3 id="the-item-menu-for-gravity-forms">The Item menu for Gravity Forms</h3> <ul> <li>Four rows sit on the Item menu itself, each with a chord and no access letter: “Entry Notes…” Ctrl+Alt+N, “Star or Unstar Entry” Ctrl+Alt+F, “Mark Entry Read or Unread” Ctrl+Alt+U, “Resend Notifications…” Ctrl+Alt+I.</li> <li>Item > Gravity &Forms (Alt+I, then F) holds the rest, each with an access letter: Show Its &Feeds (F), Show Its &Results (R), &Test This Form… (T), Change a Field T&ype… (Y), &Edit Form… (E), Ne&w Gravity Form… (W), Copy as a &QUILL Accessible Form… (Q), Copy QAF Form as a &Gravity Form… (G), then a separator, &Activate or Deactivate Feed… (A), Re&name Feed… (N).</li> <li>Review notes: the four chord rows have no access letter, so walking Item by letter cannot reach them. Every other Item row has one.</li> <li>Review notes: Ctrl+Alt+F for Star and Ctrl+Alt+I for Resend are hard to guess. The help says “Star it to come back to. Ctrl+Alt+F.” and gives no hint why F (flag) or I.</li> <li>Review notes: the labels differ between the two menus: “Notes…” on the context menu and “Entry Notes…” on Item; “Star or Unstar” against “Star or Unstar Entry”; “Copy as a Gravity Form…” against “Copy QAF Form as a Gravity Form…”. A listener who learns one name hears another.</li> </ul> <h3 id="permissions-applies-to-every-gravity-forms-command">Permissions (applies to every Gravity Forms command)</h3> <ul> <li>Each command first checks that it is on a Gravity Forms row. On any other row it says: “That is a Gravity Forms command. Choose a Gravity Forms form, entry or feed; QUILL Accessible Forms keeps no notes, feeds or notifications.” Star and Mark Read are the exception: they also work on QAF responses.</li> <li>Then the Gravity Forms permission. gform_full_access (what an administrator has) counts for all of them. The sentence is “{words} needs the Gravity Forms permission {capability with spaces}. You are {role} on {site}.” For example: “Reading an entry’s notes needs the Gravity Forms permission gravityforms view entry notes. You are Editor on Example Site.”</li> <li>The permission each command needs (NEEDS and CAPABILITIES in workbench_gravity_actions.py and workbench_gravity.py): Notes, gravityforms_view_entry_notes; adding a note, gravityforms_edit_entry_notes; Star, Read and Resend, gravityforms_edit_entries; feed Activate, Rename and Delete, gravityforms_edit_forms; Edit Form, gravityforms_edit_forms; New Gravity Form and Copy as a Gravity Form, gravityforms_create_form; Change a Field Type, gravityforms_create_form.</li> <li>Then the Workbench access tier. The sentence is “Workbench access on {site} is {words}. Site > Workbench Access changes it.” In Safe Mode it is “Safe Mode is on, so the Workbench only reads.” Under View As Role it is “You are viewing {site} as {Role}, which only reads. Site > View As Role ends it.”</li> <li>A row the person may not use is greyed on the menu. Its chord speaks the refusal instead.</li> <li>Review notes: “You are Editor on” has no article. The core permission sentence elsewhere says “You are an Editor on”. Two styles of the same message sit side by side in one window.</li> <li>Review notes: Change a Field Type checks gravityforms_create_form, but Edit Form, which can change every field, checks gravityforms_edit_forms. Someone who may edit forms but not create them can rebuild a whole form in the editor, yet is refused the smaller job.</li> <li>Review notes: Not Spam and Spam on a response check the WordPress capability edit_posts (requirement “forms”). They do not check a Gravity Forms permission, unlike every other Gravity entry command.</li> </ul> <h3 id="read-in-full-response-feed-result">Read in Full (response, feed, result)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter on the row, or the first row of the context menu.</li> <li>What it does: moves focus to the details pane. For a response, the pane reads “{form}, Gravity Forms, {site}, {when}.” followed by up to three answers, each “{field id}: {first 80 characters}”. A held one adds “ held as spam“.</li> <li>Review notes: the details pane names answers by field number (“3: Jane”), not by question label. Properties does better (labels from the form). The pane is what a listener hears first.</li> <li>Code: workbench_forms.py (_entry_row, _answers_sentence).</li> </ul> <h3 id="not-spam-response">Not Spam (response)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu “Not Spam”; Ctrl+Shift+A; Item > “&Approve, or Not Spam” (Alt+I, A).</li> <li>What opens: nothing. It runs on every selected comment and response at once.</li> <li>What it does: for Gravity Forms it sends the whole entry back with status “active” (PUT gf/v2/entries/{id}).</li> <li>Announced after: if every selected row was spam, “Not spam: {n} item(s) on {site}, and the site was told.” Otherwise “{N} item(s) approved on {site}.” Failures add “ Not changed: {up to three reasons}“.</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z, which sends spam again: “Put {n} items back as they were.”</li> <li>F1 (row): “A form response. Enter reads it in full. On Gravity Forms, the context menu also has Notes, Star, Mark Read and Resend Notifications, and Properties changes its answers.”</li> <li>Without permission: the edit_posts sentence, for example “… needs the Contributor role or higher. You are a Subscriber on {site}.” (the lowest role that has edit_posts).</li> <li>Keyless: it has a chord, Ctrl+Shift+A.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (_moderate), workbench_forms.py (set_entry_spam).</li> <li>Review notes: “and the site was told” is true for QAF, which reports to its spam providers. For Gravity Forms only the status changes; no spam service hears about it. The sentence overclaims.</li> <li>Review notes: on a response that is not spam, the outcome says “approved”, a comment word that means nothing for a form entry.</li> <li>Review notes: set_entry_spam sends row.raw, the entry as it was when the list loaded. It does not read the entry again first, unlike Star, Read and answers. An answer changed in wp-admin since the list loaded is overwritten with the old copy. This is a silent data-loss risk.</li> <li>Review notes: Unapprove (Ctrl+Shift+U, on the Item menu) on a response also sets it not spam, while its label says “Back to waiting”.</li> </ul> <h3 id="spam-response">Spam (response)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu “Spam”; Ctrl+Shift+S; Item > &Spam (Alt+I, S).</li> <li>What it does: sends the whole entry with status “spam”. There is no preview.</li> <li>Announced after: “{N} item(s) marked as spam on {site}.” plus any “ Not changed: …“.</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z puts it back as active.</li> <li>Code: as Not Spam.</li> <li>Review notes: the same stale-copy risk as Not Spam. The help “Hold it as spam.” is the only explanation; nothing says whether Gravity Forms then hides it from the Responses node (it moves to Held as Spam after the refresh).</li> </ul> <h3 id="notes-response">Notes… (response)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu “Notes…”; Ctrl+Alt+N; Item > “Entry Notes…” (no access letter).</li> <li>What opens, when you may add notes: a preview dialog titled “Notes - {form} entry”. Tab order: Summary (read-only text, “{n} notes on {form} entry on {site}.”), “Each change:” list (one note a line, oldest first, “{who}, {date}: {text}”, with “(note type)” when not a user note, or “This entry has no notes yet.”), then the default button “Add a Note…”, then Cancel.</li> <li>Choosing Add a Note… opens “Add a Note - {form} entry”: a multiline “Note:” box, OK (default) and Cancel. Help: “A note on this entry, kept by Gravity Forms on {site} and signed as you. Visitors never see it.”</li> <li>What opens, when you may only read: a report window “Notes - {form} entry” with the Report text, Copy All and Close. The last line is the reason, for example “Adding a note to an entry needs the Gravity Forms permission gravityforms edit entry notes.”</li> <li>Announced after: “Added a note to {form} entry on {site}.” An empty note sends nothing and says nothing.</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z deletes the note: “Removed the note from {form} entry.”</li> <li>Cancel: closing the preview leaves without adding.</li> <li>Keyless: it has a chord.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_gravity_entries.py (do_entry_notes, _add_note), workbench_gravity.py (read_notes, note_lines, add_note).</li> <li>Review notes: the notes are shown in a dialog built for change previews. The list is labelled “Each change:” and its help says “One line for each thing the change creates, replaces or skips”. Both are wrong for reading notes, and the listener hears them every time.</li> <li>Review notes: Enter in the notes list presses the default button “Add a Note…”. A person reading notes with Enter in mind ends up in the Add a Note box.</li> <li>Review notes: long notes are one list row each; a listbox truncates nothing, but there is no way to read one note in a text box or copy it, except the read-only report version for people who cannot add.</li> </ul> <h3 id="star-or-unstar-response">Star or Unstar (response)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu “Star or Unstar”; Ctrl+Alt+F; Item > “Star or Unstar Entry”.</li> <li>What opens: nothing. It works on every selected response from one site. A Gravity Forms entry is read again and sent whole with is_starred changed.</li> <li>Direction: the first selected row decides. If it is not starred, every selected row is starred, even ones already starred.</li> <li>Announced after: “Starred {n} entries on {site}.” or “Unstarred 1 entry on {site}.” Mixed sites: “Change entries from one site at a time.” Nothing chosen: “Choose a Gravity Forms or QUILL Accessible Forms response first.”</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z: “Put {n} entries back as they were.”</li> <li>Also on QAF responses.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_gravity_entries.py (_toggle_flag), workbench_gravity.py (set_entry_flag).</li> <li>Review notes: the list has no Starred column, so after the refresh nothing tells a listener which rows are starred except Properties.</li> </ul> <h3 id="mark-read-or-unread-response">Mark Read or Unread (response)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu “Mark Read or Unread”; Ctrl+Alt+U; Item > “Mark Entry Read or Unread”.</li> <li>Behaviour, outcomes and undo: as Star. Spoken “Marked read {n} entries on {site}.” or “Marked unread 1 entry on {site}.”</li> <li>Review notes: “Marked read 3 entries” reads oddly; “Marked 3 entries read” would be natural English. No Read column either.</li> </ul> <h3 id="resend-notifications-response">Resend Notifications… (response)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu “Resend Notifications…”; Ctrl+Alt+I; Item > “Resend Notifications…”.</li> <li>What opens: first a check-box dialog “Resend Notifications - {form} entry”. Its group is “Notifications to send again”, one check box per notification, each “{name}: to {recipients}”, with “, sent on {event}” for one not sent on submission and “, turned off” for an inactive one. Recipients are worked out for this entry: “the address in Email, <a href="mailto:jane@example.com">jane@example.com</a>”, “the site’s admin email”, routing as “{email} when {field} is {value}”, plus “, with a blind copy to {bcc}”. Active submission notifications start checked. OK (default), Cancel.</li> <li>Preview: “Resend Notifications”. Summary “Send {n} notification(s) again for {form} entry on {site}?”. Lines: each chosen notification, then “Each person listed gets the email again, as if the form had just been sent.” Button “Resend”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Gravity Forms sent {n} notification(s) again for {form} entry: {names}.” If it sent none: “Gravity Forms sent none for {form} entry. A notification’s own conditions may not match this entry.” No notifications on the form: “The form behind {form} entry has no notifications to send.”</li> <li>Undo: none. The change is recorded without an undo.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_gravity_entries.py (do_resend_notifications), workbench_gravity.py (entry_notifications, resend_notifications).</li> <li>Review notes: because it is recorded with no undo, Ctrl+Z straight after says “{that sentence} cannot be undone.” (history.py), with a doubled full stop (“Admin Notification. cannot be undone.”). It also blocks Ctrl+Z for every change before it, since the history never steps past an un-undoable change.</li> <li>Review notes: unchecking every box and pressing OK returns an empty list. The code treats that as Cancel and says nothing.</li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-on-a-response-and-editing-its-answers">Properties on a response, and editing its answers</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Alt+Enter on a Gravity Forms response, or Properties… (last row of the context menu), or Item > &Properties… (Alt+I, P).</li> <li>What opens: a sheet titled “{form} entry Properties - {site}”, with three pages: <ul> <li>General: Entry number, Form, Status, Received, Last changed, Sent from (a link) (all read only), then two check boxes, Starred and Read.</li> <li>Answers: one editable field per answer, in field order (1, 1.3, 2, 10). Each is labelled with the form’s admin label or label, and a part of a field gets “{field}, {part}” (for example “Name, First”). When two answers share a label, the label adds “ (field {id})“. Answers longer than 80 characters or with a line break get a multiline box. Help: “The visitor’s answer to {label}. Saving previews the change, then sends the whole response back; Ctrl+Z puts the old answer back.”</li> <li>Notes: the notes, read only, one a line. If they cannot be read, the page shows the site’s error or “Gravity Forms did not return the notes.”</li> </ul></li> <li>Saving: if nothing changed, “Nothing changed on this entry.” Changed answers get a preview, “Change Answers”, whose summary is “Change {n} answer(s) in {form} entry on {site}? Gravity Forms keeps no earlier version, so Ctrl+Z is the way back.” Lines: “{label}: {old or (empty)} becomes {new or (empty)}”. Button “Change”. Starred and Read changes alone are sent with no preview.</li> <li>Conflict check: just before writing, the entry is read again. If its date_updated moved, or any answer being changed no longer says what the sheet showed, nothing is sent and QUILL says: “This response was changed in Gravity Forms after QUILL read it. Nothing was sent; open its Properties again and make the change there.”</li> <li>Announced after: “Saved {n} change(s) to {form} entry on {site}.”</li> <li>Without permission: “Changing an entry needs the Gravity Forms permission gravityforms edit entries.” (or the Workbench access sentence), said on save. The sheet’s fields are locked earlier only by the WordPress capability edit_posts.</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z sends the old answers and flags back: “Put {form} entry back as it was.” The undo does not check date_updated.</li> <li>Status: built. The core is workbench_gravity_answers.py (answer_ids, answer_labels, set_answers) and the UI is gravity_sheet and gravity_entry_save in wordpress_workbench_gravity_entries.py, wired through wordpress_workbench_sheets.py. Tests exist in tests/unit/core/wordpress/test_wp_workbench_gaps_core.py and tests/unit/ui/wordpress/test_wp_workbench_gaps.py. Not yet in the engineering log (docs/design/wordpress.md has no entry for it); wp_missing.md still lists it as missing.</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_gravity_entries.py, workbench_gravity_answers.py, wordpress_workbench_sheets.py.</li> <li>Review notes: a choice answer (drop-down, radio, check box part) is a plain text box. Nothing offers the form’s choices, so a typo stores a value the form never allowed. The same goes for dates and numbers: no check before sending.</li> <li>Review notes: a check-box field’s parts each become a field (“Toppings, Cheese”), and the value is the choice value, not “checked”. An empty part means unchecked, which a listener must guess.</li> <li>Review notes: fields locked by edit_posts give a WordPress-role reason, then the save gives a Gravity Forms reason. A person can be told two different things for the same edit.</li> <li>Review notes: the undo sends without the date_updated check, so Ctrl+Z after someone else’s later edit overwrites it.</li> <li>Review notes: the sheet’s title is “{form} entry Properties”, with no entry number. The number is only inside General.</li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-on-a-feed-or-a-result">Properties on a feed or a result</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: a read-only sheet titled “{Gravity Feed}” or “{Gravity Result}” (the kind, title-cased), with one General page. It has one multiline field per top-level key the site sent, labelled from the key (“Addon slug”, “Meta”, “Is active”).</li> <li>Review notes: this is a raw dump. Meta is JSON text, and the key names are Gravity Forms’ own. Nothing here is editable, although Rename and Activate exist on the menu.</li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-on-a-form">Properties on a form</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: a form row is not one of the three Gravity sheet kinds, so it gets the generic sheet. That is the route’s schema if OPTIONS answers, else a read-only list of every top-level key, with fields, notifications and confirmations as JSON text.</li> <li>Review notes: the sheet title comes from the row’s title, which for a form row is its plugin cell, so the title is “Gravity Forms Properties - {site}”, not the form’s name. (The commands work around this with a replace; the sheet does not.)</li> <li>Review notes: the generic sheet is not where a form is edited (Edit Form is), but nothing on it says so.</li> </ul> <h3 id="show-its-responses-form">Show Its Responses (form)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Enter on a form row, or the first context-menu row.</li> <li>What it does: selects Responses and puts the form’s name in the filter.</li> <li>F1 (row): “A Gravity Forms form. Enter lists responses; the context menu shows its feeds and results, tests it, and changes a field type.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_commands.py (do_show_entries).</li> </ul> <h3 id="show-its-feeds-form">Show Its Feeds (form)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu “Show Its Feeds”; Alt+I, F, F.</li> <li>What it does: selects Forms > Feeds, puts form:“{title}” in the filter box, applies it, and moves focus to the list. Nothing is announced; the screen reader reads the list.</li> <li>On a non-Gravity row: “Choose a Gravity Forms form first.”</li> <li>Keyless: “Once per question about a form; Enter-and-filter on Forms > Feeds.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_gravity_forms.py.</li> <li>Review notes: filtering by title means two forms with the same title share their feeds in the view.</li> </ul> <h3 id="show-its-results-form">Show Its Results (form)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu “Show Its Results”; Alt+I, F, R.</li> <li>What it does: as Show Its Feeds, on Forms > Results.</li> <li>Keyless: “Read after a poll closes, not daily; Forms > Results is one key.”</li> <li>Review notes: a form with no quiz, poll or survey field adds no rows (load_results skips it), so the filtered list is empty and nothing from the Gravity Forms code says the form has nothing to count.</li> </ul> <h3 id="test-this-form-form">Test This Form… (form)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu “Test This Form…”; Alt+I, F, T.</li> <li>What opens: “Test This Form - {title}”, a multiline “Test values, one input a line:” box filled with lines like “input_1: QUILL test”, “input_2: <a href="mailto:test@example.com">test@example.com</a>”, “input_3_3: Quill”. Help: “Each line is a Gravity Forms input and the value QUILL will send. Change any value after the colon; delete a line to leave that input empty.” It adds “ QUILL leaves these empty: {labels}.“ for captcha, upload, signature and administrative fields. OK and Cancel.</li> <li>Check first: the values go to Gravity Forms’ validation route; nothing is saved. If refused, a report “Not Accepted - {title}” lists “{label}: {message}” lines; help “What Gravity Forms refused in the test values. Nothing was saved.”</li> <li>Preview: “Submit a Test Entry”. Summary “Send a test entry to {title} on {site}?”. Lines: on a practice site, “{site} is a practice site, so the test entry costs nothing.”; on a live site, “{site} is a live site. A practice site is better for tests: this entry is real, and its emails and feeds reach real people and services.” Then “Gravity Forms already checked these values; nothing has been saved yet.”, each “{label} ({input}): {value}”, and “Ctrl+Z afterwards moves the test entry to Gravity Forms’ Trash.” Button “Submit Test Entry”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Gravity Forms accepted the test entry to {title} on {site}, entry {id}.” plus “ It said: {confirmation}“. If refused at submission: “Gravity Forms did not accept the test entry: {reasons}”.</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z: “Moved test entry {id} to the Trash.”</li> <li>Keyless: “Run after editing a form, a few times a year (rule 9): a letter.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_gravity_forms.py, workbench_gravity_submit.py.</li> <li>Review notes: the edit box uses Gravity Forms’ input names (input_3_3), not question labels. A listener cannot tell which line is which question until the preview, which shows labels. This is the biggest usability gap in Test This Form.</li> <li>Review notes: on a live site the warning is one line in a list. It is not a separate confirmation, and the default button still submits. Feeds such as Stripe or Mailchimp fire for real.</li> </ul> <h3 id="change-a-field-type-form">Change a Field Type… (form)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu “Change a Field Type…”; Alt+I, F, Y.</li> <li>What opens: “Change a Field Type - {title}”, a list labelled “Field:”, one row per field QUILL can convert, “{id}: {label} ({type name})”. Help: “Choose a text, email, number or choice field in this Gravity Forms form.” Then “Change Field Type”, a list labelled “New control:” with all seven types (“Single line text (text)”, “Paragraph text (textarea)”, “Email address (email)”, “Number (number)”, “Drop-down list (select)”, “Radio buttons (radio)”, “Checkboxes (checkbox)”), starting on the current one.</li> <li>Preview: “Update Gravity Forms Field”. Summary “Change {label} (field {id}) from {old} to {new}.” Lines: “Site: {site}”, “Form: {title}”, “Kept: …”, “Adjusted: …” (for example “added a starter choice so the new control can be filled in”), “Cannot carry over: …”, “QUILL will send the complete form, preserving its other fields and settings.” Button “Update Form”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Changed {label} in {title} on {site}.” If the form reads back wrong: “{title} on {site} was saved, but it reads back differently: {problems} Ctrl+Z puts the form back as it was.” Same type chosen: “The field already uses that control.” No convertible fields: “{title} has no fields QUILL can convert.” If the form changed while you reviewed: “{title} on {site} changed while you reviewed the conversion. Reopen the form and review the current fields.”</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z: “Restored {label} in {title}.” It refuses if the form changed since: “{title} on {site} changed since this conversion. QUILL did not replace the newer form.” Redo is offered.</li> <li>Without permission: “Changing a form’s fields needs the Gravity Forms permission gravityforms create form. You are {role} on {site}.” On a QAF row: “This is not a Gravity Forms form. QAF fields are changed in Form Outline.”</li> <li>Keyless: “A form-design change made rarely (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_gravity_commands.py, workbench_forms.py (GRAVITY_FIELD_TYPES, plan_gravity_field_type_change, gravity_replacement_problems).</li> <li>Review notes: the type names here (“Single line text”, “Drop-down list”) differ from the form editor’s (“Single Line Text”, “Drop Down”). One product, two vocabularies.</li> <li>Review notes: “Cannot carry over” lists raw Gravity Forms keys (“enableChoiceValue”, “randomize”) that mean nothing to a listener.</li> <li>Review notes: the help for the editor’s Type box points here to change a type (“Change a Field Type on the form changes it later”), but only 7 of the editor’s 18 types convert. A Phone or Date field never appears in the Field list, with no sentence saying why.</li> </ul> <h3 id="edit-form-form">Edit Form… (form)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu “Edit Form…”; Alt+I, F, E.</li> <li>What opens: the form editor, “Edit Gravity Form - {title}”. Tab order: <ul> <li>Title, Description (multiline), Button text, Label placement (Top aligned, Left aligned, Right aligned), Confirmation message (multiline; disabled when the default confirmation sends people to a page, with help saying so), Notification to (disabled when the Admin Notification goes to a field or by routing), Notification subject (disabled when there is no notification).</li> <li>Fields: a list, one row a field, such as “2. Email, Email, required”, with a condition said short or “has conditional logic QUILL keeps”. An empty form shows “No fields yet. Insert or Add Field adds one.”</li> <li>Buttons: Add Field… (A), Edit Field… (E), Move Up (U), Move Down (W), Remove Field (R), Condition… (N).</li> <li>Review and Save… (default, OK), Cancel.</li> <li>In the Fields list: Enter edits, Insert adds after, Delete removes, Ctrl+Up and Ctrl+Down move.</li> </ul></li> <li>Add Field / Edit Field: “Add Field” or “Edit Field - {name}”. Controls: Type (a choice of 18; disabled when editing), Label, Description, Required check box, Placeholder, Default value, Admin label, CSS class, Choices (one a line; “text = value” stores a value; a leading asterisk preselects), Content (HTML blocks only), Check box text (Consent only). Controls that do not apply to the type are hidden. OK, Cancel.</li> <li>Condition: “Condition - {name}”. When (Always show this field, Show this field when, Hide this field when), Field (only earlier fields), Test (is, is not), Value (a combo box of the field’s choices). OK, Cancel.</li> <li>Spoken inside the editor: “{name} moved to {n} of {total}.” or “{name} is already first.” / “… last.”; “Removed {name}. Answers already given to it stay in the entries.”; for a field QUILL does not build: “{name} is a {type} field. QUILL keeps it as it is; change it in Gravity Forms, or move or remove it here.”; for complex logic: “{name} has conditional logic with more than one rule. QUILL keeps it as it is; change it in Gravity Forms.”; with no earlier field: “A condition depends on a question before this one, and there is none.”</li> <li>Check before saving: if the accessibility checks find anything, a preview “Check Before Saving” says “{n} things to look at in {title} before it is saved. Cancel goes back to the editor, on the first of them.” Each finding is a line (for example “Field 3 (text) has no label. Give it one.”, “Two questions are both called Name: fields 1 and 4. A listener cannot tell them apart; …”). Button “Save Anyway”. Cancel reopens the editor with focus on the first field named.</li> <li>Preview: “Save Gravity Form”. Summary “Save {title} on {site}.” Lines: “Site: {site}”, each setting changed (“Title: {new} (was {old}).”), “Add field {n}: {name}, {type}.”, “Change {name}: {what}.”, “Remove {name}. Answers already given to it stay in the entries.”, “Fields move: {the whole order}.”, and always “Entries are never read, changed or deleted: only the form itself is sent.” Button “Save Form”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Saved {title} on {site}. Entries were not touched.” If it reads back wrong: “{title} on {site} was saved, but it reads back differently: {problems} Ctrl+Z puts it back as it was.” Unchanged: “Nothing changed in {title}; nothing was sent.” Changed on the site meanwhile: “{title} changed on the site since you opened it. Nothing was sent; open it again to see the newer form.”</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z: “Put {title} back as it was on {site}.” Redo: “Saved {title} again on {site}.”</li> <li>Without permission: “That needs the Gravity Forms permission gravityforms edit forms. You are {role} on {site}.” No gf/v2 routes: “Gravity Forms is not on {site}: the site’s REST index has no gf/v2 routes. Its REST API must be turned on in Forms > Settings > REST API.”</li> <li>Keyless: “Designing a form is occasional work, not daily (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_gravity_build.py, wordpress_gravity_editor.py, wordpress_gravity_field_dialogs.py, gravity_build.py, gravity_build_checks.py, gravity_build_save.py.</li> <li>Review notes: Cancel (or Escape) in the editor throws away every edit with no “Discard changes?” question. After the Check Before Saving loop it is easy to lose a session’s work with one Escape.</li> <li>Review notes: Remove Field takes effect at once in the editor with no confirmation. The preview catches it before anything is sent, but there is no in-editor undo.</li> <li>Review notes: the save preview says label placement as its code: “Label placement: left_label (was top_label).”</li> <li>Review notes: “Fields move:” speaks the entire field order, not what moved. On a 30-field form one move is a very long line.</li> <li>Review notes: the Choices syntax (“text = value”, leading asterisk) is power-user markup typed into a text box, with the rules only in F1 help.</li> <li>Review notes: only the default confirmation and the Admin Notification are editable; others are kept untouched, and the editor never says that more exist.</li> </ul> <h3 id="new-gravity-form-site-from-a-forms-row-or-node">New Gravity Form… (site, from a Forms row or node)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+N (New Here) on a site’s Forms node; More > “New Gravity Form…” on a form row; Alt+I, F, W.</li> <li>What opens: “New Gravity Form”, one “Title:” box. Help: “The new form’s name. The editor opens next; nothing is sent until you review and save.” Then the same editor, titled “New Gravity Form - {title}”, starting with no fields, a Submit button, the default confirmation and an Admin Notification.</li> <li>Preview: “Save Gravity Form”, summary “Make {title} on {site}.”, lines starting “New form: {title}.” Button “Make Form”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Made {title} on {site}. Entries were not touched.”</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z: “Moved {title} to Gravity Forms’ Trash on {site}, where it can be restored.”</li> <li>Without permission: “That needs the Gravity Forms permission gravityforms create form. You are {role} on {site}.”</li> <li>Keyless: “New Here (Ctrl+N) on a site’s Forms node reaches it.”</li> <li>Review notes: Ctrl+N on Forms always means a Gravity form. On a QAF-only site it says “Gravity Forms is not on {site}: …” instead of making a QAF form or saying how to make one.</li> <li>Review notes: “Entries were not touched.” after making a brand-new form is noise.</li> <li>Review notes: Ctrl+N on Forms > Responses, Feeds or Results is not mapped and gives the generic New Here sentence.</li> </ul> <h3 id="copy-as-a-quill-accessible-form-gravity-form">Copy as a QUILL Accessible Form… (Gravity form)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: More > “Copy as a QUILL Accessible Form…” on a Gravity form row; Alt+I, F, Q.</li> <li>Preview: “Copy as a QUILL Accessible Form”. Summary “Make a QUILL Accessible Forms draft from {title} on {site}.” Lines: “Site: {site}”, “New QUILL Accessible Forms draft: {title}.”, each “Cannot carry over: {thing}” (for example “The label placement cannot carry over; labels go above their fields.”), “Entries stay with the Gravity Forms form; none are copied.” Button “Make Draft”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Made a QUILL Accessible Forms draft of {title} on {site}. Delete it in Forms if you do not want it.” An invalid conversion: “QUILL could not make a valid QUILL Accessible Forms form: {first problem}”. No QAF on the site: “QUILL Accessible Forms is not on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: none. Ctrl+Z says it cannot be undone and blocks earlier undos.</li> <li>Keyless: “Moving a form between plugins is a once-off (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: every other “make” command in this area undoes with Ctrl+Z; this one tells the person to go and delete the draft themselves.</li> </ul> <h3 id="copy-as-a-gravity-form-qaf-form">Copy as a Gravity Form… (QAF form)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: on a QAF form row, context menu “Copy as a Gravity Form…”; Alt+I, F, G (“Copy QAF Form as a Gravity Form…”).</li> <li>Preview: “Copy as a Gravity Form”. Summary “Make a Gravity Forms form from {title} on {site}.” Lines: “Site: {site}”, “New Gravity Forms form: {title}, {n} fields.”, “Cannot carry over: …” lines (for example “Only the first notification carries over.”), “Responses stay with the QUILL Accessible Forms form; none are copied.” Button “Make Gravity Form”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Made {title} on {site}. Entries were not touched.”</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z moves it to Gravity Forms’ Trash.</li> <li>Without permission: the gravityforms create form sentence.</li> <li>Review notes: the new Gravity form goes straight to the site with no accessibility check, while Edit Form and New Gravity Form both run Check Before Saving.</li> </ul> <h3 id="activate-or-deactivate-feed">Activate or Deactivate… (feed)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu “Activate or Deactivate…”; Alt+I, F, A (“Activate or Deactivate Feed…”).</li> <li>What it does: reads the feed again, then previews. Title “Activate Feed” or “Deactivate Feed”. Summary “Activate {name} on {site}?”. Lines: “A {addon} feed on {form}. Submissions will reach it again.” (or “Submissions will stop reaching it.”), “Active: no becomes yes.”, “Every other setting of the feed is sent back exactly as the site has it.” Button “Activate” or “Deactivate”.</li> <li>Announced after: “{name} is now active on {site}.” or “… inactive …”. Feed not returned: “Gravity Forms did not return {name} on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z sends the whole feed as it was: “Put {name} back as it was.”</li> <li>Without permission: “Changing a form’s feeds needs the Gravity Forms permission gravityforms edit forms. You are {role} on {site}.”</li> <li>Keyless: “Switching an add-on on or off is rare and previewed (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_gravity_forms.py, workbench_gravity.py (feed_body).</li> </ul> <h3 id="rename-feed">Rename… (feed)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu “Rename…”; Alt+I, F, N (“Rename Feed…”).</li> <li>What opens: “Rename Feed - {name}”, a “Feed name:” box. Then a preview “Rename Feed”: “Rename {name} on {site}?”, lines “Name: {old} becomes {new}.” and “Every other setting …”. Button “Rename”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Renamed the feed to {new} on {site}.” The same name or a blank one sends nothing and says nothing.</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z.</li> <li>Keyless: “Renaming a feed is rare (rule 9).”</li> </ul> <h3 id="delete-permanently-feed">Delete Permanently… (feed)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: context menu “Delete Permanently…”; Shift+Delete; Alt+I, D.</li> <li>Preview: “Delete Feeds”. Summary “Delete {n} feed(s) from {site} permanently?”. Lines: “{name}: a {addon} feed on {form}, {active|inactive}.” then “Gravity Forms keeps no Trash for feeds. Ctrl+Z here makes each again with the same settings and a new number.” Button “Delete Permanently”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Deleted {n} feed(s) from {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z makes them again: “Made {n} feed(s) again on {site}.”</li> <li>Review notes: the Item menu help for Delete Permanently says “Gone for good, after you confirm by name.” A feed is confirmed with a button, not by name, and Ctrl+Z brings it back, so the same row says two contradictory things.</li> <li>Review notes: feed row F1 says “Shift+Delete deletes it”, which is the only place the key is taught for feeds.</li> </ul> <h3 id="filter-builder-on-responses-gravity">Filter Builder on Responses (Gravity)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Edit > Filter Builder… on Responses or Held as Spam on a site with Gravity Forms.</li> <li>What opens: a run of choice dialogs, all titled “Filter Builder”: Form, then “Filter on:” (Gravity Forms’ field filters), then “How:” (is / is not, only when both exist), then “Value:” (a choice of allowed answers, or a text box: “Part of it is enough”), then, when another site-side condition exists, “Keep the responses that match:” with “All of the conditions” / “Any of the conditions”.</li> <li>What it does: the conditions are sent to the site as gf/v2/entries search; the status bar uses the site’s own count.</li> <li>Without a form: “{site} has no Gravity Forms forms to filter by.” / “Gravity Forms offers no field filters for {title}.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_gravity_forms.py, workbench_gravity_search.py.</li> <li>Review notes: on a site with both QAF and Gravity Forms, Filter Builder on Responses always opens the Gravity Forms builder (gravity_filter_builder returns True whenever the site has Gravity Forms), so QAF responses cannot be filtered by their questions there.</li> <li>Review notes: five or six dialogs in a row to build one condition, each titled only “Filter Builder”, so the title never tells a listener which step they are on.</li> </ul> <h2 id="shop">Shop</h2> <p>The Shop node is WooCommerce, read through wc/v3. Products, orders, coupons, customers, reports, store settings, reviews, shipping, taxes, attributes, brands and webhooks each have a node under Shop. Every write is previewed, and a refund needs the amount typed again. None of the shop commands is on the Item menu: they are context-menu rows (Applications key or Shift+F10), plus Ctrl+N (New Here) on a node and Delete (Move to Trash) on a row. Most rows name no capability, so they are not greyed for a role that cannot use them; WooCommerce refuses and the site’s error is spoken. Undo is uneven. The newer commands (reviews, zones, taxes, attributes, brands, webhooks, customers, variations) record a Ctrl+Z. Products, orders, coupons and store settings record nothing at all.</p> <h3 id="where-the-shop-appears-in-the-tree">Where the Shop appears in the tree</h3> <ul> <li>Sites > site > Shop: products. Columns: name, price, stock, status. F1: “WooCommerce’s products, from its wc/v3 routes. Ctrl+N makes one; the context menu edits price, stock, status and categories after a preview. A site without WooCommerce says so. Shop Orders, below, are the shop’s orders.”</li> <li>Shop > Shop Orders: columns what, status, customer, total, date. Each order is read as a receipt, with no email, telephone or billing address.</li> <li>Shop > Draft Products, On Sale, Out of Stock: filters of the products list (same columns).</li> <li>Shop > Customers (name, username, registered, paying), Coupons (code, discount, used, expires), Shop Reports (report, says), Store Settings (group, setting, value), Product Reviews (product, rating, reviewer, status, date), Shipping Zones (zone, locations, methods), Tax Rates (name, rate, where, class), Tax Classes (name, slug), Attributes (name, slug), Brands (name, slug, products), Webhooks (name, topic, status, delivery).</li> <li>A site without WooCommerce: “{site} does not list WooCommerce’s wc/v3 routes in its REST index, so it has no shop QUILL can read. Membership orders from Paid Memberships Pro are under Needs My Attention > Membership Orders.”</li> <li>Node F1 for each is in workbench_plugin_nodes.py (PLUGIN_NODE_HELP), for example Coupons “WooCommerce’s coupons. Ctrl+N makes one, Edit Coupon changes one, and Delete moves one to the Trash, each after a preview.”</li> <li>Review notes: the Shop node sits under the site even on a site without WooCommerce; opening it is how you learn there is none.</li> <li>Review notes: nothing gates a shop row by capability (needs is ““ for products, orders, coupons, settings). An Editor sees every row enabled, fills in five dialogs, previews, and only then hears WooCommerce’s refusal.</li> </ul> <h3 id="properties-on-shop-rows-all-kinds">Properties on shop rows (all kinds)</h3> <ul> <li>Alt+Enter opens a generic sheet. It is built from the route’s schema when OPTIONS answers, otherwise it is a read-only list of every field the site sent, labelled from the key (“Regular price”, “Stock quantity”).</li> <li>Review notes: this is not tailored. A product can have dozens of WooCommerce fields, in WooCommerce’s order. The “Edit Product…” command and the sheet are two different ways to change a product, with different checks.</li> </ul> <h3 id="products-context-menu">Products: context menu</h3> <ul> <li>Order: Open on the Site (default, Enter), Read in Full, Edit Product…, Show Its Variations, Duplicate…, New Product…, Properties…, then More: Copy Link, Move to Trash…, Edit a Variation….</li> <li>F1 (row): “A WooCommerce product. Enter opens it on the site; the context menu edits its price, stock, status and categories, reads and edits its variations, duplicates it, and Delete moves it to the Trash, each after a preview.”</li> <li>Review notes: Enter leaves QUILL for the browser. On every other row type, Enter stays in QUILL. A listener pressing Enter to read a product is sent to the browser.</li> <li>Review notes: Move to Trash is under More, though Delete runs it.</li> </ul> <h3 id="edit-product">Edit Product…</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: five dialogs in a row, each titled “Edit Product - {name}”: “Regular price:”, “Sale price (blank for none):”, “Stock count (blank to leave):”, “Categories (a comma between):” (each filled with the current value), then a list “Status:” (published, draft, pending review, private).</li> <li>Preview: “Edit Product”, summary “Change {name} on {site}.”, the old and new of each changed field. Button “Change Product”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Changed {name} on {site}.” Bad input: “{reason} Nothing was changed.” Nothing different: “Nothing about {name} would change.”</li> <li>Undo: none recorded. Ctrl+Z undoes whatever change came before this one, perhaps on another site.</li> <li>Keyless: “Prices and stock change now and then, behind a preview (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_shop_commands.py, workbench_shop_catalog.py.</li> <li>Review notes: the Status list does not start on the product’s current status (no initial), so OK on a published product makes it published only by luck of order. If the first row is chosen by accident, a draft becomes published. The preview catches it, but only if the person reads it.</li> <li>Review notes: five modal dialogs for one change is slow. One sheet with five fields would be quicker to review and to correct.</li> </ul> <h3 id="show-its-variations">Show Its Variations</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: a report “Variations - {name}”, one variation a line. A simple product: “{name} has no variations; it is not a variable product.”</li> <li>Keyless: “Read when a variable product is in question (rule 9).”</li> </ul> <h3 id="edit-a-variation">Edit a Variation…</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: “Edit Variation - {name}”, a list “Variation:”, then three boxes (Regular price, Sale price, Stock count), then a list “Status:”.</li> <li>Preview: “Edit Variation”, “Change a variation of {name} on {site}.”, the old and new values. Button “Change Variation”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Changed {name}, {variation} on {site}.” with “ The site did not keep: …“ when it did not. A simple product: “{name} has no variations; it is not a variable product.”</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z: “Put {name}, {variation} back.”</li> <li>Conflict: “{name} was changed on {site} after QUILL read it ({fields}). Nothing was sent. F5 reads it again.”</li> <li>Keyless: “Prices and stock change now and then, behind a preview (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: Edit a Variation has an undo and a conflict check; Edit Product, its sibling, has neither.</li> </ul> <h3 id="duplicate-1">Duplicate…</h3> <ul> <li>Preview: “Duplicate Product”, “Copy {name} on {site}.”, lines “WooCommerce copies {name} on {site}, with its prices, stock settings, categories and variations.” and “The copy is a draft named with (Copy) after it; the original is not changed.” Button “Duplicate”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Made a draft copy of {name} on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: none.</li> </ul> <h3 id="new-product">New Product…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+N on Shop, Draft Products, On Sale or Out of Stock; the context menu.</li> <li>What opens: “New Product”, “Name:” then “Price:” (“Such as 12.50, or blank to set it later.”).</li> <li>Preview: lines “A new product on {site}: {name}, {type}, {price}.” and “It is made as a draft, so shoppers cannot see or buy it until you publish it (Edit Product, status published).” Button “Make Product”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Made {name} on {site}, as a draft.”</li> <li>Undo: none.</li> <li>Keyless: “Ctrl+N (New Here) on its node runs it; the context menu has it too.”</li> <li>Review notes: New Product is on every product’s menu, though it ignores the product.</li> </ul> <h3 id="move-to-trash-product">Move to Trash… (product)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Delete on the row; More > Move to Trash….</li> <li>Preview: “Move Product to Trash”, lines “{name} moves to WooCommerce’s Trash on {site}; shoppers can no longer buy it.” and “Past orders keep their lines. Restore it from Products > Trash in wp-admin.” Button “Move to Trash”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Moved {name} to the Trash on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: none. The preview sends the person to wp-admin.</li> <li>Review notes: a Trash that can only be emptied or restored in wp-admin is a dead end. Reviews, by contrast, restore with Ctrl+Z.</li> </ul> <h3 id="copy-link-and-open-on-the-site-product">Copy Link and Open on the Site (product)</h3> <ul> <li>Copy Link: Ctrl+Shift+C, the product’s address. Open on the Site: Enter, in the browser.</li> </ul> <h3 id="shop-orders-context-menu">Shop orders: context menu</h3> <ul> <li>Order: Open in the Shop’s Dashboard (default, Enter), Read in Full (“The order as a receipt, in the details pane.”), Change Status…, Notes…, Refund…, Properties….</li> <li>F1 (row): “A WooCommerce shop order. Not a membership order. The context menu changes its status, reads and adds notes, and makes a refund, which moves money and asks you to type the amount first.”</li> <li>Review notes: Enter opens wp-admin in the browser. Reading the order, which is what most visits are for, is the second row.</li> </ul> <h3 id="change-status-order">Change Status… (order)</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: “Change Status - {order}”, a list “New status:” of every status except the current one (pending payment, processing, on hold, completed, cancelled, refunded, failed).</li> <li>Preview: “Change Order Status”, summary “Shop order {n} on {site} becomes {status}.”, lines “Shop order {n} on {site}: {old} becomes {new}.”, the email it sends (for example “WooCommerce emails the customer that the order is complete. Each email is sent only if it is turned on in WooCommerce > Settings > Emails.”; for refunded, “… but no money moves: a status is only a word. Use Refund to return money.”), then “Stock and payments are not changed by QUILL.” Button “Change Status”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Shop order {n} on {site} is {status}.”</li> <li>Undo: none.</li> <li>Keyless: “Each status change can email a customer: never a bare key.”</li> <li>Review notes: a customer email is sent and there is no undo. That is correct, but the outcome sentence does not say the email went.</li> </ul> <h3 id="notes-order">Notes… (order)</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: a list “Notes - Shop order {n}”, “Notes, newest first:”, each note “{when}, {private or to the customer}, by {author}: {text}”, then two rows “Add a private note…” and “Add a note to the customer…”.</li> <li>Adding: “Add a Note - Shop order {n}”, a “Note:” box, then a preview “Add a Note”: private, “A private note on shop order {n}, {site}. The customer does not see it.” and “It says: {text}”; to the customer, “A note on shop order {n}, {site}, sent to the customer.”, “WooCommerce emails it to them now, and it shows on their order page.”, “It says: …”. Button “Add Note”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Added the note to shop order {n}.” or “… , and WooCommerce emailed it to the customer.”</li> <li>Undo: none.</li> <li>Review notes: notes are listed in a single-line choice list; Enter on a note closes the dialog with nothing done. The two “Add” rows sit after every note, so on a busy order they are a long arrow away (End reaches them).</li> <li>Review notes: the Gravity Forms notes and the shop notes are two different dialogs for the same idea.</li> </ul> <h3 id="refund-order">Refund… (order)</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: one order only (“A refund is made one order at a time, never for a selection. Select one order.”). Then “Refund - Shop order {n}”: “Amount to refund:”, “Reason (kept with the order):”, then “How:” with “Return the money through the payment gateway” or “Record the refund only; I return the money myself”.</li> <li>Preview: “Refund”, summary “Money warning: refund {amount} {currency} on shop order {n}.” Lines include the gateway sentence (“WooCommerce asks {gateway} to return {money} to the customer. This moves real money and cannot be taken back from QUILL or from WooCommerce.”), “Reason recorded: …”, “Left to refund afterwards: …”, “Stock is not put back, and WooCommerce may email the customer about the refund.”, “To go ahead, you will type the amount, {amount}, exactly.” Button “Go On to Confirm”.</li> <li>Confirm: “Type the amount, {amount}, to refund it:”. A mismatch: “The amount typed did not match, so nothing was refunded.”</li> <li>Announced after: “Refunded {amount} {currency} on shop order {n}, through the payment gateway.” or “…, as a record only.”</li> <li>Undo: none (money).</li> <li>Keyless: “A refund moves money: tier 3, never a bare key, never batched.”</li> <li>Review notes: the strongest confirmation in the Workbench, and well worded. The only gap: the “How” list starts on the gateway, so OK on autopilot moves money.</li> </ul> <h3 id="customers">Customers</h3> <ul> <li>Context menu: Read in Full (default), Customer Details, Edit Customer…, Properties….</li> <li>Customer Details: a report “Customer Details - {name}” with email and addresses. Help: “Personal details, read from WooCommerce now and kept only while this is open.” A role without list_users: “WooCommerce lets only roles that can list people read a customer’s details, and yours on {site} cannot.”</li> <li>Edit Customer…: “Change:” list (Name and email, Billing address, Shipping address), then one dialog per field, each filled. Preview “Edit Customer”, “Change {name}’s details on {site}.” Button “Change Customer”. Announced “Changed {name}’s details on {site}.” Undo: “Put {name}’s back.”</li> <li>Keyless: “Read when an order or a customer is in question (rule 9).” / “A customer’s details change rarely, behind a preview (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: editing a billing address is one dialog per address line, about ten in a row, and Cancel on the eighth loses the first seven.</li> </ul> <h3 id="coupons">Coupons</h3> <ul> <li>Context menu: Read in Full (default), Edit Coupon…, New Coupon…, Move to Trash…, Properties….</li> <li>New Coupon (Ctrl+N on Coupons) and Edit Coupon: dialogs “Code:”, “Discount:” (a percentage off, a fixed amount off the whole cart, a fixed amount off each product), “Amount:”, “Expires (YYYY-MM-DD, blank for never):”, “Uses allowed (blank for no limit):”. Preview button “Make Coupon” or “Change Coupon”. Announced “Made coupon {code} on {site}.” / “Changed coupon {code} on {site}.”</li> <li>Move to Trash (Delete): preview “Move Coupon to Trash”, lines “Coupon {code} moves to WooCommerce’s Trash on {site} and stops working at checkout.” and “Orders that used it keep their discount. Restore it from Marketing > Coupons in wp-admin.” Announced “Moved coupon {code} to the Trash on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: none for any coupon command.</li> <li>Review notes: on Edit Coupon the Discount list does not start on the coupon’s current kind, so a percent coupon edited for its date becomes a cart discount unless the person moves back to the first row.</li> </ul> <h3 id="shop-reports">Shop Reports</h3> <ul> <li>Rows: one sentence per report (sales this month, top sellers, totals of orders, products, customers, coupons and reviews). Enter reads its small table in the details pane.</li> <li>Context menu: Read in Full, Properties.</li> <li>F1 (row): “One WooCommerce report as a sentence; Enter reads its table.”</li> </ul> <h3 id="store-settings-change-setting">Store Settings: Change Setting…</h3> <ul> <li>Rows: one per setting, “group, setting, value” in words.</li> <li>What opens: “Change Setting - {label}”, a “New value:” list of WooCommerce’s choices, or a text box filled with the value in words.</li> <li>Preview: “Change Setting”, “Change a WooCommerce setting on {site}.”, lines “WooCommerce > Settings > {group} > {label} on {site}: {old} becomes {new}.” and “It takes effect for shoppers at once. Nothing else is changed.” Button “Change Setting”.</li> <li>Refusals: a password-style setting, “{label} is a password-style setting, so QUILL neither reads it aloud nor changes it. Change it in WooCommerce > Settings in wp-admin.”; a multi-select, “{label} takes several values at once; change it in WooCommerce > Settings in wp-admin, where each is a check box.”; same value, “That is its value already. Nothing was changed.”</li> <li>Announced after: “Changed {label} on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: none.</li> <li>Keyless: “Store settings change a few times a year (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: the choice list does not start on the current value, so a listener cannot hear what is set without leaving the dialog.</li> </ul> <h3 id="product-reviews">Product Reviews</h3> <ul> <li>Context menu: Read in Full (default), Approve, Hold, Mark as Spam, Reply…, Edit Review…, Move to Trash…, Properties….</li> <li>Approve, Hold, Mark as Spam: work on the selection (one site: “Change reviews on one site at a time.”).
