Preview “{Approved|Held|Marked as spam} Reviews”, “{n} review(s) on {site}.” Announced “{what}: {n} done.” or “… {m} not done.” with a report window when more than one or any failed.</li> <li>Reply…: “Reply - {title}”, a “Reply:” box; preview “Reply to Review”, “Under {reviewer}’s review of {product}:”, the text, “It shows on the product’s page as soon as it is sent.” Button “Send Reply”. Announced “Replied to the review on {site}.” Undo: “Moved the reply to the Trash.”</li> <li>Edit Review…: “Review:” then “Rating (1 to 5):”; preview lines “{Field}: {old} becomes {new}.” and “The reviewer is not told.” Announced “Changed the review on {site}.”</li> <li>Move to Trash (Delete): preview lines are each review’s details and “Ctrl+Z takes them out of the Trash again.”</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z on all of these.</li> <li>Review notes: the shop’s best-behaved area, with selection, outcomes, undo and conflict checks. It shows what the product, order and coupon commands are missing.</li> </ul> <h3 id="shipping-zones-tax-rates-tax-classes-attributes-brands-webhooks">Shipping Zones, Tax Rates, Tax Classes, Attributes, Brands, Webhooks</h3> <ul> <li>Zones: Locations… (one box, “country US, state US:AZ, postcode 85001, continent EU”, a comma between), Methods… (a list of methods plus “Add a method…”; on a method, “Do:” Turn it off/on, Change its cost, Remove it), New Shipping Zone…, Rename…, Delete…. The zone for everywhere else: “The zone for everywhere else covers what no other zone does; it has no locations.” / “The zone for everywhere else cannot be deleted; remove its methods instead.”</li> <li>Tax Rates: Edit Tax Rate… (Name, Rate (percent), Country code, State code, Postal codes, City), New Tax Rate…, Delete…. Delete preview: “There is no Trash. Ctrl+Z makes the rate again with the same fields.”</li> <li>Tax Classes: New Tax Class…, Delete… (“Every rate in it goes with it, and products in it are taxed as standard.”).</li> <li>Attributes: Terms… (a list of values plus add; on a value, “Do:” Rename it, Delete it), New Attribute…, Rename…, Delete….</li> <li>Brands: New Brand…, Rename…, Delete….</li> <li>Webhooks: Pause or Activate…, New Webhook… (Name, Send when, Delivery address; “it must start with https://”), Delete… (“Webhooks have no Trash, so this cannot be undone; Pause stops it without deleting it.”).</li> <li>Undo: made items delete again; renames and edits put back; a deleted tax rate is made again; other deletes have none.</li> <li>Keyless: “Set up once and changed a few times a year, behind a preview (rule 9).” Deletes: “A delete for good, with no Trash: never a bare key (rule 4).”</li> <li>Review notes: the Delete command for zones, attributes and brands is labelled “Delete…” while the help on the Item menu says Delete Permanently is “confirm by name”. Here it is a preview button, which is fine, but three words for the same idea (Delete, Delete Permanently, Remove) appear in one product.</li> <li>Review notes: removing a shipping method sends at once after the preview and records no undo.</li> </ul> <h2 id="events">Events</h2> <p>The Events Calendar lives at Sites > site > Events Calendar, read through tribe/events/v1. Upcoming events come first, and Past Events, Venues, Organizers, Event Categories and Event Tags are below it. Times are said in each event’s own time zone, with your computer’s time added. A recurring occurrence from Events Calendar Pro is refused for Edit, Trash and Categories and Tags, with a pointer to wp-admin. Writes are previewed. Events, venues and organizers have no undo; event categories and tags do.</p> <h3 id="where-events-appear-in-the-tree">Where events appear in the tree</h3> <ul> <li>Events Calendar (columns title, when, venue, status), F1: “The Events Calendar’s events from now on, soonest first, through tribe/events/v1. Ctrl+N makes one; times are in each event’s own time zone, and yours is said too.”</li> <li>Past Events, Venues (name, where, phone), Organizers (name, phone, website), Event Categories (name, slug, events), Event Tags (name, slug, events).</li> <li>An event’s details: “{title}, {when}, {zone} ({abbr}). On your computer’s clock it starts {time}. At {venue}. Cost {cost}.” and, for a recurring one, the recurring sentence.</li> <li>A site without the plugin: “{site} does not list The Events Calendar’s tribe/events/v1 routes in its REST index, so it has no events QUILL can read.”</li> </ul> <h3 id="events-context-menu">Events: context menu</h3> <ul> <li>Order: Open on the Site (default, Enter), Read in Full, Edit Event…, New Event…, Events Between Dates…, Copy Link, Properties…, then More: Move to Trash…, Categories and Tags….</li> <li>F1 (row): “An event in The Events Calendar. Edit Event changes it after a preview; Delete moves it to the Trash; Categories and Tags sets its terms. A recurring event says so and is changed in wp-admin.”</li> <li>Review notes: as with products, Enter goes to the browser.</li> </ul> <h3 id="new-event">New Event…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+N on Events Calendar or Past Events; the context menu.</li> <li>What opens: dialogs titled “New Event”: “Title:”, “Starts (YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM):”, “Ends (YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM):”, “Time zone (blank for the site’s):”, “Cost (blank for none):”, then “All day:” (No, it has times / Yes, all day), then “Venue:” (No venue, then each venue).</li> <li>Preview: “New Event”, “Make {title} on {site}.”, lines starting “A new event on {site}: {title}, {when}.” and “Times are in {zone}.” Button “Make Event”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Made {title} on {site}, as a draft.” Errors: “An event needs a title. Nothing was made.” / “The event ends before it starts. Nothing was made.”</li> <li>Undo: none.</li> <li>Keyless: “Ctrl+N (New Here) on its node runs it; the context menu has it too.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_event_commands.py, workbench_events.py, plugin_events.py.</li> <li>Review notes: there is no way to choose an organizer or categories at creation; categories need a second command.</li> <li>Review notes: typing “YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM” by hand is error-prone; no date picker or “tomorrow at 7” understanding.</li> </ul> <h3 id="edit-event">Edit Event…</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: the same five boxes, filled, then “All day:”, which does not start on the event’s current setting.</li> <li>Preview: “Edit Event”, “Change {title} on {site}.” Button “Change Event”. Announced “Changed {title} on {site}.” Nothing different: “Nothing about {title} would change.”</li> <li>Recurring: “This is one occurrence of a recurring series from Events Calendar Pro. The REST API gives each occurrence but not the series’ rule, so the series is changed or removed in Events in wp-admin, not here.”</li> <li>Undo: none.</li> <li>Keyless: “An event’s details change now and then, behind a preview (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: the help and the PLUGIN_ACTION_ROWS text both promise “title, start, end, time zone, venue and cost”, but Edit Event never asks for the venue; it keeps the old one. A wrong promise.</li> <li>Review notes: “All day:” starting on “No” means an all-day event edited for its cost becomes timed unless the person changes it back.</li> </ul> <h3 id="events-between-dates">Events Between Dates…</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: “From (YYYY-MM-DD):”, “To (YYYY-MM-DD):”, then a report “Events from {from} to {to} - {site}”, one event a line, or “No events in those dates.” Bad dates: “The last date is before the first.”</li> <li>Keyless: “Asked when planning, now and then (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: a report rather than a filtered list, so you cannot act on what you find.</li> </ul> <h3 id="move-to-trash-event-venue-organizer">Move to Trash… (event, venue, organizer)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Delete on the row; the context menu (under More for events).</li> <li>Preview: “Move {Event|Venue|Organizer} to Trash”, lines “{name} moves to the Trash on {site}.” and “It leaves the calendar; restore it from Events > Trash in wp-admin.” (venues: “Events at it lose their venue; restore it from Events > Venues > Trash.”; organizers: “Events lose this organizer; restore it from Events > Organizers > Trash.”).</li> <li>Announced after: “Moved {name} to the Trash on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: none; restore is a wp-admin hand-off.</li> </ul> <h3 id="categories-and-tags-event">Categories and Tags… (event)</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: “Categories and Tags - {title}”, “Categories (a comma between):” then “Tags (a comma between):”, each filled with the current names.</li> <li>Announced after: “Changed {title}’s categories and tags on {site}.” plus “ The site did not keep: …“ when it did not. A conflict: “{title}’s categories changed on {site} after QUILL read them…” Unchanged: “{title}’s categories and tags would not change.”</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z: “Put {title}’s terms back.”</li> <li>Keyless: “An event’s terms are set when it is made (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: names typed must match existing terms; there is no list to choose from, though QUILL has just read them.</li> </ul> <h3 id="venues-and-organizers">Venues and Organizers</h3> <ul> <li>Venue menu: Read in Full (default), Edit Venue…, New Venue…, Move to Trash…, Properties…. Organizer menu: the same with Organizer.</li> <li>New and Edit: “Name:”, then one dialog per field. Venue: Address, City, State or province, Postal code, Country, Phone, Website. Organizer: Phone, Website, Email. Preview button “Make Venue” / “Change Venue” (or Organizer). Announced “Made the venue {name} on {site}.” / “Changed the venue {name} on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: none.</li> <li>Review notes: eight dialogs to make a venue, and a blank answer on an edit clears that field, which the preview shows but which is easy to do by Enter-Enter-Enter.</li> </ul> <h3 id="event-categories-and-tags">Event Categories and Tags</h3> <ul> <li>Menu: Read in Full (default), New Event Category… (or New Event Tag…), Rename…, Delete…, Properties….</li> <li>New: one “Name:” box, preview “A new {category} on {site}: {name}.” Announced “Made the {category} {name} on {site}.” Undo deletes it: “Deleted the {category} again.”</li> <li>Rename: preview “{old} becomes {new}.” and “Its events keep it under the new name.” Undo: “Named it {old} again.”</li> <li>Delete: preview “There is no Trash: its events lose it, and keep everything else.” Announced “Deleted the {category} {name} on {site}.” No undo.</li> <li>Keyless: “A delete for good, with no Trash: never a bare key (rule 4).”</li> </ul> <h2 id="seo">SEO</h2> <p>SEO means Yoast SEO only, read through yoast/v1 and each item’s meta. Other SEO plugins are not read here. Sites > site > SEO lists the newest posts and pages with their SEO title, description, indexing and focus keyphrase. SEO > Site SEO gives the site’s scores and the jobs only Yoast’s own screens can do. Search Appearance reads what a search engine shows. Edit SEO changes one item. The Bulk SEO Editor changes many items from one list. Edits are previewed and undo with Ctrl+Z.</p> <h3 id="where-seo-appears-in-the-tree">Where SEO appears in the tree</h3> <ul> <li>SEO: columns title, seo_title, description, index, keyphrase. F1: “Yoast SEO for the newest posts and pages: the title and description search engines are given, whether it is indexed, and its focus keyphrase. Edit SEO changes them.”</li> <li>SEO > Site SEO: columns what, says. Rows include, among the scores, the wp-admin-only jobs: “Search appearance for each content type”, “Site representation and social profiles”, “Optimise SEO data (indexing)”, “Redirects, schema and breadcrumbs”, “Related keyphrases and rank tracking”, “Yoast’s AI features”, each with why and Enter opening wp-admin.</li> <li>No Yoast: “{site} does not list Yoast SEO’s yoast/v1 routes in its REST index, so QUILL has no Yoast SEO to read there. Titles and descriptions another SEO plugin registers are on each item’s Properties.”</li> <li>Review notes: the node is called “SEO” but is Yoast-only. A Rank Math or All in One SEO site hears only the error.</li> </ul> <h3 id="seo-item-context-menu">SEO item context menu</h3> <ul> <li>Order: Search Appearance (default, Enter), Edit SEO…, Open on the Site, Bulk SEO Editor…, Properties….</li> <li>F1 (row): “A post or page with its Yoast SEO. Search Appearance reads what a search engine shows; Edit SEO changes the title, description, keyphrase, indexing and social title, description and image; the Bulk SEO Editor changes many in one window.”</li> </ul> <h3 id="search-appearance">Search Appearance</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: a report “Search Appearance - {title}” with the title, address and description a search engine shows, each with “… (cut off after {n} characters)” where it would be cut.</li> <li>Keyless: “Enter runs it on an SEO row, its default.”</li> </ul> <h3 id="edit-seo">Edit SEO…</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: dialogs titled “Edit SEO - {title}”: “SEO title:”, “Meta description:”, “Focus keyphrase:”, “Social title:”, “Social description:”, “Social image address:”, then a list “Show in search results:” (as the content type’s default; no, kept out of search results; yes, shown in search results).</li> <li>Preview: “Edit SEO”, “Change Yoast SEO for {title} on {site}.”, the old and new of each changed field, for example “{Social title}: not set becomes {new}.” Button “Change SEO”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Changed Yoast SEO for {title} on {site}.” plus “ The site did not keep: …“ when it did not. Nothing different: “Nothing about {title}’s SEO would change.” and, where fields cannot be written, “ {fields} cannot be written here; Yoast’s panel does it.“</li> <li>Undo: Ctrl+Z: “Put {title}’s SEO back.”</li> <li>Keyless: “SEO fields are set once per post, behind a preview (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_terms_seo.py, workbench_seo.py, workbench_seo_bulk.py.</li> <li>Review notes: seven dialogs for one item, and the indexing list does not start on the current setting.</li> <li>Review notes: no running character count while typing the SEO title, though Search Appearance knows the limits.</li> </ul> <h3 id="bulk-seo-editor">Bulk SEO Editor…</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: a list window “Bulk SEO Editor - {site}”. Purpose: “Every post and page with its Yoast SEO title and description. Enter changes the selected one where it stands, after a preview; Ctrl+Z in the Workbench undoes it.” List “Posts and pages:”, each item’s title and the length of its SEO title and description; a details area; a button “Change SEO Title and Description…”. Enter does the same.</li> <li>Editing one: “Edit SEO - {name}”, “SEO title:” then “Meta description:”, then a preview. Announced “Changed Yoast SEO for {name} on {site}.” Locked: “Your role cannot edit {name}, so Yoast keeps its SEO locked.”</li> <li>No routes: “{site}’s Yoast SEO has no bulk editor routes; it came with Yoast SEO 28.” Empty: “Yoast SEO’s bulk editor gave no posts or pages on {site}.”</li> <li>Requirement: wpseo_manage_options (greyed without it).</li> <li>Keyless: “A window opened for an SEO pass now and then (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: the Bulk SEO Editor is on every SEO item’s menu, though it opens the whole site.</li> </ul> <h3 id="open-in-dashboard-site-seo-row">Open in Dashboard (Site SEO row)</h3> <ul> <li>Enter on a Site SEO job row opens “Yoast’s own screen for this, in your browser.”</li> <li>Review notes: half the Site SEO node is pointers to wp-admin. That is honest, but it is the plugin’s hand-off.</li> </ul> <h2 id="memberships">Memberships</h2> <p>Paid Memberships Pro shows up in two places. Sites > site > Memberships lists levels, orders, subscriptions, renewals due and discount codes, read through PMPro’s Abilities API (WordPress 6.9 and PMPro 3.9 or later). On a person in Users, the context menu has Show Memberships, Check Membership Access, Change Membership Level and Cancel Membership, and the person’s Properties sheet gains an Access page. Membership orders are kept apart from WooCommerce’s shop orders, and every surface says so. Changes are previewed, with the subscription and gateway that stop named. Nothing here has an undo.</p> <h3 id="where-memberships-appear-in-the-tree">Where memberships appear in the tree</h3> <ul> <li>Memberships (no list of its own): F1 “Paid Memberships Pro levels, orders, subscriptions and renewals, when its Abilities API is available.”</li> <li>Membership Levels: name, signups, initial, billing, expires. Detail: “{name}. Signups: … Initial payment: … Recurring billing: … Expires after: …”</li> <li>Orders: code, status, total, gateway, date, member, level. F1 “Paid Memberships Pro order summaries, not WooCommerce shop orders. Card and billing details are never shown.”</li> <li>Subscriptions and Renewals Due (due now or within thirty days): subscription, status, next payment, billing, gateway, member.</li> <li>Discount Codes: code, uses, used, starts, expires, levels. F1 “Paid Memberships Pro discount codes. Look Up a Discount Code reads one by its code; New Discount Code makes one after a preview. The full list is in wp-admin unless the site offers a discount-codes ability.”</li> <li>Needs My Attention > Membership Orders: pending, in review or failed membership orders on every site.</li> <li>Without the abilities: one row, “Paid Memberships Pro abilities are not exposed on this site. They require WordPress 6.9 or later and Paid Memberships Pro 3.9 or later.”</li> <li>Review notes: the Discount Codes list is usually just a pointer row to wp-admin, so the node promises a list it rarely has.</li> </ul> <h3 id="membership-row-context-menu-every-pmpro-row">Membership row context menu (every pmpro row)</h3> <ul> <li>Order: Read in Full (default), Look Up a Discount Code…, New Discount Code…, Properties….</li> <li>F1 (row): “A Paid Memberships Pro level, membership order, subscription or discount code. Alt+Enter reads its details.”</li> <li>Review notes: the two discount-code commands appear on a level, an order and a subscription row too, where they have nothing to do with the row. A level row has no level command at all.</li> <li>Review notes: Properties is a raw read-only sheet of the item’s fields.</li> </ul> <h3 id="look-up-a-discount-code">Look Up a Discount Code…</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: “Look Up a Discount Code”, a “Code:” box.</li> <li>Announced after: the code’s sentence, “Discount code {code}: {n} uses, used {m} times, starts {date}, expires {date}, for {levels}.” Not found: “There is no discount code {code} on {site}.”</li> <li>Keyless: “A code is looked up when a member asks about it (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: the answer is only spoken. There is no window to read it again or copy it.</li> </ul> <h3 id="new-discount-code">New Discount Code…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+N on Discount Codes; the context menu.</li> <li>What opens: five dialogs titled “New Discount Code”: “Code:”, “Level numbers (a comma between):” (“From Memberships > Membership Levels.”), “Initial payment with the code:”, “How many uses (0 for unlimited):”, “Expires (YYYY-MM-DD, or blank for never):”.</li> <li>Preview: “New Discount Code”, “Paid Memberships Pro on {site} gets this discount code.”, lines “Code {CODE} on {site}: {uses}, starts {today}, expires {date}.” and per level “Level {n} (level {n}): initial payment {amount}, …”. Button “Make the Code”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Made the discount code {CODE} on {site}.” Missing parts: “A discount code needs a code and at least one level, so nothing was made.”</li> <li>Requirement: manage_options.</li> <li>Undo: none.</li> <li>Review notes: levels must be typed as numbers learned in wp-admin, though the Membership Levels node lists them. The preview passes no level names, so it says “Level 2 (level 2)”, which says the number twice and the name never.</li> <li>Review notes: the code is upper-cased silently.</li> </ul> <h3 id="show-memberships-on-a-person">Show Memberships… (on a person)</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Users > a person > context menu “Show Memberships…” (third row).</li> <li>What opens: a report “Paid Memberships Pro - {name}”, one level a line, “{level}: {status}, started {date}, ends {date}”, or “No current or past memberships were returned.” Help mentions payment card details are not included.</li> <li>Without PMPro: “{site} exposes neither the Paid Memberships Pro member-memberships ability nor its pmpro/v1 routes.”</li> <li>Review notes: the dates are the site’s raw strings, not friendly dates as elsewhere.</li> <li>Review notes: the membership rows (Show, Check Access, Change, Cancel) are on every person’s menu on every site, PMPro or not. Not found in code: any hiding or greying of them on a site without PMPro.</li> </ul> <h3 id="check-membership-access">Check Membership Access…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: the person’s context menu (fourth row); also Properties > Access page > “Check a Post or Page…” button.</li> <li>What opens: “Check Membership Access - {name}”, “Post or page (its number, or words from its title):”.</li> <li>Announced after: “{name} can see {post} on {site}, through {levels}.” or “{name} cannot see {post} on {site}. It needs one of these levels: {levels}.” Nothing matches: “Nothing on {site} matches {words}.” On the Access page, the answer lands in the page’s box instead.</li> <li>Keyless: “Asked when a member reports a locked page, now and then.”</li> <li>Review notes: words from a title take the first search result without saying so or offering a choice. A common word can check the wrong page.</li> </ul> <h3 id="change-membership-level">Change Membership Level…</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: “Change Membership Level - {name}”, a “New level:” list (“{name} (level {id})”) when the site has the levels ability; otherwise a “Level number:” box (“From Memberships > Membership Levels in wp-admin.”).</li> <li>Preview: “Change Membership Level”, “Give {name} {level} on {site}.”, lines “{name} gets {level} on {site} now, with no checkout and no payment taken.”, what ends (“{old} ends.”) and each subscription that stops, or “They have no current membership, so nothing they have ends.” and “Cancel Membership takes it away again, after its own preview.” Button “Change Level”.</li> <li>Announced after: “{name} now has {level} on {site}.” Same level: “{name} already has {level}. Nothing changed.” Not a number: “A level is a number, so nothing changed.”</li> <li>Undo: none.</li> <li>Keyless: “Members change level at checkout; staff do it rarely (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: the fallback asks for a number from wp-admin.</li> <li>Review notes: no undo for a change that stops a recurring payment; the preview says so only for cancellation.</li> </ul> <h3 id="cancel-membership">Cancel Membership…</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: when the person has more than one current level, “Cancel Membership - {name}”, “Level to cancel:”.</li> <li>Preview: “Cancel Membership”, “Cancel {name}’s {level} membership on {site}.”, lines “{name} loses {level} on {site} now.”, each subscription and gateway asked to stop (or “QUILL found no active recurring subscription for it, so no gateway is asked.”), “Nothing is refunded: refunds are made in the gateway or in Memberships > Orders.”, “This cannot be undone from QUILL. A cancelled subscription is not restarted; the person would check out again.” Button “Cancel Membership”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Cancelled {name}’s {level} membership on {site}.” No membership: “{name} has no current membership on {site}.”</li> <li>Tier: destructive. Undo: none.</li> <li>Keyless: “A cancellation is rare and stops payments: never a bare key.”</li> <li>Review notes: the dialog’s own Cancel button sits next to “Cancel Membership”. A listener hears “Cancel Membership, button” and “Cancel, button” side by side, and Escape is the safe one. A button named “End Membership” would avoid the clash.</li> <li>Review notes: tier 3, but confirmed by one button, while a refund asks for the amount typed.</li> </ul> <h3 id="the-members-access-page-properties">The member’s Access page (Properties)</h3> <ul> <li>Alt+Enter on a person, on a site with pmpro/v1, adds a page “Access” with a read-only box “Can this member see it (Paid Memberships Pro)”, first saying “Choose Check a Post or Page, then type a post or page’s number or words from its title. Paid Memberships Pro says whether this member can see it.” The button “Check a Post or Page…” works on read-only sheets too.</li> </ul> <h2 id="redirects">Redirects</h2> <p>Redirects come from the Redirection plugin’s redirection/v1 namespace, under Sites > site > Redirects with its 404 Log below. You can add, change, turn on or off, delete and test redirects, and turn a 404 into a redirect. After you change a post’s slug, QUILL offers a redirect from the old address. Redirects that Yoast SEO Premium or Rank Math keep are not read; their screens open in wp-admin. No redirect command records an undo.</p> <h3 id="where-redirects-appear-in-the-tree">Where redirects appear in the tree</h3> <ul> <li>Redirects: columns source, target, code, hits, state (“on” or “off”). Detail: “{source} leads to {target} with a {code} redirect, on; used {n} times, last on {date}.” plus “ The source is a pattern (regular expression).“ F1: “The Redirection plugin’s redirects: add with Ctrl+N, change, test, turn off.”</li> <li>Redirects > 404 Log: columns address, when, from. Detail: “{address} was asked for and not found on {date}, from {referrer}. Redirect This Address makes a redirect from it.” F1: “Addresses visitors asked for that were not found; redirect any of them.”</li> <li>Without the plugin: the node holds one Administration Areas row for Redirects, whose words include “Redirection; Yoast SEO Premium and Rank Math keep redirects in their own screens”. Any command says “{site} does not have the Redirection plugin’s REST namespace, so QUILL cannot change its redirects. Administration Areas says where they are kept.”</li> <li>Lists read at most five pages of 200.</li> </ul> <h3 id="redirect-row-context-menu">Redirect row context menu</h3> <ul> <li>Order: Read in Full (default), Test an Address…, Add Redirect…, Change Redirect…, Turn On or Off, Delete Redirect…, Open on the Site, Properties….</li> <li>404 row: Read in Full (default), Redirect This Address…, Test an Address…, Properties….</li> <li>F1 (rows): redirect “One redirect: from, to, its code and hits. Test an Address follows it.”; 404 “An address that was not found. Redirect This Address sends it somewhere.”</li> <li>Properties: a raw read-only sheet of the plugin’s fields.</li> </ul> <h3 id="add-redirect">Add Redirect…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Ctrl+N on Redirects; the context menu.</li> <li>What opens: three dialogs titled “Add Redirect”: “Old address, from this site (such as /old-page/):”, “New address it leads to:”, then “Kind of redirect:” (301, moved for good; 302, found elsewhere for now; 307, for now; 308, for good). If the site has more than one group, “Group to keep it in:” (“Redirections” first; a turned-off group says “(turned off)”).</li> <li>Preview: “Redirect Preview”, “On {site}: add a redirect.”, lines “From: {path}”, “To: {target}”, “Kind: {code}”. Button “Save Redirect”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Saved on {site}: {from} now leads to {to}.” No group: “The Redirection plugin on {site} has no group to keep a redirect in. Add one under Tools, Redirection, Groups, then try again.”</li> <li>Requirement: manage_options. Undo: none.</li> <li>Keyless: “Ctrl+N (New Here) on Redirects reaches it; no chord of its own.”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_sweep.py, workbench_redirects.py, workbench_sweep_places.py.</li> <li>Review notes: no undo for a new redirect, though deleting what was just made would be safe.</li> <li>Review notes: the preview does not warn when the new redirect makes a chain or loop, though Test an Address can work that out from the list already read.</li> </ul> <h3 id="change-redirect">Change Redirect…</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: the same three dialogs, titled “Change Redirect”, filled. The group is kept, or asked when unknown.</li> <li>Preview: “Redirect Preview”, “On {site}: change this redirect.” Button “Save Redirect”. Announced as Add.</li> <li>Undo: none.</li> </ul> <h3 id="turn-on-or-off">Turn On or Off</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: a Yes/No question, title “Turn Off” (or “Turn On”), “Turn Off the redirect from {source} on {site}?”, buttons “Turn Off” and “Cancel”, with Cancel the default.</li> <li>Announced after: “The redirect from {source} is off.” / “… is on.”</li> <li>Undo: none.</li> <li>Review notes: a reversible on/off with a confirmation but no Ctrl+Z is backwards: every other on/off in the Workbench undoes.</li> </ul> <h3 id="delete-redirect">Delete Redirect…</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: a Yes/No question, “Delete Redirect”, “Delete the redirect from {source} to {target} on {site}? The Redirection plugin keeps no Trash, so it is gone for good.” Buttons “Delete Redirect” and “Cancel” (default).</li> <li>Announced after: “Deleted the redirect from {source} on {site}.”</li> <li>Undo: none.</li> <li>Keyless: “A destructive, rare job: never a bare key (rule 4).”</li> <li>Review notes: the row help says “Gone for good, after you confirm by name”, and the Administration Areas text says “deleted after a confirmation by name”. The code asks a plain Yes/No; nothing is typed. The help is wrong.</li> <li>Review notes: QUILL has the redirect’s fields, so an undo that makes it again would be easy and would match feeds and tax rates.</li> </ul> <h3 id="test-an-address-1">Test an Address…</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: “Test an Address”, “Address on this site to follow:”, filled with the row’s source. Help: “QUILL follows the redirects it read from this site. Nothing is sent.”</li> <li>Then a report “Test an Address” with, for example, “/a/ leads to /c/: /a/ to /b/ (301) then /b/ to /c/ (301). A chain: one redirect straight to the end is faster. Tested here on the list as read; the plugin’s own options, such as case, trailing slashes and query parameters, decide in the end. Open on the Site tries it live.” A loop: “{address} goes round in a loop: … Visitors get an error; change one of these redirects.” None: “No redirect read from this site matches {address}.” Blank: “Give an address on the site, such as /old-page/.”</li> <li>Keyless: “Asked when a link is reported broken, now and then (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: a clear, useful answer. It uses the list as last read, so a redirect added in wp-admin since is missed until F5.</li> </ul> <h3 id="redirect-this-address-404">Redirect This Address… (404)</h3> <ul> <li>What opens: the Add Redirect dialogs titled “Redirect This Address”, with the 404’s address filled in.</li> <li>Announced after: “Saved on {site}: {from} now leads to {to}.”</li> <li>Keyless: “Reviewing the 404 log is a monthly job (rule 9).”</li> <li>Review notes: the 404 stays in the log after it is redirected; nothing marks it done.</li> </ul> <h3 id="add-a-redirect-after-a-new-slug">Add a Redirect after a new slug</h3> <ul> <li>When it opens: after a post’s slug is saved on a site with Redirection, and the address changed.</li> <li>What opens: a Yes/No question, “Add a Redirect”, “{title} moved from {old} to {new}. Add a redirect on {site}, so links to the old address still arrive?”, buttons “Add Redirect” and “Cancel”.</li> <li>Announced after: “Redirect added: {old} now leads to {new}.” No group: “No redirect added: the Redirection plugin on {site} has no group to keep one in.”</li> <li>Review notes: it uses the default group without asking, unlike Add Redirect, and a 301 without offering the kind.</li> </ul> <h2 id="multisite">Multisite</h2> <p>QUILL has no multisite network administration. WordPress’s REST API has no network routes, so there is no Network Sites node, no network users or network themes, and no list of a network’s subsites. Each subsite is connected and worked on as its own site in the Sites node. Three things touch networks: Promote to Network on a plugin in Across Sites, a Network Admin row in each site’s wp-admin Screens list, and the scope matrix’s reasons, which explain why each network-wide operation is not offered.</p> <h3 id="what-there-is-not">What there is not</h3> <ul> <li>Not found in code: a node for network sites, wp/v2/sites, any detection that a connected site belongs to a network beyond the plugin-schema check below, or a way to add a subsite.</li> <li>The design log (docs/design/wordpress.md, “Multisite network admin”) records the choice: “core registers no network-admin routes at all. Sites, network users, network plugins and network themes are wp-admin only. On a subsite, the ordinary routes work per site, and QUILL already treats each subsite as its own connection.”</li> <li>The scope matrix (workbench_scope_matrix.py) answers every network cell with a reason, such as “WordPress’s REST API has no network-wide form of this; each site of a network is changed as its own site.”, “Network-enabling a theme has no REST route; use Network Admin > Themes.”, “Making a super admin has no REST route; use Network Admin.”, “Network settings have no REST route; use Network Admin.”, “Application passwords are per site, even on a network.”</li> <li>Review notes: a person managing a 20-site network must connect each subsite by hand, with its own application password. Nothing says this in the Sites node or in F1.</li> </ul> <h3 id="promote-to-network-1">Promote to Network…</h3> <ul> <li>How to reach it: Go > Across Sites (Ctrl+0), then Plugins, then a plugin row, context menu “Promote to Network…”.</li> <li>What it does: for each site that has the plugin, QUILL asks the site’s plugin schema (OPTIONS wp/v2/plugins) whether “network-active” is a status. Only a multisite offers it.</li> <li>Refusals: on a non-plugin row, “Promote to Network works on a plugin, in Across Sites > Plugins. {reason}”, for example “WordPress has no network-wide terms.” No network: “{site} is not a multisite network: its plugin schema has no network-active status, so the plugin stays per site.” Not allowed: “Promote to Network needs a super admin of {site}’s network.”</li> <li>Practice crossing: when the practice site and real sites are both in scope, a Yes/No question titled “Promote to Network: the Practice Site and Real Sites Together”, with “Change {site} Too” or “Change Them Too”, Cancel the default.</li> <li>Preview: “Promote to Network”, summary “{plugin} runs on every site of each network below, and no single site can deactivate it. WordPress refuses unless you are a super admin.” Lines: “{site}: becomes network-active” for each, then “Not changed. {why}” for the others. Button “Promote”.</li> <li>Announced after: “{plugin}: network-active on {good} of {n} networks.” A report “Promote to Network: Each Site” when any failed, “Done. …” or “Not done. …” a line.</li> <li>Requirement: the row names activate_plugins; the handler also checks it on each site before asking. Undo: none (recorded without one, so Ctrl+Z says it cannot be undone and stops there).</li> <li>Keyless: “Done once per plugin on a network, rarely (rule 9).”</li> <li>Code: wordpress_workbench_across.py, workbench_across_ops.py, workbench_places.py, workbench_scope_matrix.py.</li> <li>Review notes: when no site qualifies, only the first site’s reason is spoken; the others are lost.</li> <li>Review notes: each connected subsite counts as a network, so two subsites of one network say “network-active on 2 of 2 networks”, and WordPress is asked twice.</li> <li>Review notes: there is no “Demote” or network-deactivate, and no undo, for a change the summary itself calls one no single site can reverse.</li> <li>Review notes: the check activate_plugins is a single-site capability; the real requirement (super admin) is only known when WordPress refuses.</li> </ul> <h3 id="network-admin-row-wp-admin-screens">Network Admin row (wp-admin Screens)</h3> <ul> <li>Where: Sites > site > Overview > wp-admin Screens, a row “Network Admin”, address network/, “Only on a multisite network, and only for a Super Admin; REST has no network routes.” Enter opens it in the browser.</li> <li>Review notes: the row is listed on every site, single sites included (it has no namespace gate), so a single-site owner is offered a screen that does not exist.</li> </ul> <h3 id="plugin-rows-on-a-subsite">Plugin rows on a subsite</h3> <ul> <li>A plugin that is network-active counts as active (site_admin.py and do_toggle_plugin treat “network-active” as active).</li> <li>Review notes: Activate or Deactivate offers “Deactivate” on a network-active plugin and sends status inactive from the subsite. QUILL does not check whether WordPress allows that from a subsite; any refusal is the site’s own error, spoken as it comes.</li> </ul> <h2 id="review-notes">Review notes</h2> <p>The review notes moved to the retired root <code>complete.md</code> on 2026-11-18; current implementation status is now in <code>report.md</code>. This walkthrough moved here from the root <code>how.md</code> the same day.</p> </main> </body> </html>
